CRITICAL: `allowed_tabs` is stamped into the session at LOGIN (admin-login returns it → `setSession`), so a newly-granted admin must LOG OUT and back IN for the tab to appear/work — an existing session won't pick it up. Any NEW action added behind one of these tabs must be added to the correct `TAB_ACTIONS[...]` list or a granted non-superadmin admin will get 403. Supersedes the old "Automation + Accounting are superadmin-only" language throughout the docs.

**168. There are TWO unrelated "CIQ" features; the SPECIALIST CIQ-topics one was fully REMOVED (2026-07-01) — any remaining `ciq_*` reference belongs to the MEMBER feature.** GONE: the specialist/expert "CIQ Topics" tag feature — table `ciq_assignments` (dropped), `load_data`'s `ciq` array, the `ciqMap` (expert_id→topics) built in `AdminPortal`, the CIQ handling in `save_specialist`/`delete_specialist`, and the "CIQ Topics" section + `CIQ_TOPICS` list in `SpecialistsPanel` (add/edit forms + profile view). UNTOUCHED and unrelated: the member "CIQ One Page Plan" — tables `client_ciqs`/`ciq_priorities`/`ciq_answers`/`ciq_priority_snapshots`, the `ciq_load_*`/`ciq_create`/`ciq_save*` actions (`actions/ciq/`), `components/shared/MemberCIQ.jsx`, and `members.ciq_enabled`/`ciq_vfos_managed`. If you see `ciq` in the codebase now, it is the member feature — do not assume it is specialist topics.

**169. The frontend is THEMED — every color in new frontend code must be a `var(--vfo-*)` reference, never a raw palette hex (2026-07-02).** Dark mode works by swapping the `--vfo-*` palette under `[data-theme="dark"]` in `src/index.css`; a hardcoded `#ffffff` background or `#16264a`/`#002973` text renders as an unreadable light box / invisible navy text in dark mode (this bit the KPI stat cards and the Accounting `color: NAVY` headings). Rules: card/page/tint/input surfaces + borders + ink/muted/faint text → the matching var; navy as TEXT → `var(--vfo-heading)` (flips to white in dark); navy inside GRADIENTS (headers/accent strips) → keep literal `#002973`; brand blues (`#125ecc`, `#0095ff`, `#0a85e8`) and status colors (greens/reds/ambers) are theme-fixed literals; white TEXT on colored buttons stays `#fff`. The theme is applied per-portal via `usePortalTheme()` (`src/lib/theme.js`, localStorage key `vfo_theme`, `data-theme` on `<html>`); login pages, the role picker, and ALL public token pages are always light — a new portal-level page must call `usePortalTheme()`, a new public page must NOT.

**170. `MemberShowroom` builds its CSS by appending hex-alpha suffixes (`${color}33`), so its dark palette must be RAW HEXES, not `var(--vfo-*)` (2026-07-02).** The in-portal showroom reads `document.documentElement.dataset.theme` at render and swaps `bg_color`/`text_color` to dark literals (`#001b4f`/`#eef3ff`); `var(...)33` would produce invalid CSS. The website widget (`vfo-widget` bucket) is a separate file and keeps its member-configured colors — untouched by portal theming.

**171. Lost/Removed members CANNOT log in (member_login 403) but their `member_logins` row is kept (2026-07-02, v535).** `actions/auth/member-login.ts` checks `members.elite_status` AFTER passcode verification (so a wrong password still reads "Invalid credentials" and doesn't leak status) and returns 403 "Your membership is no longer active…" for Lost/Removed. Flip the member back to Active and the same credentials work immediately — never delete login rows to block access. Client/specialist logins are separate types and unaffected; the legacy `login` action only authenticates admins so there is no bypass. Existing sessions survive until their 8h expiry — the block is at login only.

**172. In-place bulk edits (perl -pi / sed) while `npm run dev` runs make Vite HMR ingest half-written files — the preview browser can wedge on a stale error overlay even after the files are fixed (2026-07-02).** `vite build` passing while the dev overlay still shows an HMR failure means the overlay is stale: restart the dev server/preview rather than debugging phantom syntax errors. Prefer single atomic writes (Node script writing each file once) over multiple sequential in-place substitutions when the dev server is up.

**173. The 90-Day-Plan training track (`program_training_tasks`) now has THREE `task_type` values — `'section'` rows are label-only and must be excluded from EVERY completion count (2026-07-02).** The training track is still a 2-level DB model (`program_training_phases` → `program_training_tasks`); "sections with sub-steps" are faked with `task_type`: `'section'` = a bold sub-heading (no status dropdown, no `status_options`, NEVER counted), `'substep'` = a normal checkable step rendered indented inside the enclosing section box, `'dropdown'` (default/legacy) = a normal flush step. Both `MSMTracking.jsx` (admin) and `MemberMSMTracking.jsx` (member) share two module-level helpers: `countableTasks(list)` = `list.filter(t => t.task_type !== 'section')` — used in totalTasks, `getPhaseState`, the per-phase `In progress · X/Y` chip, the two "90 Day Plan / phase complete" `.every()` counts, `PlanStatusBadge`, and the member `TrackHero` steps; and `groupTasks(list)` which folds each section + its contiguous sub-steps into one node so the UI can enclose the group in a tinted box. **If you add a new consumer of `program_training_tasks` that counts completion, it MUST filter out `'section'` rows or phases will never complete.** Backend needed NO change (`load-training-track.ts` selects `*`). Applied to VFO Holistic Planning (program 1) + Partnership Fast Track (program 2), MSM 1–4. Also fixed here: the admin phase title used a hardcoded navy (`#002973`) invisible in dark mode → now `var(--vfo-heading)` (see gotcha #169).

**174. Wistia training videos: `VideoTask` iframes a `fast.wistia.net/embed/iframe/<mediaId>` URL, and Wistia `/s/<slug>` share links must be resolved to that via oEmbed BEFORE storing in `program_training_tasks.video_url` (2026-07-02).** The member `VideoTask` (`MemberMSMTracking.jsx`) branches on `url.includes('wistia') || url.includes('loom')`: those → responsive 16:9 `<iframe src={video_url}>`; else YouTube via the YT IFrame API. **Loom** (added 2026-07-02) rides the same iframe branch, but its share links `https://www.loom.com/share/<id>?sid=…` must be rewritten to `https://www.loom.com/embed/<id>` before storing (strip the `?sid`) — no oEmbed needed. Jake supplies Wistia **share** links like `https://elitert.wistia.com/s/<slug>` — those are share PAGES (not iframe-able; wrong X-Frame + not just the player), and the `<slug>` is NOT the media hashed id. Convert each with Wistia's oEmbed: `GET https://fast.wistia.com/oembed?url=<share-url>` → the JSON `html`/embed carries the real `https://fast.wistia.net/embed/iframe/<mediaId>` (mediaId ~10 chars) — store THAT. The oEmbed `title` is a free correctness check that the link maps to the intended sub-step. **Second route, used 2026-08-31 when oEmbed was not to hand: the share PAGE's own meta tags carry both answers** — `<meta name="twitter:player" content="https://fast.wistia.net/embed/iframe/<mediaId>?twitter=true">` and `<meta name="twitter:title" content="<video title>">`; `curl` the share URL and grep for them (a markdown-converting fetch strips them, so read the raw HTML). **Why this trap costs more than it looks:** a stored share link does NOT fail loudly — it contains the string `wistia`, so it takes the iframe branch like a valid embed and frames the share page, so the symptom is a rendered-but-wrong box, not an error. Admin `MSMTracking.jsx` does NOT render video (member-only) — it always shows the status dropdown, so setting `video_url` doesn't change the admin view. Adding a new video later = one `UPDATE program_training_tasks SET video_url=… WHERE id=…` (the player already handles it); no code change. As of this session **all 28** sub-steps have `video_url` set (Holistic 12, PFT 16) — a mix of Wistia and Loom. Wistia links play on the live site (shipped in `live-34`); the **Loom** ones only play once the `VideoTask` Loom branch deploys (pending on branch `claude/tax3-green-fix`). Wistia media URLs also come as `/medias/<id>` (the last path segment IS the hashed id — embed directly, no oEmbed needed).

**175. The Regular-Priorities MAP 4 follow-up is a 3-tier ladder (follow-up → reminder → stall bell), each tier gated by `map4_form_submitted_at IS NULL` and stamped with its own idempotency column (2026-07-02, v536).** `actions/regular/map4-followup-sweep.ts` (daily cron jobid 11 @09:30, `automation_REGULAR_map4followup_sweep`) runs THREE passes in one invocation: (1) **follow-up email** — `map4_meeting_date <= today-2` AND `map4_followup_sent_at IS NULL` → drafts `REGULAR_map4followup`, stamps `map4_followup_sent_at`; (2) **reminder email** — `map4_followup_sent_at <= now()-2d` AND form not submitted AND `map4_reminder_sent_at IS NULL` → drafts `REGULAR_map4reminder` (template id 174), stamps `map4_reminder_sent_at`; (3) **stall bell** — `map4_reminder_sent_at <= now()-2d` AND form not submitted AND `map4_stall_notified_at IS NULL` → inserts `notifications` (dismissible FYI) to **Tracy (`tnmiller@elitert.com`) + the assigned PF** via `pfNotificationRecipient(assigned_pf)` (falls back to the shared `admin` bell), stamps `map4_stall_notified_at`. Tiers 2/3 are **event-based** (keyed off the prior step's timestamp, not the meeting date) so a delayed follow-up never double-fires. Timing basis differs from the meeting-date tier 1 on purpose. Columns `map4_reminder_sent_at` + `map4_stall_notified_at` were added to `client_priority_tracks` (migration `add_map4_reminder_stall_columns`). Client SUBMISSION (`submit-map4-form.ts`) notifies **Tracy + assigned PF** too — **Tray V-D (`tvaldes@elitert.com`) was removed from ALL MAP 4 notifications** this session. Any NEW consumer that decides "is this MAP 4 step done" must respect these three columns, not just `map4_followup_sent_at`. Test by backdating the relevant prior-step timestamp (e.g. `map4_followup_sent_at = now()-interval '3 days'`) with the form unsubmitted, then firing the cron.


**176. EVERY in-portal bell notification now routes through `utils/notify.ts notifyByRule(supabase, ruleKey, {...})` — a raw `supabase.from("notifications").insert()` bypasses the Notification Editor and is a regression (2026-07-03, v537/v538).** The `notification_rules` table (130 rows, deny-all RLS, edited in Automation -> Notification Editor) can override recipients (jsonb array; dynamic tokens `ASSIGNED_PF`/`TEAM_MEMBER`/`ASSIGNED_ADMIN` resolved per-call from the site's `dynamic` map), disable the notification (`enabled=false`), and — for sweep tiers — change the delay (`delay_days`, read by sweeps via `getRuleConfig()`). `recipients=null` = the code default passed as `defaultRecipients`, so an unedited system behaves exactly as before. A NEW notification needs: a rule key at the call site + a seed row in `notification_rules` (else it fires with code defaults but is INVISIBLE in the editor). Sweeps must check `cfg[key]?.enabled === false` BEFORE stamping the tier's idempotency column, so a disabled tier can still fire after re-enabling. Wrapper helpers all take the key: `notifyJakeFailure({ruleKey,...})`, `insertTaxNotifications(sb, key, recipients, row, dynamic?)`, `notifyTracyClientPaid({ruleKey,...})`, `notifyAssignedPf(sb, key, ...)`. `clearJakeFailure*()` now clears by TITLE for any recipient (editor re-routes must not break auto-clears).

**177. `notification_rules.kind='email'` rows gate a sweep reminder-email's TIMING and on/off only — recipient overrides on them are silently ignored by design (2026-07-03).** The Gmail draft targets the stalled party (client/advisor/accountant/specialist) directly; its wording lives in Email Templates. The Notification Editor renders these rows without a recipients editor, but the API would accept a recipients save — it just has no effect.

**178. DELETING a `notification_rules` row does NOT silence its notification — a missing rule means "use code defaults", so the bell fires again and vanishes from the editor (2026-07-03).** To kill a notification: set `enabled=false` (stays visible, dimmed OFF, reversible). To remove one permanently: delete the row AND remove/revert the `notifyByRule` call in code (this is how the three LOGIN_*_setup_completed rules were removed same-day).

**179. `mark_notification_read` REFUSES `dismissible=false` rows (400) — action-required bells clear ONLY via their completing handler's direct DB update (2026-07-03).** The bell's "Mark all read" previously nuked action-required rows; it now filters them client-side AND the server guards. Handlers that legitimately clear action-required rows (`pcadmin-pricing`, `create-member`, reviewer-notes saves, `clearJakeFailure`) write `read=true` directly with the service-role client and are unaffected. Never "fix" a stuck action-required bell by routing it through `mark_notification_read`.

**180. BoldSign `Declined`/`Expired`/`Revoked` events are handled by an additive early-return branch in BOTH webhook handlers (standalone `boldsign-webhook` v40 — its 5th approved extension — AND the embedded `maybeHandleBoldSignWebhook` in `router/webhooks.ts`); both chain service-role to `automation_AGREEMENT_declined` (2026-07-03).** The handler (`actions/agreements/declined.ts`, service-role gated) resolves `{source: map1|tax|advisor|accountant|specialist, row_id}` and fires the per-pipeline action-required rule `<AREA>_agreement_declined`. A change to this behavior must touch BOTH handlers, and the standalone one still needs `--no-verify-jwt` on every deploy (gotcha #10). These action-required bells have NO completing handler — they persist until the flow moves on; a superadmin can clear one in the DB if truly stale.
**181. EVERY templated outbound email must be registered in the Email Templates tab AND delivered through the send-mode layer — the email twin of gotcha #176 (2026-07-03).** Every email now honors a per-template **Draft/Send toggle** (`email_templates.send_mode`, default `false` = Gmail-draft-only; editable per email + per-section "All draft / All send" in Automation → Email Templates). Delivery is draft-FIRST: the draft is always created, then `utils/email-delivery.ts` fires Gmail `drafts.send` only when the toggle is on — a failed send leaves the draft in Drafts, and a missing template row means draft-only. Adding a NEW automated email therefore means FOUR registrations in the same PR: (1) an `email_templates` row (pipeline + template_name, seeded via migration/MCP); (2) delivery through the layer — inline-MIME sites call `gmailDraftFetch(supabase, accessToken, encodedRaw, pipeline, templateName)` (a fetch-compatible drop-in that returns a Response), `draftGmail()` sites pass `sendMode: { supabase, pipeline, templateName }`; a raw `fetch(".../gmail/v1/users/me/drafts")` bypasses the toggle and is a REGRESSION; (3) a `TEMPLATE_META` entry — **as of 2026-07-20 the catalog lives in `components/shared/templateMeta.js` (EmailTemplatesPanel + StepEmailsChip both import it; see gotcha #248)** — under the right pipeline, inserted at its position in process order, with a plain-English label + recipients string (`'To: … · Auto-Cc: …'`) — an unregistered row still renders, but at the bottom with its raw template_name; (4) the sandbox reroute still applies in send mode (sandbox recipients → sandbox email), so send mode is safely testable in sandbox — but with sandbox ON and NO `sandbox_email` configured the resolver yields an empty To and the email is SKIPPED (some old handlers used to fall back to the real recipient); keep `sandbox_email` set on every `pipeline_sandbox_config` row. As of 2026-07-04 there are NO unmanaged automation emails left — the former hardcoded set was ported to template rows (migration `email_templates_hardcoded_ports`): member rev-share confirmations → `CONTRACT_member_revshare|first/subsequent` (MAP 1), `TAX_member_revshare|retainer/implementation` (TAX), `PIP_member_revshare` (PIP); strategic-partner rev-share → `STRATEGIC/strategic_partner_revshare` (util takes `subjectSuffix`, not a full subject); payment failures → `CONTRACT_installment_charge_failed` + `TAX_implementation_charge_failed`; `TAX_deposit_refund`; Connect setups → `SPECIALIST_PAYOUT/specialist_connect_setup` + `STRATEGIC/strategic_group_connect_setup`. This SUPERSEDES gotcha #163. Only the two ad-hoc utility edge functions (`draft-agreement-pdfs`, `boldsign-template-fields` — internal admin tools) still draft outside the template system. Notifications have the same rule via gotcha #176 (notifyByRule + `notification_rules` seed row → visible in the Notification Editor). **Recipients are template-driven too (same session):** `to_list`/`cc_list`/`bcc_list` mix raw emails with role tokens (`CLIENT`/`MEMBER`/`ASSIGNED_PF`/`SPECIALIST`/`ADVISOR`/`ACCOUNTANT`/`TEAM_MEMBER`/`TEAM`/`RECIPIENT`) resolved per-send by `resolveTemplateRecipients(supabase, tmpl, ctx, isSandbox, sandboxEmail)`; a new handler must pass its ctx (at minimum `RECIPIENT` = its built-in target) and must NOT hardcode counterparty Cc — seed the template config instead (see migration `email_templates_recipient_seeds` for the house style: client emails To CLIENT / Cc MEMBER + ASSIGNED_PF; specialist emails To SPECIALIST / Cc Tracy; advisor/accountant To role / Cc TEAM_MEMBER; Anton + Paul Bcc'd on nearly everything; the 4 standalone link emails To RECIPIENT only).

**182. Browser reload loses `location.state` — any view context passed via `navigate(..., {state})` must ALSO be recoverable from the URL or sessionStorage (2026-07-06).** This was the "reload on Tax Planning lands back on Holistic" bug: `ClientDetail` resolved the program from `location.state?.program_id || ?program=`, and only Member-Overview chips put `?program=` in the URL — every other entry point died on reload (backend `msm_load_client_home` then defaults to Holistic). Fix pattern: `ClientDetail` now SELF-HEALS its URL — once the program resolves, an effect writes `?program=<id>&tab=<activeTab>` via `window.history.replaceState`, deliberately NOT `navigate()`/`setSearchParams()`, so react-router never observes the change (no location-effect re-fires, no history entries; the data loader keys off `[clientId]` only). A stale `?tab=` carried over from a different program self-corrects to `home` via `validTabsForProgram()` (which mirrors the render's tab conditions). Portal-level tabs persist via sessionStorage instead: `adminActiveTab` (+ section keys), `memberActiveTab`, `specialistActiveTab`, `clientActiveTab`. A NEW portal tab/view must follow one of these two patterns or a reload dumps the user back to the default view. Related logo rule (same session): the top-left wordmark always lands on a CLEAN portal home — `ClientDetail`'s logo clears `adminActiveTab`+`adminOpenView` (admin) or sets `memberActiveTab='profile'` (member) before navigating, and `AdminPortal`'s own logo removes `adminActiveTab`; a header handler that leaves restore-keys pointing at the old view makes "home" reopen that view.

**183. PFT engagement-track phase counts must go through `visiblePhaseTasks(phase, gateStatus)` — the "Does the Accountant need a third meeting?" gate makes tasks mutually exclusive, so counting all non-auto tasks leaves the phase permanently un-green (2026-07-06).** The "Accountant Meeting 2" phase holds FOUR non-auto DB tasks but renders at most three: "Meeting 3 confirmation email" only when the gate = Yes, the Meeting-2 copy of "Accountant decision confirmation email" only when the gate = No (gate unanswered → neither). The badge showed "In progress · 3/4" with all three visible steps complete. `PFTEngagementTrack.jsx` now derives EVERY count — the phase badge `N/M`, `getPhaseState()` done/green, the load-time auto-expand, and the hero totals — from two shared module-level helpers, `computeGateStatus(phases, progress)` + `visiblePhaseTasks(phase, gateStatus)`, which mirror `renderTask`'s hiding rules exactly (this also fixed a latent hero over-count of the hidden Meeting-3 email). Same family as gotcha #173 (`countableTasks`): ANY completion count over conditionally-rendered tasks must share the render's visibility predicate, or "all visible steps done" never equals "counted done".

**184. A Stripe `customer_balance` PaymentIntent reports `amount_received=0` until it is FULLY funded — the partial total must be derived from the `payment_intent.partially_funded` event, not read off the PI (2026-07-06, v542).** This was a real bug caught in testing the VFO Specialist Revenue bank-transfer flow: on a partial bank-transfer credit Stripe fires `payment_intent.partially_funded` but leaves `pi.amount_received` at 0 (it only populates on full funding, at which point `payment_intent.succeeded` fires). To show "received so far" you must compute `received = pi.amount - pi.next_action.display_bank_transfer_instructions.amount_remaining` (both in the smallest currency unit → /100 for dollars). The `router/webhooks.ts` partially_funded block persists this to `specialist_revenue_requests.bank_transfer_amount_received` and rings the `SPECREV_partial_funding_bell` FYI to Jake; the request stays `payment_status='requested'` (NOT a new 'processing' stage — deliberately, so the payout-sweep 48h/96h reminder ladder keyed on 'requested' keeps running) until `payment_intent.succeeded` flips it to `received`. v541 shipped reading `amount_received` (always 0 → bell said "$0 of $X"); v542 fixed the derivation. **SUPERSEDED 2026-07-07 (v556):** the rotating per-request bank-transfer flow was replaced by the house-account model (#189) and its code/columns/templates removed — the `payment_intent.partially_funded` SPECREV webhook block, the `bank_transfer_amount_received` column, and the `SPECREV_partial_funding_bell` rule no longer exist. Kept for historical context (the `customer_balance` `amount_received=0`-until-fully-funded behaviour itself is still a real Stripe fact).

**185. Stripe payment-method enablement AND webhook-endpoint event subscriptions are PER-MODE (test vs live) — enabling/subscribing in one mode does nothing for the other (2026-07-06).** Bank transfers as a customer-facing payment method is a per-mode dashboard setting: it was enabled + confirmed working in TEST mode this session, but must ALSO be enabled in the LIVE-mode dashboard before real bank-transfer requests work. Likewise a brand-new webhook event type like `payment_intent.partially_funded` must be added to BOTH the test-mode and live-mode webhook endpoints' subscribed-events list — the test-mode endpoint was updated this session; the live-mode endpoint still needs it (else live partial-funding events never reach the handler and the partial bell/derivation silently never fires). Same per-mode caveat already applies to the failure-event handlers (gotcha family around the "these handlers only fire if the Stripe endpoint subscribes to the events, both modes" note in SESSION_REFERENCE). **SUPERSEDED 2026-07-07 (v556):** the rotating per-request bank-transfer flow was replaced by the house-account model (#189) and its code/columns/templates removed — the `payment_intent.partially_funded` subscription is no longer needed by SPECREV. The general per-mode enablement/subscription principle still holds for any future event.

**186. Public token pay pages must be CLICK-to-proceed, never auto-redirect to Stripe Checkout on load — Checkout's `cancel_url` returns the payer to the same page, so a load-time redirect creates an inescapable back-button loop (2026-07-06, v543).** Hit on `/specialist-revenue-pay?token=` (`SpecialistRevenuePayPage.jsx`): the page fired the ACH checkout redirect in a load effect, so hitting Stripe's back button (cancel_url → this page) instantly re-redirected — the payer could never escape. Fix: land on a review screen (amount + specialist name + method note + a "Continue to secure payment" button) and redirect ONLY on the button click. Every OTHER public pay page was audited and already followed this pattern (MAP 1 `/pay`, `/tax-pay`, `/pip-pay`, advisor, accountant, specialist-onboarding, and both card-update pages); this was the sole auto-redirect. Any NEW public `?token=` pay/checkout page must be click-to-proceed for the same reason. (Related ordering note from the same follow-up: when a change seeds an email-template placeholder AND ships the code that fills it, deploy the code FIRST or do both in one maintenance window — otherwise a live email fired in the gap renders the literal placeholder; here `[TRANSACTION_DETAILS_ROW]` was seeded before the v543 deploy but no requests existed so nothing leaked.)

**187. NEVER destructure only `{ data }` from a supabase-js call in webhook/automation code — always destructure `{ data, error }` and check `error`, or a genuine DB failure is indistinguishable from "no matching row" and fails 100% silently (2026-07-07).** This was the root cause of the specialist $99/mo license invoice/receipt + Stage 4→5 automation going silently dark: the Stripe webhook returned 200 (so nothing looked broken), but the claim-UPDATE that stamps `lic_last_invoice_id` was failing every time with a real Postgres error the code never looked at. The fix pattern going forward: every `supabase.from(...).select/update/insert(...)` in `router/webhooks.ts` and automation handlers should check `error` and `console.error` + bail explicitly rather than silently falling through an `if (!data) return`. This bug likely existed for a while, not just "since a Stripe API change" — the visible signal (a confirmation email from a DIFFERENT code path) kept firing, masking that the quieter automation step was dead.

**188. A PostgREST schema-cache anomaly can make writes to ONE specific column fail with "column does not exist" (42703) even though the column has existed for a month, reads/other-column-writes work fine, and a manual schema-cache reload (`NOTIFY pgrst, 'reload schema'`) does not fix it — route that write through a `SECURITY DEFINER` RPC function instead of a plain REST `.update()` (2026-07-07).** Diagnosed on `specialist_onboarding.lic_last_invoice_id` (added 2026-06-05, migration `specialist_lic_last_invoice_id`): direct `curl` GET/PATCH against `/rest/v1/specialist_onboarding` resolved the column fine, but the SAME write issued by the deployed edge function via `supabase-js` reliably threw 42703, before AND after a `NOTIFY pgrst, 'reload schema'`. Root cause is infra-level (likely multiple PostgREST replicas with inconsistent cache state) and not fixable from application code or a raw NOTIFY. **The reliable workaround:** create a small `SECURITY DEFINER` Postgres function that does the exact same write in plain SQL (pin `search_path`, `REVOKE ALL FROM PUBLIC` **AND explicitly from `anon`/`authenticated`** — a bare `REVOKE ... FROM PUBLIC` does NOT strip Supabase's default per-role grants, confirmed via `get_advisors` flagging `anon_security_definer_function_executable` until the explicit per-role revokes were added; `GRANT EXECUTE ... TO service_role` only), then call it via `supabase.rpc("fn_name", {...})` instead of `.from(table).update(...)`. RPC calls only need PostgREST to resolve the FUNCTION NAME (a coarser, more stable cache entry) — the function body then runs as native SQL with full, always-current column visibility, sidestepping the per-column cache split entirely. If a write to an established column starts failing with "column does not exist" while reads/other-writes on the same table are fine, suspect this class of bug before assuming a code regression — see `claim_specialist_license_invoice` (migration `claim_specialist_license_invoice_rpc`) for the pattern.

**189. VFO Specialist Revenue "house account" model: ONE shared Stripe customer per mode (sandbox/live) gives a permanent ACH account/routing every specialist pushes to; "Mark payment received" draws the amount from the pooled cash balance via a confirmed `customer_balance` PaymentIntent — and if that draw does not immediately succeed the PI MUST be canceled or it sits open and silently swallows a later transfer into the pool (2026-07-07, v554→v555).** The pending/confirm flow replaced the earlier rotating per-request bank-transfer approach. `house-account.ts ensureHouseAccount` creates-or-reuses one Stripe customer per `sandbox` flag, reads its bank-transfer `funding_instructions`, and persists account/routing in `specialist_revenue_house_account` (sandbox boolean PK, deny-all RLS). `send-request.ts` with `payment_method:'pending'` inserts the request `payment_status='pending'` + lines, sends NO email, returns the house details. `confirm-received.ts` (`specialist_revenue_confirm_received`, self-gates `auth.isSuperadmin`) trusts the admin's eyeball confirmation and creates+confirms a `customer_balance` PI to pull the amount from the pooled balance; **on anything other than immediate success it cancels the PI** (`{ok:false,not_funded:true}`) so no stray open PI can later absorb funds; on success flips the request to `received` and chains invoice/receipt + payout. CRITICAL: the confirm PI carries NO `metadata.pipeline` (only `request_id` + `payment_kind=specialist_revenue_house`) precisely so `router/webhooks.ts` ignores it — the house-account confirm path is deliberately NOT webhook-driven. (Update 2026-07-07, v556: the old rotating per-request bank-transfer webhook blocks in `webhooks.ts` — the `payment_intent.partially_funded` SPECREV block + the `requested`+bank_transfer widening on the ACH-clearance `payment_intent.succeeded` block — were removed as dead code once this model fully superseded them; the live ACH-link `processing→received` handling is preserved. There are now no `bank_transfer_*` columns co-existing with this model.) Because all house customers pool into one cash balance, a `customer_balance` draw is the ONLY way to attribute a specific specialist's push to a specific request; there is no per-transfer webhook to key on.

**190. Specialist-revenue reconciliation + period-total surfaces must count ONLY `payment_status='received'` requests — `pending`/`requested` is expected money, not reconciled revenue (2026-07-07).** Latent over-count fixed this session: `SpecialistReconciliationPanel.jsx` now filters to `payment_status='received'` before summing, and `SpecialistRevenuePanel.jsx` computes viewer money totals + the By-member rollup off a `receivedFiltered` set (the By-specialist list still shows all statuses by design). With the house-account model a request can sit in `pending` indefinitely until an admin marks it received, so any total that includes non-received rows double-counts money that has not actually landed.

**191. The Growth Plan MSM lives in TWO places and every Growth notification resolves its recipient from the SCORE row, not the Get Started row — a MSM change made after the plan is scored must propagate to `growth_plan_scores.assigned_admin_email` or notifications keep going to the score-time MSM (2026-07-08, v559).** The assigned MSM is stored on the member-keyed `growth_plan_four_ways.assigned_admin_email` (set on the "Get Started" step, independent of score versioning) AND snapshotted onto `growth_plan_scores.assigned_admin_email` when a score is generated (`save-score.ts` requires it). The overdue sweep (`overdue-sweep.ts`) plus the member-progress FYI and the plan-completed FYI (`notify.ts`) all read the SCORE row. So changing the MSM on Get Started updated four_ways but left the score-row snapshot stale — FYIs kept routing to the previous MSM (real bug: reassigning Alec's MSM to Jake still sent overdue FYIs to the old MSM). Fix: `save-four-ways.ts` now also updates the current (`is_current=true`) score row's `assigned_admin_email/name` whenever a MSM is saved, so the change propagates without a full re-score. A re-score also re-snapshots it. (This two-place split exists because the MSM must be assignable on Get Started BEFORE any score row exists.)

**192. The Growth Plan is variant-driven (advisor | accountant) with ZERO backend variant logic — content is selected frontend-side by `getGrowthConfig(variant)` and the variant is DERIVED from the member's category, never stored (2026-07-08).** `variantForMember(member)` = `member_category === 'accountant' ? 'accountant' : 'advisor'`; `AdminGrowthPlan` derives it and threads `variant` through every sub-view (and `MemberPortal`/`MemberGrowthPlan` derive it for the member portal, gated to advisor+accountant). All question/action/type/category copy lives in `src/components/growth/constants.js` under `ADVISOR_CONFIG`/`ACCOUNTANT_CONFIG`; score MATH is variant-agnostic in `src/components/growth/scoring.js` (takes `sections`). The backend (`save-score`/`save-four-ways`/`load`) is GENERIC storage — the accountant's Compliance-Factory `'areas'` answers ride in `raw_answers` (JSONB) and the `compliance` action category is just a stored string, so enabling the accountant plan needed NO backend/DB change (only `growthPlan={true}` on `AccountantsPanel` + the member-portal gate). The new `'areas'` question type (3 fixed compliance areas + dynamic "others", each row scored, per-question average → section % exactly like a normal section question; Q12 also captures a manager Name) is computed in `scoring.js areaAverage` and rendered by `AreasEditor` in `GrowthScoring.jsx`. 4-Ways priority ranking is a TRUE ranking — a number can't be reused across focuses; `Growth4Ways.jsx` enforces it by disabling already-taken numbers (both variants). Advisor Type 3 still locks the "Existing Clients via VFO" focus to N/A; the accountant has no locked focus.

**193. A prop/variable referenced only inside a NESTED component that doesn't receive it throws a runtime `ReferenceError` that `npm run build` (esbuild/Vite) does NOT catch — only rendering the component surfaces it; treat a green build as necessary-but-not-sufficient (2026-07-08).** Threading the new `variant` prop through the growth/one-page tree, `GrowthOnePage` rendered `<GrowthAddPriority variant={variant}>` from inside the nested `AdminAddPriorities` component, which never received `variant` as a prop → `Uncaught ReferenceError: variant is not defined` at render, blanking the One Page Plan behind the ErrorBoundary — even though the production build compiled clean (there is no `no-undef` lint in the build). When threading a new prop through a component that has nested sub-components, pass it into EVERY nested sub-component that references it, and click-through the affected pages; the build won't flag the miss.

**194. `npm run dev` sessions reported every error to the production Sentry dashboard tagged `environment=production` — before chasing a Sentry issue, open an event and check its `url` tag; `localhost:5173` = dev noise, not a production bug (2026-07-08).** `src/main.jsx` called `Sentry.init` unconditionally with a hardcoded `environment: 'production'`, so every hot-reload/runtime error of in-progress code during a dev session (including mid-edit states of identifiers that never existed in any commit) landed in the same dashboard as real user errors — 9 phantom "production" issues (the escalating `variant`/`save`/`justSaved` ReferenceErrors from the gotcha-#193 session, the ciq-removal mid-edit bundle, week-old mid-refactor states, one browser-extension fetch TypeError) were triaged 2026-07-08 and ALL proved absent from every deployed tag. Fixed: `enabled: import.meta.env.PROD` (Vite sets PROD only in `vite build`, i.e. what `npm run deploy` ships) + `ignoreErrors: [/non ISO-8859-1 code point/]` (browser-extension fetch calls surface through Sentry's fetch wrapper misattributed to the app bundle — a known false-positive class; no app fetch puts dynamic data in a header). Useful triage tools: `git tag --contains <commit>`, `git log -S <identifier> --all`, and grepping `git show origin/gh-pages:assets/index-<hash>.js` (a genuinely undeclared identifier survives minification verbatim).

**195. A side effect gated on "all of N tasks complete" must run its check on EVERY gate task's save path — the Stage-2 exec-vote opening only checked in the reviewer-notes branch, so completing the notes BEFORE the rev-share proposal meant voting NEVER opened (no bells to Anton/Paul, `vote_r1_opened_at` NULL) (2026-07-08).** `actions/onboarding/save-progress.ts` fired the "Executive vote needed" notifications + the `vote_r1_opened_at` stamp only inside the `tracy_general_notes`/`tim_tax_risk_notes` branch; the `rev_share_prepared` branch only prompted reviewers whose notes were missing. Notes-first completion (confirmed live on a test walkthrough: notes 18:15:31, proposal 18:15:35, no bells, NULL stamp) silently stranded the onboarding before the first exec vote. Fixed by extracting `maybeOpenExecVoting()` and calling it from BOTH branches, so completion order is irrelevant. Workaround on a stranded row running a pre-fix deploy: re-save the notes task — the notes branch re-runs the gate check. (The exec "vote needed" + reviewer-notes prompts are deliberate RAW `notifications` inserts — non-dismissible action-required rows with handler-cleared lifecycles, predating the #176 rule-driven layer.)

**196. Every migration applied live via the Supabase MCP (`apply_migration`) MUST also be committed as a file in `supabase/migrations/` in the same session — an MCP-applied migration exists ONLY in the database, and an environment rebuilt/branched from the repo's migrations silently lacks it (2026-07-08).** Found by the license-fix review: the `claim_specialist_license_invoice` RPC (gotcha #188 — the SOLE idempotency guard for license invoice/receipts, since `license-invoice-receipt.ts` always sends when invoked) was applied live 2026-07-07 (DB migration versions 20260707181121/181155) but never committed; a rebuilt environment would silently reproduce the exact silent-failure bug it fixed (claim call errors → early return → no receipt, no Stage 4→5 advance — failing closed, but invisible again). Committed 2026-07-08 as `20260708130000_claim_specialist_license_invoice_rpc.sql` (byte-accurate `CREATE OR REPLACE` + idempotent revoke/grants so re-applying against the live DB is a no-op). When writing a parity file for an already-live object, pull the truth with `pg_get_functiondef()` — don't reconstruct it from memory.

**197. Stripe Checkout `mode=subscription`: defer the first charge with `subscription_data[billing_cycle_anchor]` + `proration_behavior='none'`, NOT `trial_end` — and respect BOTH anchor bounds (2026-07-09).** `trial_end` works but the hosted page renders it as "31 days free / Start trial" (Stripe-fixed header, uneditable; a `custom_text[submit][message]` note is fine-print at best). `billing_cycle_anchor` renders as a plain "$X per month starting <date>" subscription — but the anchor must satisfy two Stripe constraints simultaneously: (a) it must be in the FUTURE at subscription creation, which happens at Checkout COMPLETION, not session creation — cap the session with `expires_at` (min 30min) so a stale link can't complete after the anchor passes; (b) it cannot be LATER than the next natural billing date (creation + one billing interval) — a "roll to next month at a fixed hour" computation overshoots this by HOURS when the charge day equals today's date (live error: "The `billing_cycle_anchor` cannot be later than next natural billing date"). Fix pattern in `actions/specialist-revenue/recurring-checkout.ts`: compute a `naturalLimit` (now + 1 month, minus safety margin), prefer the charge day at 17:00 UTC with a 2h minimum buffer, fall back to 00:05 UTC on the same date when 17:00 overshoots, hard-clamp for the residual midnight edge. Bonus of the anchor mechanism: a charge day equal to today with >2h lead time charges TODAY instead of waiting a month.

**198. EVERY Stripe subscription creation emits a $0 invoice (`billing_reason=subscription_create`) that fires `invoice.finalized` AND `invoice.paid` — an invoice-event consumer routed by customer id MUST skip `amount_due <= 0` rows (2026-07-09).** With a deferred first charge (trial or anchor), the subscription-create invoice is a $0 stub, but it still finalizes and auto-"pays" instantly, so without the guard the recurring specialist-revenue branches would have spawned a bogus $0 `specialist_revenue_requests` row at SETUP time and fired the invoice/receipt + payout chain on it. Guard added in both `processSpecrevRecurringInvoicePaid` and the `invoice.finalized` block (`router/webhooks.ts`). The specialist LICENSE flow was immune only by accident — its first invoice is a real $99 charge (`billing_reason=subscription_create` is its FIRST-payment signal, the opposite convention) — so do not copy the license branch's billing_reason logic into a deferred-start subscription.

**199. Newer Stripe API versions moved `current_period_end` OFF the subscription object onto the subscription ITEM — derive next-billing/anchor dates via `trial_end || current_period_end || items.data[0].current_period_end || billing_cycle_anchor` (2026-07-09).** Cousin of gotcha #76 (invoice subscription refs). Symptom found live: `specialist_revenue_recurring_plans.next_billing_date` stayed NULL after activation and the setup-confirmation email fell back to placeholder text, because the fetched subscription had no top-level `current_period_end`. Fixed in the recurring `checkout.session.completed` block (`router/webhooks.ts`).

**200. A Stripe Dashboard deep link needs the ACCOUNT id in the path and a `/test/` segment in sandbox — and the cash-balance sub-route isn't directly linkable, so link to the customer page instead (2026-07-09).** To let an admin eyeball incoming bank transfers on the pooled VFO house account before confirming, the "View account activity in Stripe" link points at the customer page, not the (non-linkable) cash-balance tab: `https://dashboard.stripe.com/{acct_id}/{'test/' if sandbox}customers/{customer_id}`. Both pieces are mandatory — omit `{acct_id}` and Stripe bounces to whatever account is logged in; omit the `/test/` segment in sandbox and it lands on the LIVE account's (empty/absent) customer. The account id is not in a normal Stripe response, so `ensureHouseAccount` (`actions/specialist-revenue/house-account.ts`) captures it via `GET /v1/account` at house-account creation (non-fatal if unreadable) and stores it in `specialist_revenue_house_account.stripe_account_id`; `load.ts` surfaces it on each request's `account` object; `specialistRevenueShared.jsx MarkReceivedButton` builds the mode-aware URL from `account.stripe_account_id` + the request's own `stripe_customer_id` + `sandbox` flag.

**201. The public showroom widget reads `experts` with the PUBLIC anon key, so that key is column-restricted to 7 safe columns — a new widget column needs a matching GRANT or the fetch 401s (2026-07-09).** *[Annotated 2026-08-09: this grant now has **THREE** anon consumers, not one — the embeddable widget, and `public/store/data.js` behind the `/store/` 3D bookstore (the grant itself is 8 columns since #231). A column added or removed here must be reflected in BOTH files, and neither fails loudly if it is forgotten — see **#345**.]* `widget/vfo-widget.js` (now tracked in the `vfo-edge-functions` repo; deployed by uploading to the `vfo-widget` Storage bucket) fetches `experts?select=id,name,short_bio,long_bio,headshot_image` with the anon key. Migration `20260709160000_experts_anon_column_grants.sql` did `REVOKE SELECT ON public.experts FROM anon, authenticated` + `GRANT SELECT (id,name,short_bio,long_bio,headshot_image,status,sort_order)`, so an anon `select=*` (or `select=...,email`) now returns 42501 permission-denied — `email`/`stripe_account_id`/`revenue_decision`/`D&B_*` are no longer anon-readable. **If the widget ever needs a new column, GRANT it here too or the widget breaks.** The portal is unaffected (it reads `experts` via the edge fn on `service_role`, which bypasses column grants). The `experts` "Public read" RLS policy (`status='Active'`) is unchanged — this is column-level GRANT hardening layered on top of it. Rollback: `GRANT SELECT ON public.experts TO anon, authenticated;`.

**202. The Regular Priorities tab's VISIBLE step order is Postgres heap order, NOT `task_order` — and by product decision it must stay that way; new surfaces must MIRROR it, never "fix" it (2026-07-09).** `msm_load_regular_phases` fetches `program_client_tasks(*)` embedded with no ordering and `RegularPrioritiesTab` renders tasks as-returned, so e.g. MAP 5 shows "Email to VFO Specialist confirming outcome" (`task_order` 1) as the LAST row. The user explicitly ruled: do NOT sort the loader and do NOT reorder the template — the visible order is the accepted truth. Any new surface listing regular-track steps (e.g. the Client Overview engine, `actions/clients/overview-regular.ts`) must fetch through the SAME query shape and preserve the embed order. Note the asymmetry: MAP 1 / Tax / PFT tabs load via `msm_load_client_track`, which DOES sort by `task_order` client-side — match each tab's loader semantics, not a global rule.

**203. The tax extra-meeting YES outcome never rewrites `tax_final_decision` — it stays `'ExtraMeeting'` forever; the agreement chain starting is the only Yes-path proof (2026-07-09).** `actions/tax/extra-meeting.ts` Yes branch writes pricing + `tax_via_extra_meeting=true` and chains sendagreement WITHOUT touching `tax_final_decision`; only the No branch flips it to `'No'`. Any consumer branching on the tax decision state must treat `tax_final_decision='ExtraMeeting'` + `agreement_sent='Yes'` as the Yes path (the MAP 1 twin is `c15_via_extra_meeting` + `c16_sent`), or the whole retainer/implementation cascade looks not-applicable and the plan reads as finished. Handled in `actions/clients/overview-tax.ts`.

**204. The ERT/VFOS Documentation vault section is ADMIN-write / owner-READ-only — `section='ert'` must be explicitly REJECTED in every OWNER write handler because the `*BucketFor` helpers silently default an unknown section to the general bucket (2026-07-09).** The third vault section (added to member/client/specialist vaults) lives in its OWN `ERT_BUCKETS` map (`member-ert-docs`/`client-ert-docs`/`specialist-ert-docs`, private, service-role-only, no RLS policy) in `actions/vault/ert-shared.ts` — NOT the `MEMBER_/CLIENT_/SPECIALIST_VAULT_BUCKETS` section maps. Admins add/remove through ONE unified admin-only set `admin_ert_{list,upload_url,download,delete}` (`actions/vault/admin-ert.ts`, keyed by `{entity, key}`, all in `ADMIN_ONLY_ACTIONS`). Owner READS are wired through the existing per-entity handlers: each `*_vault_list` (+ `specialist_vault_admin_list`) returns an `ert` array via `listErt()`, and each portal download routes `section==='ert'` to `ERT_BUCKETS`. Owner WRITES (`vault_upload_url`/`vault_delete`/`client_vault_*`/`specialist_vault_*`) hard-reject `section==='ert'` — MANDATORY for member+specialist because `memberBucketFor`/`specialistBucketFor` fall back to the general bucket for any unknown section (a missing reject would let a portal user upload into ERT via the general bucket). Frontend: `VaultSections.jsx` gained per-section `readOnly` (owner) + per-section `actions`/`params` overrides (admin routes ERT through `admin_ert_*`); `ertVaultSection.js` builds both descriptors. NOTE (pre-existing, unrelated latent bug found in passing): `vault_upload_url` trusts `body.member_number` (not member-scoped, not admin-only) — a member could mint an upload URL into another member's general/sensitive vault; not fixed this session, flag if touching member vault auth.

**205. The BoldSign signed-agreement document-id column name DIFFERS per pipeline, and the "copy the signed agreement into the ERT vault on sign+pay" hook fires at the entity-CREATION / first-payment step (the keyed vault must exist AND payment must be done), keyed to the PAYER (2026-07-09).** Doc-id columns: `pipeline_map1.boldsign_doc_id`, `client_tax_plans.boldsign_doc_id`, BUT `advisor_onboarding.boldsign_document_id` / `accountant_onboarding.boldsign_document_id` / `specialist_onboarding.lic_boldsign_document_id`. The shared best-effort helper `utils/ert-agreement-copy.ts copyAgreementToErtOnce()` downloads the fully-signed PDF (`GET https://api.boldsign.com/v1/document/download?documentId=`, key via `getBoldSignKey(isSandbox)` reading `pipeline_sandbox_config.sandbox_mode`) and uploads it to `ERT_BUCKETS[entity]/<key>/`, then stamps the idempotency guard `ert_agreement_copied_at` (added to all 5 tables). It NEVER throws/blocks the caller. Hook points + destination: specialist → `automation_SPECIALIST_createspecialist` (go-live; keyed by the just-assigned `expert_id`); advisor → `automation_ADVISOR_createmember`; accountant → `automation_ACCOUNTANT_createmember` (BOTH createmember handlers are already payment-gated on `invoice_sent_at`; the accountant copy is ALSO gated on `invoice_sent_at` so unpaid VFO Associates are skipped); MAP 1 → `automation_CONTRACT_confirmationemail` (first-payment `isFirst` block); Tax → `automation_TAX_confirmationemail` (retainer only — the implementation payment reuses the same agreement, so copy once). Destination = the PAYER's vault: client-paid MAP1/Tax → the client's ERT vault (`client_id`); member-paid-on-behalf (`member_paying_on_behalf=true`, which makes the member signer1) → that member's ERT vault (always `clients.member_number` — there is NO separate payer-member column); onboarding → the entity's own vault. Copy is skipped automatically when the doc-id is null (e.g. the MAP 1 Undecided static-PDF path).

**206. Supabase blocks direct SQL `DELETE FROM storage.objects` via the `storage.protect_delete()` trigger — deleting a vault file needs the Storage API, not MCP SQL (2026-07-09).** Attempting `delete from storage.objects where bucket_id=...` errors `42501: Direct deletion from storage tables is not allowed. Use the Storage API instead.` So a "delete this client/member and ALL traces" cleanup via MCP can clear every DB row but CANNOT remove the vault file(s) — those must go through the app (`admin_ert_delete` / the vault Remove button, which requires a valid `admin_sessions` token) or the Supabase Storage dashboard. A short-lived admin session can be minted by inserting an `admin_sessions` row (`token`/`email`/`expires_at`; the email must exist in `allowed_admins` to resolve as admin) to call `admin_ert_delete`, then deleting the session row. External sandbox artifacts (Stripe test customer, BoldSign sandbox document) are likewise unreachable from the DB — only their dashboards.

**207. `load_data` builds its `members` payload from `member_plugin_settings` merged with a HARD-CODED WHITELIST of `members` profile columns — a new `members` column is invisible to the member portal + admin list until it's added to that map (2026-07-10).** `actions/data/load-data.ts` does `member_plugin_settings.select('*')` then `.map()` spreads each plugin row and overlays a FIXED set of profile fields pulled from a `profileMap` keyed by `plugin_member_number` (it is NOT `members.select('*')`). So the member-profile columns added this session (`headshot_image` / `bio` / `website_url`) had to be appended to that map literal to reach `MemberPortal` (`memberData`, sourced from `loadCachedData()`) and the admin `allMembers` list (e.g. the `TrackHero` header avatar). The profile EDIT + DETAILS views are UNAFFECTED by this — they read the full row via `member_profile_load` (`select('*')`) and save via `member_profile_save` (whole-object upsert), so a new column round-trips there with ZERO backend change; only the shared `load_data` list needs the explicit passthrough (and therefore a `vfo-admin-api` deploy). Same trap already applied to `trading_name` (see its row in tables/members.md). Frontend note: the admin members list keys on `plugin_member_number`, and `load_data` aliases `member_number: m.plugin_member_number` on each row.

**208. Growth Plan One Page priority numbers are PERSISTED (`growth_plan_actions.plan_number`), not positional — and every display surface must SORT by `plan_number`, not `action_number` (2026-07-10).** Before this session the number was computed at render (an array index / `orderOnePage` counter), so completing/parking a priority renumbered the rest. Now a top-level priority is assigned a permanent `plan_number` server-side the FIRST time it enters the plan — `assignPlanNumbers()` (`actions/growth/plan-numbers.ts`) = (highest plan_number ever used on this plan, ANY g3_status) + 1, filling NULLs in `action_number` order — called from `growth_plan_save_actions` (when an update sets `g3_status='one_page_plan'`) and `growth_plan_add_action` (top-level custom). It is NEVER cleared or reused, so a parked→re-added priority keeps its number and a brand-new one always gets the next-highest (monotonic; gaps from removals persist by design). Sub-tasks have no `plan_number` (they render `parent.N`). **The trap:** `action_number` is the fixed CATALOG slot (a re-added default keeps its low 1–20 value), so ordering rows by `action_number` while numbering them by `plan_number` makes a re-added priority (#9) slot visually between #1 and #3. `grouping.js orderOnePage` (tops), the completed-items list (`GrowthOnePage.jsx`), and `GrowthBuildPlan.jsx` all sort by `(plan_number ?? Infinity) || action_number`. `displayNumbers()` (grouping.js) is the single label helper (top = plan_number, sub = parent.plan_number + sibling index). Migration `20260710130000` added the column + backfilled existing plans in their prior display order (`(g3_action_type='new') desc, action_number`).

**209. Growth Owned By / Assisted By is a shared admin-aware combobox, and the "you were added" bell fires on SAVE (server-side), never on select — and `growth_plan_load_admins` is ADMIN-ONLY so members never load the admin list (2026-07-10).** The combobox `NameCombo` (moved to shared `components/growth/ui.jsx`, used by Build + One Page + Add-Priority rows) merges plan-local typed names with the full system admin list (tagged "Admin") via `buildNamePool()`. Admins load the list with `growth_plan_load_admins` (ADMIN_ONLY — a member view calling it would 403, so `GrowthOnePage`/`GrowthAddPriority` only fetch it when `isAdmin`; members get type-and-add over plan-local names only). The `GROWTH_assignee_added` FYI is decided BACKEND-side in `growth_plan_save_actions` / `growth_plan_add_action` (compare the pre-save owned/assisted value → if a NEW value matches an `allowed_admins.name`, ping that admin, skipping the acting admin), so it fires only when the plan is actually saved — matching the "not the moment it's selected, in case of a mistake" requirement. `notification_rules` key `GROWTH_assignee_added` (recipient token `ASSIGNED_TO`, resolved per-event to that admin). Separately, `GROWTH_progress_update` now fires on EVERY real status change (member OR admin — was member-only), skipping self-notification when the actor is the assigned MSM. **UI note:** the `NameCombo` portal menu closes on page-scroll to stay aligned to its input, but the scroll-close handler MUST exclude scrolls that originate inside the menu (`menuRef.contains(e.target)`) or a long (admin) list can't be scrolled without vanishing.


**210. Growth Credits purchase was BROKEN for members (ADMIN_ONLY) and trusted a client-sent price; four GC actions were entirely UNGATED — pricing is now server-side and every GC action is explicitly gated (2026-07-10).** `gc_create_checkout` sat in ADMIN_ONLY_ACTIONS (every member Buy click 403'd — the original bug) and charged whatever `price` the browser sent (a member could have bought 20 credits for $1). Now: prices come ONLY from the `GC_PACKAGES` map in `actions/gc/create-checkout.ts` (1/$100, 10/$950, 20/$1,800; the frontend mirrors them for display only); `gc_create_checkout`/`gc_redeem`/`gc_load_balance`/`gc_load_transactions`/`gc_load_redemptions` are MEMBER_SCOPED (the stale `gc_load_history` key was replaced by the real `gc_load_transactions`); `gc_add_credits`/`gc_manage_service`/`gc_update_redemption`/`gc_load_all_redemptions`/`gc_load_accounting` are ADMIN_ONLY (they were UNGATED — a member session could have granted itself credits). The redemption queue actions are additionally tab-gated (`TAB_ACTIONS.growth_credits` — the per-admin-grantable Growth Credits page in the Admin Editor); `gc_load_accounting` is under `TAB_ACTIONS.accounting`; `gc_manage_service` under `TAB_ACTIONS.automation`. `gc_load_services` returns the sandbox config + inactive services to ADMIN callers only (members get active services, no config). Redemptions now insert `status='pending'` and are marked `fulfilled` (or rejected + credits refunded) on the Growth Credits page; the purchase/spend MSM bells (`GC_credits_purchased`/`GC_credits_spent`, recipient = `members.assigned_msm` name mapped to email via `loadAdminNameMap`, fallback = the 4 MSM_TEAM_EMAILS in `utils/msm.ts` mirroring MSMTracking.jsx TEAM_MEMBERS) link to `/admin?tab=growth_credits`.

**211. GC Stripe fulfillment is metadata-keyed and ACH-safe — `checkout.session.async_payment_succeeded` MUST be subscribed on the Stripe webhook endpoint (both modes) or ACH credit purchases never land (2026-07-10).** `gc_create_checkout` sets `metadata.pipeline='GROWTH_CREDITS'` + member_number + credits and reads sandbox mode from the new `pipeline_sandbox_config` row `GROWTH_CREDITS` (toggle in Automation & Config → Growth Credits). The webhook's shared `fulfillGrowthCredits()` fulfills on `checkout.session.completed` ONLY when `payment_status==='paid'` (card) and on `checkout.session.async_payment_succeeded` (ACH settles later); `checkout.session.async_payment_failed` has an additive GC branch (Jake FYI via `FAILURE_ach_first_payment`, no credit). Fulfillment is idempotent against Stripe event retries via a `gc_transactions.stripe_session_id` lookup guard. Card grosses up with the standard `(base+0.30)/(1-0.029)` formula (same as every other pipeline); ACH charges the flat price with `verification_method=instant`. Success/cancel URLs derive from the request `Origin` when it's in ALLOWED_ORIGINS (so localhost dev returns to localhost — previously hardcoded prod, which forced a re-login and hid the success banner) else fall back to vfoportal.com; success carries `?gc_success=1&m=<method>` for the method-aware banner + balance polling.

**212. AdminPortal `?tab=&section=` deep links are consumed then STRIPPED via `history.replaceState` — and the effect must read `window.location.search`, not router state (2026-07-10).** After applying the tab/section, the deep-link effect calls `window.history.replaceState({}, '', '/admin')` so the URL self-heals and a reload restores from sessionStorage instead of re-hijacking to the deep-linked tab (this bit the bell's View-all link: going home then reloading bounced back to Notifications). The effect re-runs when `allMembers` loads, and react-router's `location.search` does NOT update after replaceState — so the effect reads `window.location.search` to see the already-stripped URL (gotcha #182 family: replaceState, never navigate, for self-heals). New `activeTab` values this session: `notifications` (available to EVERY admin like the bell — no grant, not in the tab bar, reached via the bell's View all / `/admin?tab=notifications`) and `growth_credits` (grantable via `allowed_tabs`, checkbox in the Admin Editor, enforced server-side by `TAB_ACTIONS.growth_credits`).

**213. "Clearing" a notification never deletes it — read rows live in the Notifications page Archive and are hard-PURGED after 90 days; personal reminders insert `notifications` rows DIRECTLY (the documented exception to #176) (2026-07-10).** The bell and page only flip `read=true`. The full-page admin view (`load_notifications_page`: `scope` current|archive + `kind` all|action|fyi|reminder, paginated, recipient-scoped like the bell; bulk `mark_notifications_read` clears dismissible+recipient-scoped rows only) exposes the history for the first time. `automation_NOTIFICATIONS_purge` (PUBLIC handler, service-role Bearer gate, daily cron `notifications-purge-daily` 10:30 UTC jobid 15) permanently deletes READ rows older than `PURGE_AFTER_DAYS=90` (`actions/notifications/purge-sweep.ts`); unread rows are never touched. Personal reminders: `personal_reminders` table (RLS deny-all), `reminder_create/load/delete` are ADMIN_ONLY and scoped to `auth.session.email` (never a body email); `fire_at` is computed in Deno by `utils/timezone.ts zonedTimeToUtc` (two-pass Intl offset technique, DST-safe); delivery is `automation_REMINDER_sweep` (cron `reminder-sweep-5min` jobid 14, every 5 minutes — reminders fire within ~5 min of the chosen wall time) which INSERTS the bell row directly because a user-authored reminder has no `notification_rules` key — pipeline `REMINDER`, violet row styling (bell + page), `fired_at` stamped only after a successful insert so transient failures retry.

**214. Note visibility is a per-row `visibility` column ('internal' | 'shared') enforced in the LOADERS by caller role — and amber/teal are RESERVED for the visibility tags (2026-07-10).** Columns added to 5 tables (migration `20260710190000`): `client_notes` + `member_program_notes` default `'internal'` (they were always admin-only, so nothing became member-visible retroactively); `member_meetings`/`coaching_meetings`/`coaching_renewals` default `'shared'` (the member portal always showed those notes). Member-side enforcement: `client_notes_load_shared` (the ONE new action; ownership via `denyIfNotOwnClient`, returns shared-only — powers the member ClientDetail "Notes from your team" card); `load_member_program_notes` filters `visibility='shared'` for non-admin callers (powers the member MSM "Program Notes" card and the Advanced Coaching member Home tab); the meeting/coaching/renewal loaders REDACT `notes` to null on internal rows for members (the row itself stays visible). Member callers can never create or flip an `'internal'` note (forced `'shared'` in add/update). Clients/specialists still see zero notes (deny-by-default allowlists unchanged). UI: shared `NoteVisibility.jsx` — `SaveVisibilityButtons` (every in-scope note form has TWO save buttons: Save internal / Save & share with member) + `VisibilityBadge` and `noteTint` (amber = Internal, teal = Shared with member, tag + row-background hue; no other note tag may use these colors). Also fixed in the same pass: `load_data` now strips `members.notes` for non-admin callers — the whitelisted member payload had been shipping admin profile-notes text to every logged-in member.

**215. PostgREST MULTI-ROW inserts NULL-fill missing keys — they do NOT fall back to the column default (2026-07-13).** A bulk `.insert([...])` unifies the key set across every row in the array; any row missing a key gets an explicit NULL. This silently broke membership plan activation: only row 1 of the 12-row year ledger carried `status`, so rows 2–12 got `status=NULL` → NOT NULL violation → the WHOLE insert failed → the plan never activated while the webhook still returned 200 (the error only went to the invisible function console — see #217). Fix pattern: build bulk-insert rows with an IDENTICAL key set, setting "default" values explicitly (`status: isFirst ? 'paid' : 'scheduled'`).

**216. Member-membership Stripe traffic routes EXCLUSIVELY by metadata, never the customer-id cascade — and off-session pull idempotency keys embed the charged row-set (2026-07-13).** Every membership Checkout Session / PaymentIntent carries `metadata.pipeline='MEMBER_MEMBERSHIP'` + `metadata.payment_kind` (`membership_setup` | `membership_first_payment` | `membership_pull` | `membership_termination_fee`) + `metadata.plan_id`, on BOTH the session and its `payment_intent_data`/`setup_intent_data` — the webhook blocks in `router/webhooks.ts` match on those keys and membership Stripe customers deliberately match no cascade table (`pipeline_map1` → … → `specialist_onboarding`), so forgetting the metadata makes events no-op silently. The sweep's off-session charges use Idempotency-Key `membership-pull-<plan>-<date>-<rowid.rowid…>` — keyed by the exact row-set so a same-day retry of the same rows replays safely while a DIFFERENT set of due rows (e.g. after an admin backdates a row post-run) gets a fresh key; a plain plan+date key replayed the morning's success for a different charge. ACH first payments settle via `checkout.session.async_payment_succeeded` — that event (and `_failed`) must stay subscribed on the Stripe endpoint, both modes (same requirement as GC, #211).

**217. Edge-function console output is INVISIBLE through the MCP `get_logs` — debug live webhook paths with a temporary deny-all trace table (2026-07-13).** `get_logs(service='edge-function')` returns request-level rows only (method/status/duration); `console.log`/`console.error` lines never appear, so a webhook that returns 200 while silently no-op'ing gives you nothing to go on. Proven technique from the #215 hunt: create a throwaway `webhook_debug` table (RLS deny-all like everything else), sprinkle `await supabase.from("webhook_debug").insert({ note })` at each guard/branch, deploy, replay the exact event from the Stripe dashboard (event page → Resend), SELECT the trace to find the failing guard, then REMOVE the traces and DROP the table before wrap-up.

**218. The Client Overview tab has its OWN step-machine, separate from the frontend trackers — mirror any tracker phase/step gating into `overview-*.ts` (2026-07-13).** The trackers (`map1/ClientTrackViewV2.jsx`, `pft/PFTEngagementTrack.jsx`) and the admin **Client Overview** engines (`actions/clients/overview-engine.ts` + per-track `overview-map1.ts` / `overview-pft.ts` / `overview-regular.ts` / `overview-tax.ts`) independently derive completion/next-action from the SAME `program_client_tasks` + `client_progress`. So a conditional that makes a step non-applicable in a tracker (e.g. the Initial Contact "Who is completing the tracking…" VFOS/Member gate that greys the 3 sub-steps unless "Member") must ALSO be applied in the matching overview engine by setting those `Step`s `applicable:false` — the engine treats `applicable===false` steps as invisible to warnings/next-action/state. Miss it and the overview fires a spurious "X not recorded but Y already happened" skipped-step warning and points next-action at a step the tracker has greyed out. (This session: the VFOS/Member gate needed the same `applicable:false` in `overview-map1.ts` + `overview-pft.ts`.)

**219. Accountant onboarding "Direct/Advisor" is a DISPLAY relabel only — the stored `accountant_partnership` values are unchanged; and the connected-advisor CC rides a central `extraCc` param, not per-template edits (2026-07-13).** The Stage-1 step reads "Direct or Advisor Partnership" with options **Direct** / **Advisor**, but the DB still stores `'No accountant partnership'` (Direct) / `'Accountant Partnership'` (Advisor) — the labels are mapped in the UI ONLY (`AccountantOnboarding.jsx` dropdown + `AccountantAutomationPanel.jsx` Detail). Do NOT change the stored values: `send-agreement.ts` / `ceo-countersign.ts` / `decision.ts` (PDF pick, pricing $4k vs $2k, template branch) all still `=== "Accountant Partnership"`, and in-flight rows carry the old strings. The **CC Connected Advisor** feature (Advisor path only) CCs a picked advisor member on every accountant-facing onboarding email via a new optional 6th param `extraCc` on the shared `resolveTemplateRecipients` (appended to the resolved `cc`, deduped/validated, suppressed in sandbox). It is wired at **8** accountant handlers — decision / decline-email / send-agreement / payment-email / confirmation-email / invoice-receipt / login-setup-email / sweep — passing `ob.accountant_partnership === "Accountant Partnership" ? ob.cc_advisor_email : null`. **`ceo-countersign.ts` is intentionally excluded** (internal CEO email). The advisor email/name are denormalized onto `accountant_onboarding.cc_advisor_*` at pick time (`save_accountant_cc_advisor`) and cleared when the step flips back to Direct (`save-partnership.ts`). A new accountant-facing email handler that should CC the advisor must add the same gated `extraCc` arg.

**220. Specialist per-ecosystem content lives in `experts.ecosystem_content` (jsonb); the flat top-level columns MIRROR the primary (first) ecosystem; Tax is shared (2026-07-13).** _[Updated 2026-07-16 — see #232: `ecosystem_content` is now an ordered ARRAY (multiple entries per ecosystem allowed), read via `entriesFor()`; the flat `short_bio` now holds the COMBINED bios of all entries (not just the primary's); the flat `D&B_*` still mirror the primary/first entry.]_ A specialist can serve multiple VFO ecosystems with a DIFFERENT `short_bio` + (non-tax) `D&B_*` per ecosystem. Those per-ecosystem write-ups are stored in `experts.ecosystem_content = { "<ecosystem>": { short_bio, "D&B_...": ... }, ... }` (additive jsonb column, `20260713190000_experts_ecosystem_content`). The flat top-level `short_bio`/`D&B_*` columns hold the FIRST (primary) ecosystem's copy so the public widget/showroom + agreement/BoldSign flows (which read the flat columns) are UNCHANGED — on save, `SpecialistsPanel.jsx` mirrors `ecosystem_content[ecos[0]]` into the flat columns. Legacy rows (`ecosystem_content IS NULL`) are read as a single-ecosystem profile whose content IS the flat columns. **The Tax fields** (`D&B_tax_risk_mindset`/`D&B_tax_risk_notes` + the 4 `D&B_audit_risk_*`) are SHARED — one set per person, stored ONLY in the flat columns (never per-ecosystem) — and surface in the UI (both edit + read-only profile) ONLY under the `Tax Planning` ecosystem, as their own section between Details & Benefits and Revenue Share. `save_specialist`/`load_data` are generic column pass-throughs, so **no backend code changed** — only the additive column + a heavy `SpecialistsPanel.jsx` frontend rewrite. `ecosystem_content` is admin-only (NOT added to the anon `experts` column grant — #201), so the widget never sees it. Also: `vfo_ecosystem_assignments` can hold DUPLICATE `(expert_id, name)` rows (one specialist had `Wealth Management` twice), which rendered a strategy twice until the UI began deduping via `[...new Set(ecoMap[id])]` — dedupe any ecosystem list read from `ecoMap`.

**221. Signed agreements filed into the ERT vault should be named with the person's name — pass `name` to `copyAgreementToErtOnce()` (2026-07-14).** The vault filename comes from the `label` arg to `copyAgreementToErtOnce()` (`utils/ert-agreement-copy.ts`), which builds `dest = <key>/<16hex>_<safeLabel>.pdf` and the list UI strips the `<16hex>_` prefix. The helper now takes an optional `name` param: when set it appends `-<name-with-spaces→hyphens>` to the label BEFORE the existing safeLabel sanitizer, e.g. `label:"VFO-Accountant-Agreement", name:"Anil Grandhi"` → `VFO-Accountant-Agreement-Anil-Grandhi.pdf`. All **5** current sign-and-file callers pass it — advisor/accountant `create-member.ts` (member name), `onboarding/create-specialist.ts` (`expert.name`), `tax/confirmation-email.ts` + `pipeline/contract-confirmation-email.ts` (`clientName`, i.e. the subject client even when a member paid on their behalf). A NEW caller MUST pass `name` or the file falls back to the bare label. **Retroactive note:** the `ert_agreement_copied_at` guard means this only affects agreements filed AFTER the deploy; pre-existing vault files keep their old names unless renamed by hand. Related: #204–#206 (the ERT vault is admin-write / owner-read; the BoldSign doc-id column name differs per pipeline).
**222. PowerShell `sort -V` is NOT version-aware — derive the max `live-N` / `backend-good-*` tag with git's own `--sort=v:refname` (2026-07-14).** In PowerShell, `sort` aliases `Sort-Object`, which silently accepts `-V` and returns LEXICAL order — `git tag -l 'live-*' | sort -V` reported `live-9-new-model-sale` as the max when the true max was `live-64`. (`tail` doesn't exist either.) Use `git tag -l 'live-*' --sort=v:refname | Select-Object -Last 1` (same for `backend-good-*`). The bash-style one-liner in older docs/prompts is only safe in an actual bash shell.


**223. Supabase MCP `execute_sql` with multiple `;`-separated statements returns ONLY the LAST statement's result (2026-07-14).** A batch like `SELECT a; SELECT b;` (or `UPDATE ...; SELECT verification;`) silently discards every result but the final one — the earlier statements DO execute, you just can't see their output, which reads as "the first query returned nothing". Run verification SELECTs as separate `execute_sql` calls, or fold multi-checks into ONE statement (scalar subqueries: `SELECT (SELECT count(*) FROM x) AS a, (SELECT count(*) FROM y) AS b`).

**224. Advisor/accountant onboarding notifications carry NO `client_id` — an action-required (`dismissible:false`) onboarding bell can only be cleared by matching its deep link, so the link MUST embed `onboarding=<id>` (2026-07-14).** MAP 1/Tax action-required bells are cleared by their completing handler via `client_id` scoping, but onboarding rows aren't clients: `notifyByRule` is called without `client_id`, so the extra-meeting completion handlers (`actions/{advisor,accountant}/extra-meeting.ts`) clear via `pipeline='ADVISOR_ONBOARDING'|'ACCOUNTANT_ONBOARDING'` + `dismissible=false` + `read=false` + `link LIKE '%onboarding=<id>%'`. Any NEW action-required onboarding bell must (a) put `onboarding=<id>` in its `link` and (b) be cleared by its completing handler with that same scoping — a bell whose link lacks the id is permanently unclearable (#179: `mark_notification_read` refuses `dismissible=false` rows).
**225. `formatDate(d)` in the track tabs splits a `YYYY-MM-DD` string (`MM/DD`) — feeding it a full `timestamptz` renders mojibake AND can be a day off (2026-07-15).** `TaxPrioritiesTab.jsx`'s `formatDate` (and the PIP/MAP1 equivalents) do `d.split('-')` and return `parts[1]/parts[2]`, so a `timestamptz` like `2026-07-15T00:54:33.2+00` renders as `07/15T00:54:33.2`. Worse, the stored value is UTC (`new Date().toISOString()` on the server), so a late-evening-local stamp is already the NEXT calendar day in UTC (00:54 UTC = the 14th in US zones) — `formatDate` would show `07/15` for a "7/14" event. To display a `timestamptz` column, use a local formatter (`formatStamp` added this session: `new Date(ts).getMonth()+1 / getDate()`, zero-padded), NOT the date-only `formatDate`. Date-only DB columns (e.g. `pip_scheduled_date`, `completed_date`) are fine through `formatDate`.

**AMENDED 2026-08-12 (v734) — the MIRROR-IMAGE error, and it was live in shipped code.** #225 warns about pushing a `timestamptz` through the date-only formatter. The opposite is just as wrong and less obvious: **`new Date('YYYY-MM-DD')` parses as UTC midnight, so a bare Postgres `date` column formatted through `Date` renders the PREVIOUS day everywhere west of Greenwich** (`new Date('2026-08-12')` is `2026-08-11 20:00` ET). `AdvisorOnboarding`/`AccountantOnboarding`'s `formatDate` was doing `d.includes('T') ? d.split('T')[0] : d` before splitting — i.e. **slicing the timestamptz down to its UTC date part**, the same off-by-one in the other direction, and it was live. Both kinds of column sit side by side in these tables (`pay1_date`, `retainer_date`, `c17_followup_sent_date` are bare DATEs; every `*_email_sent_at` is timestamptz), so **the only safe formatter branches on the shape** — `/^\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}$/` → split the string; otherwise `new Date()` + `getMonth()`/`getDate()`. That branching form is now used by every AI PC Admin date added this session. Full detail in **#383**.

**226. The public `/tax-upload` page (`TaxUploadPage.jsx`) is SHARED — it backs BOTH the VFO Tax Planning "Request Tax Returns" step AND the MAP 1/holistic invoice-email "Upload Tax Documents" button (2026-07-15).** *[Annotated 2026-08-07 (v709/v711): the MAP 1 INVOICE-EMAIL HALF IS RETIRED — `contract-invoice-receipt.ts` no longer mints `tax_upload_token` or links `/tax-upload` at all; tax-priority clients get a static Tax Form URL instead and returns are requested by the tax-track step in BOTH programs. The page is still shared (Request Tax Returns + Request Additional Information), and every `/tax-upload` link already sitting in an inbox from an older first-payment email still resolves — see **#341** and **#340**.]* There is exactly one tax-returns upload page, keyed on `clients.tax_upload_token` → the private `client-tax-returns` bucket → `UPLOAD_tax_return_uploaded` / `TAX_returns_received` notify. A visual or behavioral change here affects both flows. It is now wrapped in the shared branded `TokenShell` (navy header + wordmark + white card), like every other public token page (pay/decide/setup) — do not revert it to a bare page. The upload-notify path (`actions/vault/upload-notify.ts`) branches: a client WITH a `program_id=4` `client_tax_plans` row stamps `tax_returns_received_at` + fires `TAX_returns_received` (Tracy/Tray); everyone else keeps the `UPLOAD_tax_return_uploaded` FYI (Tray/Tracy) — so holistic upload behavior is unchanged. (Recipients were both trimmed of Tim + Tracy added to the holistic rule 2026-07-21 — see #250.)

**227. `card_fee_waived` = the ONLY card-fee exemption in the system, and it has exactly ONE writer per table (2026-07-15).** Payment-continuation setup-link clients were promised old-system pricing, so `migration_backfill_map1`/`migration_backfill_tax` stamp `card_fee_waived=true` in `stripe_mode='setup_link'` ONLY (existing-mode and every organic pipeline row leave it null). Four readers suppress the 2.9%+$0.30 card gross-up when set: the chargescheduled sweep charge amount, `automation_TAX_charge-implementation`, the card_update webhook's `card_processing_fee` recompute, and `normalize.ts buildMap1Rows` display. Do NOT add new writers or repurpose the flag for discounts — it means "this client pays base on card", nothing else. Related display rule from the same session: `buildMap1Rows` prefers the admin-entered `pay{N}_date` over charge timestamps for rows with `legacy_migrated_at` set (hand-migrated rows carry stale `pay{N}_paid_at`/`invoice_email_sent_at` stamps that otherwise show every installment as the migration date).

**228. Off-session recurring-charge Idempotency-Keys must be LOGICAL (no date component) (2026-07-16).** The chargescheduled sweep's old `chargescheduled-<client_id>-P<n>-<today>` key meant a lost post-charge status write (the sweep created the PaymentIntent in Stripe but its follow-up `payN_status` write failed) re-created the SAME installment's PaymentIntent the NEXT night under a fresh date-suffixed key — a real double charge. ACH makes this worse: its success webhook lands DAYS later, so the card-only webhook backstop that saves the row does not exist, and the sweep re-fires in the meantime. The key is now the date-less `chargescheduled-<client_id>-P<n>`, so within Stripe's 24h idempotency window a re-select replays the SAME PaymentIntent instead of minting a second one. Also (v612): ALWAYS error-check the post-charge status write — the sweep now alerts Jake ("charge SUCCEEDED in Stripe but row status write FAILED — fix payN_status manually") rather than letting the failed write silently re-arm the charge. Refines #15: auto-rotate applies to RETRY-able failures (a genuine new attempt), NOT to a charge's LOGICAL identity (the same installment must keep the same key).

**229. `payment_intent.payment_failed` for OFF-SESSION charges is a LATE-ACH signal, not a duplicate (2026-07-16).** The old blanket rationale — "`payment_intent.payment_failed` skips off-session installments because the sweeps alert synchronously" — is CARD-ONLY.
