Before hunting a bug on a mystery sandbox transfer, check the `tr_` id shape + the (absent) description/metadata: a bare `tr_3...` with no description is sandbox demo noise, not a VFO money movement. `see #270`.

**271. The "Additional information required" step (Tax 1 - Diagnostic) is now a plan-COLUMN button step, PROGRAM-AGNOSTIC — its 3 legacy dropdown sub-task rows still exist in `program_client_tasks` with their old `client_tax_progress` data but are hidden UNCONDITIONALLY in the FE; do not resurrect them (2026-07-23). ALWAYS-APPLIES (tax track).** *[Annotated 2026-08-07: this entry's contrast with the tax-returns stamp still holds but its shape changed — `additional_info_received_at` has NO `program_id` gate at all, while `tax_returns_received_at` is now program-shaped rather than program-4-only (program 4 unconditional, Holistic request-gated). See **#340**.]* The step kept its 2-option dropdown ('Additional info required' / 'No additional info required') but its 3 dropdown sub-steps ("Email to obtain information required sent" / "Information received" / "Information passed to VFO-L") were REPLACED by a "Request additional information" email button + inline compose card + AI PC Admin cascade, cloning the Request Tax Returns pattern but PROGRAM-AGNOSTIC (identical in Holistic program 1 and VFO Tax Planning program 4) and carrying requester free text. `automation_TAX_request_additional_info` (`actions/tax/request-additional-info.ts`) is in BOTH `ADMIN_ONLY_ACTIONS` and `TAX_PLANNER_ALLOWED_ACTIONS` — the step is on the three-surface planner-editable whitelist (#262), so admin OR the allocated tax planner can send (guarded by `denyIfNotPlannerPlan`). The email (template `TAX_request_additional_info`, Draft mode) carries the free text via `[REQUESTED_INFO]` (trim + `\n`→`<br>`) and the SAME secure `/tax-upload?token=` link (reuses `clients.tax_upload_token`). New plan columns `additional_info_requested_at` / `additional_info_received_at` (migration `20260723120000`) drive the two AI PC Admin sub-steps. On ANY client upload, `actions/vault/upload-notify.ts` finds the NEWEST plan with `additional_info_requested_at NOT NULL` and ALWAYS-overwrites `additional_info_received_at` (re-greens on EVERY upload) + fires `TAX_additional_info_received` (Tracy/Tray) + `TAX_planner_additional_info_uploaded` (allocated planner). A resend re-stamps `requested_at` and CLEARS `received_at`. CRITICAL asymmetries: (a) unlike `tax_returns_received_at`, this stamp has NO `program_id` gate; (b) the returns cascade and the additional-info cascade can BOTH fire on ONE shared-link upload, by design. The 3 legacy child task names are filtered UNCONDITIONALLY at every FE render/count site — their DB rows are retained but never displayed. `see #271`.

**272. Planner bell TITLES must name the client — `notifyAllocatedPlanner` substitutes a `[Client Name]` token (lazy `clients` lookup) and dedupe `"unread"` is title-keyed, so per-client titles scope dedupe PER client (2026-07-23).** The user requires every allocated-planner bell to say WHICH client it concerns. `utils/tax-planner-notify.ts notifyAllocatedPlanner` now substitutes a `[Client Name]` token in the bell title/message (a lazy `clients` lookup by `plan.client_id`, performed only when the token is present, inside the existing try/catch). All planner-bell titles carry the name this session (upload-notify returns bell → `${name} uploaded their tax returns …`; the new additional-info bell → `${name} uploaded the additional information you requested`; `allocate-planner.ts`, `invoice-receipt.ts`, `revshare.ts`, `save-task.ts`, `revshare-sweep.ts` all use `[Client Name]`). Important SIDE EFFECT: `notifyAllocatedPlanner`'s dedupe key is the notification TITLE with `dedupe:"unread"` — so per-client titles now scope the dedupe per client (previously two different clients' identical titles could cross-suppress each other's bell). Any NEW planner bell MUST include the client name in its title, both for the user requirement and to keep dedupe correctly per-client. `see #272`.

**273. `client_notes` WRITES are planner-callable — the three note write actions (`add_client_note`/`update_client_note`/`delete_client_note`) sit in BOTH `ADMIN_ONLY_ACTIONS` and `TAX_PLANNER_ALLOWED_ACTIONS` with in-handler group-scope guards; planners intentionally READ internal notes (2026-07-23). ALWAYS-APPLIES.** The Tax Planner portal gained the SAME per-phase Notes chips/panels as the admin tax track (shared `PhaseNotesButton`/`PhaseNotesPanel` + the "Save internal" / "Save & share with member" two-button visibility toggle; amber Internal / teal Shared badges per #214). To let the allocated planner WRITE notes, the three note write actions were added to `TAX_PLANNER_ALLOWED_ACTIONS` (`constants/role-gates.ts`) while STAYING in `ADMIN_ONLY_ACTIONS` — per #257 the planner allowlist is the operative gate for a `tax_planner` caller (it SKIPS the tab gate and the ADMIN_ONLY gate), so each write MUST carry its OWN in-handler whole-GROUP-scope guard: `add_client_note` → `denyIfNotPlannerClient(client_id)`; `update_client_note` / `delete_client_note` → the NEW `denyIfNotPlannerNote(note_id)` (`utils/tax-planner-ownership.ts`), which resolves the note's `client_id` FIRST (403 on blank/missing note or out-of-group client) then runs the same group check — fail-closed, and a no-op (null) for admin callers. READS: planners intentionally see ALL notes — `load_client_notes` is planner-allowlisted and UNFILTERED (internal = team-wide INCLUDING tax planners, a deliberate product decision, so no visibility filter for the planner role); the member surface stays the SEPARATE shared-only `client_notes_load_shared`. Author attribution is the planner's login name via the existing `created_by: session?.name` path (`tax_planner_login` returns `name`). FE: exactly six note gate sites in `TaxPrioritiesTab.jsx` changed from `!readOnly && !plannerMode` to `!readOnly` (normal phase + merged Tax 5 card + post-spec, each a button/panel pair); members stay excluded. A FUTURE notes surface for another restricted role MUST add its own in-handler guard AND decide its own visibility filter — do not assume the planner "read-everything" rule generalizes. `see #273`.

**274. "Client decision 2" (Tax 5) is a PLAIN dropdown whose TWO status strings (`'Continue DD'` / `'Move to Implementation'`) are LOAD-BEARING exact-match values, resolved BY NAME (not the old `tax_dd_implementation` sentinel) in both repos (2026-07-23). ALWAYS-APPLIES (tax track).** The task previously used a `status_options='tax_dd_implementation'` sentinel that drove a two-button (Continue DD / Move to Implementation) FE branch and a `byCode("tax_dd_implementation")` lookup in the overview. This session it became a plain dropdown: FE `TaxPrioritiesTab.jsx` DELETED the `task.status_options === 'tax_dd_implementation'` two-button branch (the task now falls through to the generic dropdown+StepDate), and BE `actions/clients/overview-tax.ts` added a `byName(name)` helper so `moveToImpl = statusOfTask(byName("Client decision 2")) === "Move to Implementation"` (was `byCode("tax_dd_implementation")`). The old `else if (code === "tax_dd_implementation")` step branch in overview-tax was deliberately LEFT — after the sentinel swap it falls to the generic `else` whose `applicable: reach` is IDENTICAL for Tax 5 phases (`phaseReach` returns `continueReach` for any phase starting "Tax 5"), verified equivalent. The `status_options` sentinel is now DEAD: `program_client_tasks` ids 99 + 132 (one per program) were swapped `'tax_dd_implementation'` → `'Continue DD|Move to Implementation'` (a plain pipe-delimited two-option dropdown). Downstream consumers of the two status STRINGS are UNCHANGED and load-bearing: FE `tax5bUnlocked` unlock, the "(Moved to implementation)" grey-outs of "VFO specialist introductions / discussions" + "Confirm ready for implementation", statusColors green mappings, and the planner whitelists. Renaming the task name OR either option string breaks the Tax 5 unlock + the overview `moveToImpl` skip logic in BOTH repos — grep for the exact `"Client decision 2"` name and both option strings before any rename. `see #274`.

**275. Phase-collapse convention — EVERY phase/stage tracking surface defaults a FULLY-DONE phase to collapsed on mount; a NEW surface must follow, and manual toggles always win after mount (2026-07-23).** Completed phases/stages start collapsed on load across all tracking tracks. The canonical rules: (a) compute the default from the component's OWN done-predicate — `getPhaseState(phase) !== 'done'` (tax), `phaseState(tasks, prog) !== 'done'` (member training), `stageNState`-mirrors (onboarding) — NOT an ad-hoc re-count; (b) the allDone form is `tasks.length === 0 || tasks.every(done)` (the `=== 0 ||` so an empty / all-`auto` phase counts as done and collapses — the old `length > 0 &&` left them expanded); (c) onboarding stage defaults are computed ONCE per loaded row (inline mirrors of stage1/2/3State inside the load effect, incl. the accountant partnership sub-step + VFO-Associate stages 1-2 forced collapsed) so later manual toggles and live `setOb` updates are NEVER overwritten; (d) PFT confirmed-branch phases (VFO-Associate / VFO-FT) open only WHILE incomplete: `branchConfirmed && !allDone`. Surfaces updated this session: `TaxPrioritiesTab.jsx`, `RegularPrioritiesTab.jsx`, `PipMeetingsTab.jsx`, `MemberMSMTracking.jsx` (both MemberClientTrackView and the newly-added MemberTrainingView collapse), `AdvisorOnboarding.jsx`, `AccountantOnboarding.jsx`, `PFTEngagementTrack.jsx`. Already-correct (NOT touched): `SpecialistOnboarding.jsx`, `map1/ClientTrackViewV2.jsx`, admin `MSMTracking.jsx`. A NEW phase surface MUST default done→collapsed with its own predicate and the `=== 0 ||` form, and must let manual toggles win after mount. `see #275`.

**276. Tax planner revenue-share confirmation email routes To the GROUP, Cc the planner (2026-07-24).** `utils/tax-planner-payout.ts` (`transferPlannerShare`, templates `TAX_planner_revshare|retainer` + `|implementation`, program-agnostic — covers Tax Planning program 4 AND holistic Tax Priorities): the confirmation email goes **To** the tax-planning group's `tax_planning_groups.contact_email` (resolved by `planner.member_type`→group `name`, the SAME mapping as the payout destination) and **Cc**s the planner via the `TAX_PLANNER` token in `cc_list`; it is addressed "Dear [Group Name]," and names the planner ("…client allocated to [Planner Name]…") in the body. There is **NO `GROUP` role token** in `resolveTemplateRecipients` (tokens: RECIPIENT/CLIENT/MEMBER/ASSIGNED_PF/TAX_PLANNER/SPECIALIST/ADVISOR/ACCOUNTANT/TEAM_MEMBER/TEAM), so the group email is fed in as the handler's `RECIPIENT` (the built-in To that the empty `to_list` falls back to) — do NOT expect a GROUP token to exist. Fallback: if a group has no `contact_email`, To reverts to the planner so the email is never unaddressed. The email only drafts on a SUCCESSFUL group transfer (`status === "Yes"`). **SANDBOX collapses ALL recipients to the single sandbox email** (the `isSandbox` early-return in `resolveTemplateRecipients`), so a sandbox test verifies body copy only, NEVER the To/Cc routing. The payout MONEY already went to the group Connect account — this session aligned the email destination with it. `see #276`.

**277. The Payment Continuation tax backfill takes the 3-way split as per-IMPLEMENTATION dollars (scaled to the engine's dollars-of-total storage), and MUST settle EVERY sweep-rescuable payout leg for the already-collected retainer — including the PLANNER leg (2026-07-24).** `PaymentContinuationTab.jsx` + `actions/migration/backfill-tax.ts`. TWO load-bearing invariants: **(a) split is per-IMPLEMENTATION dollars, stored as dollars-of-total.** The operator enters/sees the IMPLEMENTATION split — the dollars that actually pay out when the implementation fee is charged (member / tax planner / VFOS; preset `'1/3 Member, 1/3 Tax Planner, 1/3 VFOS'` auto-fills thirds of the implementation amount, VFOS absorbs the rounding remainder; Custom must sum to the implementation amount ±0.01; the split section is HIDDEN + Save unblocked when implementation is 0). The retainer was settled on the old system and pays out NOTHING, so its historical split is NOT stored (retainer = amount/date/receipt record only). The backend VALIDATES the entered split sums to the implementation amount, then SCALES to the engine's dollars-of-total convention for storage: `scale = totalForSplit / implAmt` (totalForSplit = `total_fee` provided else `retainer + implementation`), `member_share = round2(mImpl * scale)` etc. — ratio-preserving, so the engine's per-payment proration (#252: a share is ALWAYS dollars of the total, portion = `(share/totalGross) × paymentReceived`) pays out EXACTLY the entered per-implementation dollars on the implementation charge. Payload keys are the explicit `impl_member_share` / `impl_tax_planner_share` / `impl_vfos_share` (unambiguously per-implementation). `tax_planner_share` stores NULL when 0/blank — the nightly sweep's planner-leg query filters `.not("tax_planner_share","is",null)`, so a 0 row would be enumerated every night while `transferPlannerShare` skips it without writing a status. **(b) settle EVERY sweep-rescuable leg at backfill.** A payment already collected on the old system must have every revshare leg a sweep could later "rescue" pre-settled, or the sweep pays a phantom cut. The backfill settles the retainer's MEMBER leg (`retainer_rev_paid`) AND its PLANNER leg (`retainer_planner_paid` = `"N/A — No Share Due"` + `retainer_planner_completed_at`) because a NULL planner leg + a settled member leg + a receipt + an allocated planner is EXACTLY the sweep's stranded-planner-leg rescue signature (#265) — without settling it, once a planner is allocated (via the Allocate Tax Planner step) the sweep would transfer the planner a portion of that old-system retainer. Any NEW backfill/migration of an already-collected payment MUST settle every leg the relevant sweep can rescue, not just the member leg. Cross-ref #252 (dollars-of-total math), #265 (stranded-leg rescue signature), #230 (Payment Continuation collision/`force` guard, `legacy_source='old-system'`). `see #277`. **ALWAYS-APPLIES (money).**

**278. Headshot image pipeline convention — every upload routes through ImageCropModal (500×500 JPEG q0.85, white-flattened) + the `upload_headshot` action (1-year cache as of v652); every headshot grid/list `<img>` carries `loading="lazy" decoding="async"` (2026-07-24). ALWAYS-APPLIES (Frontend).** The `headshots` public storage bucket shares the `supabase.co` host with the REST API; because showroom/list grids rendered every headshot EAGERLY and the bucket held large PNG originals (110 objects, avg 230kB / max 1.9MB / 24.7MB total, 108 PNG, served `max-age=3600`), image downloads starved API calls and produced multi-second skeletons across SPA navigation — the portal-wide slow-load this session root-caused and fixed. The convention now has THREE load-bearing parts: (a) **all headshot uploads go through `ImageCropModal`** (`src/components/admin/ImageCropModal.jsx`), which exports the crop as `toDataURL('image/jpeg', 0.85)` at 500×500 flattened onto white (~25kB vs ~300kB PNG) — `MembersPanel.jsx` / `SpecialistsPanel.jsx` / `TaxPlannersPanel.jsx` all build the upload `File` as `'headshot.jpg', {type:'image/jpeg'}` and save under a `.jpg` filename; (b) **the `upload_headshot` handler** (`actions/specialists/upload-headshot.ts`, edge) sets `cacheControl: "31536000"` on `.upload()` so every object is served `Cache-Control: public, max-age=31536000` — safe because every upload gets a fresh timestamped filename (no stale-cache risk); (c) **every headshot grid/list `<img>` carries `loading="lazy" decoding="async"`** (the two expert grids in `MembersPanel`, the list/profile imgs in `SpecialistsPanel`, `MemberShowroom.jsx` avatar, both `MemberPortal.jsx` grid imgs). The 2026-07-24 backfill re-encoded ALL 111 legacy objects in place (sharp: ≤500px, JPEG q82, transparency-preserving) via Supabase CLI `storage rm`+`cp` under the SAME object names → 24.7MB → 2.2MB, avg 20kB, max 54kB, 110 image/jpeg + 1 image/png (`20260629234146_Shay Novak.png`, real transparency, kept PNG), all `max-age=31536000`. The `.png` object NAMES that now contain JPEG bytes were LEFT unchanged — **Content-Type is authoritative, browsers honor it, and DB `headshot_image` values reference those names, so never "fix" the extensions**. A future upload path that bypasses the crop modal reintroduces multi-hundred-kB originals; a NEW grid without lazy loading re-saturates the shared `supabase.co` host and re-starves API calls. `see #278`. **ALWAYS-APPLIES (Frontend).**

**279. Supabase CLI storage ops quirks — `cp` cannot overwrite, `rm` needs `--yes`, `--cache-control` is passed RAW, drive-letter src paths parse as URL schemes, and HEAD lies about the cache header (2026-07-24).** Learned re-encoding the `headshots` bucket in place. (a) **`supabase storage cp` CANNOT overwrite an existing object** — it returns `409 Duplicate`; you must `supabase storage rm` the object first, then `cp` the replacement. (b) **`supabase storage rm` prompts `y/N` interactively** — in a non-interactive shell (no TTY) the prompt reads EOF and every `rm` SILENTLY FAILS unless you pass `--yes`. (c) **`--cache-control` is passed to the storage API RAW**, so it must be the FULL header value `max-age=31536000`, not the bare seconds `31536000` (seconds-only stores a malformed header). Note the supabase-js `.upload({cacheControl})` path is DIFFERENT — there you pass the bare seconds string and the SDK emits `max-age=` (see #278/`upload-headshot.ts`). (d) **Windows drive-letter source paths (`C:\…`) parse as URL schemes** by the CLI — use a RELATIVE path from the linked project dir instead. (e) **A HEAD request on a public storage object reports `Cache-Control: no-cache`** regardless of the object's real metadata — only a GET returns the true metadata-driven header, so verify cache headers with GET, never HEAD. `see #279`.

**280. Membership invoices/receipts are driven by the `year_start`/`year_end` stamp on `member_payment_schedule`, NOT by the plan row — the renewal pass advances `plan.renewal_date` but deliberately leaves `start_date` alone, so the plan no longer describes year 1 by the time year 2 is invoiced (2026-07-24). ALWAYS-APPLIES (money).** `automation_MEMBERSHIP_invoicereceipt` (`actions/membership/invoice-receipt.ts`, PUBLIC + service-role gate) is chained by the Stripe webhook from THREE points, all preserved as service-role HTTP fetches: card first payment (after `checkout.session.completed` → `activateMembershipPlan`), ACH first payment (`checkout.session.async_payment_succeeded`), and every later pull (`payment_intent.succeeded` where `metadata.payment_kind` is `membership_pull` or `membership_first_payment`; `membership_termination_fee` is deliberately EXCLUDED — a termination fee is not a membership payment). WHICH documents go out is DERIVED FROM THE LEDGER, never passed in: rows sharing a `year_start` are one membership year, and the row with the earliest `due_date` in that group is the year opener that earns an INVOICE + receipt; every later row gets a RECEIPT ONLY. `kind` = `transfer`/`first` when no rows exist with a smaller `year_start` (`plan.transfer` decides which), else `renewal` for an opener, else `monthly`. `year_start`/`year_end` are stamped at BOTH insert sites — `activate.ts` (year_start = basisDate, year_end = the computed renewal) and `sweep.ts` renewal pass (year_start = the renewal 15th just reached, year_end = `monthShift(+12,15)`) — a NEW schedule-row insert site MUST stamp them or its rows become invisible to the year grouping. Idempotency is `docs_emailed_at` on the paid row, so a redelivered Stripe event cannot double-send. `activateMembershipPlan` now returns `activated|refreshed|failed`: `refreshed` means the member re-used the setup link to SWAP their payment method and MUST NOT re-send the confirmation email (which fires once, at genuine first sign-up only, never at renewal). Documents file to the **`member-ert-docs` ERT vault** keyed by `member_number` (NOT Google Drive, unlike MAP 1 — user requirement), best-effort: a vault failure logs and never loses the already-sent email. `member_payment_plans.prior_payments_made` is ADMIN-ENTERED on the transfer form because the system has no record of payments collected on the old platform; it falls back to `(12 - remaining)` only for plans predating the field. Member-facing copy says **"ERT Membership"** everywhere — never "advisor"/"accountant" (the stored `category` is literally `'advisor'`). The whole engine is **category-agnostic**: `category` appears ONLY in `plan-save.ts` (validate + store) and `plans-load.ts` (filter), never in the handler, the confirmation email, the PDF templates, `activate.ts`, `sweep.ts` or any webhook branch — templates are pipeline-scoped and the FE is one component rendered twice with a `category` prop — so Accountant Membership Fees behaves identically to Advisor. `see #280`.

**282. `allocateDocNumber` returns its candidate even when the reservation INSERT fails, so a caller that trusts it blindly issues duplicate accounting numbers — a new owner column must ALSO be added to `document_numbers_exactly_one_owner` (2026-07-24). ALWAYS-APPLIES (money).** `utils/doc-numbers.ts` retries on `23505` (number taken) but on ANY OTHER insert error it logs and `break`s, then falls through to `return candidate` — an UNRESERVED number. Membership hit this the day it shipped: the migration added `document_numbers.member_payment_plan_id` but did NOT add it to the `document_numbers_exactly_one_owner` CHECK, which counts exactly one of `client_id` / `advisor_onboarding_id` / `accountant_onboarding_id` / `specialist_onboarding_id`. Every membership insert therefore summed to 0 and failed with `23514`, the count query kept returning 0, and TWO different payments were both issued `INV-59524-0001` / `REC-59524-0001` — silently, because the swallow made it look like success. Fixed by migration `20260724140000_document_numbers_membership_owner` (adds the column to the CHECK + a matching `ON DELETE SET NULL` FK). Guard added in `actions/membership/invoice-receipt.ts`: after allocating, it SELECTs the claimed numbers back out of `document_numbers` and, if any is missing, bells Jake via `notifyJakeFailure` and returns 500 WITHOUT sending — `docs_emailed_at` stays unset so the next delivery retries cleanly. TWO rules follow: (a) any NEW `document_numbers` owner column MUST be added to the CHECK in the same migration; (b) any NEW caller of `allocateDocNumber` should verify the reservation landed rather than trusting the return value — the shared helper's swallow was deliberately left unchanged because nine other pipelines depend on its current contract. `see #282`.

**283. `terminated` does NOT free a member's plan slot — only `canceled` does (2026-07-24).** The partial unique index is `member_payment_plans_one_live_idx ON (member_number) WHERE status <> 'canceled'`, so a `terminated` plan still occupies the slot and creating a replacement fails with `duplicate key value violates unique constraint "member_payment_plans_one_live_idx"`. This is a known open item from the 2026-07-17 audit, restated here because it surfaces as a confusing FE error ("Could not save plan: duplicate key…") with no hint about the cause. To re-plan a terminated member, flip the old row to `canceled` (`update member_payment_plans set status='canceled', canceled_at=now()`). Related index trap on the ledger: `member_payment_schedule_plan_due_membership_key` is `(plan_id, due_date) WHERE kind='membership'` (#239), so ageing a test row onto a date another membership row already holds also 23505s. `see #283`.

**281. The membership card processing fee exists for exactly ONE combination — `advisor_model === 'New Model'` AND card — and when it does not apply the fee must not be MENTIONED anywhere a member can see (2026-07-24). ALWAYS-APPLIES (money).** Legacy Model members pay face value on card, and NOBODY pays a fee on ACH. Three charge sites share the gross-up and must stay in step: `actions/membership/setup-checkout.ts`, `sweep.ts` and `terminate.ts` all compute `amountCents` as `Math.round((base + 0.30) / (1 - 0.029) * 100)` when `feeApplies` — identical to `advisor/stripe-checkout.ts`, `accountant/stripe-checkout.ts`, `msm/pip-stripe-checkout.ts`, `gc/create-checkout.ts` and the MAP 1 backfill. On a $1,500 pull that charges **$1,545.11**, a fee of **$45.11** — and the "2.9% + $0.30" label is ACCURATE, because Stripe's cut on $1,545.11 is 0.029 × 1545.11 + 0.30 = $45.11, leaving ERT exactly $1,500. (Do not mis-derive this as $44.90; the gross-up divides by 0.971, it does not multiply by 0.029.) The DISPLAYED fee is NOT re-derived: like every other pipeline, membership stamps what Stripe actually took — `member_payment_schedule.card_processing_fee`, written at settle time in both webhook branches as `amount_received/100 − base`, mirroring the `parseFloat((amountReceivedCents / 100 - baseAmount).toFixed(2))` convention used for `pipeline_map1.card_processing_fee` and friends. `cardFeeFor` in `actions/membership/shared.ts` survives ONLY as a fallback for payments that settled before that column existed. A combined catch-up charge stamps the SAME total on every row it covered, so read the fee from the primary row and NEVER sum it. When `feeApplies` is false the invoice's two fee rows + the blue "a card processing fee will also apply to each scheduled payment" footnote, the receipt's Card Fee Breakdown box + the "incl. card fee" bar label + the Next Payment fee note, the email's `[CARD_FEE_TEXT]` sentence AND the schedule line's "each with the same 2.9% + $0.30 card processing fee added" clause ALL render empty — the word "fee" never reaches the member (verified live: a NEW MODEL member paying by ACH sees no fee wording anywhere). `see #281`.

**284. A tax planner share with NO allocated planner is HELD, not skipped — `Awaiting Planner Allocation` is a real, non-terminal status that parks money and raises an action-required bell, and allocating a planner releases it immediately (2026-07-25). ALWAYS-APPLIES (money).** Before this, `transferPlannerShare` (`utils/tax-planner-payout.ts`) early-returned `{status:"skipped"}` when `tax_planner_id` was null — no DB write, no alert. The client was still charged in full and the member + strategic legs still paid, so a third of the engagement quietly stayed in the VFO balance with nothing on any screen saying so. Now: a missing SHARE still returns `skipped` (genuinely nothing to pay), but a share with no planner writes `{kind}_planner_paid = PLANNER_SHARE_WITHHELD` (`"Awaiting Planner Allocation"`, exported from the same module) and fires `notifyJakeFailure` with `ruleKey: 'TAX_planner_share_withheld'`, `actionRequired: true` (migration `20260725120000` adds the `notification_rules` row so it is re-routable). The status is deliberately **non-terminal**, so the revshare handler's `plannerTerminal` guard lets it be retried. THREE release paths, in order of speed: (a) `actions/tax/allocate-planner.ts` pays it out the instant a planner is allocated — this is the primary path and is why the operator does not wait for the 02:30 sweep; (b) `actions/tax/revshare-sweep.ts`'s planner-retry query now accepts the status (`.or(…is.null,…eq.Failed,…eq."Awaiting Planner Allocation")`) as the backstop; (c) any later revshare re-fire. The bell clears itself on ANY terminal outcome (`Yes` / `Money Mapping` / `N/A — No Share Due`) via `plannerWithheldTitle(clientName, clientRef)` — Money-Mapping and no-share-due resolve a held share just as truly as a transfer does. **The allocate-time release carries the SAME gates as the sweep and must keep them:** the leg must be non-terminal, its `{kind}_receipt_number` must be set, and its `{kind}_rev_paid` must already be terminal (`memberResolved`) — without the last one, allocating a planner on a freshly-paid plan would pay the planner before the client's Tax 4 Continue / Tax 5 Proceed click (the #265 bug), and without the "never revisit a terminal leg" rule it would re-pay a migrated client's old-system retainer (#277). A payout failure inside the release is caught and swallowed so it can never fail the allocation itself — the sweep retries. Cross-ref #265, #277, #251/#252. `see #284`. **ALWAYS-APPLIES (money).**

**285. Every leg of a revenue split must be prorated by the SAME denominator the payout engine uses — hardcoded `/2` and `/n` shortcuts agree with it only when the payments happen to be equal (2026-07-25).** Both bugs lived in `actions/payments/normalize.ts` and both were invisible on live data. `buildTaxRows` computed the member leg as `(share / totalFee) * thisPayment` but the VFO leg as `vfos_share / 2` — correct only while retainer == implementation, which is true of every migrated tax plan and of nothing else. `buildMap1Rows` did the same thing one step removed: member prorated against `gross_fee`, VFO divided by the installment count, so they agreed only while `gross_fee` was unset or equal to `net_invoice`. Both now route every leg through one local `rsShareFor()` helper. The rule generalises beyond display: a share is ALWAYS dollars of the whole engagement and a payment's cut is `(share / grossTotal) × thisPayment` (#252) — anything that "knows" there are two payments, or four, is guessing. **A second rule about legacy payments, decided the same session and easy to get backwards:** whether to SHOW a derived breakdown for an already-settled payment depends on whether the POLICY changed, not on whether it is settled. A migrated **tax retainer** shows `—  settled on old system` with NO dollars, because it was collected under a 2-way policy and the stored split now describes a 3-way one — deriving figures would state a payout that never happened. A migrated **MAP 1 installment** KEEPS its dollars and changes only the status wording to `Settled on old system`, because MAP 1's split is the same before and after migration, so those figures are accurate and hiding them would lose real information. `see #285`.

**286. A step's completion must derive from the column that actually drives behaviour, never from a free-text `client_tax_progress.status` that merely echoes it (2026-07-25).** "Allocate to Advanced Tax Planner" stored the chosen planner's NAME as its progress status while the load-bearing value is `client_tax_plans.tax_planner_id`. Five migrated plans carried the name of a departed employee (`Tim Gacsy`) with a NULL `tax_planner_id`: the dropdown correctly rendered `-- Select --` (the name resolves to no `tax_planners` row) while the step showed **green/Done** and counted toward the phase pill and the hero percentage — a step reporting complete for a client where nobody was allocated and nobody could be paid. Fixed in `TaxPrioritiesTab.jsx` by adding the case to `isTaskStatused()` (`tax_planner_select` / `Allocate to Advanced Tax Planner` → `livePlan?.tax_planner_id != null`) and by narrowing the step's own `isAllocated` from `!!allocatedId || !!p.status` to `!!allocatedId`. **`isTaskStatused()` is the single home for every "this step does not live in `client_tax_progress`" rule** (it already held `tax_hlm_confirm`, `tax_presentation_link`, `tax_returns_request`, `Additional information required`) and feeds the hero count AND `getPhaseState`; the per-phase-card `doneTasks` counter had drifted into keeping its own duplicate copy of those rules, so it was collapsed to call the helper. Add new cases THERE, not inline. The stale strings were left in place deliberately — they are inert once completion keys off the id, and selecting a real planner overwrites them. `see #286`.

**287. The purchase-email policy is system-wide: a CARD purchase gets the invoice/receipt and NO payment-confirmation email; ACH gets the confirmation at purchase and the documents at settle; a CHECK still gets both at clear (2026-07-26). ALWAYS-APPLIES (money / email).** A card clears the instant the buyer submits, so the invoice/receipt lands in the same breath and a separate "we received your payment" note reads as a duplicate. ACH sits 2-4 days in flight, so the confirmation exists to break that silence and the documents follow at settlement. The check path is deliberately EXEMPT (confirmation + documents both fire at check-clear). **Any NEW payment pipeline MUST follow this shape.** Covers MAP 1 payment 1, Tax retainer, Advisor, Accountant, PIP, Specialist background check, Specialist monthly licence, SpecRev one-time and Membership first sign-up; Growth Credits sends no purchase emails at all; MAP 1 installments 2-4 and Tax implementation already had none. **WHERE THE GATE LIVES IS LOAD-BEARING and differs per pipeline.** For MAP 1 (`actions/pipeline/contract-confirmation-email.ts`) and Tax (`actions/tax/confirmation-email.ts`) the webhook still chains the confirmation handler for BOTH methods and the gate sits INSIDE the handler, because those handlers also own non-email payment-1 side effects that must still run for a card — the PF "client paid" bell, the ERT vault agreement copy, Tracy's new-case email and the load-bearing `c24_email_sent` stamp. **A call-site gate there would silently kill all of them.** The Specialist licence (`actions/onboarding/license-confirmation-email.ts`) is also gated inside the handler, for a different reason: TWO racing webhook call sites chain it (`checkout.session.completed` + the invoice-paid processor) and both must obey the same rule (see #108). Straight call-site gates are correct only where the handler owns nothing but the email — Specialist background check (`!isCardS`), SpecRev one-time (`!srIsCard`), PIP (`isCardP` branch), Membership (`mmMethod === "ach"`). MAP 1 / Tax record the skip as **`Skipped - Card (Receipt Only)`** (`constants/confirmation-status.ts CONFIRMATION_CARD_SKIP`, mirrored in the frontend at `src/lib/confirmationStatus.js`) in `pipeline_map1.confirmation_status` / `client_tax_plans.retainer_confirmation_status`. That value is **terminal-equivalent to `"Sent"` for every idempotency guard** — a replayed webhook must not re-raise the PF bell — but it deliberately does **NOT** stamp `*_confirmation_email_sent_at`, which is what keeps a manual admin resend possible. Any surface that reads those columns must treat CARD_SKIP as resolved, not pending, or it shows a step that is never coming. `see #287`.

**288. An auto-created session worktree can be cut from a STALE LOCAL main — verify `git rev-list --count HEAD..origin/main` = 0 in EVERY pre-existing worktree before editing, testing or deploying (2026-07-26). ALWAYS-APPLIES (deploy / ops).** This session's react worktree was created by the app from a local `main` that was **17 commits behind `origin/main`**, while the edge worktree in the same session happened to be current — so "one worktree is fine" proves nothing about the other. It was caught only by accident: the dev server was rendering old UI. **The danger is asymmetric.** A stale REACT worktree wastes time and produces confusing merge conflicts. A stale EDGE worktree would `supabase functions deploy` a build that silently REVERTS every backend change merged since the stale base — over the live function, with no error and no diff to look at. The check is one command per worktree at session start: `git fetch origin && git rev-list --count HEAD..origin/main` must print `0`. The fix is `git merge origin/main` into the worktree branch (resolve conflicts, re-run the verification gate), never a fresh worktree on top of the stale one. Related: gotcha #146 (the Bash cwd persists — always `cd` to the right worktree before a deploy). `see #288`.

**289. The advisor/accountant confirmation handlers no longer chain `_invoicereceipt` — the WEBHOOK owns receipt sequencing on every path, and the AI PC Admin cascade step labels are now canonical across five surfaces (2026-07-26).** Before this, `automation_ADVISOR_confirmationemail` / `automation_ACCOUNTANT_confirmationemail` each chained their `_invoicereceipt` downstream, which is why a card buyer got confirmation-then-receipt instantly and an ACH buyer got nothing at all until clear. Now `checkout.session.completed` chains ONE action selected by method (card → `*_invoicereceipt` directly, ACH → `*_confirmationemail`) and `payment_intent.succeeded` (the ACH settle) chains `*_invoicereceipt` (+ PIP revshare) directly. **The confirmation handlers chain nothing.** Re-adding a chain inside them double-sends the invoice and receipt on the ACH path — the handler header comments say this explicitly; do not "restore" it. Second half: every sign / countersign / link / pay / receipt cascade now shares ONE wording set across **five** admin surfaces — Tax (`TaxPrioritiesTab.jsx sharedSteps`), MAP 1 (`ClientTrackViewV2.jsx`, all three blocks **plus** the chips map), `AdvisorOnboarding.jsx`, `AccountantOnboarding.jsx`, `PipMeetingsTab.jsx`: *"Engagement agreement created and sent for signing" / "Engagement agreement signed" / "Engagement agreement signed by CEO" / "Payment link sent (ACH or Card choice)" / "Payment collected" / "Invoice and receipt created and emailed to client"*. MAP 1's old "Payment made" + "Payment received" pair is merged into the single "Payment collected"; the advisor/accountant visible "Confirmation email sent" rows are deleted (ACH still sends it server-side — the preview chips moved onto "Payment collected"). **Renaming any of these steps means editing all five surfaces in the same change** — grep the exact label string, because the labels are also what the StepEmailsChip captions and several done-predicates are keyed to. A track step whose done-predicate reads a confirmation status must accept BOTH `"Sent"` and `CONFIRMATION_CARD_SKIP` (the tax retainer step keyed on `"Sent"` alone and would have stuck un-done forever on card rows). `see #289`.

**AMENDED 2026-08-12 (v734) — the canonical six now span SEVEN surfaces, not five, and the member portal is one of them.** Two stragglers were brought in line: **`SpecialistOnboarding.jsx` Stage 4** (*"Agreement sent"* / *"Agreement signed by specialist"* / *"Agreement signed by CEO"* / *"Payment link sent"*) and **`MemberMSMTracking.jsx`**'s member-portal cascade (*"Agreement sent to client"* / *"Client signed"* / *"CEO signed"* / *"Payment received"* / *"Invoice/receipt sent"*). So the grep-before-rename rule now covers `TaxPrioritiesTab.jsx`, `ClientTrackViewV2.jsx`, `AdvisorOnboarding.jsx`, `AccountantOnboarding.jsx`, `PipMeetingsTab.jsx`, `SpecialistOnboarding.jsx` **and `MemberMSMTracking.jsx`** — and note the last of those is **client-facing**, so a wording change there is visible outside the company. The rename was verified safe first, not after: MAP 1's `STEP_EMAIL_TPLS` already used the canonical strings so its label-keyed lookup was untouched, the specialist chips are keyed on their own separate titles (*"Onboarding agreement"*, *"Licensing fee"*), the specialist `specialist_onboarding_progress` task_keys are historical strings and did NOT move, and the member portal has no chips at all. *"(ACH or Card choice)"* was also checked for truthfulness on the specialist licensing row (`license-checkout.ts` really does branch `card` / `us_bank_account`) — a unified label that lies about one pipeline is worse than an inconsistent one. See **#382** for the label-as-lookup-key mechanism in full.

**290. A public token decision page must NEVER commit the decision on page load — corporate email link-scanners execute the page's JavaScript and will record it (2026-07-27). ALWAYS-APPLIES (Frontend).** On 2026-07-27 the two drafted Tax implementation-decision emails were sent; within seconds plan 49 (Aaron Burkhart) recorded `implementation_final_decision='Decline'` at 13:48:07 UTC, closing a **$10,000** implementation engagement. The nine public decide pages all ran `useEffect(() => { processDecision() }, [])`, POSTing `?token=&decision=` straight out of the query string on mount — so merely OPENING the link recorded the decision. The logs show a CORS preflight followed by the POST, i.e. a real browser environment ran the page's JS; scanner versus human is **indistinguishable**, because no provenance (IP, user-agent, click) is recorded anywhere. Every decide page is now **show-then-confirm**: initial `status` is `'confirm'`, the mount effect ONLY validates the URL params (missing token/decision → the same error view, with no button), the page renders `components/shared/DecisionConfirmCard.jsx`, and `processDecision()`/`run()` fires exclusively from that card's button `onClick`. The nine pages + their actions: `TaxPostReviewDecidePage` (`automation_TAX_postreviewclientdecision`), `TaxImplementDecidePage` (`automation_TAX_implementfinaldecision`), `TaxDecidePage` (`automation_TAX_finaldecision`), `DecidePage` (`automation_PCADMIN_finaldecision`), `AdvisorDecidePage` (`automation_ADVISOR_clientdecision`), `AccountantDecidePage` (`automation_ACCOUNTANT_clientdecision`), `PftFtDecidePage` (`automation_PFT_ftresponse`), `PftDecidePage` (`automation_PFT_undecided_response`), `SpecialistRevShareFinalPage` (`automation_SPECIALIST_revsharefinal`). **Any NEW public token page that records a decision or choice MUST follow this pattern.** Param quirks that are easy to break when copying a page: **`PftDecidePage` reads `choice`, NOT `decision`**, and **`DecidePage` carries `serviceLevel` (interpolated into the Yes confirmation message) plus `clientRef` (still posted)**; `SpecialistRevShareFinalPage` deliberately keeps its own non-`TokenShell` layout. Colour scheme is deliberate and user-approved: **red is reserved for the two money-destroying decisions** (refund the retainer, decline the implementation), soft "No" declines are slate `#64748b`, ExtraMeeting / questions are blue `#125ecc`, positive is green `#16a34a`. **This is near-zero risk, not zero** — a few scanners simulate clicks. Frontend-only: handlers, tokens, request bodies, email templates and button URLs are unchanged, so links already in flight keep working (the recipient just sees a confirm screen and clicks once more). `see #290`.

**291. Removing a departed staffer takes FOUR layers, and deleting their portal account is the one that changes the least — it silently converts every bell addressed to them into an unreadable void (2026-07-27). ALWAYS-APPLIES (Notifications).** Tim Gacsy left ~2026-07-13 and his `allowed_admins` row was deleted, so nothing in the portal could render a notification addressed to `tgacsy@elitert.com` — but his email was still a hardcoded constant in the tax + specialist-onboarding notification code, so bells kept being inserted for him and nobody ever saw one. It surfaced on 2026-07-27 when the phantom `$10,000` implementation Decline (#290) raised exactly ONE bell and that bell went to Tim. The 2026-07-21 cleanup (#250) had covered only the tax-return allowlist + the two upload rules, which is precisely the trap: a partial removal looks done. **The four layers, all of which must be checked:** (1) **the account** — `allowed_admins` (and any `*_logins` row); (2) **hardcoded code constants** — grep the EMAIL (`tgacsy`) *and* every alias identifier (`TIM_EMAIL`, `TAX_OWNERS`, ad-hoc arrays like `["tgacsy@elitert.com","tnmiller@elitert.com"]`), because a symbol rename hides the address from an email-only grep; (3) **`notification_rules`** — BOTH the live `recipients` override column and the `default_recipients` display column, per rule (#250); (4) **existing `notifications` rows** — unread bells already addressed to them are orphaned forever (action-required ones can never be cleared, since the clearing handler matches on recipient/title). The 2026-07-27 reroute replaced Tim with a routing RULE rather than another individual: tax client-decision bells (`TAX_impl_client_proceed`/`_decline`, `TAX_postreview_client_proceed`/`_refund`) and the Tax 4 meeting nudge (`TAX_tax4_decision_needed`) now go to the **assigned PF + the allocated tax planner** (nudge: + Tracy), via `taxDecisionRecipients()` in `utils/tax-notify.ts`, with **Tracy as the never-empty fallback** (`TAX_OWNERS = [TRACY_EMAIL]`); the specialist-onboarding tax-risk-notes prompts went to Tracy. **Two follow-on traps.** (a) The specialist progress `task_key`s `tim_tax_risk_notes` / `tracy_general_notes` were deliberately NOT renamed — they are stored strings read by the Stage-2/Stage-4 gates; but because BOTH note prompts now address Tracy, the auto-clear on note-save had to be re-keyed from recipient+link to the exact TITLE, or saving one note cleared the other's prompt. Consolidating two prompts onto one recipient always breaks a recipient-keyed clear. (b) **Dynamic token names are not standardised**: `TAX_planner_returns_uploaded` and the other `notifyAllocatedPlanner` rules use `ALLOCATED_TAX_PLANNER` while the new decision rules use `TAX_PLANNER` — two names for the same concept. A call site MUST pass whichever token its own rule's `recipients`/`default_recipients` actually contain, or a Notification-Editor override silently resolves to nothing. `see #291`.

**292. A bell whose recipients span two portals needs a per-recipient link — `notifyByRule`'s `links` map, not one shared admin URL (2026-07-27). ALWAYS-APPLIES (Notifications).** `NotifyByRuleArgs` gained an optional `links?: Record<string, string>` keyed by **lowercase final-recipient email**; each inserted row uses `links[recipient.toLowerCase()] ?? link`. It exists because the rerouted tax client-decision bells (#291) now land in BOTH an admin bell and the **tax-planner portal** bell, and a planner has no `/admin/...` route — the shared `/admin/client/<id>?tab=tax` link bounces them to login (the #18 cousin: the link must be a real route *for the recipient's portal*, not just a real route). Planner rows therefore get `/tax-planner/client/<client_id>?program=<program_id||1>`; PF and Tracy rows keep the admin link. Applied at three call sites (`implement-final-decision.ts`, `postreview-client-decision.ts`, `revshare-sweep.ts`'s Tax 4 nudge) and threaded through `insertTaxNotifications` (`utils/tax-notify.ts`) as `row.links`. **The extension is purely additive** — the map is optional and absent it every one of the ~70 existing `notifyByRule` call sites inserts byte-identically. Any NEW bell whose recipient set crosses a portal boundary (planner, specialist, member, client) MUST supply the map; a single link is only safe while every recipient is an admin. `see #292`.

**293. The standalone Tax Planning go/no-go is ONE step whose stored decision string is exactly `'Proceed'`, and whose Refund path deliberately writes NO progress status at all (2026-07-27). ALWAYS-APPLIES (tax track).** The old shape was two Set Up tasks — `tax_greenlight` ("Tax Plan Greenlight", Go/Stop) followed by a separate "Refund Paid" task greyed out unless Greenlight = `Stop`. Both were skippable (the phase-done math filtered the refund step out on any non-Stop plan), so a plan could run the whole diagnostic with no explicit go decision recorded, and the refund fired with no reason captured. They are now **ONE admin decision step**: `program_client_tasks` id **116** keeps its `status_options='tax_refund'` sentinel but is renamed **"Tax Plan Green/Red Light - Refund $500 Deposit if unable to proceed based on the information provided"** and moved to `phase_id=26` (**Tax 1 - Diagnostic**), right below "Additional information required". **POSITION UPDATED 2026-08-07 — it is NO LONGER the last step of Tax 1.** It sat at `task_order=8`/last from 2026-07-27 until the 2026-08-07 reorder moved "Client risk profile complete" (117) and "Allocate to Advanced Tax Planner" (118) AFTER it; it is now **`task_order=6`**, with 117 at 7 and 118 at 8, and the hidden legacy rows 120–122 at orders **3–5** (per #271). Nothing in this gotcha's done-math depends on the position — but **do not quote "last step of Tax 1" from here**; see #339 for the reorder and its warning consequences. Task **115** (`tax_greenlight`) is **DELETED** along with its three `'Go'` `client_tax_progress` rows; the **Set Up** phase now holds ONLY "Deposit Paid" (`tax_deposit_pi`, task 114). The task row is program-agnostic like every template row, but the Set Up / deposit flow is **program_id=4 (standalone Tax Planning) only** — Holistic (program 1) clients arrive via MAP 1 and never render Set Up. **The two load-bearing invariants.** (1) **`'Proceed'` is an exact-matched stored string in BOTH repos** — the FE shared helper `isTaskStatused` (`components/admin/tax/TaxPrioritiesTab.jsx`) and the BE `overview-tax.ts` `tax_refund` branch both compare `status === 'Proceed'`; `statusColors` also keys on it. Renaming the task OR the `'Proceed'` string breaks done-math in both repos at once. (2) **The Refund path writes no progress status whatsoever** — clicking Refund only opens the inline email-preview card, and a completed refund closes the step purely through `client_tax_plans.deposit_refund_status === 'succeeded'`. So done = `status === 'Proceed' || deposit_refund_status === 'succeeded'` in both repos, and the overview step is now **always applicable** (the old `applicable: greenlightStop` + `noWarn: true` are gone — an undecided legacy plan therefore raises a truthful skipped-step warning until an admin clicks Proceed, which is correct and expected; plans 45/46/47 lost their old `'Go'` rows in this change). **The handler contract changed:** `automation_TAX_depositrefund` now **REQUIRES** a `reason` body field and returns **400 `"reason required"`** without it; the trimmed reason is HTML-escaped, newlines become `<br>`, and it is injected into the new **`[Refund Reason]`** token of `email_templates` id 181 (`TAX_deposit_refund`) via a **function replacement** (`.replace(/\[Refund Reason\]/g, () => reasonHtml)`) so a `$` sequence in the typed reason is not read as a `$&`/`$1` backreference. Template 181's body is `Hi [Client First],` → `[Refund Reason]` as a PLAIN paragraph → the refund sentence → questions → thanks → sign-off; there is deliberately **no intro sentence**, because the admin's typed reason IS the message body. Refund columns, the PF bell and idempotency are otherwise unchanged. **Surface visibility is asymmetric and deliberate:** the client/member `readOnly` track **hides the step entirely unless a refund actually happened** (`if (readOnly && !refunded) return null`) — the go/no-go is an internal call; the tax-planner portal shows it **visible but LOCKED** (standard `renderTask` planner wrapper, `not-allowed` cursor) because the new task name is **deliberately ABSENT from both `PLANNER_EDITABLE_TASK_NAMES` whitelists** (#262) — this step is admin-only, and adding it to either whitelist would be a regression, not a fix. The Proceed click is **one-way in the UI** (buttons are replaced by the pill and never return), mirroring the Tax 3 decline pattern; the Refund card itself is copied from the `tax_3_decision` decline card (subject line, full body, mid-body reason textarea, Cancel + Send Refund, send disabled until a reason is typed AND `deposit_payment_intent_id` exists). `see #293`.

**294. Not every `tax_planners` row means the same thing — `planner_role` splits the table into 'Tax Planner' and 'Team Member', and what that role gates has CHANGED (2026-07-27; AMENDED IN PLACE 2026-08-10, v717/v718). ALWAYS-APPLIES (tax track).** The Tax Planners admin section gained a second person-type. A **Team Member** logs into the SAME tax-planner portal with the same capabilities and the same restrictions (same `tax_planner_logins` row, same `tax_planner` role, same `TAX_PLANNER_ALLOWED_ACTIONS` allowlist, same whole-group ownership guards — #257 is unchanged). New column `tax_planners.planner_role` (`text not null default 'Tax Planner'`, CHECK `tax_planners_planner_role_check` = `'Tax Planner' | 'Team Member'`; migration `20260727120000_tax_planner_roles.sql`).

**AMENDED 2026-08-10 — TEAM MEMBERS ARE NOW ALLOCATABLE, and the block described below is GONE.** The original design made a team member permanently un-allocatable; the business turned out to need the opposite. Katie Williams (`tax_planners` 14, Innovation Consulting Group) and Bridger Silvester (15, SDP) are per-partnership **hand-off coordinators**: they are allocated to a plan FIRST, Tray books the ROI meeting off the back of that allocation, and the coordinator then replaces themselves with the Tax Planner who will actually run the plan. So as of v717: the `400 "Team members cannot be allocated to tax plans"` branch is **DELETED** from `actions/tax/allocate-planner.ts`; the admin allocation picker and `tax_planner_portal_clients`'s `group` roster now **include** team members (labelled `— Team Member`); and `tax_allocate_planner` went **back into `TAX_PLANNER_ALLOWED_ACTIONS`** (a coordinator must be able to hand the plan on from their own portal), fenced by `denyIfNotPlannerPlan` plus the pre-existing check that the target planner is inside the caller's group. The hand-off chain that rides on the role is **#357**.

**What SURVIVES the amendment, and still bites.** (1) **The role is still read at allocation time and still branches behaviour** — `allocate-planner.ts` selects `planner_role` and fires a *different* bell per role, and `ready-for-tax3.ts` re-reads it to decide whether to raise the "allocate a Tax Planner" prompt. **There is still no separate notification gate and no separate payout gate**: every planner bell (`utils/tax-planner-notify.ts notifyAllocatedPlanner`, `taxDecisionRecipients`) and the whole planner payout leg (`utils/tax-planner-payout.ts`) key off `client_tax_plans.tax_planner_id`, so **a Team Member holding a plan now DOES receive planner bells and IS a payout destination for as long as they hold it** — which is exactly why the hand-off is supposed to happen before any money leg resolves. Any NEW writer of `tax_planner_id` still owes itself a `planner_role` read, not to reject the value but to fire the right side of the chain. **The picker/leaderboard filtering clause of the original entry now applies only to the KPI allocation leaderboard** (`TaxPlannerKpiPanel.jsx`), which still counts planners only. (2) **`member_type` still stores the PARTNERSHIP / Tax Planning Group name — semantics deliberately UNCHANGED**, because it is load-bearing in `utils/tax-planner-ownership.ts` (group scoping), `actions/tax-planners/portal-clients.ts` (roster) and `utils/tax-planner-payout.ts` (payout destination). The UI labels invert the intuition: **"Member Type" = `planner_role`, "Partnership" = `member_type`** (`TaxPlannersPanel.jsx`, list filter group + profile view + both forms). Do not "fix" `member_type` to mean the role. (3) **~~Every allocation/leaderboard surface filters the role client-side too~~ — SUPERSEDED 2026-08-10 for the pickers.** The admin allocation dropdown (`activePlanners` in `TaxPrioritiesTab.jsx`) and the planner-portal `group` roster (`portal-clients.ts`) now **include** team members, each rendered with a `— Team Member` suffix (and an amber chip on the admin selection); only the **KPI allocation leaderboard** (`TaxPlannerKpiPanel.jsx`) still filters them out. A NEW planner-picking surface should therefore SHOW the role rather than hide the person. **Portal behaviour.** `tax_planner_portal_clients` returns **`self_role`** and, per entry, **`planner_role`**; the roster (and therefore the returned `group`) is the WHOLE partnership. For a team-member caller the default id set is **the whole roster** — self is never prepended, because a coordinator holds few or no plans of their own and forcing self in would show an almost-empty list; `body.planner_ids` is still intersected with the roster and an EMPTY intersection falls back to all. `PlannerClientsList.jsx` mirrors it: all checkboxes default CHECKED (labels append `— Team Member` for team members), unchecking the last one snaps back to all-checked, and the filter button reads **"Tax Planners"** (dropdown header "Show clients by planner") instead of "Group". The normal-planner path is byte-identical to before in both repos. **Scope is unchanged:** a team member sees ONLY their own partnership's clients — the existing group-scope guards do the work. **`save_tax_planner`** sanitizes `planner_role` to the two values, PRESERVES the stored role when an edit omits it (partial-payload aware — it re-reads the row), and returns **400 `"Team members require a partnership"`** when the effective role is Team Member with a blank effective `member_type` (with no partnership a team member's portal would show nothing at all); the FE mirrors the check. **Certifications are hidden for a Team Member** (data untouched), and the list row's right-side chip shows `Team Member` INSTEAD of the "N plans" chip. **`website_url` is RETIRED** — removed from the planner UI (Edit form + profile view) and from the `save.ts` write whitelist, so the column and its stored values survive but are now dead and will silently never update; don't reintroduce a writer without re-adding it to the whitelist. Cross-ref **#357** (the hand-off chain the role now drives) and **#358** (the ROI meeting step it hands off around). `see #294`.

**295. A public, crawlable page on this site MUST be a static file in `public/`, never a React route — SPA deep routes serve a real HTTP 404 to anything that does not run JavaScript (2026-07-28). ALWAYS-APPLIES (Frontend).** The frontend deploys to GitHub Pages with vite `base: '/'`. GH Pages has no SPA rewrite: a request for a path that is not a real file returns **404** and serves `public/404.html`, which stashes the path in `sessionStorage` and redirects to `/` so the router can restore it. That works for a human in a browser and fails for **everything else** — search-engine crawlers, security-vendor reputation scanners (Sophos, Talos, Google Safe Browsing, SmartScreen), link unfurlers, and archive bots all see a 404 or an empty shell, because the content only exists after JS executes. So a page whose entire purpose is to be *found and read by a machine* — privacy policy, terms, about, contact — must be a real file: `public/privacy.html`, `public/terms.html`, copied verbatim to the dist root by vite and served with a genuine **200**. These are standalone documents with inline `<style>` (no shared stylesheet, no bundle) and must re-state the design language by hand — navy band `linear-gradient(160deg,#002973,#0d47a8,#125ecc)`, Inter from Google Fonts, white card `border-radius:16px`, headings `#002973`. Link to them with a plain `<a href="/privacy.html">`, NOT a react-router `<Link>` (a `Link` would push a client-side route the router cannot resolve). **Two files must be updated in lockstep whenever routes change:** `public/robots.txt` gains a `Disallow:` line for every new sensitive top-level route prefix (login/portal/token pages — currently `/admin`, `/member`, `/client`, `/specialist`, `/tax`, `/pay`, `/payout-setup`, `/decide`, `/advisor`, `/accountant`, `/pft`, `/map4`, `/update-card`, `/connect-card`, `/membership-pay`, `/set-password`), and `public/sitemap.xml` gains a `<url>` for every new PUBLIC page. A new public page that never reaches the sitemap is invisible to the vendors the pages exist to satisfy; a new token/login route that never reaches robots.txt invites crawlers into `?token=` URLs. `public/.well-known/security.txt` follows the same static-file rule. **The root page `/` is the same trap and it was missed on the first pass:** `/` returns a genuine 200, but the body is only the `index.html` shell, so everything painted by `RolePicker.jsx` — company description, address, footer — is invisible to a non-executing scanner. A SophosLabs Intelix report on 2026-07-28 measured the served page at **1584 bytes** whose only readable text was the `<title>`, and returned **"Likely Clean" but "Uncategorized"** with a scattered category guess (Information technology 43%, News 37%, then *Parked domains* and *Anonymizers* at 27%) — the model was guessing from a URL string. **Identity that must be machine-readable therefore belongs in `index.html` itself, not in a component:** a `<noscript>` block (outside `#root`, inert for real users, the only body text a crawler gets) plus a JSON-LD `Organization` block in `<head>` (name, description, address, telephone, email, `sameAs`) — which took the shell to 4172 bytes of real business identity without changing a pixel. Re-check this whenever the landing page is restyled: moving identity text into JSX silently un-does it. **Sequencing:** submit to a vendor for reassessment only AFTER that content is deployed, or the review reads an empty shell and comes back Uncategorized again. **THE SAME TRAP, THIRD FORM — and the worst one: EVERY client-side route we email returned a real HTTP 404.** `/tax-upload?token=`, `/pay?token=`, `/tax-decide?token=`, `/payout-setup?token=`, `/set-password?token=` — all of them served 404 + `404.html`, rescued only by that file's JavaScript. A human never noticed; a scanner stops at the 404, and **a URL that 404s for a scanner while rendering a working page for a human is the CLOAKING signature used by phishing**, which is very plausibly what fed both the Sophos Time-of-Click warning and the 2026-07-27 spam placement. Found because mail-tester independently reported `[404 - Error : Not found]` and docked 0.5; confirmed on five production routes and in the user's own DevTools. **Diagnosing it needs the Network tab with "Preserve log" TICKED** — the rescue redirect wipes the log otherwise, and the Console shows nothing at all because a 404 is a server response, not a JS error. **The fix is `scripts/emit-route-pages.mjs`, a postbuild step wired into `npm run build`:** it copies `dist/index.html` into `dist/<route>/index.html` for the **30 public emailed routes**, so GitHub Pages serves `/x?token=` as a 301 to `/x/?token=` ending in a genuine **200** — token preserved (verified against production), one hop SHORTER than the old rescue, and already-sent links unaffected. mail-tester went 9.5 → **10/10**. **MAINTENANCE OBLIGATION: any NEW route whose URL is emailed to a client, member, specialist or partner MUST be added to the `ROUTES` array in that script**, or it silently reverts to 404-for-scanners. Portal/login routes are deliberately EXCLUDED (never emailed, and `robots.txt` disallows them); `public/404.html` remains the fallback for everything not in the list. Cross-ref #151 (reference `public/` assets as a plain `/file.ext` in `index.html` — Vite prepends the base once). `see #295`.

**296. A SPECREV recurring plan stuck in `setup_pending` is COMPLETELY SILENT — the payout-sweep reminder ladder only covers one-off requests, so nobody is told (2026-07-28). FIXED 2026-07-28 (v667 — payout-sweep Pass 3; see #298/#299).** Dan Zimanski's recurring plan (`specialist_revenue_recurring_plans` id 5, $800/mo, `charge_day` 15, LIVE, created 2026-07-14) sat in `setup_pending` for **14 days** with **zero** reminder and zero escalation. The reminder ladder in the payout sweep (48h nudge + 96h Tracy bell) queries **`specialist_revenue_requests`** — the one-off table — and never looks at `specialist_revenue_recurring_plans`. Nothing else watches the column either, so an abandoned recurring setup fails open and stays invisible until a human happens to look at the row. **FIXED the same day (2026-07-28, v667):** the payout sweep gained a **Pass 3** that runs the identical 48h reminder email (`SPECREV_recurring_setup_reminder`) / 96h Tracy bell ladder against `specialist_revenue_recurring_plans` still in `setup_pending`, guarded by the new `setup_reminder_sent_at` / `setup_pf_notified_at` columns; separately `checkout.session.expired` now raises an abandonment bell the moment a session lapses (#299). Dan's plan 5 was the first row it fired on. The paragraphs below remain the diagnostic reference. **The diagnostic signature, worth recognising on sight:** in live Stripe the customer (`cus_Usvkfkvk33k9db`) showed an **ACTIVE Financial Connections bank link but NO subscription, NO payment method and NO payments**. That combination means the payer completed the *bank-login* step of Checkout and then abandoned the **final authorize** click — FC-active is NOT proof of setup, only of a bank handshake. No money ever moved. **Durable setup-link semantics** (the same shape as #268): the emailed setup link stays valid for as long as `status='setup_pending'` — every click mints a **fresh 1-hour Checkout session**, so a stale link is never the problem and re-sending is always safe. Once the plan flips `active` the link deliberately refuses with *"already set up"*. **Timing constraint that makes this urgent rather than cosmetic:** `charge_day` is capped **1–20 by design** so an ACH pull settles inside the same month, and a plan whose setup lands after its charge day slips a whole month (Dan missing Aug 15 pushes the first auto-charge to Sep 15). A live end-to-end control test the same day (a throwaway $1 plan, id 6) went from Payment-Input creation to **ACTIVE in 99 seconds**, proving the `VFO_SPECIALIST_REVENUE_RECURRING` webhook path itself is healthy — the gap is purely the missing reminder. `see #296`.

**297. Link a payout recipient's EXISTING Stripe Connect account into `stripe_account_id` BEFORE any money lands, or the payout engine silently mints a DUPLICATE Express account and strands the real one (2026-07-28).** `utils/specialist-revenue-payout.ts` treats a recipient row with a NULL/blank `stripe_account_id` as "never onboarded": it **creates a fresh Express account and stamps the new id back onto the row**. If that person already completed Connect onboarding under a different account (created by hand in the dashboard, carried over from another pipeline, or set up before the row existed), the engine has now bound the row to an empty, unonboarded account — while the real one, with the verified bank and `capabilities.transfers === 'active'`, is orphaned and unreachable. The failure is quiet in both directions: the transfer is refused (per #159 a created-but-unonboarded account is "awaiting setup", not an error), and re-sending the setup link onboards the *duplicate*, leaving two accounts for one person. **The fix is ordering, not code** — write the known-good `acct_…` into the recipient row (`members.stripe_account_id` / `experts.stripe_account_id`) **before** the first payout attempt. Done this session for member **58088 (Jack Branch)** → `acct_1TUsTuRxTjQta1sO`, his pre-existing account, linked ahead of the first $800 SPECREV split so the engine would find it rather than create one. **Whenever you onboard a payout recipient who might already exist in Stripe, check for an existing account first and link it**; the engine's create-if-missing branch is a convenience for genuinely new recipients, not a reconciliation step. Cross-ref #159 (verify `capabilities.transfers === 'active'` before transferring; Connect accounts and transfers must share one sandbox/live mode) and #268 (never email a raw single-use `connect.stripe.com` link — use the durable `/payout-setup?token=` page). `see #297`.

**298. SPECREV Checkout deliberately does NOT set `payment_method_options[us_bank_account][verification_method]='instant'` — and `checkout.session.completed` fires at SUBMIT, so a recurring plan can be ACTIVE with an UNVERIFIED bank (2026-07-28). ALWAYS-APPLIES (Stripe / payments).** Both SPECREV checkout builders (`actions/specialist-revenue/recurring-checkout.ts` and `actions/specialist-revenue/checkout.ts`) used to pin `verification_method='instant'`, which restricts the hosted page to the **Financial Connections bank-login flow ONLY**. A payer whose bank is unsupported, who hits MFA trouble, or who simply bails at the final authorize click then has **no other way through** — which is exactly how Dan Zimanski produced six expired sessions plus an ACTIVE Financial Connections link with zero completions over 14 days (#296). Removing the parameter falls back to Stripe's default `automatic`, which offers **instant bank login first AND manual account/routing-number entry with 1-2 business-day micro-deposits** as the fallback. The three values are `automatic` (the default when the parameter is omitted) / `instant` / `microdeposits`; **never re-add `'instant'` to a SPECREV builder** — it is a narrowing, not a hardening. *(Extends to MAP 1 `/pay` and Tax `/tax-pay` as of 2026-09-08 — both unpinned in v816 with the webhook work #475 describes; the rule now reads "never re-pin it on any builder that has been unpinned".)* **The consequence you must design around:** on the manual-entry path Stripe fires **`checkout.session.completed` at submit time, while micro-deposit verification is still pending**, so the recurring plan flips `setup_pending → active` **before the bank is verified** and the portal shows a healthy plan. That state is not a bug — the customer has 10 days to verify through Stripe's own emailed hosted page, and if the bank is still unverified when `charge_day` arrives the first invoice simply fails and surfaces through the EXISTING `invoice.payment_failed` handler (`SPECREV_recurring_payment_failed_bell`; the plan stays `active` and Stripe retries). **Do not add a second "is it really verified?" gate** — the failure path already exists, and a new gate would only hold up plans that are fine. Timing note that makes this matter operationally: micro-deposits add 1-2 business days on top of the normal ACH settle, so a plan set up on the manual path wants roughly three days of head-room before its `charge_day` (capped 1-20 by design, #296). `see #298`.

**299. `checkout.session.expired` is how abandonment is detected — it must be SUBSCRIBED on the Stripe endpoint (both modes), it is handled for the two SPECREV pipelines ONLY, and an expired session carries SESSION-level metadata only (2026-07-28). ALWAYS-APPLIES (Stripe / payments).** Before this there was no abandonment signal anywhere in the system: a payer who opened a hosted page and walked away left the row exactly as it was and nothing ever looked at it (the #296 root cause — "he says he's done, and there is nothing on our end"). `router/webhooks.ts` now carries ONE additive, isolated block on `event.type === "checkout.session.expired"` that branches on **`session.metadata.pipeline`**: `VFO_SPECIALIST_REVENUE_RECURRING` resolves the plan by `metadata.plan_id` and acts **only while `status='setup_pending'`**; `VFO_SPECIALIST_REVENUE` resolves the request by `metadata.request_id` (falling back to `session.customer` for sessions minted before that stamp existed) and acts **only while `payment_status='requested'`**. Each carries the standard `event.livemode` vs row `sandbox` mode-mismatch guard and raises `SPECREV_checkout_abandoned_bell` (default Tracy + Jake, `dedupe:"unread"`, dismissible FYI, message says the link they already have still works). **Three traps.** (1) **The event only arrives if the endpoint subscribes to it** — the same class of config dependency as `invoice.finalized` (#198) and `checkout.session.async_payment_succeeded` (#211). It IS subscribed on both the live and sandbox endpoints as of 2026-07-28; a newly created Stripe endpoint that omits it makes abandonment detection silently stop with no error anywhere. (2) **An expired session has no PaymentIntent, so `payment_intent_data[metadata]` is ABSENT from the event** — only `session.metadata` survives. The one-off builder previously stamped the request id *only* under `payment_intent_data`, so an expiry could not be routed at all; `checkout.ts` now ALSO appends `metadata[request_id]` at session level. **Any Checkout session whose expiry you might ever want to consume must carry its routing keys in the SESSION metadata, not only in `payment_intent_data` / `subscription_data`.** (3) **The handler is deliberately scoped to those two pipelines** — every other pipeline's expired sessions fall through untouched, and neither `pipeRow` nor the `stripe_customer_id` cascade is involved. **Any new abandonment consumer MUST branch on `session.metadata.pipeline` inside the same block** rather than acting on every expired session, or it will bell on MAP 1 / Tax / onboarding sessions that expire routinely and harmlessly. Expiry timing differs by mode: the recurring session is capped to **1 hour** via `expires_at` (so the billing anchor is still in the future at completion), while a one-off Checkout session uses Stripe's **24 hour** default. `see #299`.

**300. Migration setup-link AUTO-RESEND: a sweep-minted token is born with `reminder_sent_at` already stamped, the 48h filter is two-legged, and idempotency comes from SUPERSESSION — not from a stamp (2026-07-28). ALWAYS-APPLIES (Backend data patterns).** `actions/pipeline/contract-check-reminder-sweep.ts` gained `sweepMigrationSetupLinks`, running nightly (04:00 UTC) **after `sweepUnclearedChecks` and BEFORE the check-reminder early-returns**, so a disabled `MAP1_check_payment_reminder_email` rule can never skip it. It considers the **newest UNUSED token per `(pipeline, row_id)`** in `migration_setup_tokens` and runs a 48h reminder email (`CLIENT_PAYMENT_CONTINUATION` / `setup_link_reminder`, tokens `[FIRST_NAME]` / `[CONNECT_LINK]` / `[PAYMENT_SCHEDULE]`, guarded by the new `reminder_sent_at`) plus a 96h bell (`MIGRATION_setup_link_stall_bell`, Tracy + Jake, link `/admin/client/<id>?tab=map1|tax`, guarded by the new `pf_notified_at`). Steve Manseau sat **13 days** on an EXPIRED link with quarterly payments unrun, which is why the expired case does not email at all. **The four legs that must ALL survive any refactor.** (1) **An expired token is never emailed** — instead the sweep **AUTO-RESENDS**: it mints a fresh 7-day token with `created_by='sweep'` and **`reminder_sent_at` stamped AT MINT**, because that email *is* the new token's reminder; the new token therefore stays quiet until it expires in its own turn, giving the cycle `fresh link → 96h bell → resend at expiry`. (2) **The candidate filter is two-legged and asymmetric** — `isExpired(t) ? sweepMintCount(t) < MAX_AUTO_RESENDS : !t.reminder_sent_at`. An already-reminded token deliberately RE-ENTERS the tier once it expires (that is what drives the next resend); a live token is filtered by its stamp as usual. (3) **Idempotency is by SUPERSESSION, not by a stamp** — the minted token becomes the row's newest, so the old one drops out of the candidate set on the next run. That is precisely what makes a FAILED mint self-healing: nothing suppressing is stamped, so the resend genuinely retries tomorrow night. Do not "tighten" this by stamping before the insert succeeds. (4) **`MAX_AUTO_RESENDS = 3` counts `created_by='sweep'` rows for the `(pipeline,row_id)` UNCONDITIONALLY** (not "this run", not "since the last human send"), and the **bell wording is four-way truthful off a per-run `resentRows` set** — *reach out* (link still live) / *"a fresh one has been automatically emailed to them"* (this run resent) / *"re-send a fresh one from their Payment Continuation row"* (expired but not resent this run) / *"automatic link re-sends are exhausted after 3 attempts"* (cap hit). A refactor that collapses those four legs into "expired or not" produces a bell that lies to Tracy about whether the client currently holds a working link. Per-client sandbox resolution goes through `loadSandboxConfigForClient` (#251), and sandbox-on with no `sandbox_email` REFUSES to email rather than mailing the real client. The reminder's "Your upcoming payments:" block is the shared `utils/migration-schedule.ts buildMigrationScheduleHtml(pipeline,row)` — extracted VERBATIM from `actions/migration/send-setup-link.ts`, which now calls it, so the two emails can never drift. Rule keys `MIGRATION_setup_link_reminder_email` / `MIGRATION_setup_link_stall_bell` live in the NEW Notification Editor area **"Payment Continuation"**. Cross-ref #230 (`migration_setup_tokens` are single-use — `used_at` is consumed by the `card_update` webhook branch, which is exactly why `!used_at` is the eligibility test) and #176 (the bell routes through `notifyByRule`). `see #300`.

**301. `migration_setup_tokens.reminder_sent_at` on an EXPIRED token is supersession BOOKKEEPING, not a distinct reminder email — any consumer counting or displaying "emails sent" must apply the `reminderCounted` rule or it double-counts (2026-07-29).** When `sweepMigrationSetupLinks` finds a token that has already EXPIRED it does not email that token at all — it **auto-resends**, minting a replacement 7-day token (`created_by='sweep'`) and emailing *that*. As part of the same pass it also stamps `reminder_sent_at` on the **old, expired** token. That stamp is pure bookkeeping — it records "this token has been superseded", and there is **no email whose content corresponds to it**. The only email sent in that pass is the new token's *mint*. So a naive tally of `(token mints) + (rows with reminder_sent_at)` counts the same physical email **twice**: once as the replacement token's mint, once as the superseded token's stamp. The rule that separates a GENUINE same-link reminder from a supersession stamp is `reminderCounted` in `supabase/functions/vfo-admin-api/actions/migration/outstanding-links.ts`: a `reminder_sent_at` counts as a real reminder **only when `reminder_sent_at < expires_at`** (i.e. the token was still live when the nudge went out) **AND** (`created_by !== 'sweep'` **OR** the stamp is more than **12h** after the token's mint). The second leg exists because a sweep-minted token is born with `reminder_sent_at` stamped AT MINT (leg 1 of #300) — that stamp is the mint itself, not a follow-up, and only a stamp well after the mint can be a separate nudge. **This shipped wrong once:** the Outstanding Payment Links loader in **v670** counted the raw stamp alongside the mint and reported an inflated "emails sent" per link; **fixed in v671**. Any future surface that reports reminder counts, builds a per-token history timeline, or drives an escalation off "how many times have we chased this" must reuse `reminderCounted` rather than reading `reminder_sent_at` directly. Cross-ref **#300** (the auto-resend ladder itself — mint-time stamping, the two-legged candidate filter, and idempotency by supersession rather than by a stamp) and #230 (`migration_setup_tokens` are single-use; `used_at` is the consumption marker). `see #301`.

**302. The Payment Continuation / migration handlers resolve sandbox PER CLIENT via `loadSandboxConfigForClient` as of v672 — a new migration handler that reads `pipeline_sandbox_config` directly re-opens a SPLIT-BRAIN where one half of the flow runs live and the other runs sandbox (2026-07-29). ALWAYS-APPLIES (Stripe / payments).** The per-case test-member override (#251) was applied across the TAX/MAP1/PIP money paths in 2026-07-21, but the **migration / payment-continuation** family was written separately and every one of its five handlers read the GLOBAL `pipeline_sandbox_config` row inline. With the global toggle LIVE, that produced two mutually incompatible halves for the SAME test-member case: the backfill stamped `sandbox` from the per-client resolution used elsewhere while `migration_connect_checkout` minted the /connect-card Stripe Checkout session against the **LIVE** secret key, and `migration_send_setup_link` addressed the real client rather than the `sandbox_email`. Observed on **2026-07-29**: the /connect-card page served to the test member was a **live-mode Stripe page and a REAL card was entered on it**. No charge occurred (the session is `mode:'setup'` — nothing is charged there), and no real client was ever affected because for a real client the global live result and the per-client result are identical.
