But the test case could never complete — the card saved against the live customer, while the charge sweep resolved the row per-client to sandbox and looked for a payment method that lived in the other Stripe account. **The five converted handlers** (all under `supabase/functions/vfo-admin-api/actions/migration/`): `backfill-map1.ts` (`'MAP 1'`, `clientId`), `backfill-tax.ts` (`'TAX'`, `clientId`), `connect-checkout.ts` (pipeline from the token row, `tok.client_id`), `send-setup-link.ts` (pipeline from the row, `row.client_id`), `stripe-lookup.ts` (`body?.client_id ?? null`). `grep -rn pipeline_sandbox_config actions/migration/` must stay at **ZERO matches** — that grep is the regression test. **Two details that are easy to lose in a refactor.** (1) **`migration_stripe_lookup` takes `client_id` as OPTIONAL** — the lookup can legitimately run before any row exists, and the helper's null path falls straight through to the global config, byte-identical to the old inline read. The admin FE (`src/components/admin/migration/PaymentContinuationTab.jsx`, `doLookup()`) now passes the `clientId` prop it already holds, so in practice the tab always resolves per-client; a NEW caller of that action that omits `client_id` silently reverts to global-only resolution and can show a test member the wrong Stripe account's cards. (2) **`send-setup-link`'s refuse-to-email guard must read the helper's `sandboxEmail`, not the raw config row** — sandbox-on with no `sandbox_email` still returns 400 rather than mailing the real client, and because the force-flip preserves the global `sandbox_email`, a forced test-member send redirects exactly as a globally-sandboxed one does. The helper is force-ON-only and fail-safe on every error path, so non-test clients are byte-identical before and after. Cross-ref **#251** (the override itself, member `59524`, `constants/test-sandbox.ts`) and **#300** (the setup-link reminder sweep, which already used the per-client helper). `see #302`.

**303. NEVER initialize a payout leg's status variable to a TERMINAL value and then put the transfer behind a condition — the fall-through writes "resolved" onto money that was never sent, and no sweep will ever look at it again (2026-07-29). ALWAYS-APPLIES (money).** Both `actions/pipeline/contract-revshare.ts` (MAP 1) and `actions/tax/revshare.ts` (Tax) opened with `let revPaidValue = "N/A — No Share Due"` and only performed the Stripe transfer inside `if (!isMoneyMapping && shareAmount > 0 && member.stripe_account_id)`. A share that WAS due but whose member had no `stripe_account_id` — or a run where the `STRIPE_KEY` env lookup failed — fell straight past that block and wrote the terminal `'N/A — No Share Due'`: **no transfer, no bell, no failure status, and a row that BOTH sweeps treat as resolved**, so it could never be retried. On the tab and in every audit it is byte-for-byte indistinguishable from a genuine zero-share row. Nothing ever fired it in production (see the audit at the end), but the hole was live in the two largest money pipelines. **The pattern that replaces it, and the five things that must all hold.** (1) **NEW `utils/member-share-held.ts`** exports `AWAITING_CONNECT = "Awaiting Connect Setup"` — a **NON-terminal** status — plus `memberShareHeldTitle(pipeline, clientName, clientRef)`. It is modelled directly on `PLANNER_SHARE_WITHHELD` / `plannerWithheldTitle` in `utils/tax-planner-payout.ts` (**#284**); when you need a "money is parked, visibly" state, copy that shape rather than inventing a third one. (2) **The handler must be an EXHAUSTIVE branch chain with no fall-through** — `isMoneyMapping` → `shareAmount <= 0` → `!member.stripe_account_id` (writes `AWAITING_CONNECT` + an **action-required** bell, rule keys `MAP1_member_share_held` / `TAX_member_share_held`) → `!STRIPE_KEY` (writes `'Failed'` + the existing `FAILURE_*` bell) → transfer. Every reachable state now has its own explicit write; there is no "default" value. On a successful transfer both handlers clear **BOTH** the FAILED title and the held title. (3) **A non-terminal status is only safe if EVERY retry path accepts it** — and this is the leg that would have parked the money forever. `actions/tax/revshare-sweep.ts` gated its retry on `isRetry = revShare === "Pending" || revPaid === "Failed"`, and the handler writes `rev_share='Completed - Revenue Share'` **even when the share is held**, so a held leg matched NEITHER predicate and would have dropped out of the sweep permanently — a *worse* outcome than the bug being fixed. Widened to `|| revPaid === AWAITING_CONNECT` with the new reason label `awaiting-connect`. **Before introducing any new non-terminal status, evaluate every sweep predicate that guards its table against it.** The MAP 1 sweep (`contract-revshare-sweep.ts`) needed **NO** change — it has no `isRetry` gate at all and enumerates any non-resolved leg that has a receipt (reason `never-attempted`); that asymmetry between the two sweeps is real and must be re-checked, not assumed. (4) **The completion stamp is gated on a TERMINAL outcome.** `*_rev_completed_at` is written ONLY for `Yes` | `Money Mapping` | `N/A — No Share Due` — a held or failed leg no longer reads as finished to anything downstream (mirrors the `isTerminalSuccess` gate in `tax-planner-payout.ts`). (5) **The bell title must be RECONSTRUCTABLE so it self-clears** — the release path re-derives the exact title through `memberShareHeldTitle(...)` and marks it read; a title built ad-hoc at the raise site can never be found again and the bell becomes permanent noise. **Refund paths must settle a held leg.** `actions/tax/refund.ts` now, after a successful retainer refund, moves a leg sitting at `AWAITING_CONNECT` to `'N/A — No Share Due'` + the completion stamp + `clearJakeFailure` — otherwise the widened sweep would later pay a member share out of money that has already gone back to the client. Any new refund/reversal path owes the same settle (same obligation as **#277** for backfills). **Two more surfaces that had to move with it.** `actions/payments/normalize.ts revShareStatus()` returns **`"Awaiting payout setup"`** for the held value instead of falling through to the misleading `"Pending"`; and `actions/msm/pip-revshare.ts` got the missing-`STRIPE_KEY` hole closed — **PIP was the ONLY pipeline that already handled the missing-account case correctly** (its `else if (!member.stripe_account_id)` branch), but it still has **NO retry sweep at all**, so its bell deliberately says *"manual re-fire"* rather than promising an automatic retry. The `actions/migration/backfill-tax.ts` warning that used to say transfers "stay Pending" was reworded to the held / action-required-bell reality. FE mirrors: an amber `#b9451d` *"awaiting payout setup"* note + an explicit `legNote` branch in BOTH `map1/Map1PricingSplitCard.jsx` and `tax/PricingSplitCard.jsx` (parallel to the existing `Awaiting Planner Allocation` handling), and `AutomationPanel.jsx revDecision()` gained an explicit `'Share held — payout setup needed'` branch. **Historical-damage audit (read-only, live, 2026-07-29): ZERO genuinely swallowed shares.** Every existing `'N/A — No Share Due'` leg carrying `member_share > 0` — 12 MAP 1 legs (Manseau / Mason / Shinen / Cito / Brody / Slosser / Hurst) and 3 tax retainer legs (Montgomery, Hill ×2) — is `legacy_source='old-system'`, i.e. the deliberate **#277** backfill settle, not a swallow. No member with an open share leg lacks a Connect account today. Cross-ref **#284** (the planner-leg precedent this copies), **#265** and **#277** (the sweep rescue gates that correctly ignore a held value), **#159** (a created-but-unonboarded Connect account rejects transfers — "awaiting setup" is a real state, not an error), **#176** (every bell goes through `notifyByRule`) and **#268** (send the durable `/payout-setup?token=` link, never a raw `connect.stripe.com` URL, when chasing the missing account). `see #303`.

**304. `member_contribution` is a DISCOUNT funded by the member, and Member Share is entered ALREADY NET of it — the payout engine must never subtract it again, and must never scale the entered share by net/gross (2026-07-29). ALWAYS-APPLIES (money).** USER-CONFIRMED SEMANTICS: the client pays `gross_fee − member_contribution`, the member absorbs the difference, and the admin types the **Member Share net of that contribution** into the pricing form. Everything downstream must therefore treat the entered share as the exact number of dollars to pay, full stop. **Two independent bugs both violated that, in the same handlers.** (1) **The MAP 1 member leg scaled the share by net/gross.** It computed `(member_share / gross_fee) × thisPayment`, so the moment a member contribution made `net_invoice < gross_fee`, the entered share **silently shrank** — the member paid for the discount twice. Replaced with a cumulative-difference split that pays the entered share IN FULL across the installments: `cumShare(i) = Math.round((memberShareRaw * i / totalPayments) * 100) / 100; shareAmount = cumShare(paymentNum) - cumShare(paymentNum - 1)` (rounding-safe — the installments sum exactly to the entered share). This is **numerically identical for every existing row** (all have `gross == net`, or a blank gross). The **strategic-partner leg's gross-proration is UNCHANGED** — only the member leg has these semantics; do not "unify" them. (2) **The member-contribution deduction blocks in both handlers were deleted.** They were **dead code**: the deduction only ran when `member_contrib_status === "Pending"`, and **nothing in either repo has ever written `'Pending'`** — the only value ever written is `'Applied'` (by the two backfills and by the handlers themselves). They were also a **latent overpay-on-retry bug**: the block flipped the status to `'Applied'` **BEFORE** the transfer, so any retry (sweep or manual re-fire) recomputed the share **without** the deduction and would have transferred more than the first attempt intended. The tax copy was doubly dead — **`client_tax_plans` has no `member_contribution` column at all.** Both handlers still SUBSTITUTE their `[CONTRIBUTION_LINES]` / `[CONTRIB_NOTE]` email tokens, now with `""`, so a raw token can never leak into a member-facing email — **remove the value, never the substitution.** **The frontend must mirror the engine.** `Map1PricingSplitCard.jsx`'s member row uses the identical cumulative-difference math (its strategic / VFOS rows are unchanged), because a display that disagrees with the payout is how a wrong number gets believed (same obligation as **#252**). `TaxAutomationPanel.jsx` dropped a dead `Detail l="Member contribution"` row that rendered `member_contrib_status` on a table with no amount column. Cross-ref **#252** (a `share` is ALWAYS dollars of the TOTAL engagement — that rule still governs the strategic and planner legs, and the member leg still pays dollars-of-total, just split by cumulative difference rather than by a net/gross ratio) and **#303** (the exhaustive branch chain these same two handlers were restructured into in the same PR). `see #304`.

**305. The tax specialist allocation model: ONE picker serves both programs, a NULL `expert_id` IS the "Other #N (See notes)" allocation, there is deliberately no unique constraint — and deleting a specialist without first deleting its progress rows CORRUPTS plan-level step state (2026-07-29). ALWAYS-APPLIES (tax track).** *[Annotated 2026-07-31: the **NAMING half** of this gotcha is extended by **#311** — the NULL-`expert_id` allocation is now named by the admin and stored as server-prefixed **`Custom - <name>`**, with `Other #N (See notes)` retained only as the no-name API fallback (no live rows carry it). Everything else below — the NULL-`expert_id` showroom guarantee, the deliberate absence of a unique constraint, the no-dedupe rule on the unlinked path, and above all the **delete-hazard ordering** (progress rows FIRST) — is UNCHANGED and still load-bearing.]* `client_tax_specialists` (id PK, `tax_plan_id` FK ON DELETE CASCADE, `expert_id` FK → `experts`, `specialist_name` NOT NULL, `status`, `created_at`) shipped with **no unique constraint and no delete path**. A double-click therefore created twins — client Michael Harritt, plan **72**, rows **51** and **52**, same expert **6130**, **240 ms apart** — because the FE dedupe filters against state that is only refreshed AFTER the `await`. There is exactly **ONE** picker: `TaxPrioritiesTab.jsx` serves **BOTH** program 1 (Holistic) and program 4 (Tax Priorities), so any change here lands on both programs at once (#269 still governs which plans render). **"Other" is a NULL `expert_id`, and that is the whole point.** Migration `20260729210000_tax_specialist_other_option.sql` does `ALTER TABLE client_tax_specialists ALTER COLUMN expert_id DROP NOT NULL` (+ a `COMMENT ON COLUMN`). An "Other" allocation points at **no `experts` row at all**, so it can never enter the specialists directory and can never leak into a member showroom or the public widget — the user's explicit requirement, and the reason this is a NULL rather than a placeholder `experts` row (cross-ref **#231** / **#201**). **There is deliberately NO unique constraint**: dedupe lives in the handler, and a constraint would collapse legitimate multiple "Other" rows on one plan. **`actions/tax/add-specialist.ts`** accepts `other: true` → inserts `expert_id: null` with a **SERVER-CONTROLLED** name `Other #N (See notes)` where **N = the highest existing number on that plan + 1**, parsed from the plan's existing NULL-expert rows via `/^Other #(\d+)/` — **not the row count**, so removing "Other #1" never makes the next add reuse "#2". The normal (real-expert) path additionally gained an **idempotent-add guard**: the same `tax_plan_id` + `expert_id` returns the existing row with `deduped: true` instead of inserting a twin — that is the double-click fix. **The Other path never dedupes**, by design. **NEW `actions/tax/remove-specialist.ts` → action `tax_remove_specialist`** (+1 action, 441 → **442**), registered in `router/dispatch.ts` and added to `TAX_PLANNER_ALLOWED_ACTIONS` so **admins AND planners** may remove (per the user). **Guard order is load-by-id → verify the row's REAL `tax_plan_id` matches the body's → `denyIfNotPlannerPlan`** — never trust a body-supplied parent id (**#257**); resolving the parent from the loaded row is what makes the group-scope check meaningful. **THE HAZARD THE HANDLER EXISTS TO AVOID, and the reason a naive `DELETE` is unsafe:** `client_tax_progress.tax_specialist_id` is FK **ON DELETE SET NULL**. Deleting a specialist row therefore REWRITES that specialist's progress rows to `tax_specialist_id = NULL` — and NULL there does **not** mean "no specialist", it is the **PLAN-LEVEL** row that `save-task.ts:71` looks up with `.is("tax_specialist_id", null)`. The orphans then collide with the real plan-level row on the same `task_id`, and `maybeSingle()` corrupts plan-level step state. So `remove-specialist.ts` **DELETEs the specialist's `client_tax_progress` rows FIRST and aborts without touching the specialist row if that delete fails.** Any future deleter of a `client_tax_specialists` row owes the same ordering. **FE (`TaxPrioritiesTab.jsx`).** Module const `OTHER_SPEC_VALUE = '__other__'` — a string sentinel that cannot collide with a numeric expert id; a permanent trailing `<option>Other (See notes)</option>` appended **AFTER** the dedupe `.filter()` (so it is always offered and always repeatable) on **both** the admin and planner option lists, appended to the already-resolved `effectiveSpecialists`. A `Remove` control sits in each allocated-specialist card header gated on `!readOnly` — the **same gate as Add**, so members never see it — styled with the file's existing destructive literal `#e74c3c`. `specialistStepCount()` counts `localProgress` keys ending `_${spec.id}` (the exact inverse of the `${taskId}_${taxSpecialistId}` key built in `saveTask`) so the confirm text states the REAL number of steps about to be destroyed; on success it strips those progress keys plus the `spec_${id}` expand key and reuses `loadSpecialists()`. `addSpecialist()` also gained the error surfacing it never had. Cross-ref **#257** (planner auth: the allowlist is not an ownership check), **#269** (program scoping of tax plans), **#260** (the merged `Tax 5 … (Specialist Allocation)` card this picker lives on) and **#231** (never let a non-directory entity reach a showroom). `see #305`.

**306. The assess-form store is FLAT YEAR-1 TOTALS PLUS AN OPTIONAL THREE-FIELD `year2` GROUP AND OPTIONAL `notes` as of 2026-08-18: `client_tax_plans.assess_form` holds `{ fee, taxes_without_plan, taxes_with_plan, cash_outlay, year2?, notes? }` — the per-strategy list is GONE from new submissions, there are STILL only THREE historical shapes, and TEN validation messages are mirrored verbatim FE/BE (2026-07-29 — **AMENDED IN PLACE 2026-08-06 for `taxes_without_plan`, AGAIN 2026-08-17 for the four-total rewrite, and AGAIN 2026-08-18 for the optional year-2 group + notes; the superseded strategy shape is retained below as HISTORY because rows in that shape still exist, and this gotcha was deliberately NOT renumbered**). ALWAYS-APPLIES (tax track).** The "Assess tax planning opportunities (and enter presentation details)" step (`program_client_tasks` 89 program 1 / 123 program 4, sentinel `assess_form` — ONE shared `AssessTaxForm` in `TaxPrioritiesTab.jsx` serves BOTH programs) stopped being a placeholder single-textarea and became the structured form: **Fee** (single dollar) + a shared **strategy list** (each row = name + 4 dollar amounts: Investment Cost Year 1 / Year 2, Gross Savings Year 1 / Year 2 — **the display labels dropped the "+" on 2026-08-12; the KEYS `invest_y2` / `gross_y2` are unchanged**). **Nothing derived is stored**: per-strategy totals (`Y1+Y2+`), section totals, and the ENTIRE Net Tax Savings section (`Gross − Investment`, per strategy per period) are computed at render — so any future consumer (a presentation email, a Tax 3 ROI surface, an accounting export) must compute totals/net itself AND handle BOTH stored shapes, because legacy `{question_1}` rows exist and still render their old single-question read-only view (`Array.isArray(assess_form.strategies)` is the shape switch; the call site always passes `livePlan?.assess_form || {}` so a stamped-but-null row falls to the legacy "—" branch instead of flipping back to edit mode). **Validation is MIRRORED in FE (`AssessTaxForm.handleSubmit`) and BE (`actions/tax/save-assess-form.ts`) and must stay in lockstep**: fee required ≥ 0 (400 "Fee is required"); ≥ 1 strategy AFTER silently dropping rows whose name AND all four amounts are blank (a trailing untouched "+ Add strategy" row must never block submit — do not "tighten" the drop filter); every amount on a kept row REQUIRED (blank → 400 "All amount fields are required"), non-negative (400 "Amounts must be non-negative numbers"), rounded to 2 dp. The live Net/Summary display deliberately coerces blank→0 while typing; only submit enforces required. `tax_save_assess_form` stays AUTH in `ADMIN_ONLY_ACTIONS` + `TAX_PLANNER_ALLOWED_ACTIONS` with `denyIfNotPlannerPlan`, NO chains / emails / notifications, and this was a payload change, NOT a new action. **AMENDED 2026-08-06 — the stored shape gained a THIRD top-level key and it is REQUIRED: `assess_form` is now `{ fee, taxes_without_plan, strategies:[…] }`.** `taxes_without_plan` (“Total Taxes without a Tax Plan”) is validated in the SAME FE/BE lockstep as `fee`, with the identical message *“Total Taxes without a Tax Plan is required”* in `AssessTaxForm.handleSubmit` and `actions/tax/save-assess-form.ts`. Because the handler **REBUILDS the whole jsonb on every submit** (it has never merged), a re-save is what upgrades an older row — and a row submitted before 2026-08-06 simply has no such key. Any consumer must treat its absence as “legacy, needs a re-save” rather than as zero; `tax_generate_presentation` does exactly that and 400s with *“Re-save the Assess form — it was submitted before the Total Taxes field existed”* (#336). **AMENDED IN PLACE 2026-08-17 — THE STRATEGY LIST IS GONE; THE STORED SHAPE IS FOUR FLAT TOTALS.** `assess_form` is now **`{ fee, taxes_without_plan, taxes_with_plan, cash_outlay }`** (numbers, 2 dp) and nothing else. **Everything above about strategy rows — the blank-row drop filter, the per-row required amounts, the computed per-strategy Net/Summary table, the *“At least one strategy is required”* / *“Each strategy needs a name”* / *“All amount fields are required”* / *“Amounts must be non-negative numbers”* messages — is HISTORY**: no submission has written a `strategies` array since this change, and those four messages no longer exist on either side. The four inputs are labelled, in entry order, **“Total Tax without the Plan”** (`taxes_without_plan`), **“Total Tax with the Plan”** (`taxes_with_plan`), **“Total Cash Outlay for the Strategies”** (`cash_outlay`) and **“VFO Services Tax Planning Fee”** (`fee`) — **the labels ARE part of the contract**, because the required-field messages name them verbatim. **THREE historical shapes now exist and ALL THREE must keep rendering read-only:** the original `{question_1}` placeholder, the 2026-07-29→2026-08-17 `{ fee, taxes_without_plan, strategies:[…] }`, and the current four totals. FE shape detection is **`existingData?.taxes_with_plan != null` → new shape; else `Array.isArray(existingData?.strategies)` → old structured; else legacy `question_1`** — the new key is tested FIRST, because a legacy row has neither. `assessComputeRows` / `assessSectionSum` / `AssessSummaryTable` / `ASSESS_AMOUNT_*` survive in `TaxPrioritiesTab.jsx` **solely to serve that old-structured READ-ONLY branch** — nothing writes them, do not delete them, and do not resurrect them as inputs. **The FE/BE lockstep list is now FIVE messages**, validated in this exact order on both sides (`AssessTaxForm.handleSubmit` and `actions/tax/save-assess-form.ts`): *“VFO Services Tax Planning Fee is required”*, *“Total Tax without the Plan is required”*, *“Total Tax with the Plan is required”*, *“Total Cash Outlay for the Strategies is required”*, then the cross-field *“Total Tax with the Plan must be less than Total Tax without the Plan”*. That last check is **STRICTLY** less, and it is what guarantees `taxes_without_plan > 0` for the deck's division — which is why the save path needs no separate greater-than-zero rule. The handler still **REBUILDS the whole jsonb on every submit**, so a re-save is still the only upgrade path and there is still no backfill. **The generator is now STRICT NEW-SHAPE-ONLY:** `tax_generate_presentation` refuses any row that still carries `strategies`, or that is missing any of the three newer totals, with *“Re-save the Assess form — it uses the old strategy format; re-enter the four totals”* — the older *“…before the Total Taxes field existed”* message quoted earlier in this entry **no longer exists in code** (#336). **AMENDED IN PLACE 2026-08-18 (v759) — THE SHAPE GAINED AN OPTIONAL `year2` GROUP OF THREE AND AN OPTIONAL `notes` STRING; THE YEAR-1 KEYS DID NOT MOVE.** `assess_form` is now:

```
{ fee, taxes_without_plan, taxes_with_plan, cash_outlay,
  year2?: { taxes_without_plan, taxes_with_plan, cash_outlay },
  notes?: string }
```

**THE ASYMMETRY IS DELIBERATE AND LOAD-BEARING: `year2` carries THREE fields and NO `fee`.** The "VFO Services Tax Planning Fee" is a **single engagement-level figure** covering the whole plan — entered once, stored flat at the top level, and attributed **entirely to year 1** by the deck (which is exactly why slide 29's One-Time Fee is the literal `$0` template text and there is no `PLANNING_FEE_Y2` token, #336). **A per-year fee was designed and built during this session and then REVERTED on user direction**; `scripts/roi-presentation/make_v4.py` carries a tripwire assert that no `{{PLANNING_FEE_Y1}}`/`{{PLANNING_FEE_Y2}}` has crept back into the master. **Do not add a `year2.fee`.**

**THIS DID NOT CREATE A FOURTH HISTORICAL SHAPE — THERE ARE STILL THREE, AND THE FLAT 4-KEY ROW *IS* THE CURRENT SINGLE-YEAR SHAPE.** Say it that way when anyone asks: the year-1 keys are byte-for-byte what they were, `year2`/`notes` are purely additive and optional, so every row written between 2026-08-17 and 2026-08-18 is already a **valid, current, one-year** row needing no re-save and no migration. FE shape detection is unchanged (`taxes_with_plan != null` → current; else `Array.isArray(strategies)` → old structured; else legacy `question_1`). **That property is also what made the v759 backend deploy WINDOWLESS** — unlike the 2026-08-17 four-total ship, the OLD deployed frontend's four-key payload still validates and still stores against the NEW handler, so there was no interval in which either side rejected the other. Do not "tidy" year 1 into a nested `year1` group; that would forfeit both properties at once.

**THE MESSAGE LIST IS NOW TEN, still validated in EXACTLY this order on both sides** (`AssessTaxForm.handleSubmit` in `TaxPrioritiesTab.jsx` and `actions/tax/save-assess-form.ts`), **byte-identical including the U+2014 EM DASH** in every suffix:
1. *"VFO Services Tax Planning Fee is required"* — **validated FIRST, and its suffix-free wording is a REVERSION, not an oversight.** It briefly read with a year suffix while the per-year-fee design was alive; when the fee went back to engagement-level the suffix came off. Do not "restore" it.
2. *"Total Tax without the Plan — Year 1 is required"*, *"Total Tax with the Plan — Year 1 is required"*, *"Total Cash Outlay for the Strategies — Year 1 is required"*, then the cross-field *"Total Tax with the Plan must be less than Total Tax without the Plan — Year 1"*.
3. The identical four with **`— Year 2`** — applied **ONLY when at least one year-2 field is non-blank**.
4. *"Notes must be 5000 characters or fewer"* (checked against the TRIMMED string).

Both sides build the per-year strings by concatenating the same suffix constant onto the same label, so the labels remain part of the contract. **ALL THREE YEAR-2 FIELDS BLANK MEANS THE GROUP IS ABSENT, NOT INVALID** — a planner who ticks "Add Year 2" and then leaves it empty must never be blocked, and both sides apply that identical test before validating anything in the group. Absent is a perfectly good one-year plan.

**BOTH OPTIONAL KEYS ARE WRITTEN ONLY WHEN THEY HAVE CONTENT, AND THE REBUILD IS THE DELETE PATH.** The handler has never merged — it rebuilds the whole jsonb every submit — so un-ticking "Add Year 2" (or clearing the notes) simply omits the key from the payload, and the re-save is what **removes** it from storage. There is no separate delete action and none is needed. The FE keeps the typed year-2 values in component state while the toggle is off, so a mis-click does not destroy them. **`year2`'s mere PRESENCE is what selects the deck's shape** — absent → 27-slide single-year, present → 29-slide two-year (#374) — so writing an empty-but-present group would silently change the deliverable.

Cross-ref **#336** (the generator that consumes this jsonb, now on template v4), **#374** (the deck is 27 OR 29 slides again, decided by `year2`), #262 (the step remains on the planner editable whitelists), #269 (program scoping), #254 (sentinel-first trigger). `see #306`.

**307. The advisor implementation fee is TWO admin-entered per-program values on the onboarding row as of v679 — never a hardcoded $4,000, never one unit price; the agreement tokens are SUFFIXED to dodge the tax pipeline's pre-existing `[IMPLEMENTATION_FEE]`, and the backend guard fails SOFT so the FRONTEND decision gate is the real boundary (2026-07-30). ALWAYS-APPLIES (advisor pipeline).** `$4,000` used to be hardcoded in TWO places at once: `agreement_templates` id=9 `html_body` (literally, in BOTH its VFO Fast Track and its PFT section) and `actions/advisor/ceo-countersign.ts` (`(selectedVfoFt ? 4000 : 0) + (selectedPft ? 4000 : 0) + (selectedCorporate ? 600 : 0)`). The two programs are priced independently and negotiated per advisor, so a NEW Stage 1 step **"Implementation Value"** — sitting between "Team Member Responsible" and "Preliminary Meeting Decision" in `AdvisorOnboarding.jsx` — now takes **two separate dollar inputs, one per program** (the user explicitly rejected a single unit price applied twice). Migration `20260730100000_advisor_implementation_value.sql` adds `implementation_value_vfo_ft numeric`, `implementation_value_pft numeric`, `implementation_value_at timestamptz` to `advisor_onboarding`. **The charge is `vfo_ft_value×(vfo ticked) + pft_value×(pft ticked) + $600 Corporate`**; ACH is charged exactly that, and card keeps the UNCHANGED `(amt+0.30)/(1−0.029)` gross-up in `stripe-checkout.ts`. **No email template changed** — every advisor email already rendered `[Total Amount]` off `payment_amount`.

**The five things that must all hold.**
(1) **The tokens are `[IMPLEMENTATION_FEE_VFO_FT]` and `[IMPLEMENTATION_FEE_PFT]`, and the suffixes are LOAD-BEARING.** `actions/tax/send-agreement.ts` already substitutes an unrelated token literally named **`[IMPLEMENTATION_FEE]`** on the tax agreement. Agreement substitution is a flat `String.replace` over `html_body` with no pipeline namespacing, so an unsuffixed advisor token would have been a same-name collision across two pipelines' templates — one careless copy-paste of a template body and the wrong pipeline's number renders. **Before adding ANY new agreement token, grep every `actions/*/send-agreement.ts` for the bare name first.** The substitution emits the NUMBER only (`4,000`) — the `$` lives in the template HTML, so never re-add a `$` in the replacement.
(2) **`send-agreement`'s 400 guard fails SOFT — the FE gate is the real boundary.** `automation_ADVISOR_sendagreement` returns 400 `"Implementation values not set on onboarding row"` unless BOTH values are `> 0`, but its **three chain callers** (`decision.ts`, `extra-meeting.ts`, `client-decision.ts`) fire it over service-role HTTP and return `{success:true, …, sendagreement_result:<the 400 body>}` — deliberately, so a send failure can never block recording the decision itself. The error is therefore **buried in a nested field nobody reads**, and an unpriced row would silently record a Yes with no agreement sent. What actually prevents that is `decisionBlocked = !onboarding_team_member || !bothValuesSet` in `AdvisorOnboarding.jsx`, which disables the Yes/Undecided/No buttons and shows a three-variant hint. **Any NEW surface that records an advisor decision (a second admin panel, a token page, a sweep auto-advance) MUST re-check both values itself** — inheriting the backend guard alone gets you a soft failure.
(3) **The legacy fallback is PER LEG, not global.** `ceo-countersign.ts` reads `Number(ob.implementation_value_vfo_ft) > 0 ? … : 4000` and the same for PFT, because rows already in flight when the step shipped have both columns NULL and must still price correctly. Do not "clean up" the `4000` literals until every in-flight advisor row is priced. `totalAmountStr` is cents-safe (`Number.isInteger` → no decimals, else exactly 2), and the same integer-vs-2dp formatting was applied in `payment-email.ts`, `confirmation-email.ts` and `invoice-receipt.ts` so whole dollars still render `4,000` rather than `4,000.00`.
(4) **The save action is a PARTIAL update, and `implementation_value_at` is kept-if-existing.** `save_advisor_implementation_value` (`actions/advisor/save-implementation-value.ts`, AUTH, `ADMIN_ONLY_ACTIONS` next to `save_advisor_team_member`; +1 action, 442 → **443**) takes `{ onboarding_id, vfo_ft_value?, pft_value? }`: an **absent** key is left untouched, `null`/`""` clears, anything else must parse finite and `> 0` (rounded 2 dp) or that key 400s on its own. The two FE inputs commit **independently** on blur/Enter and send only their own field, so they can never clobber each other. `implementation_value_at` = `existing ?? now()` when BOTH final values are `> 0`, else `null` — the `existing ??` is what stops an edit to one dollar value from wiping a date an admin hand-corrected through `save_onboarding_step_date` (the column is on that handler's ADVISOR whitelist).
(5) **`agreement_templates` backup row id=22 MUST stay `active=false`.** The pre-change `html_body` of id=9 was preserved as a NEW row id=22 (`payment_plan='Backup 2026-07-30'`, title suffixed `(backup 2026-07-30 pre-implementation-fee)`) — the `(pipeline, service_level, payment_plan, payer_type)` unique constraint is why the `payment_plan` had to be altered rather than copied verbatim. `send-agreement.ts` selects with `.eq("active", true).order("id", {ascending:false}).limit(1)`, so **flipping id=22 to `active=true` would make it WIN over id=9** (22 > 9) and quietly ship the old hardcoded-$4,000 agreement. Restore by copying its `html_body` back onto id=9, never by activating it.

**Two deliberate non-touches, both user decisions — do not "finish the job" without asking.** The Undecided-path static bucket PDF `advisor-onboarding-agreements/Advisor_Implementation_Agreement.pdf` (fetched by `actions/advisor/decision.ts`) **still says $4,000** and was knowingly left alone; it is a reference attachment, not the signed document (the BoldSign path renders fresh HTML→PDF per advisor, cf. #79). The **accountant pipeline is untouched** and still hardcodes its checkbox pricing (4000/2000 + the $600 corporate add-on) in `actions/accountant/ceo-countersign.ts` — the two pipelines are now deliberately asymmetric. Cross-ref **#118** (the same Stage-1 decision gate this extends — team member responsible), **#249** (`save_onboarding_step_date`'s per-pipeline `*_at` allow-list, which this column joins), **#79** (the bucket PDF is a reference copy; BoldSign renders the real one) and **#193** (the two inputs are rendered by a plain `implField(key)` call, NOT a nested component, so they keep their identity across renders and stay in the buffer's scope). `see #307`.

**308. The "Additional information required" step completes via EITHER a vault upload OR a WRITTEN text submission — both stamp the ONE `additional_info_received_at`, and the text lives in an APPENDED `{text, at}` array that must never be overwritten (2026-07-30, v680). ALWAYS-APPLIES (tax track).** #271 shipped the step with exactly one way to answer: click the secure `/tax-upload?token=` link and upload a file, at which point `actions/vault/upload-notify.ts` stamps `client_tax_plans.additional_info_received_at`. But most of what a planner asks for is not a document ("which state did you file in?", "the rental is my wife’s, not the LLC’s"), so clients replied to the email instead — invisible to the step, which stayed amber forever — or gave up. The `/tax-upload` page now also carries a free-text box. **NEW PUBLIC handler `actions/vault/tax-text-submit.ts` → action `vault_tax_text_submit`** (+1 action, 443 → **444**), registered in `PUBLIC_HANDLERS` next to `vault_tax_upload_notify`. Body `{ token, text }`. **The design turns on ONE fact: the text path stamps the SAME `additional_info_received_at` column the upload path stamps** — which is why the step’s existing done-logic needed no change at all, and why **any new consumer of “did the client respond?” must consider BOTH paths** rather than looking for a file. **The store is APPEND-ONLY.** Migration `20260730110000_tax_additional_info_response.sql` adds `client_tax_plans.additional_info_responses jsonb` (nullable, `add column if not exists`, plus a column comment); each submission pushes `{ text, at }` onto the existing array — **never overwrite it**, because one outstanding request can draw several clarifications and the planner needs all of them (the admin UI lists them newest-first). **The handler’s guard order and its error codes are the contract.** Token → `clients` by `tax_upload_token` with the lookup **error-checked** (500 on a DB error, **403 "Invalid link"** on a genuine miss — #187); text trimmed and hard-capped at **5000 chars**, empty → 400; then the newest plan with `additional_info_requested_at NOT NULL` (`.order("id", {ascending:false}).limit(1)`) or **400 "No open request for additional information"** — a client cannot use a stale link to write into a plan with nothing outstanding. One `update` writes the appended array and the stamp together. **It fires the two EXISTING notification rules and adds NO new `notification_rules` rows**: `TAX_additional_info_received` (Tracy + Tray; its message explicitly says “written explanation (no file)” so the reader knows not to hunt for a document) and `TAX_planner_additional_info_uploaded` via `notifyAllocatedPlanner` (dedupe `unread`, client-named title per #272). Both are wrapped in one try/catch so a notification failure can never lose the client’s answer. **The bell link is deliberately `/admin/client/<id>?tab=tax&program=<n||1>`, NOT the upload path’s `?tab=vault`** — text lives on the plan, not in the vault, so pointing at the vault would send the reader somewhere the answer does not exist. **The ADMIN’s own request free text is STILL NOT STORED anywhere** — `[REQUESTED_INFO]` is interpolated into the Gmail draft and lost; only the CLIENT’s replies are persisted, so the admin UI can show what was said back but not what was asked. FE: `TaxUploadPage.jsx` gains an “Or write an explanation” section (textarea + Send, disabled while empty/sending, a separate `noteError` state so an upload error and a text error can never overwrite each other), and `TaxPrioritiesTab.jsx`’s additional-info renderer gains a **“Client explanation” chip row** after the AI PC Admin cascade, cloned from the assess-step submitted-chip pattern (green dot, green Submitted pill, `formatStamp` of the newest entry, ▼ caret, expand key `addinfo_resp_${task.id}`) expanding to a newest-first read-only panel. ONE shared component serves BOTH programs (1 Holistic + 4 Tax Planning), so #271’s program-agnostic property is unchanged. Cross-ref **#271** (the step this extends), **#272** (client-named planner bell titles), **#176** (bells go through `notifyByRule`), **#187** (error-check the lookup), **#225** (`formatStamp`, not `formatDate`, for a timestamptz). `see #308`.

**309. Every `*_upload_url` signed-URL MINTER must be role-gated or session-scoped — “in no gate list” means UNGATED, not safe (2026-07-30, v680). ALWAYS-APPLIES.** `vault_upload_url` (`actions/vault/upload-url.ts`) mints signed upload URLs into the private `member-vault` / `member-tax-returns` buckets, taking `member_number` **from the request body** — and it appeared in **no entry of `constants/role-gates.ts` at all**. Role gates are deny-by-OMISSION for *roles*, but a plain AUTH action absent from every list is simply reachable by any authenticated session: **any logged-in member could mint an upload URL into ANY other member’s folder, including their tax returns.** A **write-only hole** — `vault_list` and `vault_download` were already in `MEMBER_SCOPED_ACTIONS`, so nothing could be read back; the exposure was writing (overwrite / plant) into someone else’s sensitive vault. **The fix is `MEMBER_SCOPED_ACTIONS`, and the choice of gate is the interesting part.** The middleware rewrite for that list overwrites `body.member_number` with the caller’s own value before dispatch, which is exactly the confinement needed, and it leaves admins untouched (`MembersPanel` → `MemberVault admin=true` still passes any member’s number). **`ADMIN_ONLY_ACTIONS` would have been WRONG** — the member portal itself uploads through this action (`MemberPortal.jsx:177` → `MemberVault admin=false` → `VaultSections` “+ Add document”), so admin-only would have silently broken every member’s own vault. **The going-forward rule: when you add a new signed-upload action, grep `role-gates.ts` and be able to NAME the gate that covers it.** A minter that takes its target key from the body and cannot name its gate is a hole. Note the sibling minters already do this correctly by a different route — `client_vault_upload_url` is in the 5-entry `CLIENT_ALLOWED_ACTIONS` deny-by-default list and scopes to `auth.callerClientId`; `specialist_vault_upload_url` scopes to `auth.callerSpecialistId`; `vault_tax_upload_url` is PUBLIC but token-gated on `clients.tax_upload_token`. **Pre-existing inconsistency found in the same sweep and deliberately NOT changed:** `vault_delete` is in `ADMIN_ONLY_ACTIONS` yet `VaultSections` renders a **Remove** button to members that 403s on click — a dead control rather than a hole, but it should be either hidden for members or member-scoped like its upload twin. Cousin of **#142** (service-role bypasses RLS → never trust a body id) and **#256** (a gate entry that matches no dispatched action protects nothing — this is its mirror image: a dispatched action that matches no gate entry is protected by nothing). `see #309`.

**310. "Request documentation" is a DURABLE per-`(entity_type, entity_key, section)` token whose ROW is the only credential for a PUBLIC signed-upload minter — the bucket and the path are derived server-side FROM THE TOKEN ROW and never from the request body (2026-07-30, v681 + the owed v682). ALWAYS-APPLIES.** The admin-side button on the Sensitive / General section of a client / member / specialist vault (`vault_request_docs`, AUTH, `ADMIN_ONLY_ACTIONS`) drafts a Gmail carrying a `/vault-upload?token=` link; the recipient uploads with no login and the files land in that entity+section's private bucket. It clones the tax "Request additional information" pattern (#271) but is program/pipeline-agnostic, and it needed NEW token infrastructure because members and specialists had no public upload token at all (only `clients.tax_upload_token` existed).

**The token is DURABLE, one per (entity, section), and reused forever.** `vault_upload_tokens` (migration `20260730120000_vault_request_docs.sql`; `unique (entity_type, entity_key, section)`, deny-all RLS in the SAME migration per #141, anon probe `Content-Range: */0`) is minted with `token32()` on the first request and **reused on every resend**, stamping `last_requested_at` — so every email ever sent against that section keeps working. That is the point (a client who finds last month's email can still answer), and it is also the caveat: **there is no revoke** short of deleting/rotating the row, which silently kills every link already in the wild. `last_upload_at` is stamped best-effort on arrival and is non-fatal.

**The token row is the WHOLE credential for the PUBLIC pair, and that is safe only because nothing in the body selects a destination (cousin of #309).** `vault_request_upload_url` and `vault_request_upload_notify` are deliberately **PUBLIC** (registered in `PUBLIC_HANDLERS`, deliberately NOT in `ADMIN_ONLY_ACTIONS` — `constants/role-gates.ts` carries a comment saying so, because the next reader of #309 will otherwise "fix" them). The confinement is two things together: the unguessable token, and the fact that **bucket + path are computed entirely from the token row**. The minter reads `{entity_type, entity_key, section}` off the row, maps the bucket through `VAULT_REQUEST_BUCKETS` (`client` → `client-tax-returns`/`client-documents`, `member` → `member-tax-returns`/`member-vault`, `specialist` → `specialist-tax-returns`/`specialist-documents`), and writes to `` `${entity_key}/${rand16}_${safeName}` `` with the filename stripped to `[a-zA-Z0-9._-]` and capped at 120 chars. The request body contributes ONLY `token` + `filename` — **never a member number, client id, section or bucket.** A future "let them pick which section" parameter would hand an anonymous caller a destination selector and must not be added; mint a second token instead.

**`/vault-upload` is deliberately upload-ONLY — the free-text box is the TAX flow's feature, not a missing one here.** `VaultUploadPage.jsx` is a `TaxUploadPage` clone with the "Or write an explanation" section removed on purpose: a typed answer only means something where a step is waiting on it (#308's `additional_info_received_at`), and this feature is attached to a vault section, not to a step. Do not "restore parity" — there is nowhere to put the text.

**The Cc is done by an UNSET ROLE TOKEN, which drops SILENTLY.** The single seeded template (`email_templates` pipeline `VAULT`, `template_name` `VAULT_request_documentation`, `to_list` `["RECIPIENT"]`, **`cc_list` `["MEMBER"]`**, `send_mode=false` → Draft) serves all three entity types. `vault_request_docs` passes `resolveTemplateRecipients` a ctx of `{ RECIPIENT: person.email, MEMBER: memberEmail }` where `memberEmail` is resolved **only when `entity_type === 'client'`** (via `clients.member_number` → `members.email`); member and specialist requests pass `null`, the `MEMBER` role token resolves to nothing and the Cc list comes back empty. **So "no Cc on member/specialist requests" is achieved by OMISSION, not by a branch** — which means anyone reusing this template for a NEW entity type must decide that entity's `MEMBER` ctx explicitly or it will silently ship with no Cc and nobody will notice. Note the deploy ordering trap this created: **live v681 predates the Cc edit and passes no `MEMBER` ctx at all, so against v681 every draft — client included — goes out with NO Cc; the client-request member-Cc only exists once v682 ships.**

**A new entity type must extend `resolveVaultPerson` AND `VAULT_REQUEST_BUCKETS` TOGETHER — they live in different files.** `resolveVaultPerson` (exported from `actions/vault/request-docs.ts`, also imported by the notify handler) resolves `client` by `clients.id`, `member` by `members.member_number`, and **falls through to `experts` by id as its DEFAULT branch** — it is only safe because `VAULT_ENTITY_TYPES` is validated first in `vault_request_docs`. `VAULT_REQUEST_BUCKETS` lives in `actions/vault/request-upload-url.ts`. Add an entity to one and not the other and you get either a person with nowhere to write or a bucket with nobody to name; also extend the `entity_type` CHECK on the table and `linkFor()` in `request-upload-notify.ts` (the admin deep link differs per entity: `/admin/client/<id>?tab=vault` · `/admin?member=<n>&feature=vault` · `/admin?tab=specialists&section=specialist_search&expert=<id>`).

**This is a SEPARATE SYSTEM from `/tax-upload` — never conflate the two.** `clients.tax_upload_token` + `/tax-upload` + `vault_tax_upload_url`/`_notify`/`vault_tax_text_submit` are untouched and remain the tax-planning path. **An upload arriving through `/vault-upload` fires NO tax stamps** — `tax_returns_received_at` and `additional_info_received_at` belong exclusively to the `/tax-upload` flow, so a client who answers a tax request through a Request-documentation link will file the document correctly but will NOT green the tax step. The only signal this path raises is the new `notification_rules` key **`VAULT_requested_doc_uploaded`** (area Uploads, bell, `default_recipients` = Tray `tvaldes@elitert.com` ONLY), wrapped in try/catch so a bell failure never loses the upload. As with #271/#308, **the ADMIN's typed request text is stored NOWHERE** — `[REQUESTED_INFO]` is interpolated into the Gmail draft and lost; there is no record of what was asked, only of what came back. Placements are the SIX admin surfaces only (client/member/specialist × sensitive/general): the member and specialist portals pass no `requestDocs` prop, the ERT/VFOS third section never gets one (#204), and the client sensitive section additionally rides the `isTaxAdmin` `canView` gate plus `!readOnly` so a tax planner never sees it. Cross-ref **#309** (the minter-gating rule this satisfies by a different route — PUBLIC + token, like `vault_tax_upload_url`), **#141** (deny-all + anon probe), **#271** (the pattern cloned), **#308** (the text path that is deliberately NOT cloned), **#181** (the template ships in Draft mode) and **#204** (the ERT section is admin-write/owner-read and out of scope). `see #310`.

**311. "Tax 6 - Implementation" is a PER-SPECIALIST phase as of v683: its four tasks live in `client_tax_progress` keyed by `tax_specialist_id`, a plan-level (NULL) row for one of them is DEAD data, and phase done requires at least one allocated specialist — plus the off-directory allocation is now a server-prefixed `Custom - <name>` (2026-07-31). ALWAYS-APPLIES (tax track).** Tax 5 has been per-specialist since the allocation card shipped (#260/#305), but `Tax 6 - Implementation` was still a single plan-level checklist: its four steps were answered ONCE for the whole plan however many specialists were implementing. A plan can carry five specialists running five different strategies on different timelines, and one of them stopping says nothing about the other four — so Tax 6 now mirrors Tax 5's shape exactly.

**THE STORAGE MOVE IS BREAKING FOR EXISTING ROWS.** The four Tax 6 tasks are now written to `client_tax_progress` with a non-NULL **`tax_specialist_id`** (FE progress key `${task.id}_${spec.id}`, saved through the EXISTING `tax_save_task` with `tax_specialist_id` — **no new action, +0 on the action count**). **A `tax_specialist_id IS NULL` row for a Tax 6 task is therefore unreachable by every new reader.** Eight such rows existed at the cutover — clients Marc Ehrhardt (129, plan 89) and Henry Mennig (132, plan 92), all four tasks `Completed` 2026-07-30 — and were **backed up to `C:\Users\jakel_fjetgbx\tax6-progress-backup-2026-07-31.md` (restore SQL included) and DELETED** with user approval, then re-entered per specialist through the new UI. If you find a plan-level Tax 6 row in future, it is legacy or a bug, not state.

**THERE ARE FOUR CONSUMERS OF TAX 6 DONE-NESS AND THEY MUST MOVE TOGETHER.** (1) FE `getPhaseState` gained a `phase.name === 'Tax 6 - Implementation'` branch — **done** = at least one specialist AND every specialist has every non-auto task statused (per-spec keys), **active** = any per-spec status, else **pending**. The old plan-level reads would never have seen the per-spec keys, so without this branch the phase would have read pending forever. (2) The FE card pill counts **specialists × tasks**, not tasks. (3) The FE hero counts: Tax 6 is the ONLY phase in `phasesAfterSpec`, and it was **removed from BOTH the total and the done plan-level reduces** and re-added once as its own per-specialist term (`taxSpecialists.length × tax6SpecTasks.length`), mirroring the existing `tax5aSpecTasks` handling — leaving it in the reduce AND adding the term double-counts it; removing it without the term drops it entirely. (4) BE `actions/clients/overview-tax.ts` gained a `t.phase_name === "Tax 6 - Implementation"` branch **before** the generic fallback, emitting one step per allocated specialist labelled `<task> — <specialist>` off the per-specialist progress map with `applicable: implReach`.

**THE OVERVIEW DELIBERATELY REUSES TAX 5's SCOPE STRING.** Those per-specialist Tax 6 steps are tagged **`scope: spec-<specialist id>` — the SAME scope the Tax 5 branch emits** — so one specialist's Tax 5 and Tax 6 steps form a single ordered sequence for the skipped-step scanner. Giving Tax 6 its own scope would raise false "step 3 answered but step 2 blank" warnings across two *different* specialists on every multi-specialist plan. Do not split them.

**PHASE DONE REQUIRES ≥1 SPECIALIST — this is a deliberate behavior change.** A plan with no allocated specialists renders "No specialists allocated yet." and can never read Done. Previously the plan-level `every()` over an empty task set was vacuously true. Nothing has been implemented when nobody is implementing it, so the new answer is the correct one — but any code that assumed "Tax 6 done" is reachable without specialists is now wrong.

**CHIP PRECEDENCE IS `Stopped` > `Done` > `In progress` > `Not started`, AND "ANSWERED" INCLUDES `Stopped` AND `N/A`.** Per specialist box: ANY of the four steps `Stopped` → red **Stopped** (a stop is the headline even if the other three are complete); else all four answered → green **Done**; else some answered → blue **In progress · n/4**; else grey **Not started**. "Answered" is any non-empty `status` — `Completed`, `Stopped` and `N/A` all count, because the question has been decided either way. The box default-expands until all four are answered (expand key **`tax6_spec_<id>`**, distinct from Tax 5's `spec_<id>`), per the phase-collapse convention (#275). **`removeSpecialist` must clear BOTH expand keys** — it does; a new per-specialist expand key added later must be cleared there too or it leaks onto whichever specialist next reuses that id.

**REMOVAL IS NOW OPTIMISTIC BECAUSE THE RE-FETCH SWALLOWS ITS ERRORS.** `removeSpecialist` cleared the progress and expand keys but relied on `loadSpecialists()` — whose `catch` is silent — to drop the row itself, so a hiccup in the re-fetch left a deleted specialist rendered until a page reload (user-reported). It now calls `setTaxSpecialists(p => p.filter(...))` immediately after backend success, with the re-fetch demoted to reconciliation. The backend delete ordering of #305 (progress rows FIRST) is unchanged and still load-bearing.

**THE CUSTOM NAMING CONTRACT — the server owns the prefix.** The picker's trailing option is relabelled **"Custom"** (the module sentinel `OTHER_SPEC_VALUE = '__other__'` is UNCHANGED) and selecting it reveals a name input (`maxLength` 80) with **Add disabled until it is non-empty**. The body carries `custom_name`; `actions/tax/add-specialist.ts` runs `sanitizeCustomName` — collapse whitespace → trim → strip a leading case-insensitive `"custom -"` → 80-char cap — and inserts **`${CUSTOM_PREFIX}${name}` = `Custom - <name>`**. **The prefix is built server-side and the leading-prefix strip is what makes it idempotent**: a client sending `"Custom - Bob"` stores `Custom - Bob`, not `Custom - Custom - Bob`. **An empty or absent `custom_name` falls through to the legacy `Other #N (See notes)` max+1 numbering**, retained for API back-compat and for rows already in the wild — the FE can no longer reach that path, and as of 2026-07-31 **zero `Other #N` rows remain live** (the single one, Ehrhardt plan 89, was converted to `Custom - Steven Cox` by the user). **`expert_id` stays NULL on BOTH paths**, so #305's guarantee that an off-directory allocation can never reach a member showroom or the public widget (#231/#201) is untouched, and **neither path dedupes** — several customs on one plan are as legitimate as several "Other"s were.

**Display-only wording rides along and must not be confused with state.** The greyed-out Tax 5 steps on a `Client decision 2 = 'Move to Implementation'` plan now read "(Due Diligence Skipped - Moved to Implementation)" (was "(Moved to implementation)"). That is the `greyNote` string ONLY — **the two load-bearing status strings `'Continue DD'` / `'Move to Implementation'` of #274 are UNCHANGED**, as is the BE `byName("Client decision 2")` resolution. Cross-ref **#305** (the specialist allocation model this extends — its delete-ordering hazard is unchanged, its "Other" naming half is superseded here), **#260** (the merged Tax 5 card and the exact-string phase names — do NOT rename `Tax 6 - Implementation`), **#274** (the decision-2 strings), **#275** (phase-collapse), **#269** (program scoping) and **#231**/**#201** (never let a non-directory entity reach a showroom). `see #311`.

**312. Member-to-member links are TWO independent models as of 2026-07-31 (v685): an INTRODUCTION is DIRECTIONAL and lives on `members.introduced_by_member_number` with the tier in `members.connection_type` (which is what the INTRODUCER earns, not the introduced member), a CONNECTION is MUTUAL / untyped / unlimited and lives as a canonical pair row in the new `member_connections` table, and `members.connected_member_number` is now LEGACY corporate-parent-only — every non-corporate value was CLEARED, so any code still reading it to answer "who is this member connected to" is a bug. ALWAYS-APPLIES.**
Before this session there was exactly ONE link: `members.connected_member_number` (a single self-referencing pointer) plus an optional `connection_type` tier, stored on one member's row and rendered only on that member's profile. That one slot was being asked to mean three different things at once — a revenue-sharing introduction, a peer relationship, and the corporate-parent pointer — and because it was one-way and single-valued, the *other* member's profile showed nothing and a second relationship overwrote the first. The two concepts are now separate stores with separate semantics, and the old column keeps only its third meaning.

**INTRODUCTIONS ARE DIRECTIONAL AND THE TIER IS THE INTRODUCER'S EARNINGS.** NEW column `members.introduced_by_member_number` (text, FK → `members.member_number` `ON DELETE SET NULL`, migration `20260731120000_introduced_by_member_number.sql`) answers "who introduced THIS member" — **one introducer per member, no more**. The tier stays in the EXISTING `members.connection_type` (`'5% - Regular Advisor'` / `'10% - Accredited Introducer'` / `'10% - Accredited Mentor'` / `'20% - Accredited Introducer + Mentor'`) and is now labelled **"Introducer Benefit"** in the UI, because it describes what the person named in `introduced_by_member_number` earns — read it off the INTRODUCED member's row, pay it to the INTRODUCER. **The tier field is now shown for EVERY member category including accountants** (it used to be hidden behind `member_category === 'accountant'`). The same migration MOVED the three rows that already carried a tier out of `connected_member_number` into the new column (Marjorie Wang 59470 ← Jason Bright 59119; Violetta Terpeluk 59515 ← Deb Snyder 59414; Todd Veillon 58088-FC6 ← Jack Branch 58088) — a typed old-style link WAS an introduction, which is why the tier column could be reused unchanged. There is no dedicated action: `member_profile_save` is a whole-row **spread upsert** of the `profile` payload, so the new column persists through the ordinary Save button with no handler edit.

**CONNECTIONS ARE CANONICAL UNORDERED PAIRS.** NEW table `public.member_connections` (`id` identity PK, `member_a` / `member_b` text FK → `members.member_number` `ON DELETE CASCADE`, `created_at`, **CHECK `member_a < member_b`**, **UNIQUE `(member_a, member_b)`**, RLS enabled + `"Deny all access" USING(false)` in the same migration per #141 — anon probe returns `Content-Range: */0`). Migration `20260731130000_member_connections.sql`. **The CHECK is the whole design**: because a pair is always stored with the lexicographically smaller member number first, the same link can never exist twice in opposite orders, and "am I connected to X" is one symmetric lookup rather than two. **Every writer must normalize before touching the table** — `member_connection_add` / `member_connection_remove` (`actions/members/connection-add.ts` / `connection-remove.ts`, AUTH, both in `ADMIN_ONLY_ACTIONS`, body `{member_a, member_b}`) sort the two numbers themselves, so a caller may pass them in either order. **Both are idempotent by design:** add treats a `23505` unique violation as success (double-clicks, retries), remove treats a missing row as success (the desired end state is reached either way). Add also 400s on empty/equal member numbers and on a member number that does not exist in `members` — an explicit lookup, error-destructured per #187, so a DB failure can never read as "not found".

**`connected_member_number` IS LEGACY — CORPORATE-PARENT ONLY.** The `member_connections` migration backfilled all **240** one-way links as pairs and then **CLEARED `connected_member_number` on every non-corporate row**; as of 2026-07-31 **zero live rows carry a value**. The column survives for exactly one writer: the Add Advisor **corporate** flow (`add_member_full`, driven by the "Connected Member \*" picker that appears only when the new member is a Corporate type), which uses it as the parent pointer. `load_data` still returns it for that purpose. **Anything else that reads it for connection meaning is wrong** — that is not a style opinion, it is a data statement: the values are gone. The PFT auto-link in `actions/accountant/create-member.ts` was converted accordingly: it no longer sets `connected_member_number` on the members insert and instead writes a normalized `member_connections` pair **after** the members row exists (both sides are FKs), best-effort inside try/catch with `23505` swallowed — **a connection failure must never block accountant creation**.

**361. The LEGACY platform is still live and still reacting to the same Stripe customers we now bill — a duplicate document with the SAME number in a DIFFERENT Drive folder is a two-systems collision, NOT a webhook redelivery (2026-08-11, diagnosis only, no code change). (Migration / MAP 1.)** Deanna Haines (client 146, `20028-001`) produced two identical `REC-20028-FT1-0002` receipt drafts for one ACH clearance, which looks exactly like the #327/#328 Henry Mennig redelivery. It is not, and **the two are told apart by the Drive folder, not by the timestamps**.

**The discriminator.** A genuine redelivery writes both PDFs into the **same** folder seconds apart (Mennig: two `REC-59380-001-0001.pdf` in folder `1YiIpyE8…`, 34 s apart). A two-systems collision writes them into **different folders under different roots**: ours landed 12:31:25 in *"Deanna Haines - 20028-001"* (naming `Name - ref`) under **VFO Clients** `1B4m9zaj…` = `GOOGLE_DRIVE_FOLDER_ID`; the second landed 12:33:51 in *"Deanna Haines (20028-FT1)"* (naming `Name (ref)`) under **VFO Services** `1RDQzbgw…`, a folder created **2026-05-21**. `contract-invoice-receipt.ts` searches Drive by the exact name `${first} ${last} - ${clientRef}`, so our code can neither find nor create the parenthesised legacy folder, and nothing in either repo references that root. **Our backend ran exactly once** — `rec2_email_sent_at` and `updated_at` are the same millisecond, and a second run of that handler would have re-stamped both.

**Why it started when it did, and who is exposed.** The old platform was never switched off; it simply had nothing to collide with until a migrated client's payment cleared *after* cutover. Flood (legacy receipt Jul 10, migrated Jul 16), Vang (Jul 13 / Jul 17) and Moses (Jul 30 / Aug 5) all have legacy-folder receipts that **predate** their own `legacy_migrated_at`. Haines (migrated Aug 5, cleared Aug 11) is the first overlap. **12 legacy MAP 1 rows still have future installments** — Rossi Aug 15, Mennig Aug 18, Nnadi Sep 1, Bitzer Sep 4, then Slosser, Flood, Hurst, Vang, Cruts, Moses, Burkhart, Haines. **The receipts are paper; the unanswered question is money** — a legacy *charger* still armed on the same `stripe_customer_id` would be a double charge, and our `_chargescheduled_sweep` has **no legacy exclusion** (see #362). The fix is not in this codebase: find and disable the legacy Stripe listener (a second webhook endpoint or a polling scenario on the same live account), or give it a skip-list of migrated customer ids. Cross-ref **#327**, **#328**, **#230**. `see #361`.

**362. `backfill-map1.ts` pre-settles the rev-share AND strategic legs of every installment flagged already-paid, and `isResolved` then skips that installment FOREVER — so a migrated row can collect money through OUR pipeline while its share is permanently stamped "no share due" (2026-08-11). (Migration / revenue share.)** This is #277 working as designed, with a failure mode #277 did not name.

**What the migration writes.** For any installment where the admin sets `paid: true`, `actions/migration/backfill-map1.ts:162-176` stamps `pay{i}_status='succeeded'`, `rec{i}_rev_share='Completed - Revenue Share'` (or `'Completed - Money Mapping'`), **`rec{i}_rev_paid='N/A — No Share Due'`**, `rec{i}_rev_completed_at=nowIso`, and — when a strategic share exists — `rec{i}_strat_paid='N/A — No Share Due'` + `rec{i}_strat_completed_at=nowIso`. The `else` branch writes only `pay{i}_status=null`. **The tell is the timestamp: `rec{n}_rev_completed_at` equal to `legacy_migrated_at` to the millisecond means the migration wrote it, not a payment.**

**Why it is terminal.** `contract-revshare.ts:43-51` treats `rev_share` set + not `'Pending'` + `rev_paid` ∈ {`Yes`, `Money Mapping`, `N/A — No Share Due`} as **resolved** and returns `{skipped:true}` immediately. Nothing re-opens it — not the nightly `_revshare_sweep`, not a webhook, not a later payment. So if the installment was *not* in fact collected on the old platform (Haines payment 2: the user checked Stripe Transfers and **nothing had ever gone to the member**), the share is silently never paid, and the row asserts the opposite. **Before manually settling one of these, check Stripe Transfers first** — the pre-settle exists precisely to stop a double payout, so treating it as always-wrong is as dangerous as trusting it. Also note `contract-revshare.ts` sets `rev_share='Pending'` *before* attempting anything, so a mid-run failure leaves a non-terminal state that the sweep DOES retry — only the three terminal `rev_paid` values latch. Cross-ref **#277**, **#303**, **#335**, **#361**. `see #362`.

**363. Accounting → Holistic Planning Revenue / Reconciliation are ATTRIBUTION views, not payout reports — they derive Member and Strategic from the CONFIGURED shares and never read `rec{n}_rev_paid` / `rec{n}_strat_paid` (2026-08-11). (Accounting.)** `holisticShared.jsx clearedPayments()` computes, per cleared installment, `member = member_share ÷ n`, `strategic = strategic_partner_share ÷ n` and `vfos = amount − member − strategic`, keyed only off `pay{i}_status ∈ {succeeded, processing, check_pending}`. **No payout column is consulted anywhere in either panel** (both import the same helper). So a legacy row whose legs are pre-settled per **#362** displays a full member + strategic split that our system will never pay — Haines August 2026 showed `$1,350.00 / $607.50 / $607.50 / $135.00` with both payout legs reading `N/A — No Share Due`. The payout truth lives on the client's MAP 1 pricing card (`Map1PricingSplitCard.jsx`, which renders "settled on old system" from `rec{i}_strat_paid`), **not in Accounting**. Consequence worth stating: for those rows "Elite VFO Income" is *understated* by whatever is attributed to shares nobody pays. Reviewed and deliberately left as-is; if it is ever changed, the options recorded were (a) leave it as income attribution, (b) grey the cells with a "settled on old system" marker, (c) drop the legs and let the full amount land in Elite VFO Income. Cross-ref **#362**, **#277**, **#252**. `see #363`.

**364. The strategic-share Stripe memo is built at FOUR call sites — immediate handler AND nightly retry sweep, on BOTH MAP 1 and Tax — so changing it in one place is a half-fix (2026-08-11, v719-pending). (Revenue share / strategic.)** `transferStrategicShare` receives the `description` fully-formed from its caller, and there are four callers: `actions/pipeline/contract-revshare.ts:256`, `actions/pipeline/contract-revshare-sweep.ts:137`, `actions/tax/revshare.ts:288`, `actions/tax/revshare-sweep.ts:174`. The same fan-out applies to `draftStrategicPartnerEmail`, which each of those four also calls. **A memo edited only in the immediate handler silently reverts to the old wording whenever the leg is paid by the retry sweep instead** — which is the common path, because a strategic leg lands on the sweep any time the partner's Connect account was not yet live at payment time (#159).

**The 2026-08-11 change, and the split that made half of it free.** `Strategic Member: (${memberNumber}) ${memberName}` → **`Strategic Partner: ${member.member_type || ""}`** at all four sites (each already had the group name in scope as the `memberType` argument, so nothing new had to be fetched). In parallel, `email_templates` **178** (`STRATEGIC`/`strategic_partner_revshare`) had its `subject` moved from `[Member Name]` to **`[Partner Name]`** and its `cc_list` from `[]` to Tracy + Tray. **The template half needed NO deploy** — `[Partner Name]` was already substituted by `utils/strategic-partner-email.ts` — **but the memo half is code and does.** Two standing consequences: **178 is ONE shared row serving both MAP 1 and Tax**, so any edit to it lands on Tax Plan IQ's emails as well as Action Coach's; and the **member number is now absent from the strategic Stripe memo** (it survives on the member leg's memo and in the email body's payment-details table, which is why the body was deliberately not touched). Cross-ref **#159**, **#252**, **#277**, **#326**. `see #364`.

**THE FRONTEND CONTRACT.** `load_data` gained a **top-level `member_connections` key** (the raw `{member_a, member_b}` pair rows, one extra query in the existing `Promise.all`) alongside the per-member `introduced_by_member_number` in the roster merge. `AdminPortal.jsx` captures it into `memberConnections` state in `loadAllData` and passes it, with `onOpenMember={openMemberProfile}`, into all three `MembersPanel` mount sites (advisors / accountants / strategic). The profile details view renders **two cards**: **"Introductions"** with two direction-labeled sub-sections — *Introduced By* (this member's introducer, tier chip inline next to the name) and *Introducer Of* (everyone whose `introduced_by_member_number` points back at this member, each row chipped with **their own** `connection_type`) — and a separate **"Connections"** card of mutual partners with no chips, because a connection has no tier. Corporate-type members are excluded from both cards; they stay in the unchanged Corporate Members card.

**THE EDIT-TAB "CONNECTIONS" CARD WRITES IMMEDIATELY AND DELIBERATELY BYPASSES THE SAVE BUTTON.** Every other card on the Edit Profile tab buffers into `profile` state and commits on Save via `member_profile_save`. The Connections card cannot: its data lives in a different table, so each per-row **Remove** and each typeahead pick fires `member_connection_remove` / `member_connection_add` on the spot and then calls `onDataChange()` (= `AdminPortal.loadAllData`) to re-read. **Leaving the Edit tab without pressing Save does NOT undo a connection change**, and a future "discard changes" affordance must not pretend otherwise. The Introductions card on the same tab behaves the *opposite* way — it is ordinary buffered profile state (typeahead → `introduced_by_member_number`, select → `connection_type`, "Remove Introduction" clears both) and needs Save. Two adjacent cards, two different commit models: do not "unify" them without moving the pair rows somewhere the profile upsert can reach.

**CROSS-PROFILE NAME LINKS RIDE `openMemberProfile` + A `navClickCount` ADOPT.** Every name in all three cards is a link that calls `AdminPortal.openMemberProfile` — the same callback the Member Overview list uses — so an advisor's profile can open an accountant's. A **cross-category** jump remounts `MemberDirectoryView` and its `useState` initializer picks the seeded `sessionStorage` selection up; a **same-category** jump does not remount, so the `navClickCount` effect was extended to **ADOPT** a seeded selection (look the saved member up in `allMembers`, select it, restore the feature tab, scroll to top) instead of only clearing when the key is absent. Ordinary tab navigation still wipes the key first and therefore still clears the selection. Any new "open this member" entry point must seed that same key or the same-category case silently does nothing.

Cross-ref **#141** (deny-all + anon probe on the new table), **#187** (destructure `error`), **#193** (thread a new prop into every nested component that uses it — `onOpenMember` / `memberConnections` pass through `MembersPanel` → `AccountantsPanel`/`AdvisorsPanel`/`StrategicMembersPanel` → `MemberDirectoryView` → `MemberProfile`), and **#207** (a new `members` column is invisible to the portal until it is whitelisted in the `load_data` merge). `see #312`.
