**327. Stripe REDELIVERS a webhook when our own chain outruns its ~30s timeout, and `router/webhooks.ts` has NO event-id dedupe — so every side effect chained off a webhook needs its OWN idempotency latch or it runs twice (2026-08-04, proven by a live incident). ALWAYS-APPLIES — money/email.** `maybeHandleStripeWebhook` does all of its work — DB updates plus `fetch`-chained `automation_*` calls that mint PDFs, upload to Drive and create Gmail drafts — **before** it returns 200. Stripe waits ~30s for that 200; if it does not arrive, Stripe treats the delivery as failed and **sends the identical event again**. Nothing in the router remembers that `evt.id` was already processed, so the second delivery re-runs the whole branch from the top.

**The live case.** Henry Mennig (client 132, `59380-001`, MAP 1 continuation payment 3, $1,050). The `payment_intent.succeeded` chain — invoice/receipt PDF → Drive upload → Gmail draft — took **longer than 30s**; Stripe redelivered; the MAP 1 P2-4 branch had no guard, so `automation_CONTRACT_invoicereceipt` ran **twice** and produced **two identical Gmail drafts carrying the SAME receipt number** `REC-59380-001-0001`. **Only ONE charge was taken** (the sweep's Stripe idempotency key held), and only **ONE** revshare draft was produced — because `contract-revshare.ts` has an `isResolved` guard and the receipt path had none. **That asymmetry is the proof**: same event, same two deliveries, guarded chain fired once, unguarded chain fired twice. Timestamps in the DB, not inference, established it. v696 was investigated and ruled out.

**What was fixed, and what was not.** The MAP 1 P2-4 branch now widens its `pipeline_map1` select to `rec2/3/4_email_sent` and skips the invoicereceipt chain (with a `console.log`) when `pipeRow[`rec${paymentNum}_email_sent`] === true`. **That closes the realistic late-retry case only** — two deliveries genuinely overlapping in flight both read `false` and both fire. **Every OTHER webhook branch remains exposed**: the P1 ACH branch, tax, advisor, accountant, PIP, specialist, and the revshare chain (which is protected only by its own `isResolved` check). **The systemic fix was proposed and deliberately NOT built:** ack Stripe immediately (return 200) and run the chains under `EdgeRuntime.waitUntil`, which removes the timeout pressure entirely; a real event-id dedupe table would remove the rest. Until one of those ships, **treat "this webhook branch fires exactly once" as false.**

When adding any side effect to `router/webhooks.ts`, name the column or row that proves the effect already happened and check it first. A side effect with no latch is a duplicate waiting for a slow Drive upload.

Cross-ref **#328** (why the receipt number itself is not a latch), **#319** (the same "a slow write did NOT fail" lesson on the browser side — an abort is not a rollback), **#302**, **#187**. `see #327`.

---

**328. A reused receipt NUMBER is not idempotency — `contract-invoice-receipt.ts` reuses `recN_number` when it is already set, which makes a duplicate run look identical instead of being prevented; `recN_email_sent` is the actual latch and until 2026-08-04 NOTHING read it (2026-08-04).** The handler's design is deliberate and correct on its own terms: if `rec{N}_number` is already populated it reuses it rather than burning a new sequence value, so a re-run does not invent a second receipt number for one payment. **The trap is that this reads like a dedupe and is the opposite of one** — it guarantees the second run produces a document *indistinguishable* from the first, which is exactly what turned the #327 redelivery into two byte-identical Gmail drafts sharing `REC-59380-001-0001` rather than into an obvious error.

The handler *does* write **`rec{N}_email_sent = true`** after a successful draft. **That column existed, was written, and was read by nothing** — no caller, no sweep, no webhook branch consulted it before invoking the chain. As of 2026-08-04 the MAP 1 P2-4 webhook branch reads it (#327); no other caller does.

Two consequences. (1) **When you need "has this receipt already gone out?", the answer is `rec{N}_email_sent`, never the presence of `rec{N}_number`** — the number is set early and survives failures. (2) A duplicate run also leaves a **real orphan in Drive**: the first run's PDF is uploaded and its `drive_id` is then overwritten by the second run's, so the original file stays in the folder with nothing pointing at it. One such orphan (`REC-59380-001-0001.pdf`) is known and was left in place.

Cross-ref **#327** (the redelivery that exposed this), **#181**. `see #328`.

---

**329. Accountant member types are PARTNERSHIP-QUALIFIED — `"Implementation - VFO FT (Direct)"` / `"(Advisor)"`. Plain `"Implementation"` is an ADVISOR-pipeline value and was copied into the accountant pipeline when it was cloned, so the first automation-created accountant was born with the wrong type (2026-08-04). ALWAYS-APPLIES — accountant pipeline.** `actions/accountant/create-member.ts` hardcoded `member_type: "Implementation"` and **never read the Direct/Advisor choice the onboarding had already recorded**. The value is legitimate — in the **advisor** pipeline, which the accountant pipeline was cloned from. In the accountant pipeline it is simply not one of the options an admin can pick, so every accountant the automation created carried a type no human would have chosen.

**The mapping is the same one `save-partnership.ts` and the agreement-PDF picker already use:** `ob.accountant_partnership === "Accountant Partnership"` → **`"Implementation - VFO FT (Advisor)"`**, anything else → **`"Implementation - VFO FT (Direct)"`**. (The dropdown's stored value says "Partnership" where the UI says "Advisor" — see #58; do not "tidy" the stored string.) **VFO Associate accountants are deliberately NOT mapped** — that option does not exist yet, and a comment in the file marks the spot.

**One row shipped wrong and was data-fixed, not migrated:** **Anil Grandhi, member 30006**, the first-ever automation-created accountant (born 2026-07-14), was corrected by superadmin to `'Implementation - VFO FT (Direct)'` with a matching `member_type_history` audit row. The in-flight onboardings — Kevin Johnson 16, Cordelia Ekwueme 17, Ade Rogers 21 (already paid), Doug Moini 23, all Advisor-partnership — will now be created correctly.

**The durable lesson is about clones, not about accountants.** The accountant pipeline is a file-for-file copy of the advisor pipeline, so any advisor-shaped literal in it is suspect until checked against the accountant enum: member types, agreement templates, email pipelines, dropdown values. When a cloned handler writes a *constant* where the original wrote a constant, confirm the constant is legal in the new pipeline's vocabulary.

Cross-ref **#58** (the same `accountant_partnership` value picking the BoldSign template + the agreement PDF), **#291**. `see #329`.

---

**330. A MANUAL resend is deliberately not the same event as an automatic one — it mints a fresh token, restarts the ladder, and never counts toward the auto-resend cap; and it stamps the reminder timestamp only-if-null and only-on-success (2026-08-04).** The Accounting → Outstanding Payment Links panel gained two "Resend email" buttons (Holistic + Tax tabs only — **not** on the tax implementation-links, specrev, or membership-fee sections), and their semantics are load-bearing:

- **Payment Continuation rows** reuse `migration_send_setup_link` with the new optional **`body.reminder: true`**, which swaps the template to `CLIENT_PAYMENT_CONTINUATION` / **`setup_link_reminder`** (id 209, "Friendly reminder:"). The default path is byte-identical to before. A manual mint keeps the **operator's** email in the token's `created_by` (not `"sweep"`), which is exactly why it **never counts toward the sweep's 3-auto-resend cap** — and because the token is fresh (7 days), the reminder/bell ladder **restarts** on it. So an expired link and a cap-reached row are precisely the rows the button exists for. Note this path stays **SUPERADMIN-only** — it mints a Stripe/token artifact, not just an email.
- **New Clients First Payment Link rows** use the NEW `accounting_resend_first_payment_link` (`TAB_ACTIONS.accounting` — any admin with the Accounting tab, same gate as the loader that renders the row). It re-runs the nightly reminder for ONE row by calling the sweeps' **own** senders (`sendStallReminderEmail` + `buttonsPay1Payment` for MAP 1, `sendReminderEmailUnified` + `buttonsTax3Payment` for Tax, all newly `export`ed with no restructuring), so the email is byte-identical to what the sweep would have produced — same template, same member-pays variant, same sandbox redirect. **Do not fork those senders.**

**The stamping rule is the subtle part.** `pay1_reminder_sent_at` / `payment_reminder_sent_at` is written **only when currently null** and **only after a draft actually succeeded**. Only-if-null means a manual nudge suppresses the sweep's imminent duplicate without rewriting the real first-reminder date; only-on-success means a failed draft leaves the row untouched so the nightly sweep still tries — stamping a reminder that never went out would silently disarm it. **`*_pf_notified_at` is never touched**: the PF escalation ladder keeps its own clock.

Both handlers refuse with **400** once the payment is no longer outstanding (`pay1_status` / `retainer_status` non-null) or when the row has no `checkout_token`, rather than drafting a button-less email.

Cross-ref **#268** (never email a raw `connect.stripe.com` link — the durable `/payout-setup` token pattern), **#310**, **#309** (deny-by-omission: the new action is in a named gate on purpose), **#251** (sandbox is resolved per client inside the reused senders). `see #330`.

---

**331. `/tax-upload` is ONE page, ONE URL and ONE `clients.tax_upload_token` shared by THREE different email flows — so nothing on that page can be conditioned on "which email did they come from"; the "Or write an explanation" box is gated by the PUBLIC `vault_tax_upload_context`, whose payload is a single boolean that must never be widened (2026-08-04). ALWAYS-APPLIES — public token pages.** *[Annotated 2026-08-07 (v709/v711): the THREE emails are now TWO — the first-payment email stopped carrying a `/tax-upload` link entirely (**#341**). Everything below still applies, and applies with MORE force to the links already sent: `clients.tax_upload_token` is durable, so old first-payment emails still resolve to this page and still cannot be told apart from the other flows. That is precisely why the Holistic tax-returns stamp is REQUEST-GATED (**#340**).]* The Request Tax Returns email, the Request Additional Information email and the first-payment email all embed the **same** `/tax-upload?token=` link, because `clients.tax_upload_token` is one durable per-client value, not a per-request one. There is no per-flow token, no distinguishing query param, and a referrer cannot be trusted — **so "show this section only for flow X" is not implementable on the client at all.** From v680 to v699 the page simply rendered both sections to everyone, and a client who arrived from the returns-only or first-payment email could type an explanation, press Send, and receive `400 "No open request for additional information"` — a real dead end that existed for nine days before anyone framed it as one.

**The fix is a context call, and its shape is the point.** `actions/vault/tax-upload-context.ts` (`vault_tax_upload_context`, **PUBLIC**, registered in `PUBLIC_HANDLERS` immediately beside `vault_tax_text_submit`) takes `{ token }`, validates it against `clients.tax_upload_token` — **400** no token, **403 "Invalid link"** on a miss, 500 on a DB error (#187) — and returns **`{ success: true, allow_text: <bool> }`**. **That boolean is the ENTIRE payload and widening it is a security regression, not a feature.** This is an unauthenticated endpoint whose only credential is a value sitting in an old email: it must disclose one bit and nothing about the client, the plan, the request text, or how many plans exist. A future "while we're in here, also return the requested-info text so the page can show what was asked" is exactly the change this rule forbids. (Same confinement shape as `vault_tax_upload_url` / `vault_request_upload_url` — the token row is the whole credential and the body selects nothing; #309/#310.)

**Visibility is deliberately IDENTICAL to submittability, which means the predicate is written twice and both copies must move together.** The gate re-runs the exact query `vault_tax_text_submit` uses to decide whether to accept — newest `client_tax_plans` row with `additional_info_requested_at NOT NULL`, `order id desc limit 1`. **If you change one, change the other.** Diverge them and the box appears where Send 400s (the bug this closed) or hides where Send would have worked (worse — the client sees no way to answer and nothing logs it).

**Two consequences worth stating out loud.** (1) **The gate is server state, not the link, so every `/tax-upload` link already in a client's inbox self-corrects on load** — nothing needs re-issuing and no previously sent email became wrong. (2) **A client with an open additional-info request sees the box on ANY of their `/tax-upload` arrivals**, including re-opening a months-old Request Tax Returns email. That is deliberate: it is the same page reading the same client state, and a written answer is exactly as valid from that door as from the other one. Do not add per-flow logic to "fix" it — there is no per-flow signal to add it from.

On the react side, `allowText` **defaults to `false`** so the box cannot flash in and then vanish for a dropzone-only client, and the context `fetch` `.catch(() => {})`s — a context failure degrades to "no box", never to an error card over a page whose primary job (dropping files) still works.

Cross-ref **#308** (the box itself, and the shared `additional_info_received_at` stamp that makes an upload and a typed answer equivalent), **#310** (the `/vault-upload` clone that removed the box outright, for the related reason that a typed answer only means something where a step is waiting on it), **#309**, **#187**. `see #331`.
---

**332. A membership PAUSE is schedule-shape + `renewal_date` and NOTHING else — the plan never leaves `status='active'`, and the `kind='pause'` marker rows are invisible to every money consumer ONLY because all of them filter `kind='membership'` (2026-08-04). ALWAYS-APPLIES — money.** "Pause this member's payments for N months" could have been modelled as a plan status, and deliberately was not. A new status would have to be taught to the nightly charge pass, the renewal pass, the renewal-notice pass, the waive pass, terminate/cancel eligibility, the panel, the Payments page and every future reader of `member_payment_plans.status` — and every one of those is a place to get it wrong once. Instead `membership_pause` (AUTH, superadmin in-handler, months 1–12) changes exactly three things: **the member skips N months of charging, every remaining payment moves N months later, and the membership year ends N months later.** The plan stays `active` the entire time, so nothing keyed off `status` learns a new state.

**The vacated month slots get MARKER rows** on `member_payment_schedule` with **`kind='pause'`**, `status='waived'`, `amount_due` 0, labelled *"Paused — August 2026"* per month or as ONE combined *"Paused — Aug 2026 – Oct 2026 (3 mo)"* row (a single-month window reads *"Paused — Aug 2026 (1 mo)"* — that range-label edge case shipped wrong in v702 and was fixed in v703). **Those markers are safe for exactly one reason, and it is a reason that can be revoked by accident:** every money consumer filters `kind='membership'`. That was verified reader-by-reader before shipping — the charge pass, the renewal guard, the $0-waive pass, the invoice/receipt engine (four sites), terminate and cancel voids, `send-reminder`, `all-payments-load`, the Stripe webhook blocks and the confirmation email. **So the standing rule is: ANY NEW READER of `member_payment_schedule` MUST filter `kind` or consciously decide what `'pause'` means to it.** A new "total collected this year" query that forgets the filter does not crash — it silently counts a $0 row as a payment, which is the class of bug this whole design is trying to make impossible.

**Four mechanics carry invariants and are not free to reorder.** (1) **The shift is applied in DESCENDING `due_date` order**, because the duplicate-charge index is a partial unique on `(plan_id, due_date) WHERE kind='membership'` — moving the earliest row first would collide with the row still sitting where it is about to land (#315). Ascending order is a 23505 waiting for the second-longest plan. (2) **`period_label` is regenerated only when it still equals `periodLabel(old due)`** — an annotated label like *"August 2026 (includes 1-month catch-up)"*, or an annual *"Membership year 2026–2027"*, is preserved verbatim rather than overwritten with a bare month. (3) **`year_end` extends +N on ALL rows of the affected `year_start` group; `year_start` is NEVER touched** — the group key is what makes rows one membership year (#280), so moving it would split a year in half. (4) **`renewal_date` advances +N pinned to the 15th**, which is the whole reason **#235** (every row of a year strictly before its renewal) survives a pause: shift the rows without shifting the renewal and the last row of a long pause lands on or past it, permanently blocking the renewal guard.

**Two smaller decisions worth keeping.** `renewal_notice_for` is **deliberately left stale** so the 30-day notice pass re-arms and warns the member for the NEW renewal date (#333) — clearing it would have been the intuitive move and would have been wrong in the other direction only by luck. And `preview: true` runs the identical computation and **writes nothing**, which is what the panel auto-calls to render "Payments continue on X / Renewal date moves X → Y" — the preview and the commit cannot drift because they are one code path.

**Pauses stack** (a second pause re-runs the shift against the already-shifted schedule and writes a second `member_payment_plan_pauses` audit row), and when there are **no scheduled rows left to move** — an annual plan mid-year, or a monthly year already fully collected — the marker anchors at the OLD renewal date, `rows_shifted` is 0 and `resume_due_date` is NULL: that pause only extends the year. The audit table is **audit only**; nothing reads it to make a billing decision.

Cross-ref **#315** (never two membership rows on one date for a plan; the partial index), **#239** (why the index is partial in the first place), **#280** (`year_start`/`year_end` group semantics), **#316** (member-facing counts are in MONTHS), **#235** (the always-15th renewal rule the shift must not break). `see #332`.

---

**333. Membership renewal used to be entirely silent; the 30-day notice + meeting flow closes the WARNING gap only — `renewal_notice_for` is stamped ONLY after a successful draft, the meeting bell is cleared ONLY by the outcome handler, and `membership_plans_load` + `membership_renewal_meeting_outcome` are the TWO accounting-tab exceptions in an otherwise superadmin `membership_*` family (2026-08-04). ALWAYS-APPLIES.** Before v700 the sweep rolled a plan into its next membership year the moment `renewal_date` arrived — new ledger, next-year terms applied, charging resumed — with **no advance email of any kind**, and `auto_renew` defaults to `true` on every plan. The member's first signal that they had been committed to another year was the charge. That is what pass 0 fixes.

**The idempotency stamp is the load-bearing part.** `member_payment_plans.renewal_notice_for` holds the `renewal_date` the notice was last sent FOR, and is written **only after the Gmail draft actually succeeds**. A missing template, a token refresh failure, a member with no email — every one of those simply leaves the stamp alone and retries on tomorrow's sweep, and no plan can be double-noticed for one renewal. Stamping first ("mark it, then send") would have converted every transient Gmail hiccup into a permanently skipped notice. The re-notice predicate is `renewal_notice_for <> renewal_date`, which means the pass **re-arms automatically whenever the renewal date moves** — including after a pause (#332). The window is `(today, today+30]`, **strictly future**, so a plan that actually reaches its 15th is renewed by pass 1 rather than noticed by pass 0. That comparison lives in JS, not in the query, because PostgREST cannot compare two columns and NULL has to read as not-noticed.

**`[Renewal Terms]` is the renewal pass's arithmetic written a second time, and the two copies must move together.** The notice quotes `(next_year_amount ?? annual_amount) − (next_year_credit_note ?? 0)` — **exactly** what pass 1 will bill. An earlier draft of this pass computed it differently and was caught in review before deploy. Quoting a member a renewal price the system then does not charge is the worst outcome this feature has available to it; if you touch either formula, touch both.

**The meeting request is a token page with a one-bit payload (the #331 shape).** `member_payment_plans.renewal_meeting_token` is durable — **minted once per plan, reused by every subsequent notice, and persisted BEFORE the email is drafted** so a link that goes out always resolves — and it is the ENTIRE credential for the PUBLIC `membership_renewal_meeting_request`. The response is a bare `{ success }` and **must never be widened**: an unauthenticated endpoint whose only key sits in an old email must not disclose the member, the plan or the terms. It is **idempotent per `(plan_id, renewal_date_for)` open row**, so a double-click or a re-opened email renders the "already received" state instead of queueing a second meeting.

**The bell is action-required and has exactly one exit.** `MEMBERSHIP_renewal_meeting_requested` fires with `dismissible:false` to `rhopson@elitert.com` by default, linked to the member's Membership Fees card, and is cleared **only** by `membership_renewal_meeting_outcome` — on BOTH outcomes, matched on pipeline + `dismissible=false` + `read=false` + `link LIKE %member=N%`. `cancel` additionally flips `auto_renew=false` and drafts `MEMBERSHIP_cancel_confirmation` (id 213) inside a `try/catch`, because a Gmail failure must not lose the outcome that was already recorded.

**~~Two actions in the `membership_*` family deliberately have NO in-handler superadmin check~~ — SUPERSEDED 2026-08-07 (v707): the WHOLE `membership_*` family is now accounting-tab-gated with no in-handler superadmin check anywhere, and `MembershipFeesPanel`'s `isSuperadmin` prop is fed `canSeeTab('accounting')` rather than the superadmin flag, so a granted admin works EVERY money control. See #338 — the paragraph below is retained as the historical v700 shape, and its "do not restore the check" instruction now applies to the entire family rather than to two exceptions.** `membership_plans_load` (read-only; the in-handler check was REMOVED in v700, leaving `TAB_ACTIONS.accounting` as the whole gate, exactly like `accounting_outstanding_links_load`) and `membership_renewal_meeting_outcome` (records a conversation, moves no money). **Rachael Hopson runs the renewal meetings and is not a superadmin** — that is the entire reason, and it is commented at both sites in `role-gates.ts` and in `plans-load.ts`. Correspondingly the react panel now takes an `isSuperadmin` prop and renders **every money control** — create/setup, links, auto-renew, next-year terms, Pause, Terminate, cancel, resend/reminder — behind it, while the read-only cards, the schedule and the Renewal meeting section are visible to any accounting-tab admin. **Any new `membership_*` action that moves money or mints a Stripe artifact keeps its superadmin check.**

**Two operational facts that are easy to forget and both break the feature quietly.** (1) **Both templates are Draft mode** (ids 212/213) — a human must send each renewal notice out of Gmail Drafts; the sweep only prepares it. (2) **The emailed button is a live `vfoportal.com` URL, so the notice is only useful once the FRONTEND is deployed** — a notice sent ahead of `npm run deploy` lands the member on a 404.

**What this does NOT close: a plan with `auto_renew` OFF still lapses DARK.** It stays `status='active'`, the notice window filters on `auto_renew` so pass 0 skips it, no bell fires, and the schedule simply runs out — the member is never told and neither is an admin. That was found during this session's research, named, and deliberately left unbuilt pending a decision about what "lapsed" should mean.

Cross-ref **#331** (the one-bit public-token payload rule this reuses), **#332** (a pause re-arms the notice), **#309** (deny-by-omission — these two exceptions are gate-listed, not ungated), **#325** (the blast radius of flipping a template Draft → Send), **#334**, **#176**, **#179**. `see #333`.

---

**334. `MembershipFeesPanel`'s `run()` helper treats `res.ok` as the success flag, so a membership handler that returns `{ success: true }` reads as a FAILURE in the UI while its write has already committed (2026-08-04).** `membership_renewal_meeting_outcome` shipped in v700 returning `{ success: true }` — the shape most of the codebase's public token handlers use — and the panel's shared `run()` wrapper checks `res.ok`. The result during live testing was the worst possible presentation of a working feature: the admin clicked **Continue membership**, the outcome was stamped, `auto_renew` was updated, the bell was cleared, and the UI said the action failed. The obvious next move — click it again — is exactly what a mis-reported write invites. Fixed in v701 by returning `{ ok: true }`.

**The rule is not "always return `ok`" — it is "read the caller's success predicate before you choose a response shape".** This codebase genuinely uses both conventions in different places, and neither is wrong on its own; what is wrong is picking one without looking. When you add a handler for an existing panel, open that panel's `run()`/`callApi` wrapper first and match it. A response-shape mismatch is invisible to `deno check`, invisible to `npm run build`, and invisible to the smoke gate — it only shows up in a click-through, which is why the click-through is the gate that caught it.

Cross-ref **#333** (the handler this bit), **#144** (the other class of contract mismatch between `callApi` and a handler's response). `see #334`.

---

**335. Payment Continuation had ZERO strategic-partner awareness, so a legacy client of a STRATEGIC member migrated in would silently pay the partner's 10% share to VFO on every remaining installment — and the failure was invisible BY CONSTRUCTION: a NULL `strategic_partner_share` produces no bell, no `'Failed'` row and nothing for either sweep to retry (2026-08-05). ALWAYS-APPLIES — money.** The native MAP 1 and Tax pricing forms have understood strategic members since 2026-07-01 (`src/lib/strategicSplits.js`), but the Payment Continuation migration tool — a completely separate write path into the same two tables — never learned. It offered a two-way (MAP 1) or three-way (tax) split, stored `strategic_partner_share` as NULL, and the engine then charged the rest of the plan with the partner's cut folded into VFO's.

**The shape of the silence is the part to internalise.** `contract-revshare.ts` gates the strategic leg on a stored share **> 0**, so NULL does not read as "broken" — it reads as "this member has no partner". Both sweeps' strategic retries select only rows already stamped **`'Failed'`**, so a leg that never *started* is not a leg they can enumerate. **There is no bell, no error status and no log line anywhere in the system for this; the only evidence is a partner who was never paid.** Two live rows had already shipped this way — `pipeline_map1` **128** (client 146 Deanna Haines, member 20028) and **129** (client 147 Steve Bitzer, member 20003), both Action Coach — entered by the admin as a plain 50/50 ($2,700/$2,700 of a $5,400 net) and corrected on 2026-08-05 to **540 / 2430 / 2430**.

**The rule for any future writer of a continuation row — or any other tool that creates a MAP 1 / tax row outside the native pricing forms — is three parts and all three are load-bearing.** (1) **Detect strategic**: `clients.member_category === 'strategic_member'` **AND** `hasStrategicSplit(client.member_type)` — both fields are already injected by `msm_load_client_home`, so no extra query is needed. (2) **Store `strategic_partner_share`.** (3) **Pre-settle the legs that were collected on the OLD system.** Skip any one and the partner is silently over- or under-paid. The FE now auto-computes the split **read-only** off `strategicSplits.js`: MAP 1 off the **net invoice** (the continuation form has no `gross_fee` / `member_contribution` inputs, so net IS the gross the fractions apply to), and tax off the **WHOLE fee** (retainer + implementation) then prorated down to the implementation leg by `implAmount / taxTotal`, with **VFOS absorbing the rounding** so the four boxes sum to the implementation amount exactly.

**The pre-settle stamps exist for the #277 reason and must not be dropped as "cosmetic".** `backfill-map1.ts` writes `rec{i}_strat_paid = 'N/A — No Share Due'` + `rec{i}_strat_completed_at` on every installment marked already-paid, and `backfill-tax.ts` writes `retainer_strat_paid` + `retainer_strat_completed_at` — because that money was taken on the old system and the nightly sweep would otherwise transfer the partner a cut of a payment this system never collected. Sweep-inertness was verified reader-by-reader before shipping: the contract-revshare sweep's strategic retry filters `='Failed'`, and the main loop treats `'N/A — No Share Due'` as resolved.

**Writing an explicit NULL on absence is deliberate, and it leaves exactly one open decision.** When no strategic share is submitted the backfills store `strategic_partner_share = NULL` **explicitly** rather than omitting the key from the payload — a non-strategic force-overwrite of a row that already carried a live strategic share must not leave that share behind with no pre-settle stamps, which is the phantom-payout shape. **What is NOT handled and is a known open decision:** force-overwriting an **ORGANIC** (never-migrated) MAP 1 row leaves the *unpaid* installments' stale `rec{i}_strat_paid` values in place, so a stale `'Yes'` would suppress a legitimate future strategic payout. That is identical to the pre-existing `rec{i}_rev_paid` handling and sits behind the force-overwrite acknowledgement — recorded here rather than fixed.

**`split_type` is forced on the SERVER, not just in the UI.** A strategic tax continuation stores `split_type = 'Strategic Partner'` — the exact value the native `TaxPrioritiesTab` stamps — set inside `backfill-tax.ts`, so a direct API caller cannot store a strategic row carrying a stale `1/3 …` / `Custom` split type. Note the counterpart limitation: the split *config* (Action Coach 10/45/45 holistic and 10/30/30/30 tax; Tax Plan IQ 10/40/50 and 10/50/20/20) lives **only** in frontend `strategicSplits.js` and the backend stores whatever dollars are submitted — a direct API caller could store a non-rule split. That is a pre-existing property of the native flow too, not something this change introduced.

**Two display facts.** `Map1PricingSplitCard` now reads the strategic row's **own** per-installment status `rec{i}_strat_paid` (it was hardcoded to the member's `rec{i}_rev_paid`), so a migrated strategic engagement shows *"settled on old system"* under the strategic cells as well. And the tax strategic UI only renders once `implementation_amount > 0` (a pre-existing gate), so a **retainer-only** strategic migration stores no strategic share at all — consistent with "the retainer pays out nothing here", but worth knowing before assuming every strategic tax row has one.

Cross-ref **#277** (an old-system payment must be pre-settled, and the dollars-of-total scaling the tax backfill reuses), **#252** (shares are dollars-of-total, never percentages), **#230** (the `legacy_source='old-system'` collision guard + `force`), **#303** (never leave a payout leg's status defaulted to a terminal value), **#304**, **#285**. `see #335`.

**336. The “Generate and download presentation” ROI deck generator is a chain of exact-string and exact-id contracts — the VERSIONED bucket object, the 31 tokens, the appendix slides whose FILENAMES are not their POSITIONS, the two REQUIRED-in-lockstep validations, the step ids/sentinel, and a done-state that writes NO progress row; break any link and the failure is either a 400 the admin cannot interpret or a deck that silently renders wrong numbers (2026-08-06, v706 — **AMENDED IN PLACE 2026-08-07 for the TWO-YEAR v2 rewrite, v713, AGAIN 2026-08-12 for v3 / v729, AGAIN 2026-08-17 for the FOUR-TOTAL assess form + the ALWAYS-single-year deck, AGAIN 2026-08-18 for TEMPLATE v4 + the restored two-year mode + the generation-time slide-24 surgery, v759, AGAIN 2026-08-19 for TEMPLATE v5 — the 22nd token, the FIRST one outside `ppt/slides/`, plus TWO same-name hot-swaps on the same day, v761 — and AGAIN 2026-08-27 for TEMPLATE v6 + THE REVISED TAX FEE PROCESS: 22 → 31 tokens, `{{FEE_HALF}}` RETIRED, seven changed parts, and a SECOND independent generation-time mode, v795; the superseded numbers were REPLACED each time and this gotcha was deliberately NOT renumbered**). ALWAYS-APPLIES (tax track).** **See also #374 — the generated deck is 27 OR 29 slides, decided solely by whether `assess_form.year2` is present — and #453, the composition rule the two generation-time surgeries now depend on.** `tax_generate_presentation` (`actions/tax/generate-presentation.ts`, AUTH, **`ADMIN_ONLY_ACTIONS`**, deliberately **NOT** in `TAX_PLANNER_ALLOWED_ACTIONS` — the deck is an admin deliverable) sits on “Tax 2 - Deeper Dive” in **both** tax programs.

**The template contract, and the VERSIONED object name that makes deploys safe.** The master lives in the **private `presentation-templates` bucket** (50 MB, **no `storage.objects` policies at all** — service-role only, no upload path in code) under **exactly `ROI-template-master-v6.pptx`** (since v795, 2026-08-27; it was `…-v5.pptx` from v761/2026-08-19, `…-v4.pptx` from v759/2026-08-18 and `…-v3.pptx` from v729/2026-08-12), replaced **by a human** — through the Supabase Dashboard, or via the CLI caveat in the ENVIRONMENT GOTCHAS list. **The object name is VERSIONED ON PURPOSE.** `TEMPLATE_OBJECT` and `SLIDE_PARTS` live in the deployed function, so overwriting the object a live function still reads breaks every generation in the window between upload and deploy. Giving each revision a new name gives the deploy a **zero-breakage window** — the old function keeps reading the old object until the switch, which is atomic at deploy — and **every superseded object is deliberately RETAINED in the bucket as the rollback** (v1 and v2 both still sit there). Do not delete them and do not rename in place. **A HOT-SWAP has no such protection and must make its own** — overwriting the live object destroys the only copy of what it replaced, because retention covers superseded *versions*, not the current version's pre-edit content. The 2026-08-17 notes swap therefore uploaded **`ROI-template-master-v3-pre-notes-2026-08-17.pptx`** (the byte-identical pre-edit master) before overwriting `ROI-template-master-v3.pptx`; that object is the rollback for the notes change and must not be deleted. Give every future hot-swap the same dated backup, and see **#409** for why the overwrite itself cannot be a plain `supabase storage cp`. **v3 (2026-08-12) followed exactly this path**: upload first, then deploy v729. It differs from v2 in **only two parts** — `slide24.xml` (title *"Your investment – "* → *"Your investment: "*, and the `{{YEAR}} + {{YEAR2}}` run collapsed to the single new token `{{INVEST_YEARS}}`) and `slide26.xml` (the two guarantee paragraphs lose the blue bullet: `buNone` + flush-left, text bytes untouched, the checkmarks being literal text and unchanged).

**v4 (2026-08-18, v759) IS THE CASE THAT TEACHES WHAT THE VERSIONED-OBJECT RULE IS ACTUALLY KEYED ON: it has NO CONTENT DELTA AT ALL.** `ROI-template-master-v4.pptx` is **BYTE-IDENTICAL** to the dated backup `ROI-template-master-v3-pre-shift-2026-08-17.pptx` — SHA256 `319593587028fdad36429e3349819ddb132707462676a3722d8cfc54e8d1385b`, 27,614,869 bytes, same 570 entries / 29 slides / 27 notes parts / **21 tokens**. Not "similar", not "rebuilt to match": the same bytes under a new key, produced by `shutil.copyfile`. **So why a new version at all?** Because the rule is keyed on the delta **relative to the object the LIVE FUNCTION READS**, not relative to whatever a script last built. The second 2026-08-17 hot swap had DELETED slide 24's `{{INVEST_YEARS}}` header from the live `…-v3.pptx`; restoring that header is therefore a **token-SITE ADDITION** against the live object — squarely the versioned path — even though, measured against a backup sitting in the same bucket, nothing changed. **Uploaded BEFORE the v759 deploy, with v3 retained as the rollback**, exactly like every version before it. The lesson to carry: *always diff against the live object, never against your build artifact.*

**`scripts/roi-presentation/make_v4.py` (EDGE repo) is the canonical record of that, and it is a VERIFIER, not an editor.** It asserts the base's SHA256 against a baked constant before stamping a new name on it — *"do NOT stamp a v4 name on unknown bytes"* — then asserts 570 entries / 29 slides / 27 notes parts / 21 distinct tokens, `{{INVEST_YEARS}}` present on slide 24 (i.e. you cut v4 from the PRE-shift backup, not the post-shift live object), `{{PLANNING_FEE}}` present, and — the tripwire that matters most — **neither `{{PLANNING_FEE_Y1}}` nor `{{PLANNING_FEE_Y2}}` anywhere.** A per-year fee token was designed, built and **reverted on user direction** during the same session that shipped v4; that assert is what stops it returning by accident. It then `shutil.copyfile`s rather than repacking, deliberately, so the one property that makes this version trivially auditable survives: **v4 IS the pre-shift backup.** On a genuine template rebuild its baked `BASE_SHA256` **will not match, and that is correct** — re-derive it, and re-derive the handler's slide-24 shift constants with it.

**v5 (2026-08-19, v761) IS THE FIRST VERSION WHOSE TOKEN SET LEAVES `ppt/slides/` — AND IT REACHED ITS LIVE CONTENT IN THREE STAGES ON ONE DAY, ONLY THE FIRST OF WHICH TOOK THE VERSIONED PATH.** **Stage 1 — `ROI-template-master-v5.pptx`, built from v4 by `scripts/roi-presentation/make_v5.py` and uploaded BEFORE the v761 deploy.** Two edits: (i) slides **7–13**'s two-run title `TAX` + ` Proactive Planning ` became `VFO` + ` Tax Planning Process `, with the two-run structure and **both `rPr` blocks untouched** so the `accent2` first word survives, and (ii) a `{{PLANNER_NOTES}}` paragraph appended to **`ppt/notesSlides/notesSlide23.xml`** — the **22nd token and the first one that has ever lived outside `ppt/slides/`**. Half (i) alone would have been a hot swap (seven token-free slides, pure wording); **the new token SITE is the entire reason v5 needed a new name and a lockstep deploy.** **Stage 2 — a same-name HOT SWAP, backup `ROI-template-master-v5-pre-trim-2026-08-19.pptx`:** slide 23's speaker notes lost their six-line label list (*"Total Taxes - No Planning"* … *"Total Net Tax Benefit"*) and the existing empty spacer paragraph moved from **before** to **after** the *"Let's break that down…"* line, so the rendered layout is script line → blank line → planner notes. **Stage 3 — a second same-name HOT SWAP, backup `ROI-template-master-v5-pre-agenda-2026-08-19.pptx`:** slide 3's agenda item *"Tax Proactive Planning Process"* → *"VFO Tax Planning Process"*, a single run on a token-free slide. Both hot swaps were **rename-first** (rename the live object to its dated backup name, then upload the replacement), so the bucket now holds **NINE** ROI objects and each backup is precisely the rollback for the swap it is named after.

**STAGE 2 IS THE INSTRUCTIVE ONE: A HOT SWAP INSIDE A TOKEN-BEARING PART IS LEGAL — BUT ONLY WHILE THE TOKEN SITE STAYS BYTE-INTACT.** `notesSlide23.xml` is now a part the handler opens, patches and sweeps, so the reflex reading of the rule ("it carries a token, therefore versioned path") would have forced a v6. It did not, and the reason is the same one the rule has always rested on: **the versioned path is keyed on whether the CODE's contract with the file moves.** The handler's contract with that part is exactly one literal — `<a:t>{{PLANNER_NOTES}}</a:t></a:r>` — plus the `<a:r>` that opens it; stage 2 deleted six sibling paragraphs and moved a seventh **without touching a byte of that run**, so every index walk, assertion and guard behaves identically before and after. The test to apply is therefore **not** "is this part in `TOKEN_PARTS`?" but **"does this edit move, duplicate, restyle or delete the token's own run?"** — and because that is a much finer distinction than a filename, **prove it by diffing the token's run out of both packages**, do not eyeball it. The `expected exactly 1 run carrying {{PLANNER_NOTES}}, found N` 500 is the net if you get it wrong, which is a loud failure rather than a silent one — but it fires at generation time, on a real client's deck.

**`scripts/roi-presentation/make_v5.py` (EDGE repo) REPRODUCES STAGE 1 ONLY.** Run it against v4 and you get the deck as it was uploaded, not the deck the bucket holds now: **live v5 = `make_v5.py` output + the two recorded hot swaps**, and the two dated backups are what let you reconstruct any of the three stages. It verifies its base's SHA256 against v4's baked constant (`3195935870…`) before stamping a v5 name on unknown bytes, asserts **570 entries / 29 slides / 27 notes parts / 21 tokens in, 22 out**, matches the title edit on the **whole run PAIR and never the bare word** (every one of these slides also carries `<a:t> TAX PLANNING STEPS</a:t>`, and slides 8–13 carry `<a:t>Proactive Facilitator Establishes</a:t>` — a naive `replace("TAX","VFO")` wrecks both, and the script asserts they survive), proves the notes part's NAME rather than assuming it (`ppt/slides/_rels/slide23.xml.rels` must target `../notesSlides/notesSlide23.xml`, since a rebuild can renumber notes parts independently of slides), and asserts that **exactly those 8 parts differ**. Unlike `make_v4.py` it repacks rather than copying, preserving entry order and per-entry `compress_type`.

**v6 (2026-08-27, v795) IS THE VERSION WHERE THE DECK STOPPED HARD-CODING A MONEY SHAPE — AND THE FIRST TO ADD A SECOND, INDEPENDENT GENERATION-TIME MODE.** The template used to bake in ONE fee shape: a *"One-Time Fee"* with two 50% rows under it, both filled from the single `{{FEE_HALF}}` token. The revised tax fee process (2026-08-25, `flows/tax-fee-process.md`) gave an engagement **two** shapes — 2-payment at or below $30,000, 3-payment above it — and a template cannot branch. So v6 stops NAMING the rows and starts TOKENISING them. **SEVEN parts differ from v5 and nothing else does: slides 24, 26, 27, 28, 29 + `notesSlide26` + `notesSlide27`.** (i) *"One-Time Fee:"* → *"Total Fee:"* on 24/28/29 — the label was THREE runs (`One` / `-` / `Time Fee:`, the hyphen carrying its own `rPr`), collapsed into ONE run reusing run 1's `rPr` verbatim; **slide 29 gets the LABEL AND NOTHING ELSE** (it has no fee rows and its value stays the literal `"$0"` — there is no `PLANNING_FEE_Y2` and there must not be one). (ii) The two 50% rows on 24/28 became `{{FEE_ROW1_*}}`/`{{FEE_ROW2_*}}` and a **THIRD row was cloned one pitch (317400 EMU) lower** as `{{FEE_ROW3_*}}`; the label shapes' two runs collapse to one and the **colon moved INTO the token's value** (*"Retainer:"*, *"Initial Retainer:"*), while the value shapes keep their separate `"$"` run — **v8 (2026-09-09) later MERGED each value shape into its label shape and deleted it, so a fee row is now ONE text box; see #480.** (iii) **Slide 24's below-section moved DOWN 317400 EMU** to clear the new row — shape 1528 (*"Your investment in VFO Specialists:"*) and shape 1529 (*"Plan Investment: ${{CASH_OUTLAY}}"*, **whose text lives in an `<a:t xml:space="preserve">` run that a naive `<a:t>` scan misses**); **SLIDE 28 WAS NOT SHIFTED** — its rows sit higher and row 3 fit with 165000 EMU of clearance. (iv) Slide 27's three chips tokenised (`CHIP1_LABEL`/`CHIP1_VALUE`, `CHIP2_TEXT`, `CHIP3_VALUE`) with the literal `"50% "` prefixes gone — **chip 3's was a whole PARAGRAPH, deleted whole**, which is why chip 3's text now sits ~5.3pt higher than chip 1's first line; chip 1's amount lives in the SAME shape as its label, three centred paragraphs. (v) Slide 26's Double-Guarantee drops *"50% "*. (vi)+(vii) **The two SPEAKER SCRIPTS for slides 26 and 27 were de-percented too** — *"the initial 50% deposit"* → *"the initial deposit"* (a **deletion**), and *"an initial invoice of 50% tax planning retainer"* → *"…of THE tax planning retainer"* (a **REPLACEMENT**, because a bare deletion is ungrammatical and a presenter reads the line aloud). Neither notes part carries a token, so `notesSlide23.xml` remains the ONLY tokenised one and `TOKEN_PARTS` is unchanged. **`{{FEE_HALF}}` is RETIRED — and so is the STRING `"50%"`, package-wide:** `make_v6.py` asserts a count of **ZERO across all 570 entries**, slides and notes alike, which is a far stronger tripwire than "no 50% on the slides I edited"; `NOTES_FIFTY_SURVIVORS` is an **empty named dict** so a future deliberate exception has a documented home rather than an edit to the assert. **v6 took the versioned path for the least ambiguous reason yet — it both ADDS nine token sites and RETIRES one** — uploaded before the v795 deploy, v5 retained as the rollback. `make_v6.py` takes the **LIVE v5 object** as its base (`BASE_SHA256 = e479ad2a1b1b699315ca08757130958729bf06abbfca2e8fb4b5cdc470411d25`), so it already includes v5's two hot swaps; v6's own SHA256 is `1d8995d2b7dcf1054532295cdb1f75015332b8575637772bc9a0ce7fc89cd901`.

**⚠ A DECK THAT MIRRORS A MONEY PROCESS MUST IMPORT THAT PROCESS'S CONSTANTS, NOT RE-TYPE THEM — see #453.** `generate-presentation.ts` imports `THREE_PAYMENT_THRESHOLD` and `INITIAL_RETAINER_FLAT` from `constants/tax-fee-process.ts`, so the deck and the money can never disagree about where 2 payments become 3. The split is nonetheless computed **LOCALLY** rather than by calling `deriveFeeSplit()` — that function THROWS outside $0–$60,000 and a legacy plan with an out-of-band assess fee must still generate a deck — with the sum identity asserted in code. **The branch is `feeCents > THREE_PAYMENT_THRESHOLD * 100`, STRICT: exactly $30,000 stays 2-payment.** Two further rules, both deliberate: **the ASSESS FEE is the only signal and has to be** (the deck is generated on *"Tax 2 - Deeper Dive"*, upstream of every fee-process write, so `isThreePaymentPlan()` would answer false on every plan — do not "improve" this into a column read); and **the deck ALWAYS quotes the flat $15,000 initial retainer** — the 2026-08-26 custom-initial-retainer allowlist is deliberately NOT wired in (USER DECISION), because the deck predates the custom quote and the client sees their real figures on the agreement and the invoice.

**THE SINGLE-YEAR LAYOUT MOVED FROM THE MASTER INTO THE HANDLER — AN INVERSION, AND THE CONSTANTS ARE COUPLED TO THE TEMPLATE VERSION (v4 through v8).** Because v4 carries the header, `dropInvestYearsHeader()` in `generate-presentation.ts` now reproduces at generation time the static edit that used to live in the master: delete the **one** `<p:sp>` containing `{{INVEST_YEARS}}`, then shift every remaining `<p:sp>` whose `<a:off>` has **`x < SHIFT_MAX_X` (4800000)** and **`y >= SHIFT_MIN_Y` (1773000)** up by **`HEADER_SHIFT_EMU` (539650)**, expecting **`SHIFT_EXPECTED_2PAY` = 9 or `SHIFT_EXPECTED_3PAY` = 10** shapes — **as of v6 that is a PAIR, not one constant, and the count depends on the FEE MODE**, because a 2-payment deck has already had row 3 deleted by the time this runs. **Both numbers fell by four at template v8 (12 → 9, 14 → 10), when the four fee VALUE shapes in slide 24's left column were merged into their labels and deleted (#480).** The expected count is **PASSED IN by the caller**, never derived here, precisely so the two surgeries' assertions stay independent (#453). The `x` predicate is what protects the right-hand "Net Tax Savings" panel, which starts at `x=4876920` and sits ABOVE the deleted line — it must not move. **Both counts are asserted and a mismatch is a loud 500**, so a template rebuild that nudges a shape fails visibly instead of shipping a deck with a hole in it. It runs on the **RAW xml BEFORE substitution**, because the shape is located by the literal token substitution is about to erase — reorder those two steps and the deletion silently finds nothing. **These four numbers were measured off v4's `slide24.xml` and byte-verified to reproduce the old live master's `slide24.xml` exactly; NOTHING ELSE IN THE PACKAGE PINS THEM, so a future template rebuild MUST re-derive them.**

**THE HOT-SWAP RULE — which of the two ship paths a deck change takes is decided by WHAT IT TOUCHES, not by how big it looks.** A change confined to **token-free** slides (styling, layout, colour, bullet specs, wording on a slide carrying no `{{TOKEN}}`) ships by **replacing the SAME bucket object: no code change, no deploy, no version bump**, picked up on the very next generation. A change to **any token, the slide count, a part filename, or `<p:sldIdLst>` order** requires a **NEW versioned object name PLUS a lockstep backend deploy** — upload first, then deploy the function that names it. The 2026-08-07 restyles took the first path (proven character-identical in every text run, so nothing the filler substitutes could move); the two-year rewrite took the second. **SPEAKER NOTES USED TO BE THE CLEANEST FIRST-PATH CASE, AND AS OF v5 THAT IS TRUE OF EVERY NOTES PART BUT ONE — READ THE EXCEPTION BEFORE YOU TRUST THE RULE (2026-08-17, AMENDED 2026-08-19).** Notes live in their OWN parts — `ppt/notesSlides/notesSlideN.xml`, reached from `ppt/slides/_rels/slideN.xml.rels` — and **26 of the 27 are still exactly as described**: not in `SLIDE_PARTS`, never opened by the handler, carrying **zero** `{{TOKEN}}`s even for slides that are full of them, so rewriting one of those notes is invisible to substitution and to the leftover-token guard — hot-swap the same object, no code change, no deploy, live on the next generation. **`notesSlide23.xml` is the exception and has been since v5**: it carries `{{PLANNER_NOTES}}`, it IS opened, patched and swept (it is `NOTES_PART`, the second half of `TOKEN_PARTS`), and a change to it is a first-path hot swap **only while the token's own `<a:r>` run survives byte-intact** — see the stage-2 paragraph above, which is exactly that case done deliberately. **Do not generalise "notes are always a hot swap" from the 26; check which part you are editing.** The 2026-08-17 pass replaced the hard-typed example figures in the notes of **file `slide24.xml` (Return On Investment) and `slide25.xml` (Choose Who You Pay)** with `$xxx,xxx` / `$xx,xxx` / `xx%` placeholders — verified afterwards by re-checking that the repacked master still had 570 entries, 29 slides, 27 notes parts and all 40 tokens across the 8 token slides. **Mind the numbering trap even here:** those are FILE names; in the 29-slide master, display position 25 is `slide28.xml` (the year-1 appendix), not `slide25.xml`.

**To change the deck:** edit a COPY in Google Slides → `File > Download as .pptx` → REBUILD through `scripts/roi-presentation/` in the EDGE repo → re-upload. The lineage is **`build_master.py` (v1 tokenizer) → clone step → `edit_v2.py` → `revise_v2.py` (v2: 29 slides, 20 tokens) → `restyle_23.py` (slides 2/3 restyle) → `restyle_bullets.py` (one canonical blue bullet across display 3/4/28) → `make_v3.py` (v3: the slide-24 `INVEST_YEARS` title swap + the slide-26 guarantee de-bulleting, every edit expect-asserted against the v2 package) → `make_v4.py` (v4: verify-and-copy only) → `make_v5.py` (v5: the slides 7–13 retitle + the `{{PLANNER_NOTES}}` paragraph — **STAGE 1 ONLY**; the two 2026-08-19 hot swaps must be re-applied by hand from their dated backups) → `make_v6.py` (v6: the seven fee-process edits, built from the LIVE v5 object so those hot swaps are already in its base)**, and the **restyle scripts are NOT optional on a rebuild** — a fresh export carries the OLD styling, so skipping them silently reverts the look while still generating perfectly correct numbers. **Never hand-patch the existing master.** Every Google Slides re-export shifts the paragraph/run indices the scripts edit by, which is precisely why each asserts the expected old text (or shape/run count) at every edit site — a loud failure there means re-derive the coordinates, not skip the rebuild. `scripts/roi-presentation/fill_deck.py` is the Python reference implementation the TS mirrors (and where the arrow EMU values were measured); none of these scripts runs in production. **As of v6 `fill_deck.py` is KNOWINGLY STALE** — it predates both the planner-notes token and the fee tokens and substitutes only 20 of the 31, still knowing `FEE_HALF`, which no longer exists. Treat it as the arrow-EMU record and the original reference, not as a current mirror.

**The token contract — 22 tokens in the handler as of 2026-08-19, every one with a site in the master, and ONE of them is not on a slide at all.** `INVEST_YEARS`'s slide-24 text box, deleted from the live object by the second 2026-08-17 hot swap, is **back in template v4** — the deck is two-year again and a two-year deck must be able to say *"Your investment: 2026 + 2027"*. **Single-year decks now delete that box at GENERATION time instead** (see the slide-24 surgery below), which is a full inversion of the 2026-08-17 arrangement: what used to be baked into the master is now done dynamically, and what used to be dynamic (the header) is now static. The 2026-08-17 note that the token was "computed but dead" is **HISTORY — do not carry it forward.** The reasoning it rested on is still sound and still worth keeping, though: substitution is `split`/`join`, so a token with no site is a silent no-op, and the leftover-token guard only fires on a token left **un**replaced — never on a value computed but unused. That is why **token REMOVAL is the exception to the versioned-object rule below, while ADDING or RENAMING a token is not** — and it is also why restoring a token's SITE counts as an ADDITION: **relative to the object the live function actually reads**, v4 gives `INVEST_YEARS` somewhere to land that v3 did not, so v4 took the versioned path even though (see below) its bytes are not new at all. The v1 thirteen — `CLIENT_NAME`, `YEAR`, `RISK_NUM`, `RISK_LABEL`, `TAXES_WITHOUT`, `TAXES_WITH`, `GROSS_BENEFIT`, `CASH_OUTLAY`, `PLANNING_FEE`, `NET_BENEFIT`, `OUTLAY_PLUS_FEE`, `FEE_HALF`, `PCT_REDUCED` — **plus the seven the two-year rewrite added: `YEAR2`, `GROSS_Y1`, `GROSS_Y2`, `INVEST_Y1`, `INVEST_Y2`, `NET_Y1`, `NET_Y2` — plus the ONE v3 added: `INVEST_YEARS`** (slide 24's title years; `YEAR`/`YEAR2` survive but now appear only on the appendix slides) **plus the ONE v5 added: `PLANNER_NOTES`** — the odd one out in every respect: it lives in **`ppt/notesSlides/notesSlide23.xml`**, not on a slide; its value is **free text**, not money; it may be **EMPTY**; it may be **MULTILINE**; and it is the ONE token that does **not** go through the generic substitution loop (see `substitutePlannerNotes` below). `SLIDE_PARTS` is still **`[1, 20, 23, 24, 25, 27, 28, 29]`**, but **the constant that now governs substitution is `TOKEN_PARTS` = `SLIDE_PARTS` + `NOTES_PART`, and all THREE of its uses must stay on it** — the patch loop, the missing-part 500, and the leftover-token guard. A part that is patched but not guarded can ship a deck with a raw `{{TOKEN}}` visible on it; a part guarded but not patched 500s every generation. Values are **XML-escaped** before they touch any part. Adding, renaming or dropping a token means changing `edit_v2.py` / `make_v5.py` (and `build_master.py` if the site is a v1 slide) **and** the TS handler together — **and it puts the change on the versioned-object path above.** **Formulas, PER-YEAR as of 2026-08-18 and still INVERTED at the top:** year 1 derives from the flat totals **`W1`=`taxes_without_plan`, `T1`=`taxes_with_plan`, `C1`=`cash_outlay`** plus the engagement-level **`F`=`fee`**; year 2, when `assess_form.year2` is present, from its **`W2`/`T2`/`C2`** (#306). `taxes_with` is **ENTERED** and `gross = W − T` is **DERIVED** — the exact inverse of the old strategy-sum model, where the gross was summed and `taxes_with` fell out of it. Per year: `GROSS_Yn = Wn − Tn`, `INVEST_Yn = Cn`. **FEE ATTRIBUTION — the WHOLE fee lands on year 1 and NONE on year 2:** `NET_Y1 = GROSS_Y1 − INVEST_Y1 − F`, `NET_Y2 = GROSS_Y2 − INVEST_Y2`. **Every combined figure is the SUM of the per-year figures, computed FROM them specifically so the two can never drift apart:** `TAXES_WITHOUT = W1+W2`, `TAXES_WITH = T1+T2`, `GROSS_BENEFIT = GROSS_Y1+GROSS_Y2`, `CASH_OUTLAY = INVEST_Y1+INVEST_Y2`, `NET_BENEFIT = NET_Y1+NET_Y2`, `outlay_plus_fee = CASH_OUTLAY + F`, `fee_half = F/2`, `pct = round(GROSS_BENEFIT / (W1+W2) × 100)`. **The three identities that hold BY CONSTRUCTION — and are the checks to run against any generated deck:** `NET_Y1 + NET_Y2 === NET_BENEFIT`, `GROSS_Y1 + GROSS_Y2 === GROSS_BENEFIT`, `INVEST_Y1 + INVEST_Y2 === CASH_OUTLAY`. `year` = the **generation-time** calendar year (regenerating in January changes it), `year2 = year+1`, and **`INVEST_YEARS` is `"<year> + <year2>"` in two-year mode, `String(year)` in single-year mode** — where its shape is deleted, so that value goes nowhere. **In SINGLE-YEAR mode the six per-year appendix tokens carry no per-year data**: `GROSS_Y1/INVEST_Y1/NET_Y1` are the whole-plan figures and `GROSS_Y2/INVEST_Y2/NET_Y2` are `money(0)`, purely so all 21 slide-borne tokens substitute before both appendix slides are dropped (see below), so no viewer ever sees them. **In TWO-YEAR mode they are the real per-year figures and both slides ship.** Money is whole-dollar with commas — **`FEE_HALF` is the ONLY cents-tolerant token**; negatives use accounting parentheses, which matters because **`NET_Y1` can legitimately go NEGATIVE** on a year-1-light plan carrying the whole fee. **Slide 23 carries TWO overlapping Net-Benefit boxes** (a template artifact) — both get the same token, do not “fix” one away — and the three **$0 chips on slide 25 are literal** by design, as is the **One-Time Fee “$0” on the year-2 slide: template text, NOT a token — there is no `PLANNING_FEE_Y2` to look for.**

**`substitutePlannerNotes()` — THE ONE TOKEN THAT BYPASSES THE GENERIC LOOP, AND THE INDEX WALK THAT LOCATES IT (2026-08-19, v761).** Twenty-one tokens go through one `split(token).join(xmlEscape(value))` sweep. `PLANNER_NOTES` cannot, because **a bare `\n` inside `<a:t>` does not render as a line break** — DrawingML's soft break is the `<a:br/>` ELEMENT, a sibling of `<a:r>` inside `<a:p>` — so a two-line note has to become **run / break / run**, i.e. its replacement is *XML*, which the generic loop would (correctly) escape into visible angle brackets. The handler therefore runs a dedicated substitution on `NOTES_PART` **before** the generic loop: it replaces the **whole `<a:r>…</a:r>`** carrying the token with one run per line joined by `<a:br>`, `xmlEscape`ing each line exactly as every other value is escaped and **cloning the run's own captured `<a:rPr>` onto every rebuilt run and every break**, so nothing about the notes styling is duplicated in code. **An EMPTY value is the ordinary path, not a branch:** `"".split(/\n/)` is `[""]`, which yields a single empty invisible run — the paragraph stays and renders as nothing, so no notes means no dangling *"TAX PLANNER NOTES:"* label. The value is deliberately **NOT** a member of the `values` map for exactly this reason; feeding it through there would print the `<a:br>` markup on the deck. **The run is located by INDEX WALK — `indexOf` the exact tail `<a:t>{{PLANNER_NOTES}}</a:t></a:r>`, asserted to occur EXACTLY ONCE, then `lastIndexOf("<a:r>")`, plus a shape check that everything between them is a plain `<a:rPr>` — and NOT by regex, for a reason that cost a debugging round in simulation** (see **#420**). The exactly-once assertion is the guard on the template: a rebuild that moves or duplicates the token fails loudly with `expected exactly 1 run carrying {{PLANNER_NOTES}}, found N` instead of shipping a deck whose speaker notes read `{{PLANNER_NOTES}}`. **The notes part is dropped in NEITHER deck mode** — single-year drops the two year-appendix SLIDES, and the v2 clone step gave those no notes part at all.

**FEE ATTRIBUTION WAS AN IDENTITY YOU COULD CHECK AGAINST ANY GENERATED DECK — and since 2026-08-17 there is nothing left to check.** Under the two-year model the one-time planning fee was charged **entirely to year 1**, so `NET_Y1 + NET_Y2 === NET_BENEFIT`, `GROSS_Y1 + GROSS_Y2 === GROSS_BENEFIT` and `INVEST_Y1 + INVEST_Y2 === CASH_OUTLAY`. The identities still hold arithmetically (the `_Y2` tokens are all zero and the `_Y1` tokens are the whole plan), but **both appendix slides are dropped from every deck**, so no generated deck displays either side of them. Do not reintroduce a per-year split without reinstating the slides. **Slide 24 carries NO year at all as of 2026-08-17:** the `Your investment: {{INVEST_YEARS}}` header — added in v3, widened to 4.70in with `wrap="none"` so it could never wrap — was **DELETED from the master outright** (owner request: the year read as noise once the deck stopped showing a per-year breakdown), and the twelve left-column shapes below it were shifted **up 539650 EMU (0.59in)**, which is exactly top-of-removed-line to top-of-blue-bar, so the blue "Estimated Tax Savings" bar now starts where the header did and the gap under the "Return On Investment" title is unchanged. **The right-hand "Net Tax Savings" panel did NOT move** — it begins at y=1085760, above the deleted line, so nothing below it shifted. Shipped as a same-name hot-swap with a `-pre-shift-` backup; the shape's id was checked for animation/timing references first (none). Earlier, the year had also been **removed** from the "Estimated Tax Savings" / "Net Tax Savings" / "Plan Investment" labels — those labels used to name a single year while the values beside them were already two-year totals. Do not put the year back. The percentage-reduced box stays a **combined-view-only** claim; neither year slide carries one.

**TRAP: THE APPENDIX SLIDES' PART FILENAMES ARE NOT THEIR DISPLAY POSITIONS.** The two single-year summary slides are the parts **`ppt/slides/slide28.xml` (year 1) and `slide29.xml` (year 2)**, but `<p:sldIdLst>` orders the deck **`1–24, 28, 29, 25, 26, 27`**, so **they DISPLAY at positions 25 and 26** and the old “Choose Who You Pay” / Double-Guarantee / Congratulations slides display at **27/28/29**. The edge function addresses parts **by filename**, so `SLIDE_PARTS` is correct as written and must **NOT** be "corrected" to display numbers. The same trap bites the maintenance scripts: `restyle_bullets.py` targets **display 3/4/28 = files `slide3`/`slide4`/`slide26`**, and `make_v3.py`'s guarantee edit is likewise **display 28 = file `slide26.xml`**. Anyone editing `sldIdLst` or `SLIDE_PARTS` has to hold both numbering systems at once — verify against `presentation.xml`, never assume.

**The risk-arrow table is hardcoded in the handler and is not derivable at runtime.** The slide-20 `upArrow` is repositioned per grade to EMU x = **1→801612, 2→2566499, 3→4349395, 4→6126289, 5→7900180**, measured from the chevron strip PNG by `fill_deck.py`. If the strip artwork moves in a future template, those five numbers must be re-measured — nothing will error, the arrow will simply point at the wrong band. The grade itself comes from the **plan-level** `client_tax_progress` row (`tax_specialist_id IS NULL`) for the task **“Client risk profile complete”**, parsed `/Risk\s*([1-5])/` — another name-coupling of the #323 family.

**ALL FOUR YEAR-1 TOTALS ARE REQUIRED, validated in LOCKSTEP, and the generator is STRICT NEW-SHAPE-ONLY (2026-08-17; year-2 handling added 2026-08-18).** `assess_form` is `{ fee, taxes_without_plan, taxes_with_plan, cash_outlay, year2?, notes? }` (#306), and FE `AssessTaxForm` / BE `save-assess-form.ts` share **ten** identical messages. Change one side and you must change the other. **The generator adds four failure modes of its own**, and its earlier legacy-tolerance is GONE: a row that still carries a `strategies` array, or that is missing any of `taxes_without_plan` / `taxes_with_plan` / `cash_outlay`, 400s *“Re-save the Assess form — it uses the old strategy format; re-enter the four totals”* (the old *“…before the Total Taxes field existed”* message is deleted); `taxes_without_plan <= 0` 400s *“Total Tax without the Plan must be greater than zero”* (it is the `PCT_REDUCED` divisor); `taxes_with_plan < 0` or `>= taxes_without_plan` 400s the same less-than message the save path uses; and — **new 2026-08-18** — a `year2` group that is **PRESENT but incomplete** (any of its three totals missing/negative, or `taxes_with_plan >= taxes_without_plan`) 400s *“Re-save the Assess form — the Year 2 totals are incomplete”*. **An ABSENT `year2` is NOT a failure mode** — it is a one-year plan, and the generator's only reaction is to build the 27-slide deck. **The generator re-validates a present group rather than trusting it**, on the reasoning that the save handler only ever writes the key once all three passed, so a present-but-broken group means the row was hand-edited or written by something else. A missing/non-finite `fee` still falls to *“Submit the Assess tax planning opportunities form first”*. Since the save handler rebuilds the whole jsonb, **a re-save is the upgrade path for EVERY older row** — there is no backfill, and **the FE Generate-step readiness gate mirrors BOTH generator failure modes IN ORDER**: all four year-1 totals finite (hint: *“Re-save the Assess form — it uses the old strategy format”*), then the present-but-broken year-2 test (hint: *“Re-save the Assess form — the Year 2 totals are incomplete”*), so the button never fires a call that can only come back as an error string.

**THE DECK IS 27 OR 29 SLIDES, DECIDED BY `assess_form.year2` (2026-08-18 — this replaces the 2026-08-17 "always single-year, always 27" rule, which is now only the SINGLE-YEAR HALF of the behaviour).** `twoYear` is the mere PRESENCE of the nested `year2` group (#306/#374). **Two-year → 29 slides, nothing dropped, `INVEST_YEARS` = `"<year> + <year2>"`.** **Single-year → 27 slides**: `INVEST_YEARS` = `String(year)` (and its shape is deleted anyway), and **BOTH** `ppt/slides/slide28.xml` (year 1) and `ppt/slides/slide29.xml` (year 2) are removed from the generated package by the same four-edit surgery #374 describes, applied per slide in a loop, **plus the slide-24 header surgery** — the whole block sitting behind one `if (!twoYear)`. **The two removals touch the SAME three shared parts** — `ppt/presentation.xml`, `ppt/_rels/presentation.xml.rels` and `[Content_Types].xml` — so each is read from the zip **once** and the progressively-patched string is threaded through both iterations; re-reading `entries` per slide would silently throw the first removal away and ship a deck referencing a part that is not there. **`SLIDE_PARTS` still lists 28 and 29** so their tokens are substituted before they are dropped, which is what keeps the leftover-token guard uniform across all **9** parts of `TOKEN_PARTS` — do not "tidy" them out of that list. **The 9th, `notesSlide23.xml`, is dropped in NEITHER mode**: the two parts single-year removes are SLIDES, and the v2 clone step left them with no notes part at all. **`TEMPLATE_OBJECT` was UNCHANGED at `ROI-template-master-v3.pptx` for that 2026-08-17 change** — no token was added, renamed or dropped, so it did NOT take the versioned-object path and no new master was uploaded. **It changed to `ROI-template-master-v4.pptx` on 2026-08-18** (restoring slide 24's `{{INVEST_YEARS}}` site is a token-site addition relative to the live object) **and to `ROI-template-master-v5.pptx` on 2026-08-19** (a token site in a wholly new PART — see the v5 paragraphs above).

**The step ids, name and sentinel are load-bearing, and the done-state is NOT a progress row.** `program_client_tasks` **173** (program 1 Holistic) / **174** (program 4 VFO Tax Planning), name **“Generate and download presentation”**, `task_order=2` in “Tax 2 - Deeper Dive”, sentinel `status_options='tax_generate_presentation'` (ids 90/124 were pushed to `task_order=3` and 168/169 to **4** to make room — the Tax 2 ordering is no longer what older docs describe). **The step reads done from `client_tax_plans.generated_presentation_at` being non-NULL and writes NO `client_progress` / `client_tax_progress` row whatsoever** — wired that way in `actions/clients/overview-tax.ts` and the FE `isTaskStatused`. Anything that counts completed steps from progress rows alone will under-count this one.

**Drive behaviour, and the sharing decision that was made deliberately.** The filled deck is uploaded **multipart with metadata `mimeType='application/vnd.google-apps.presentation'`**, which is the whole mechanism by which Drive **converts it server-side into a real Google Slides file** — drop that metadata and you get an inert .pptx. It lands in an **“ROI Presentations”** folder under `GOOGLE_DRIVE_FOLDER_ID` and is shared **role=writer, type=anyone**: chosen over view-only **after** a security review surfaced the tradeoff, and **approved by the user** — treat it as a product decision, not a bug. **Regenerate creates a NEW Drive file every time** (the plan row points at the newest; old files are never deleted), Download simply opens the Slides URL, and **the Tax 2 “Send presentation link” field is deliberately NOT auto-filled** — an admin reviews the deck and pastes it manually, so `member_presentation_link` and `presentation_link` stay untouched by this feature.

**The zip pattern is a performance requirement, not a style choice.** This introduced **the first zip library in the codebase** — **fflate** (`https://esm.sh/fflate@0.8.2`). **Untouched entries are re-stored at level 0 and ONLY the edited XMLs are deflated** — the 8 slide parts **plus `notesSlide23.xml` since v5**, so 9, plus the three shared parts in single-year mode; recompressing ~27 MB of PNGs would blow the request budget. Generation still runs ~30–60s, which is why `src/lib/api.js` gained a **`LONG_TIMEOUT_ACTIONS`** map granting this action **90s** — **the #30 rule is untouched: writes still never retry**, only the timeout is per-action.

Cross-ref **#306** (the jsonb this consumes), **#314** (the assess form is editable, so the shape can change under you), **#323** (name-resolved step coupling), **#309** (deny-by-omission — the planner allowlist omission here is the gate), **#30** (no retries on writes). `see #336`.

**337. A session worktree can be cut from a STALE local main AND the freshness check can return a FALSE 0 — so the `git fetch origin && git rev-list --count HEAD..origin/main` == 0 check must run IMMEDIATELY BEFORE EVERY DEPLOY, in the same command invocation; a check that passed at session start proves nothing at deploy time (2026-08-06). ALWAYS-APPLIES (deploy / ops).** This is gotcha **#288**’s scenario actually happening, with a twist that makes it worse. The auto-created edge worktree for this session was branched from a **stale local `main` at `4edd0d7`** (PR #145, 2026-07-30) while `origin/main` had moved twelve PRs ahead — **and the session-start freshness check reported 0 behind anyway**. The result: the session’s first deploy (**v705**, ~18:29 UTC) shipped a bundle built from July-30 source and **silently REVERTED merged PRs #146–#157 on the live function for ~40 minutes**. Nothing failed at build time, nothing failed at deploy time, and `deno check` was clean — the only symptom was live traffic getting **`Unknown action` on `tax_planner_clients_load`**, an action that had existed in production for three days.

**Why a session-start check is worthless by deploy time.** Worktrees are recycled and re-pointed between sessions, `main` in the parent clone can be arbitrarily old, and a `fetch` that appears to succeed can still leave `origin/main` stale in the ref the count reads — which is exactly what produced the false 0 here. **So the check has no shelf life.** Run it as a single invocation immediately before the deploy command, and read the number yourself: `git fetch origin && git rev-list --count HEAD..origin/main` — anything but **0** means merge first.

**The remediation shape, if it happens again.** WIP-commit the in-flight work so nothing can be lost → `git merge origin/main` → **verify BOTH sides rather than trusting a clean merge**: here the merge had zero conflicts, and the proof it was complete was the **action-parity re-count — 456 on main + 1 ours = 457** → redeploy → then re-run the pipeline smoke gate **against the new version** (5/5 vs v706) and have the user re-test whatever the reverted PRs touched (the planner Clients tab). **A superseded deploy version is not a footnote — record it:** v705 served only **441** actions and existed on live for 40 minutes, and any log or Sentry event from that window should be read with that in mind.

Cross-ref **#288** (the stale-worktree trap this is the live instance of), **#145** (deploys need an explicit approval — both of this session’s deploys had one, which is why the stale one still shipped), **#146** (the Bash tool’s cwd persists — `cd` immediately before a deploy), **#112** (`--no-lock` + remove `deno.lock` before deploying). `see #337`.
---

**338. The Accounting tab runs on FULL PARITY: the `accounting` grant in `allowed_tabs` is the WHOLE boundary for everything inside that tab, money controls included, and the in-handler superadmin re-checks were deliberately REMOVED — do not "restore" them (2026-08-07, v707). ALWAYS-APPLIES.** The reported symptom was small: an admin granted the `accounting` tab saw a **blank** Accounting tab. The cause was structural. **Ten of the twelve Accounting content blocks in `AdminPortal.jsx` gated on `session.is_superadmin` with NO `else`** — a non-superadmin rendered literally nothing, and because the default section is `payments`, which was one of the ten, the tab opened blank on arrival. Underneath that, **9+ handlers carried their own `auth.isSuperadmin` 403** on top of the tab gate, so even the sections that did render would have failed on use. The user's decision was explicit and is the whole design now: *"they should see all sections and be able to do anything they want within those sections like i can."*

**What the boundary IS.** `middleware/auth.ts` reads `allowed_tabs` **live per request** (the FE session only stamps it at login), and `TAB_ACTIONS.accounting` in `constants/role-gates.ts` is the single server-side gate for every action reachable from inside the tab. Twenty-four handlers dropped their in-handler check, each leaving the comment `// accounting-tab gate (TAB_ACTIONS.accounting) is the boundary — full-parity decision 2026-08-07.` at the site, precisely so a future reader of **#309** does not read the absence as an oversight and add one back. Two gate-list edits went with it: **`specialist_revenue_confirm_received` was ADDED** to `TAB_ACTIONS.accounting` — it had been in **no gate list at all**, its in-handler check *was* its entire gate, so removing that check without the add would have 403'd every granted admin by deny-by-omission — and **`migration_send_setup_link` MOVED** out of `SUPERADMIN_ONLY_ACTIONS` into the accounting list on explicit user request, knowingly widening a control that mints a Stripe customer + setup token and that is **also reachable from the per-client Payment Continuation tab** (that second surface is widened too — deliberate, not a leak). `router/dispatch.ts` was not opened; the action count did not move.

**The rule for every NEW accounting surface, both halves of it.** A new action fired from inside the Accounting tab **MUST be named in `TAB_ACTIONS.accounting`** or a granted admin gets a silent 403 (**#167**, **#309**), **AND it must NOT be given an in-handler superadmin check** unless it is deliberately outside parity. The set that IS deliberately outside parity, and stays Jake-only: `migration_stripe_lookup`, `migration_backfill_map1`, `migration_backfill_tax`, `specialist_license_continuation_*`, `tax_update_split`, `payments_send_card_update`. One live inconsistency worth knowing: **`specialist_revenue_retry_payout` is listed in `TAB_ACTIONS.accounting` but KEPT its in-handler superadmin check** — harmless today because its only button lives in `SpecialistRevenueAutomationPanel` under the **Automation** tab (Jake-only), but if a Retry payout control is ever added inside Accounting, that check has to come off or it will 403 a granted admin.

**The frontend half, and why a blank pane is now structurally impossible.** Every Accounting content block gates on **`canSeeTab('accounting')`**, which short-circuits `true` for the superadmin, so there is exactly one predicate rather than two parallel ones that can drift. A module-level **`ACCOUNTING_SECTIONS`** list plus a terminal **"You don't have access to this section."** fallback card means an unmatched `accountingSection` renders a message instead of nothing — the original bug's failure mode cannot recur even if someone adds a section and forgets a block. **`MembershipFeesPanel`'s `isSuperadmin` prop was NOT deleted** — it is still the prop every money control in that panel keys off; both mounts now pass **`isSuperadmin={canSeeTab('accounting')}`**, i.e. the prop's *name* is now historical and its *meaning* is "may work this tab". Renaming it is safe; feeding it `session.is_superadmin` again silently reverts the feature.

**Grants live at the time of the decision:** Paul Latham, Tracy Miller, Rachael Hopson and Dan Gorriaran hold `accounting`. This is a real widening of real money controls to four named people, made knowingly. Cross-ref **#333** (whose "TWO deliberate accounting-tab exceptions in an otherwise superadmin `membership_*` family" framing this supersedes — the whole family is accounting-tab-gated now), **#309** (deny-by-omission), **#167** (an unlisted action 403s a tab-granted admin), **#256** (verify a gate name against `dispatch.ts` before trusting it). `see #338`.
