**339. `overview-tax.ts` is the BACKEND MIRROR of the frontend's `isTaskStatused` for every column-proven tax step — a sentinel the FE handles and the backend does not falls through to the generic progress-row branch and reads permanently NOT DONE; and separately, `computeTrack`'s skipped-step warnings are POSITIONAL, so a `task_order` reorder rewrites them retroactively (2026-08-07, v708). ALWAYS-APPLIES (tax track).** Two distinct traps, both live-proven in the same session, both about Client Overview disagreeing with the tax tab.

> **ADDRESS CHANGE — the rule is unmoved, the file is not.** Every `overview-tax.ts` reference below means the step machine, which was lifted **VERBATIM** into **`utils/tax-plan-steps.ts`** on 2026-08-17; `overview-tax.ts` is now a thin caller. The mirror obligation got BIGGER in the move, not smaller: `actions/tax-planners/portal-clients.ts` is a second consumer, so a sentinel branch added there-and-not-here now desynchronises the planner portal as well as Client Overview. And as of 2026-08-18 `computeTrack` emits `next_actions` (owner-bearing) alongside the legacy `next_action`, so "positional" below governs BOTH.

**The mirror.** Most tax steps prove done-ness with a `client_tax_progress` row, and `overview-tax.ts`'s generic `else` handles those. A growing minority prove it with a **column** instead — the Green/Red Light refund (#293), "Generate and download presentation" (#336), and **"Request Tax Returns"** (`status_options='tax_returns_request'`, task 172). Each of those needs its OWN sentinel branch in `overview-tax.ts`, because the generic branch looks for a progress row that will never exist. `tax_returns_request` never got one. **Zero `client_tax_progress` rows exist for task 172**, so every program-4 plan past Tax 1 carried a permanent, unfixable false *"not recorded"* warning in Client Overview — for months, on real plans, with no way for an admin to clear it. The fix mirrors the FE `isTaskStatused` helper (`TaxPrioritiesTab.jsx` ~line 1281) exactly: `done = !!plan.tax_returns_received_at`, `at = received ?? requested ?? null`, `applicable: reach`. At the fix, **28 program-4 plans existed, 21 requested, 16 received** → 16 plans' false warnings cleared and 12 kept the warning correctly. **When you add a column-proven step, add BOTH branches in the same change** — the FE one is the one you notice missing, the backend one is the one nobody notices for months.

**The positional warnings.** `computeTrack` decides "skipped step" by walking the step list **in `task_order` sequence** — so `next_action` and the whole warning scan are a function of ORDER, not of the steps themselves. **Reordering a phase therefore rewrites the warnings on plans that already exist**, retroactively, with no data change at all. The 2026-08-07 Tax 1 reorder (risk profile + planner allocation moved AFTER the Green/Red Light decision in program 4, and to the END of Tax 1 in program 1, which has no Green/Red Light step) did exactly that: roughly **16 existing plans** gained a warning, and **every NEW Holistic plan now opens with one**, because **#261** auto-seeds the risk profile at plan creation and the risk step now sits last — so a brand-new plan looks like it skipped ahead. The user was shown this consequence before the change and chose **"Accept the new warnings"**. Record that kind of acceptance: a warning nobody decided to accept is a bug report waiting to happen. Also note the reorder was **DML only** (`task_order` rewrite, no id changes), so **zero selections moved** — verify that the way this session did, by counting `client_tax_progress` rows per task id before and after. Portal sessions need a **page reload** to pick up a new order (the template is module-cached). Cross-ref **#293**, **#336**, **#261**, **#271** (the hidden legacy rows that occupy orders inside Tax 1), **#202**. `see #339`.

**340. "Request Tax Returns" is a BOTH-PROGRAMS step as of 2026-08-07 — the sentinel `tax_returns_request` now lives on program 4 (task 172) AND Holistic (task 175); the email template is picked by `(program_id || 1)` with a `|holistic` variant that falls back to the base row, and the upload stamp is DUAL-CANDIDATE with the Holistic half REQUEST-GATED, which is the only thing keeping years of already-sent `/tax-upload` links inert (2026-08-07, v709–v712). ALWAYS-APPLIES (tax track).** For two years the two tax programs asked for tax returns in genuinely different ways, and only one of them was a process. VFO Tax Planning (program 4) has had a real step — `program_client_tasks` **172**, `status_options='tax_returns_request'`, first in Tax 1 - Diagnostic — since v603. **Holistic had no step at all:** its clients were asked inside the MAP 1 first-payment invoice/receipt email, via an "Upload Tax Documents" button that minted `clients.tax_upload_token` and linked `/tax-upload`. The email worked; the process did not. There was no request stamp, no received stamp, no step to look at and no phase-scoped bell — the upload landed as a generic `UPLOAD_tax_return_uploaded` FYI and the tax track never moved.

**The step.** Holistic gained **`program_client_tasks` id 175** (`phase_id=18`, `name='Request Tax Returns'`, `task_type='dropdown'`, `task_order=1`, `status_options='tax_returns_request'`) as the FIRST step of Tax 1 - Diagnostic, mirroring program 4. The six existing phase-18 tasks shifted down one (85→2, 86→3, 87→4, 88→5, 83→6, 84→7). **Task ids did not change, so no `client_tax_progress` selection moved** — verified by per-task counts on both sides of the apply (83 = 14, 84 = 14, 85 = 13), which is the check any future `task_order` rewrite owes (#339). Migration `20260807120000_holistic_request_tax_returns.sql`, DML only, applied via MCP `apply_migration` and committed as a file (#196). Like its program-4 twin the step is **column-proven and writes NO `client_tax_progress` row** — done-ness rides `client_tax_plans.tax_returns_requested_at` / `tax_returns_received_at`, so `overview-tax.ts`'s `tax_returns_request` sentinel branch (#339) is what keeps Client Overview honest for BOTH programs now.

**Template selection, and the argument people will forget.** `actions/tax/request-returns.ts` picks `(plan.program_id || 1) === 1 ? 'TAX_request_returns|holistic' : 'TAX_request_returns'` — the plan's `program_id` had to be added to the handler's `.select()` to make that possible — and **falls back to the base name if the variant row is missing or inactive**, so a deleted/deactivated 214 degrades to program 4's wording rather than a 404. `email_templates` **214** (`TAX_request_returns|holistic`, pipeline `TAX`, **Draft mode**, To `[CLIENT]`, Cc `[ASSIGNED_PF, MEMBER, tnmiller@vfo-services.com, tvaldes@vfo-services.com]`, Bcc `[platham@elitert.com, aanderson@elitert.com]`) is a copy of **194** differing only in its opening line, and its recipients are the #326-correct `@vfo-services.com` addresses. **The load-bearing detail is the last call:** `gmailDraftFetch(..., "TAX", templateName)` must receive the name **actually used**, not the hardcoded base — that argument is what resolves the per-template Draft/Send toggle, so passing `'TAX_request_returns'` while drafting from row 214 would silently apply the wrong row's send mode. The FE mirrors the same ternary in the step's `StepEmailsChip` (`TaxPrioritiesTab.jsx`) and `templateMeta.js` carries the matching description — three surfaces, one condition, keep them together.

**The stamp is DUAL-CANDIDATE, and the Holistic half is REQUEST-GATED on purpose (Option A, the user's explicit choice).** `actions/vault/upload-notify.ts` runs **two independent lookups**: the newest **program-4** plan, selected unconditionally (unchanged semantics — that step's request is implicit), and the newest **Holistic** plan selected with `.or("program_id.is.null,program_id.eq.1")` **and** `.not("tax_returns_requested_at", "is", null)`. Every unstamped candidate gets `tax_returns_received_at`; the `TAX_returns_received` bell (Tracy / Tray) fires **exactly ONCE**; a client holding plans in BOTH programs has **both** stamped by one upload. **Two traps live here.** (1) **`.eq("program_id", 1)` is wrong** — legacy Holistic rows carry `program_id` NULL, and `.eq` excludes NULLs; the `.or(...)` form is mandatory, the same `(program_id || 1)` convention as everywhere else in the tax track (#269). (2) **The request-gate is not a nicety, it is the compatibility layer.** `clients.tax_upload_token` is **durable and per-client, not per-request** (#331), so every `/tax-upload` link sitting in an inbox from a pre-v709 MAP 1 first-payment email is the SAME URL the new step mints. Without the gate, those old links would retro-green a step for Holistic clients who were never asked. With it, an upload from a client with no requested plan falls through to the original generic FYI, byte-identically to before. **Do not "simplify" that gate away.**

**v710 exists because the first shape was wrong, and the reason generalises.** v709 shipped the Holistic candidate as an `else` on the program-4 lookup, so a client holding plans in both programs had program 4 **swallow** the upload and the Holistic plan never went green. Caught in click-testing, fixed by making the lookups independent and the stamp a loop. The lesson: on a two-program client, "the newest plan" is never one query.

**Cross-ref.** Supersedes **#269**'s "hard-gated to `program_id = 4`" clause (corrected in place there). Companion to **#341** (the email that gave the job up), **#331** (one token, one page, three — now two — flows), **#271** (the additional-info stamp beside it, which has NO program gate at all and can fire on the same upload), **#339** (the column-proven done-state mirror + positional warnings), **#226** (the shared `/tax-upload` page). **Accepted consequence, user-approved:** the 12 existing Holistic plans already past Tax 1 gained a permanent "Request Tax Returns — not recorded" warning, and every existing Holistic plan's Tax 1 reopens as not-done until returns are requested and received. `see #340`.

**341. The MAP 1 first-payment email's `[TAX_UPLOAD]` token stopped requesting tax returns entirely — the handler no longer mints `clients.tax_upload_token` and never links `/tax-upload`; tax-priority clients get a STATIC Tax Form URL instead, and the template body + the handler HTML are a MATCHED PAIR that must move together (2026-08-07, v709 / v711 / v712). ALWAYS-APPLIES (tax track).** `actions/pipeline/contract-invoice-receipt.ts` had been the de-facto tax-returns request for Holistic clients (see #340). It is not any more, and the removal is deeper than a swapped button: **`tax_upload_token` appears ZERO times in that file now** — the handler minted only `client_setup_token` (the portal-setup link) and never constructed a `/tax-upload` URL. **SUPERSEDED 2026-08-26 (v789): the portal-setup half is gone too, so the handler now mints NOTHING** — `[TAX_UPLOAD]` renders the tax-form block ALONE, and on a non-tax client the block is empty (the substitution is spelled `replace(/\[TAX_UPLOAD\]<br>/…)` so the template's trailing `<br>` is swallowed with it — the matched-pair rule below now covers the seam as well as the body). `client_setup_token` consequently has no writer anywhere; see **#446**. Anything that reasoned "the first-payment email is one of the three carriers of the tax-upload token" (#331, #226, `tables/clients.md`) is describing emails already sent, not emails this handler will send.

**What renders now.** The `TAX_PRIORITIES` const survives — the same six "— Tax —" values copied from `PIPDecisionForm.jsx` — but **only as the `hasTax` detector**; it no longer gates a token. A tax-priority client gets a static **"Tax Form"** button pointing at **`https://www.vfo-services.com/holistic-planning-form/`** (module const `TAX_FORM_URL`), rendered — until 2026-08-26 — **ahead of** the portal-setup button; a non-tax client got the portal button alone. **From v789 the portal button is deleted from all four cases: a tax client gets the Tax Form button alone, and a non-tax client gets an EMPTY block.** The block renders **four cases** — tax / non-tax × member-pays-on-behalf / client-pays — because the member-paid variant is addressed to the MEMBER while still inviting the CLIENT to act ("please ask <ClientFirst> to complete the form below" vs "since you selected tax priorities…").

**The spacing is a two-sided contract and that is why v712 exists.** The sentence *"Tracy will contact you directly / your client directly if she requires any further information, and prior to arranging any VFO Specialist meetings."* was deleted from `email_templates` rows **10** (`CONTRACT_invoicereceipt_email|first`) and **115** (the member signing/paying variant), and `[TAX_UPLOAD]<br><br>If you have any further questions` was tightened to `[TAX_UPLOAD]<br>If you…`. In the same pass the handler's **leading `<br><br>`** — which had existed only to separate the block from that sentence — was removed, leaving a single `<br>` under the Tax Form button. **Restoring either side alone reopens a gap or collapses one:** re-add the sentence without the code `<br>`s and the button crowds the paragraph; re-add the code `<br>`s without the sentence and the email grows a blank hole. Treat rows 10/115 and the `[TAX_UPLOAD]` builder as one unit. Cross-ref **#340** (where tax returns are requested now), **#331**, **#226**. `see #341`.

**342. An external asset that deployed code reads BY NAME has its lifecycle COUPLED to the deploy, and whether you may overwrite it in place depends entirely on whether the code encodes STRUCTURE about it — content-only changes hot-swap the same object, contract-changing edits need a NEW versioned name plus a lockstep deploy (generalised 2026-08-07 from the ROI template's v1→v2 move, v713). ALWAYS-APPLIES.**

**The distinction that decides it.** Ask what the deployed code knows about the asset beyond its name. If it knows only "fetch this and hand it on" — a bucket PDF attached to an email, an image, a document served as-is — then the asset is **content-only** and a replacement is a **hot swap**: overwrite the same object, no deploy, live on the next request. If the code encodes **structure** — token names it substitutes, part filenames it addresses, an expected page/sheet/slide count, offsets it seeks to — then the asset and the code are **one contract in two files**, and the only safe change is a **NEW versioned object name deployed in lockstep**.

**Why overwriting a contract-bearing asset is the trap.** The upload is instant and global; the deploy is not. Overwrite in place and every request between the two runs NEW asset against OLD code — the failure lands on real users, in a window nobody is watching, and it does not look like a deploy problem because no deploy has happened yet. With a versioned name there is **no window at all**: the old function keeps reading the old object, the new function reads the new one, and the switch is atomic at deploy. **Keep the superseded object in the bucket as the rollback** — it costs storage and buys an instant revert.

**Order of operations, both directions.** Contract change: **upload the new object FIRST, then deploy** the code that names it (never the reverse — a function naming an object that does not exist yet 500s on every call). Rollback: **redeploy the previous function**; do not try to un-upload. Content-only change: just replace the object, and if it renders wrong, replace it back.

**The worked example is #336, now run TWICE.** `ROI-template-master.pptx` → **`ROI-template-master-v2.pptx`** → **`ROI-template-master-v3.pptx`** (2026-08-12, v729: one token added and the generated slide count made variable — #374), each superseded object kept as a rollback. For **v2** the tokens, the slide count and the part filenames all moved — deployed in lockstep as v713. For **v3** the part filenames did NOT move; a single token was added (`INVEST_YEARS`) and the GENERATED deck's slide count became variable, which is enough on its own to force the versioned path — deployed in lockstep as v729. The **restyle rounds that followed took the other path**: slides 2/3/4/28 carry no token, so nothing the filler substitutes could move, and they shipped by replacing the SAME object with no code change and no deploy. Same bucket, same feature, two different correct answers on the same afternoon — which is the whole point.

**Where else this applies today.** Every bucket asset the code fetches by literal name: the onboarding agreement PDFs picked by branch (#58), the tax-planning member PDF, the ERT/VFOS vault documents. Those are content-only **as long as no handler starts parsing them** — the day one does, that asset joins the versioned class and this rule binds it too.

Cross-ref **#336** (the instance this was generalised from), **#337** (the other half of deploy safety — freshness in the same invocation), **#288** (stale worktrees). `see #342`.

---

**343. Chrome's Google-Wallet loyalty-card autofill classifies a field by REGEX OVER ITS PLACEHOLDER/LABEL TEXT and IGNORES `autocomplete="off"` — a search box whose placeholder says “member number” gets a “Manage loyalty cards…” dropdown over the results, and the ONLY fix that works is `type="search"` + `name="search"` + `autoComplete="off"` AND keeping loyalty phrases OUT of the placeholder copy (2026-08-07, frontend-only, 19 inputs / 14 files). ALWAYS-APPLIES (Frontend).**

**The failure looks like ordinary autofill and is not.** Every admin search box in this portal — members, clients, membership fees, specialists, tax planners, MSM tracking, planner clients, CIQ — sat under a Chrome dropdown offering to *“Manage loyalty cards…”*, rendered **on top of the live search results**, i.e. exactly over the thing the admin was trying to read. The reflex fix is `autocomplete="off"`. **It does nothing here.** Chrome treats that attribute as advisory for its own wallet-backed surfaces (loyalty cards, payment instruments) and suppresses them on its own terms, not the page's. **Round 1 of this fix shipped the attributes alone and the popup survived the retest** — that negative result is the whole reason this entry exists.

**What Chrome is actually keying on is the words.** The loyalty heuristic regex-matches human-readable field text — `placeholder`, and the associated `<label>` — for phrases like **“member number”**, **“member #”**, **“membership”**, “loyalty”, “rewards”. This codebase was full of them for the most innocent reason imaginable: the placeholders were written years earlier to tell a human what they could type. “Search by name or member number…” is perfect UX copy and a perfect loyalty-card trigger at the same time.

**The fix is TWO-SIDED and both sides are load-bearing.** (1) **Reclassify the input** — `type="search" name="search" autoComplete="off"` (React casing: **`autoComplete`**, not `autocomplete`). `type="search"` is what moves the field out of the text/identity class the heuristic scans; the `name` stops any leftover name-based matching; `autocomplete="off"` still suppresses the ordinary (non-wallet) autofill and costs nothing. (2) **Rewrite the copy** — drop “member” from the placeholder entirely: “Search by name or **number**…”, “Search by client, ref, **number**, or **name**…”, “Type a name or **number** — leave blank for n/a”. **Remove either half and the popup comes back.** Do not “improve” a search placeholder back to “member number” — that is the whole regression.

**`type="search"` must be made visually inert or it changes every box.** WebKit gives search inputs a clear-“X” and decoration pseudo-elements. `src/index.css` carries a **frontend-wide two-part reset** — `input[type="search"] { -webkit-appearance: none; appearance: none; }` (the element itself, which is what strips the native search chrome) **plus** `input[type="search"]::-webkit-search-cancel-button, input[type="search"]::-webkit-search-decoration { -webkit-appearance: none; appearance: none; }` (the clear-X and decoration pseudo-elements) — so a reclassified field renders **byte-identically to `type="text"`**. That rule is global on purpose: it makes `type="search"` a free, invisible change anywhere, which is what makes this fix cheap to apply to the next search box someone adds. Every patched input stayed a controlled React field — `value`/`onChange` untouched — so **search behaviour did not change anywhere**.

**The audit grep is the maintenance mechanism, and it found the worst offender.** Run **`placeholder="[^"]*[Mm]ember`** across `src` (plus a companion pass for `placeholder={…}` / single-quoted forms). It is what caught **`NewModelSaleModal.jsx`**, which the original sweep had missed entirely and which carried the strongest trigger in the codebase (“Type a name or member # — leave blank for n/a”) with none of the attributes. **Legitimate survivors of that grep are only:** `<textarea>`s (not wallet-autofill targets — the member-bio and team-names fields), and **props that merely look like placeholders**, e.g. `SearchSelect`'s `placeholder="Select a member…"`, which renders as a button label; that component's real inner `<input>` says “Search…” and is patched. Anything else the grep returns is a live bug.

**Where this binds beyond search.** Any input whose visible text names a membership/account/card number is a candidate — the rule is about **copy**, not about search specifically. Cross-ref **#134** (JSX/`.jsx` build trap), **#169** (theme vars), **#193** (a green build is not proof — this one needed a human retest twice, and the first retest is what disproved round 1). `see #343`.

---

**344. Vite's `optimizeDeps` dependency scanner CRAWLS every `.html` file under `public/`, so a standalone page there whose module script imports a BARE specifier crashes `npm run dev` at startup — the page's own importmap does not save it, because the scanner never reads importmaps (2026-08-09, `/store/`). ALWAYS-APPLIES (Frontend / dev).** `public/` is supposed to be the inert directory — files in it are copied verbatim and are not part of the module graph. That is true of the **build** and false of the **dev server**. Vite's dependency pre-bundling step globs HTML entries and, by default, that glob reaches into `public/`. The moment `public/store/index.html` landed carrying `<script type="module">` with `import * as THREE from 'three'`, esbuild's scanner tried to resolve `three` as an npm dependency, found no such package installed (it is vendored — see **#348**), and **`npm run dev` failed to boot at all**. The page itself was fine; the dev server for the entire portal was not.

**The fix is one line, and it is a pin rather than an exclusion.** `vite.config.js` now carries `optimizeDeps: { entries: ['index.html'] }`, which tells the scanner "the app entry is the only HTML you scan" instead of trying to enumerate what to skip. It is the smaller, more durable statement: new standalone pages under `public/` cost nothing and need no further config.

**It is DEV-ONLY, and that was proven rather than assumed.** `optimizeDeps` governs esbuild pre-bundling for the dev server; production Rollup ignores it and `public/` is copied byte-for-byte. At the 2026-08-09 audit the tree was rebuilt with the **pre-session** `vite.config.js` and produced the **same 555 modules transformed** as the session config, confirming the line changes nothing about what ships. Do not "clean up" this line on the grounds that it looks like a build tweak with no build effect — its whole job is in dev.

**The general shape.** Any future self-contained page under `public/` that uses an importmap — a demo, a microsite, another 3D toy — depends on this line remaining. If someone reports "the dev server won't start and the error names a package we don't have installed", look for a new `.html` in `public/` before looking anywhere else. Cross-ref **#295** (why public pages exist at all), **#348** (the vendored three.js this import resolves to), **#151** (the other `public/`-asset path trap). `see #344`.

---

**345. `/store/` is a THIRD public consumer of the specialist directory — `public/store/data.js` reads `experts` with the anon key exactly as the embeddable widget does, so the anon column grant (#201) and the visibility exclusions (#231) now have to ripple to THREE places, and the static file is the one that will not fail loudly (2026-08-09). ALWAYS-APPLIES (auth / data).** Until this session the public read surface on `experts` had one anon client: `widget/vfo-widget.js`. The VFO Library added a second, `public/store/data.js`, which fetches `experts?select=id,name,short_bio,long_bio,headshot_image,status,sort_order,vfo_accredited` plus `vfo_ecosystem_assignments?select=expert_id,name` and then applies the **same three exclusions** the widget applies: drop anything whose `status` is neither `Active` nor null, drop anyone assigned to the internal **`Member Services`** ecosystem, drop anyone flagged **`vfo_accredited`**. On production data that is **71 `experts` rows in, 52 specialists out**.

**Nothing new is exposed, and that was the design constraint, not a happy accident.** The page consumes exactly the surface #201 already made public and nothing beyond it — `ecosystem_content`, `email`, `D&B_*`, `stripe_account_id` and `revenue_decision` remain off the anon grant and are never requested. The anon key hardcoded in `data.js` is the **publishable** key that already ships inside `src/lib/api.js` and inside the widget bucket file; it is public by construction and is not a secret leak. If a future page needs a column that is not in the eight, the answer is a deliberate GRANT decision under #201, never a widened `select`.

**The maintenance hazard is silence.** `public/store/data.js` is a static file copied verbatim by the build — it is not type-checked, not linted into the app graph, and not exercised by any test. Tighten the anon grant and this page simply renders an empty shop in a visitor's browser; loosen the exclusion policy and it quietly starts showing people who are meant to be hidden. Neither shows up in a build, a `deno check` or the security advisor. **When you touch #201 or #231, grep for all three consumers and change them together.**

**Where the count lives.** The shop renders **54 shelf placements for 52 people** because a dual-ecosystem specialist deliberately appears as a differently-coloured book in each of their sections rather than being deduped — so a "the numbers do not add up" report about `/store/` is probably this, not a data bug. Cross-ref **#201** (the column grant), **#231** (the accredited + Member Services exclusions), **#147** (hide in the rendering component, not only the loader), **#162** (an ecosystem name lives in many places — `/store/` adds another hardcoded ecosystem array). `see #345`.

---

**346. "The 3D store stutters when the window is big but is fine when it is small" is almost never our code — it is the visitor's browser software-rendering with hardware acceleration switched off, and `?debug=1` on `/store/` prints the GPU string that proves it (2026-08-09). (Frontend / support.)** This cost real time before it was diagnosed, so it is written down as a support script rather than a bug. A user reported heavy stutter; the same page on the same machine measured **avg 7.0 ms / p95 9.9 ms per frame** under instrumentation. The difference was that their Chrome had **hardware acceleration disabled**, so WebGL was being emulated on the CPU by the **Microsoft Basic Render Driver** — and CPU rasterisation scales directly with pixel count, which is exactly why a smaller window feels fine and a maximised one does not.

**The diagnostic is one query parameter.** Open `/store/?debug=1` and read the overlay: it prints the unmasked GPU renderer string alongside live fps. `Microsoft Basic Render Driver`, `SwiftShader`, `llvmpipe` or a blank/software vendor is the tell. A real GPU string (Intel UHD, Iris Xe, GeForce, Radeon) means look elsewhere.

**The page already defends itself, which is why this reads as "slow" rather than "broken".** Software and integrated GPUs are auto-detected at startup and dropped to lighter profiles (see **#347**) — SOFTWARE runs a 0.55M-pixel budget, LITE 1.2M with MSAA and several point lights removed. That keeps the page usable, not fast. **The actual fix is on the visitor's side: Chrome → Settings → System → "Use graphics acceleration when available" → ON, then restart the browser.** Offer that before touching any rendering code. Cross-ref **#347** (the budget architecture the auto-detect feeds), **#278** (the other "the portal is slow" root cause, which was images). `see #346`.

---

**347. The `/store/` render loop is PIXEL-BUDGET-based rather than native-resolution, texture uploads run through a throttled 2-lane queue, and hover raycasts fire once per FRAME instead of once per event — these are not micro-optimisations, they are the load-bearing structure, and undoing any of them re-introduces the stutter class the build spent hours eliminating (2026-08-09). (Frontend / perf.)** The requirement was "it has to work for everyone", which rules out the usual approach of rendering at `devicePixelRatio` and hoping. Instead the renderer targets a **total pixel budget per GPU tier**, so the cost of a frame is roughly **independent of how large the window is** — maximising the browser lowers effective resolution rather than raising GPU load. Tiers: **FULL**, **LITE** for integrated GPUs (no MSAA, ~1.2M-pixel budget, fewer point lights), **SOFTWARE** at ~0.55M pixels (see **#346** for how a tier is chosen).

**The specific traps that were hit and fixed, each of which looks removable and is not.** (a) **Inspect mode boosts to native resolution only AFTER the pickup tween finishes** — reallocating framebuffers mid-tween lands exactly when the user is watching one object move, and makes picking up a book feel heavy. (b) **Cover textures hydrate progressively at 2x through a throttled two-lane queue** with `renderer.initTexture`, plus a per-`(specialist, ecosystem)` cache; uploading covers eagerly stalls the compositor in visible bursts. (c) **Hover raycasts run once per frame, not once per pointer event** — 1000 Hz gaming mice were firing raycasts far faster than frames, and that alone produced visible jank. (d) The **movement loop allocates nothing** per frame, keeping the GC out of the walk. (e) A **~72 fps pacing gate** plus an adaptive-resolution governor sit on top.

**Two structural notes worth keeping.** Book placement uses a **seeded hash scatter**, so a given specialist lands in a stable spot and the shelves do not reshuffle as the roster grows. Grass is **instanced** (~550 blades in one draw) and the dunes are displaced geometry — both were chosen over the naive versions for the same reason.

**How to treat a future perf report.** Confirm the GPU string first (**#346**), then measure, then change one thing. A "simplification" PR that deletes the budget governor, un-throttles texture upload or moves raycasting back onto pointer events will look cleaner and will regress the page on exactly the machines it was built for. Cross-ref **#346**, **#348**. `see #347`.

---

**348. three.js is VENDORED into the repo at `public/store/vendor/three-0.160.1.module.min.js` — no npm dependency, no CDN — and the importmap in `public/store/index.html` names that exact filename, so upgrading three means a new vendor file AND an importmap edit in lockstep (2026-08-09). (Frontend / deps.)** The 670,681-byte minified module is committed deliberately. It is **not** in `package.json`, so `npm ls three` finds nothing and the app bundle is unaffected — this is why the bare `import 'three'` inside a `public/` page could break the dev server without any dependency existing (**#344**). It is **not** loaded from a CDN either: the page is self-contained, has no third-party runtime origin, and cannot be broken by someone else's outage or a CSP tightening.

**The coupling to watch.** `index.html` carries `<script type="importmap">{ "imports": { "three": "./vendor/three-0.160.1.module.min.js" } }</script>`. The version is **in the filename**, so an upgrade is: add the new vendor file, update that one importmap line, delete the old file. Change either half alone and the page fails at module-resolution time — with a blank canvas, since the failure happens before any of the app modules run.

**Why the 670 KB in git is accepted.** The page is a standalone marketing surface that must work as a unit; a pinned, reviewed binary was judged better than a floating dependency for something with no build step of its own. Treat it the way #342 treats a versioned external asset: the **name encodes the contract**, so the replacement gets a new name rather than an in-place overwrite. Cross-ref **#344** (the dev-server consequence of the bare import), **#342** (versioned names for assets code reads by name), **#347**. `see #348`.

---

**349. A monthly transfer's setup-link amount is a QUOTE AS OF THE DAY IT WAS COMPUTED, and it only ever goes UP — the admin preview, the setup email and the payment page can all disagree, and only the payment page is right (2026-08-10). (Membership / money.)** `transferLinkPulls(plan, basisDate)` is a function of the **basis date**, and each surface passes a different one: the Set Up Payments preview passes the admin's **browser today** (at form-fill time), `send-setup-link` passes **the day the email is drafted**, and `setup-load` / `setup-checkout` / `activate` pass **the day the member actually pays**. `remaining` is fixed at plan save (`12 − prior_payments_made`), but `slots` shrinks as the fixed renewal 15th approaches, so `pullsAtLink = 1 + max(0, remaining − slots)` grows with delay. Nothing is ever *charged* wrongly — the page and Stripe both recompute live — but the **email is a stale artifact the moment it is sent**.

**Worked example that produced this entry.** $8,000/yr, renewal `2027-01-15`, `prior_payments_made = 6` (so `remaining = 6`), quoted on 2026-08-10: pay **Aug 10–14** → 6 slots → **$667** at the link. Pay **Aug 15** → the would-be Jan 15 pull collides with the renewal and is dropped (see **#350**), 5 slots → **$1,334**. Pay **Aug 21** → after-the-15th rule moves the charge day to the 1st and skips September, still 5 slots → **$1,334**. Pay **Dec 16+** → 1 slot → the whole **$4,002** at the link. The total is invariant at `remaining × per_pull`; only the front-loading moves.

**What was done about it and what was NOT.** The admin preview now states "Based on payment today" and names the **cliff date** and the amount after it (`catchUpCliff` in `MembershipFeesPanel.jsx`), and the monthly email template gained a paragraph explaining that a later setup means a larger first payment. **The email still quotes a hard number computed at send time** — a `[Quote Valid Through]` token was considered and deliberately deferred. So if a transfer member sits on the link, expect the "why is it double?" call, and answer with this entry. Cross-ref **#350** (why the boundary slot vanishes), **#315** (the one-row-per-due-date rule the catch-up folds into). `see #349`.

---

**350. A transfer charge date landing EXACTLY on the renewal date is dropped by the `untilExclusive` cap, and that drop is LOAD-BEARING — making the boundary inclusive would permanently stall the renewal sweep (2026-08-10). (Membership / renewals.)** `subsequentChargeDates(payDate, { untilExclusive: renewal_date })` breaks on `d >= untilExclusive`, so a member who pays on the **15th** with a **15th** renewal loses that final slot — which is precisely why a day-15 payer tips into catch-up one slot earlier than a day-14 payer (**#349**).

**It looks like an off-by-one and it is not.** The renewal pass in `actions/membership/sweep.ts` guards with *"any `kind='membership'` row with `due_date >= renewal_date` means year 2 already exists"*. A year-1 pull sitting **on** the renewal date satisfies that guard, so the sweep would read year 1's closing charge as an already-generated year 2, skip the renewal forever, and the plan would quietly stop billing. `buildActivatedSchedule` therefore passes the renewal cap on **every** transfer branch, including the `count`-limited one. The same hazard is documented from the other direction for NEW plans in `renewalFromPayDate`, where a day-15 payer instead gets the 15th exactly 12 months out rather than a "strictly before" date.

**Rule: never relax this to `>`, and never drop the `untilExclusive` argument to "simplify" a branch that already passes a `count`.** The member is not short-changed — the dropped slot is absorbed as catch-up at the link, the year still totals 12 pulls, and year 2's first pull lands on the renewal date itself where it belongs. Cross-ref **#349**, **#235** (the renewal guard), **#315**. `see #350`.

---

**351. `transferLinkPulls` returns NULL for anything that is not a MONTHLY transfer, and a `?? 12` fallback on that null silently rendered monthly-shaped copy into the ANNUAL transfer email for as long as the feature existed (2026-08-10). (Membership / email.)** The helper's first line is `if (!plan?.transfer || plan?.frequency !== "monthly") return null`. `send-setup-link.ts` then did `const remainingCount = linkPulls?.remaining ?? 12` — correct for a brand-new monthly plan, nonsense for an annual transfer, which has exactly **one** payment left (the renewal charge). Worse, both transfer frequencies shared **one** template whose copy was written for monthly, so an annual transfer was told *"Your next payment of $8,000.00 is collected when you complete your setup"* when the link collects **nothing** — it is save-method-only and the annual lands at renewal. The admin preview said the opposite ("Nothing due now"), which is how the contradiction surfaced.

**Fixed 2026-08-10 (v714)** by splitting the template rather than patching tokens: `annualTransfer = transfer && frequency === 'annual'` selects **`MEMBERSHIP_transfer_setup_link|annual` (id 215)**, the fallback became `?? (annualTransfer ? 1 : 12)`, and the button label on any `saveOnly` link now reads *"Save Your Payment Method"* instead of promising a payment. **It was never observed in production** — `member_payment_plans` held exactly one row (a non-transfer annual) at the time, so no transfer setup email has ever been sent; the fix is reasoned from the code and the first real annual transfer is still its true test.

**The general shape to watch:** a monthly-only helper plus a `??` default is an invisible bug, because the default is a *plausible* number rather than an error. Any new caller of `transferLinkPulls` must decide what null means for **annual** and for **fully-credited $0** plans, not inherit a monthly constant. (The fully-credited monthly case still reads "$0.00 per month" and was knowingly left alone — accurate, merely odd, and unreachable today.) Cross-ref **#349**, **#325** (these rows are SEND mode, so nobody reviews them before they go out). `see #351`.

**352. A Payment Continuation setup link is TWO surfaces telling one story — the EMAIL and the `/connect-card` page it opens — and they are written in different files that nobody makes agree (2026-08-10, v715). (Money / email.)** `utils/migration-schedule.ts buildMigrationScheduleHtml()` writes the `[PAYMENT_SCHEDULE]` block of BOTH continuation emails (`email_templates` **172** `setup_link` + **209** `setup_link_reminder`), while `actions/migration/connect-load.ts` independently computes the `amount` / `amount_label` the landing page renders. Nothing links them: the email said *"Implementation fee — $2,000.00 — charged at the implementation stage of your engagement"* and the page said *"$2,000.00 — VFO Services payment (implementation fee)"* from two separate reads of `client_tax_plans.implementation_amount`. **Changing one and not the other produces a client-visible contradiction that no test, type or build catches.**

**What shipped 2026-08-10 (v715).** On the **TAX branch only**, both surfaces stopped naming a figure — the tax implementation fee is charged only *if* the client proceeds, so quoting it in a "save your payment method" email overstated a commitment. The email block became one fixed sentence (*"This is being set up proactively as a part of your VFO Services' membership and to collect any future payments."*), and `connect-load.ts` simply stopped reading `implementation_amount`, leaving `amount = 0` so the pre-existing `amount > 0 ? amount : null` serializer sends **`amount: null`**. **The frontend needed NO change**: `ConnectCardPage.jsx` already gates its headline figure (`{amount != null && …}`) and its whole `amountRow` breakdown — the "VFO Services payment (…)" line AND the "Processing Fee $0.00" line — on that one null, a path already exercised by tax rows with a blank amount. **MAP 1 is deliberately untouched on both surfaces** (its installments are real, scheduled and already agreed).

**Three properties worth remembering.** (a) **The page is rendered LIVE at click time** from `migration_connect_load`, so every already-emailed, unclicked, unexpired link self-corrected the moment v715 went live — no resend, no token reissue; the token is a lookup key, never a snapshot (same shape as **#331**). (b) **Display-only, provably:** `connect-checkout.ts` never reads an amount, mints `mode:"setup"` with no line items, and the charge sweeps read the DB columns later — so nothing about what anyone is eventually charged moved. (c) **The `implementation_amount` selects in the two callers became vestigial** and the TAX `cols` select was narrowed to `payment_method_type`; `row` is only otherwise used for the `done` flag. **Still divergent by design:** `outstanding-links.ts:169` keeps its "Implementation fee" label — that is the ADMIN Outstanding Payment Links panel, which should keep showing the money. **The MAP 1 half stopped being untouched on 2026-08-19 (v762) — see #421**, which gave the page a past-due total mirroring the charge sweep and the email a past-due/upcoming split; the "two surfaces, one story, nobody makes them agree" warning above is unchanged, but MAP 1 now has its own deliberate, documented divergence rather than parity. Cross-ref **#349** (a quote with a shelf life), **#300**, **#302**, **#421**. `see #352`. **ALWAYS-APPLIES (money / email).**

**353. Creating an advisor / accountant / strategic member is restricted to TWO hardcoded people, and the gate is FRONTEND-ONLY — the backend accepts it from any admin (2026-08-10, investigation). (Auth / frontend.)** `src/components/admin/MembersPanel.jsx:23`:

```js
const canAddMembers = () => { const s = getSession(); return !!s?.is_superadmin || (s?.email || '').toLowerCase() === 'tvaldes@elitert.com' }
```

Checked at **three** submit handlers — Add Accountant (:157), Add Advisor (:519), Add Strategic Member (:695) — each rendering a bare **"Access Denied"**. So the answer to "who can add an advisor" is **Jake (superadmin) + Tray (`tvaldes@elitert.com`)**, and any of the other 13 `allowed_admins` fills the entire form before being refused. **Adding a person is a one-line React edit + `npm run deploy`; the three surfaces share the one helper, so a name unlocks all three at once.**

**The trap is that this looks like a role-gate and is not one.** `add_member_full` sits in `ADMIN_ONLY_ACTIONS` and **nothing else** — no `TAB_ACTIONS` entry, no `SUPERADMIN_ONLY_ACTIONS` entry, and `actions/members/add-full.ts` carries no in-handler allowlist — so the **server would accept the call from all 15 admins**, and anyone posting the action directly bypasses the restriction entirely. Treat it as a guardrail against mis-clicks, **not** a security boundary; if it ever needs to be one, it must be mirrored into `role-gates.ts` (the #309 lesson: "not in a gate list" means ungated, not safe). **Two adjacent facts that are frequently confused with it:** (a) the **onboarding** pipelines are NOT restricted — `create_advisor_onboarding` / `create_accountant_onboarding` are plain `ADMIN_ONLY_ACTIONS`, so every admin can start an onboarding; and (b) the Advisors/Accountants nav dropdowns carry **no `canSeeTab` gate at all**, so everyone sees the menu and the form — the refusal only arrives at submit. Separately, the "Team Member Responsible" dropdown IS a real 7-name list (`SALES_TEAM_NAMES` in `NewModelSaleModal.jsx` mirrored by `TEAM_MEMBER_LOGIN_EMAILS` in `constants/onboarding-team.ts`) whose absence blocks the advisor/accountant decision buttons — a *different* list, kept in sync by hand (#291's shape). Cross-ref **#309**, **#167**, **#329**. `see #353`. **ALWAYS-APPLIES (auth / frontend).**

---

**354. "The passcode works right after a reset and fails after the next logout" is NOT a backend bug — it is the browser's password manager re-filling a stale entry it was never able to update, and the cause is a login form with no `name` / `id` / `autoComplete` metadata (2026-08-10, v716). (Frontend / auth.) ALWAYS-APPLIES.** A tax planner was locked out repeatedly across several password resets. The DB forensics cleared the backend completely: `hashPasscodeSalted` and `verifyPasscodeSalted` are symmetric, her reset provably wrote (`tax_planner_logins.passcode_hash` changed) and her fresh login provably succeeded (an `admin_sessions` row seconds after the token's `completed_at`), and `login_attempts` showed **7 genuine wrong-password failures** in an 18-minute window — a client sending the wrong string, not a server rejecting the right one.

**The mechanism.** All five login pages (`AdminLogin`, `ClientLogin`, `MemberLogin`, `SpecialistLogin`, `TaxPlannerLogin`) rendered their inputs with **no `id`, no `name` and no `autoComplete`** — only `type="email"` / `type="password"` and a React `value`/`onChange`. A password manager identifies a saved credential by origin **plus the field identity**; with nothing to key on it can store an entry (on the first save it guesses) but it can **never recognise the same field later to offer an update**. So after a reset it silently keeps re-filling the dead passcode, the user does not notice because the dots look identical, and the 5-failures-per-15-minutes throttle (#H1) then converts a fixable confusion into a hard lockout. The precedent was already on file and misread at the time: planner `cgrover` needed a second setup link the day after the first, same loop.

**The fix, and why the DOM-value fallback is part of it.** Every login input now carries `id` + `name` + `autoComplete="username"` / `"current-password"`; `SetPasswordPage` and `ChangePasswordCard` carry the matching `autoComplete="username"` (a clip-hidden `readOnly` field on the latter, because a change-password form with no username field is un-updatable for the same reason) alongside their existing `new-password` fields, and `ChangePasswordCard`'s wrapper became a real `<form>` with a `type="submit"` button — managers do not reliably offer to save on a `<div>` with an onClick. **Submit then reads `state || ref.current.value`**: an extension that sets `.value` directly without dispatching a React-visible event leaves `useState` empty, so the form would post a blank passcode; the ref makes the DOM the fallback source of truth. `setSession` now stores the email that **actually authenticated** rather than the state variable, for the same reason.

**The rule.** Any new credential form — login, set-password, change-password — must carry field identity (`id`/`name`/`autoComplete`) and read its values defensively. And when a lockout report says *"it works, then it stops working"*, check `login_attempts` for genuine wrong-password rows **before** looking at the hashing: real failures with a provably-good hash means the client is sending something stale, and the password manager is the first suspect. Cross-ref **#355** (the self-service reset built in the same session), **#258** (the login-type checklist), **#343** (the other case where Chrome's autofill classifier, not our code, drove the UI). `see #354`.

---

**355. `request_password_reset` is anti-enumeration by construction — it ALWAYS answers `{ success: true }`, behind a timing floor, after a throttle that counts REQUESTS rather than failures; every one of those three is load-bearing and none may be "improved" (2026-08-10, v716). (Auth / security.) ALWAYS-APPLIES.** The action (`actions/login-setup/request-reset.ts`, **PUBLIC**) is the backend of the `/forgot-password?type=` page. It is the first handler in the system whose entire job is to reveal nothing, so the usual instincts — a helpful error, an early return, a fast path — are all regressions here.

**The four invariants.** (a) **The response body is `{ success: true }` for every outcome past validation** — unknown address, disabled row, token insert failure, missing template, Gmail refusal. Failures `console.error` and fall through. The only non-success responses are the two pre-throttle argument checks (400 on a bad `login_type` or missing email) and the 429. (b) **A `MIN_RESPONSE_MS = 1200` floor** (`settle()`) covers every success path, because "not found" returns in milliseconds while "found" waits on a Gmail round trip — without the floor the timing is the oracle the identical body was written to remove. (c) **The throttle runs FIRST, on the `reset:`-prefixed identifier**, and records **every** request, hit or miss — 5 requests / 15 min. The prefix keeps reset traffic out of the login throttle's namespace so a reset storm cannot lock the same person's *sign-in*; counting requests rather than failures is what stops an attacker probing addresses. (d) **`admin` is not a valid `login_type`** (400) — admin resets stay manual on purpose, and the admin login page deliberately carries no "Forgot passcode?" link.

**Two behaviours that surprise people and are correct.** A successful request **expires (never deletes) every prior unused `login_setup_tokens` row for that person** — so an admin-sent **14-day** setup link dies the moment its recipient uses forgot-password, and the newest link always wins; the expired rows survive for audit. And the minted token lives **1 hour**, not 14 days, because a self-service reset is a live action while an admin invite is a scheduled one.

**Reading the shape.** This is the #331 principle applied to an unauthenticated endpoint: the response is one field, and widening it (an `exists` flag, a distinct "no such account" message, an error surfaced to the page for debugging) hands an attacker a user-enumeration oracle for four portals at once. The frontend cooperates — `ForgotPasswordPage.jsx` renders the same green confirmation even when the `fetch` itself throws. Cross-ref **#331** (never widen a token-page payload), **#354** (the lockout that motivated the feature), **#356** (why its template must stay `send_mode=true`), **#310**. `see #355`.

---

**356. A template's Draft/Send mode is keyed by `(pipeline, template_name)` in TWO independent places per consumer — the SELECT that loads the body and the `sendMode` argument passed to `draftGmail` — and if they disagree the mail is created and then sits in Drafts forever with no error anywhere (2026-08-10, v716). (Email / notifications.) ALWAYS-APPLIES.** `maybeSendDraft` (`utils/email-delivery.ts`) re-reads `email_templates` using the pipeline/name it is **handed**, not the row the handler loaded; a miss is treated as "no send_mode row → leave it as a draft", which is indistinguishable from a deliberately Draft template. This exact bug was caught in-session in `request-reset.ts`: the load selected `pipeline='LOGIN_SETUP'` while the `sendMode` argument said `pipeline: 'LOGIN'`, so the reset email was drafted correctly and never sent. Nothing catches it — not `deno check` (both are strings), not a build, not a runtime error, and not the admin UI, which shows the template as "Send".

**The second half of the same trap: `send_mode=false` on a template a HANDLER depends on.** Most auto-send rows back an admin-triggered action, so Draft mode merely inserts a human. But where the *sender* is a public or sweep handler — `request_password_reset` is the first (#325 row 216) — nobody is watching the Drafts folder and the caller is told nothing, so Draft mode is a silent outage that presents as "the email never arrived". Seed such a row with `send_mode=true` in its migration and treat the flag as code, not preference.

**The check, for any new templated email.** Grep the handler for the template name and confirm it appears **twice with identical pipeline + name** — once in the `.select()` filter chain, once in `sendMode: { supabase, pipeline, templateName }` (or as the last two arguments of `gmailDraftFetch`). Then confirm the row actually exists with that exact pair and `active=true`. When an auto-send "didn't arrive", check Gmail Drafts **first**: a draft sitting there means the mail was built fine and the send-mode lookup missed, which points at these two strings rather than at Gmail. Cross-ref **#325** (the roster and what auto-send means), **#181** (every templated email routes through this layer), **#324** (the other silent-drop in the same delivery path — an unresolved recipient token). `see #356`.
---

**357. The ROI hand-off chain's bell TITLES are load-bearing strings shared by four handlers — three action-required bells are raised in one file and cleared by title prefix in three others, so rewording a title (or raising one of them from a new site) leaves a permanently un-clearable bell (2026-08-10, v717). (Notifications / tax track.) ALWAYS-APPLIES.** The tax track now has a deliberate hand-off sequence: a **Team Member** is allocated to a plan first, Tray books the ROI meeting, the Team Member replaces themselves with a **Tax Planner**, and that planner completes the assess form. Each leg is an action-required bell (`dismissible:false`), which per **#179** `mark_notification_read` REFUSES — so the ONLY way any of them ever disappears is a completing handler writing `read=true` directly, and every one of those writes matches on **`pipeline='TAX'` + `client_id` + `dismissible=false` + `read=false` + `title ILIKE '<prefix>%'`**.

**The three titles and who owns each end.**

| Title prefix | Raised by | Cleared by |
|---|---|---|
| `Book the ROI meeting for` | `allocate-planner.ts` — a Team Member is allocated and the ROI email has not gone out (rule `TAX_book_roi_meeting`, to `tvaldes@elitert.com`) | `ready-for-tax3.ts`, on **either** decision (confirm or decline) |
| `Allocate a Tax Planner for` | `allocate-planner.ts` (Team Member allocated **after** the ROI email already went out) **and** `ready-for-tax3.ts` (confirm_date while a Team Member holds the plan) — rule `TAX_team_member_allocate_planner` | `allocate-planner.ts`, on **any** allocation change |
| `Enter the tax planning opportunities for` | `allocate-planner.ts` — a Tax Planner is allocated while `assess_form_submitted_at` is null (rule `TAX_planner_assess_needed`) | `allocate-planner.ts` (any allocation change) **and** `save-assess-form.ts` (on submit) |

**The properties that make it work, none of which are obvious.** (a) **`allocate-planner.ts` clears BEFORE it re-fires**, and it clears on **any** genuine change including a clear-to-nobody — so a re-allocation never leaves the previous holder chasing a step that is no longer theirs. (b) **The chain branches on `tax_planners.planner_role` read at allocation time** (#294 as amended), and the Team Member branch further splits on whether the ROI email is already out (`ready_for_tax3_email_sent='Yes'` AND `ready_for_tax3_decision='Yes'`) — allocate a coordinator to a plan whose meeting is already booked and you skip Tray's leg entirely. (c) **`notifyAllocatedPlanner` gained an optional `dismissible`** (default `true`, so all eight pre-existing planner bells are byte-identical); the two chain bells pass `false`. (d) **Every leg is fail-soft** — the whole block is one try/catch and a bell failure can never fail an allocation, an email that has already been drafted, or a submitted form.

**The rule.** Treat these three strings as an API. Reword one and you must reword its clear in the same commit; raise one from a new handler and you must either reuse the exact prefix or ship the matching clear. There is no compile-time protection, and a stuck action-required bell is invisible to whoever *didn't* get it. Cross-ref **#179** (why the clear must be a direct write), **#272** (client-named titles, and why dedupe is title-keyed), **#291** (a bell recipient lives in four layers), **#294**, **#358**. `see #357`.

---

**358. The Tax 2 "ROI Meeting booked" step now REQUIRES a real meeting date and PERSISTS the date/time/timezone on the plan — `confirm_no_date` is dead, and the pill, the reminder sweep and the reminder email all read the persisted trio rather than the progress status (2026-08-10, v717). (Tax track.) ALWAYS-APPLIES.** `automation_TAX_readyfortax3` (sentinel `tax_3_decision`, `program_client_tasks` **90** program 1 / **124** program 4) used to offer three buttons cloned from the MAP 1 PIP pattern. It now offers two: **Send email (with date)** and the unchanged red decline. `decision:"confirm_no_date"` returns **400 `"A meeting date is required"`** — kept as a named branch precisely so an old client bundle gets a legible refusal instead of a generic one — and `confirm_date` 400s the same way unless `meeting_date` matches `^\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}$`.

**What is stored, and why it matters more than the status string.** On a successful confirm the handler writes `client_tax_plans.tax3_meeting_date` / `tax3_meeting_time` / `tax3_meeting_timezone` alongside the existing `ready_for_tax3_*` stamps (the decline path never touches them — a declined plan has no meeting). Those three columns are the ONLY source for the step's "Meeting booked for M/D/YYYY at h:mm AM TZ" pill, for the nightly sweep's assess-reminder window (**#359**) and for that email's `[Meeting Date]` / `[Meeting Time]` / `[Meeting Timezone]` tokens. **Legacy rows keep working, display-only:** plans confirmed before v717 have no `tax3_meeting_*` values, so the pill falls back to the stored progress status, and `'Yes - Confirmation email (date TBC)'` is deliberately retained in the FE `statusColors` map for exactly those rows. Do not clean it out.

**The step also moved and was renamed, both DML.** Name `"Tax 3 Confirmation Email"` → **`"ROI Meeting booked - Send Confirmation Email"`**, and `task_order` **3 → 0**, putting it FIRST in `Tax 2 - Deeper Dive` (the same `task_order=0` convention the Tax 4 confirmation step uses). Per **#339** that reorder is positional and retroactive: plans that passed the step un-completed now show a skipped-step warning on it — reviewed and accepted. The FE matches the step by **sentinel first**, which is what made the rename safe to apply before the frontend deploy. The allocation guard on the confirm path was reworded to *"Allocate a Team Member / Tax Planner before sending the confirmation email"* because either role may now hold the plan (#294 as amended). Cross-ref **#357** (the bells this handler clears and raises), **#339** (positional warnings), **#359**. `see #358`.

---

**359. `TAX_tax3_assess_reminder_email`'s `delay_days` means days BEFORE the booked meeting — it is the only rule in the system whose delay counts down rather than up — and the sweep deliberately skips WITHOUT stamping its guard on the two failure modes it expects to recover from (2026-08-10, v717). (Tax track / sweeps.)** A sixth timer block joined `actions/tax/revshare-sweep.ts` (cron `tax-revshare-sweep-daily`, 02:30 UTC). It selects plans where `tax3_meeting_date` is set and falls **between today and today + delayDays** (default **2**), `assess_form_submitted_at` is null, `tax3_assess_reminder_sent_at` is null and a planner is allocated, then drafts ONE reminder to that planner. Every other `notification_rules` delay in the system is "N days since the trigger"; this one is "N days before the meeting", and the Notification Editor cannot express that difference — the rule's `description` says so, and that sentence is the only warning a future editor gets.

**The three deliberate behaviours.** (a) **Skip-without-stamping.** A missing planner email or a failed Gmail auth `console.warn`s and `continue`s **without** writing `tax3_assess_reminder_sent_at`, so the row is retried the next night once the cause is fixed; only a successful draft stamps. (b) **The Cc cannot be expressed as a role token.** The reminder is supposed to reach the planner's own group Team Members, and no `RECIPIENT_ROLE_TOKENS` entry names that set — so the sweep computes it (same `member_type`, `planner_role='Team Member'`, Active, minus self) and passes it as `resolveTemplateRecipients`' **extraCc** argument, while `ASSIGNED_PF` resolves the ordinary way through the handler ctx (#324). **Sandbox suppresses all Cc**, so a sandbox test can never prove that list — it is code-reviewed only, exactly the #324 shape that hid the payment-continuation Cc bug for its entire life. (c) **`email_templates` id 217 ships `send_mode=false` (Draft)** by explicit user choice: the sweep builds the mail and a human sends it from Gmail Drafts. That is a *decision*, not the #356 outage — the distinction being that here somebody is expected to be watching. Cross-ref **#356** (the two-place `(pipeline, template_name)` key — `gmailDraftFetch` is passed `"TAX"`, `"TAX_tax3_assess_reminder"` to match the row it loaded), **#324**, **#358**, **#176**/**#178**. (2026-08-20: a SECOND countdown tier now exists — `TAX_tax3_assess_reminder_early_email`, default 5 business days, guard `tax3_assess_reminder_early_sent_at` — so this is no longer the only rule that counts down; the 2-day tier's window is unchanged and the early tier's is the half-open range past it, `(2-day horizon, early horizon]`, which is what keeps them off the same night.) `see #359`.

---

**360. Per-specialist notes open a NEW `client_notes.phase_name` family — `'Specialist - <name>'` — and the per-specialist Strategy beside them is ADMIN-ONLY by deliberate omission from the planner allowlist (2026-08-10, v718). (Tax track.)** Two small features share one specialist card and behave very differently.

**Notes.** The Tax 5 specialist card and the Tax 6 specialist card each carry the shared `PhaseNotesButton` / `PhaseNotesPanel`, and both write to **one thread per specialist** keyed `phase_name = 'Specialist - ' + spec.specialist_name` (helper `specPhaseName`, `tabName` `'Tax Priorities'`, expand keys `notes_spec_<id>` / `notes_tax6_spec_<id>`). This extends **#260**'s "phase names are stored in `client_notes`" to a family that is **not** a phase name at all, so a consumer enumerating phase names off `program_client_phases` will not find these threads. They surface on the client profile with a `Specialist - <name>` chip like any other. **Renaming a specialist row would orphan its thread** — safe today only because `client_tax_specialists.specialist_name` is fixed at insert (`tax_add_specialist` owns the `Custom - ` prefix, #311/#305) and nothing updates it. Planners get the buttons (gated on `!readOnly`, and the three note write actions were already planner-callable per **#273**).

**Strategy.** `client_tax_specialists.strategy` (text, nullable) is written by exactly one action — **`tax_update_specialist_strategy`** (`actions/tax/update-specialist-strategy.ts`), in `ADMIN_ONLY_ACTIONS` and **deliberately NOT in `TAX_PLANNER_ALLOWED_ACTIONS`**, which per **#309** is the whole confinement. It follows the remove-specialist guard pattern — resolve the row by id, then **verify the row's REAL `tax_plan_id` matches the body's** before writing (#257) — trims and caps at 500 chars and stores `null` for empty. The FE renders an input in the Tax 5 card header for admins only (`!readOnly && !plannerMode`, saves on blur/Enter, skips an unchanged value) and a muted truncated span everywhere else, **including Tax 6, which mirrors it read-only and must never gain an editor**. Because the save updates `taxSpecialists` state, the Tax 6 mirror is live without a reload. Cross-ref **#311** (Tax 6 is per-specialist), **#305** (specialist naming + the delete hazard), **#260**, **#273**, **#309**. `see #360`.

---

**365. An action-required bell is a CONTRACT with three obligations — a clear site for every path it can be satisfied by, a satisfied-on-fire guard, and a title nobody edits — and `notification_rules.action_required` is DISPLAY-ONLY, so it can never be the thing that makes a bell action-required (2026-08-11, v723). (Notifications. ALWAYS-APPLIES.)** `mark_notification_read` refuses `dismissible=false` rows (#179), so the ONLY way one ever retires is a handler running `notifications.update({read:true})` matched on **pipeline + `client_id` + `dismissible=false` + `read=false` + `title ILIKE '<prefix>%'`**. Three consequences that are easy to get wrong and expensive to discover:

**(a) The `action_required` column is documentation, not behaviour.** `utils/notify.ts` computes `dismissible: args.dismissible !== false` from the CALL SITE and deliberately never lets a rule row override it. Flipping `action_required` in a migration or the Notification Editor changes what a human reads in the Editor and nothing else; the code's `dismissible:false` is the whole mechanism. Keep the two in step anyway — a rule that says FYI while its call site mints action-required is a trap for the next reader.

**(b) A bell whose instruction is ALREADY carried out must not be minted.** No clear site will run a second time, so such a bell is unretirable without DB surgery. Every mint in the tax review chain therefore carries a satisfied-on-fire guard: `save-task.ts` skips Tray's review bell when its outcome is already satisfied (program-4 Proceed → Green/Red already `'Proceed'` **and** the ROI meeting already booked; program-4 Stop → `deposit_refund_status='succeeded'`; program-1 Proceed → ROI booked; program-1 Stop → `ready_for_tax3_decision='No'`), the two vault handlers skip the planner ask once the review step is answered, and `upload-notify.ts` skips the Tracy/Tray ask when every plan the upload stamped already has a planner. Ask "what would clear this, and has it already happened?" before every `dismissible:false` insert.

**(c) TWO rule keys may deliberately share ONE title.** The key controls routing and enable-ability in the Notification Editor; the TITLE controls clearing. `TAX_planner_review_step_needed` and `TAX_planner_additional_info_uploaded` are two different triggers carrying the same instruction, so they share the title `Complete the tax planner review for <Client>` and one clear site retires whichever fired. That is a feature, not a collision — but it means renaming a title is a two-key change, and it does NOT relieve you of #313 (two audiences still need two keys). Cross-ref **#179**, **#176**, **#313**, **#357** (the same title-as-API lesson one session earlier), **#366**. `see #365`.

---

**366. The tax front half is now ONE continuous action-required chain from the returns landing to the ROI presentation hand-off, and `utils/tax-review-bell.ts` is the single place its title prefixes and clear sites are written down (2026-08-11, v723). (Tax track. ALWAYS-APPLIES.)** "Tax 1 - Diagnostic" order in BOTH programs is now **1 Request Tax Returns, 2 Allocate Team Member / Tax Planner, 3 Additional information required, 4-6 the three info steps, 7 Tax planner review complete**, then program 1 ends with Client risk profile (8) while program 4 has Green/Red Light (8) then Client risk profile (9). The reorder was DML on existing ids (175/84/172/118 for the swap), so no `client_tax_progress` selection moved — but per **#339** `computeTrack` warnings are POSITIONAL and therefore retroactive on existing plans, which was reviewed and accepted.

**The NEW step** is `Tax planner review complete` (`program_client_tasks` **176** program 1 / **177** program 4), a plain dropdown — `status_options 'Proceed with tax planning|Stop tax planning'`, **no sentinel** — so `overview-tax.ts` scores it through the generic terminal `else` (status saved = done). It is on BOTH planner-editable whitelists per **#262**.

**The chain, trigger → recipient → title → what clears it.** Returns uploaded → **Tracy + Tray**, `Allocate a team member for%` (`TAX_returns_allocate_team_member`), cleared when an allocation is SET. Team Member allocated → **that Team Member**, `Allocate a Tax Planner for%` (`TAX_team_member_allocate_planner`), cleared by the re-allocation sweep — and the old roiConfirmed/not split is COLLAPSED, a Team Member allocation always produces exactly this one bell. Tax Planner allocated with the review unanswered → **that planner**, `Review the tax returns for%` (`TAX_planner_review_needed`), cleared when **"Additional information required"** is answered. That step answered `'No additional info required'` → **planner**, `Complete the tax planner review for%` (`TAX_planner_review_step_needed`); answered `'Additional info required'` → nothing until the client actually supplies it, then the SAME title from `TAX_planner_additional_info_uploaded` (#365c) via BOTH `upload-notify.ts` and `tax-text-submit.ts`; both cleared when the review step is answered. Review answered → **Tray**, `Tax Planner review complete for%`, four variants all action-required (program-4 Proceed clears on Green/Red `'Proceed'` AND ROI booked, whichever lands second; program-4 Stop on the deposit refund succeeding; program-1 Proceed on the ROI booking; program-1 Stop on a declined ROI decision, which `ready-for-tax3.ts` now clears UNCONDITIONALLY). Review answered Proceed ALSO fires, in the same save, the planner's `Enter the tax planning opportunities for%` (`TAX_planner_assess_needed`). First assess submission → the **assigned PF**, `Download the ROI presentation for%` (`TAX_assess_completed_pf`, repurposed from an FYI), naming the allocated planner, cleared when `presentation_email_sent_at` is stamped by `presentation-sweep.ts` — **the only site that stamps it** (`presentation-schedule.ts` only NULLs it, so re-scheduling after the email went out will not re-mint the bell, because the mint is first-submission-only).

**Two removals of record.** The allocation-time `TAX_book_roi_meeting` fire is GONE (Tray's booking prompt now rides the review step's Proceed) and the old `TAX_returns_received` FYI has no call site; **both rule rows are kept and marked DORMANT** for rollback and Editor visibility, and the `'Book the ROI meeting for%'` CLEAR in `ready-for-tax3.ts` **stays** because unread bells of that shape still exist in production. Bell **messages** quote step names (`complete the "Additional information required" step`); **titles do not** — adding quotes to a title strands live bells. Cross-ref **#365**, **#357** (the chain this supersedes), **#339**, **#262**, **#294**. `see #366`.

---

**367. The "ROI Meeting booked" step's No button is a PROGRAM-GATED FRONTEND affordance, and it is Holistic's only stop route (2026-08-11). (Tax track / frontend.)** `TaxPrioritiesTab.jsx` gates the "No - Declined email to client" button on `(plan.program_id || 1) === 1`, so **Holistic (program 1) keeps it and VFO Tax Planning (program 4) does not** — program 4 stops through the Green/Red Light step's $500 deposit refund instead, and offering both routes on one program was the redundancy this removed. The gate is **frontend-only by deliberate choice**: `automation_TAX_readyfortax3` still accepts `decision='declined'` from any program, so this is a guardrail rather than a boundary (the **#353** shape) and an old cached bundle degrades to "the button is there and works" rather than to a confusing server error. Two couplings to keep in mind: the program-1 Stop variant of the review bell (#366) **instructs Tray to use this very button**, so removing it from Holistic would strand that bell; and `confirm_no_date` remains dead per **#358**. `see #367`.

---

**368. A notification's message must be VISIBLE, not a hover tooltip — the bell dropdown hid every message in `title=` until 2026-08-11. (Frontend / notifications.)** `src/components/NotificationBell.jsx` rendered only `n.title` in each row and put `${n.title}\n\n${n.message}` in the wrapper's `title` attribute, so the actual instruction — the part naming the step to complete — was invisible unless the user happened to hover. It now renders `n.message` on its own wrapping lines under the title (`whiteSpace:'pre-wrap'`, `overflowWrap:'anywhere'`, the row switched to `alignItems:'flex-start'` so the Done button stays top-aligned) and the redundant `title` attribute is gone. The admin Notifications page (`NotificationsPage.jsx`) always did this correctly — the bell was the outlier. **Any new notification surface must show the full message**: bells now carry multi-sentence instructions naming steps (#366), and a truncated one is functionally a blank bell. `see #368`.

---

**369. Tax-track completion dates are DISPLAY-ONLY, enforced by a module-level component that SHADOWS the shared editable `StepDate` inside `TaxPrioritiesTab.jsx` (2026-08-11). (Tax track / frontend.)** Both tax programs' dates are now read-only 55px right-aligned `MM/DD` spans. The mechanism is deliberately blunt: the file's `import StepDate from '../../shared/StepDate'` was **removed** and a module-level `function StepDate({ value })` defined in its place, so all ~10 existing call sites (which still pass `onChange`/`disabled`) render read-only with no per-site edits and no risk of missing one. **Re-adding that import silently makes every tax date editable again.** Other tracks (MAP 1, onboarding, PFT) still import the real editable control from `components/shared/StepDate` and are unaffected — this shadow is scoped to the one file. Same change gave `Allocate Team Member / Tax Planner` a date it never rendered (the progress row always stored one), blank until someone is actually allocated, in both the admin and read-only views. Cross-ref **#242**/**#243** (the editable-date feature this narrows), **#249**. `see #369`.

---

**370. For a ONE-TIME SpecRev ACH payment, `checkout.session.completed` means SUBMITTED, not PAID — the PaymentIntent's own `status` is the only truth, and the row now has a fourth state for it (2026-08-11). (SpecRev / Stripe. ALWAYS-APPLIES.)** The handler at `router/webhooks.ts` already fetched the PI (to read `payment_method`) and threw `status` away, writing `payment_status='processing'` for every non-card path. A specialist who types their account and routing numbers instead of signing in to their bank leaves the PI in **`requires_action` / `verify_with_microdeposits`** — Stripe's dashboard renders this as **"Incomplete — The customer must complete additional steps"**, which is its GENERIC `requires_action` tooltip, not microdeposit-specific wording. No debit has been attempted. The row said "processing" and template 168 told them *"we have received your payment"*; both were false. Found live on Jackie Meyer's $756.07 (request 27, `pi_3U3DfaRwdhysCa6F1NOiX71L`).

**The state machine now:** `requested` → on `checkout.session.completed`, branch on `srPi.status` — card or an already-`succeeded` ACH → `received` + invoice/receipt + payout; `requires_action` → **`awaiting_verification`** + the `SPECREV_payment_verify_bank` email (**218**); anything else → `processing` + the old confirmation. Then `payment_intent.processing` advances `awaiting_verification` → `processing`, and `payment_intent.succeeded` → `received`. **The `succeeded` guard MUST accept `awaiting_verification` as well as `processing`** — see **#371**.

**This does NOT contradict #298.** That gotcha is about RECURRING plans, where an unverified bank surfaces at charge day through `invoice.payment_failed`, and it correctly says not to add a gate. The one-time path had **no failure surface at all** (see **#372**), which is the whole difference. Likewise the v667 decision to stop pinning `payment_method_options[us_bank_account][verification_method]='instant'` stands — manual entry is deliberately allowed because `instant` locked out banks Financial Connections doesn't support. **Do not re-pin `instant` to "fix" this**; the fix is telling the truth about the resulting state. All four link-bearing SpecRev templates (164/170/188/208) now carry a one-line tip steering people to bank sign-in, and 208's old sentence actively recommending manual entry was removed.

Routing note: every SpecRev webhook block resolves the request by `stripe_customer_id` with `.maybeSingle()`. **One Stripe customer per request is an UNENFORCED invariant** — true in the data today (verified: zero duplicates), but if `send-request.ts` ever starts reusing a customer across requests, every one of these blocks breaks at once, not just the new ones. `see #370`.

---

**371. Adding a value to a status column means auditing EVERY guard that ENUMERATES that column — a `=== 'processing'` check is a trap door, not a filter (2026-08-11). (ALWAYS-APPLIES.)** Introducing `payment_status='awaiting_verification'` (#370) silently broke the ACH clearance path on first write: `payment_intent.succeeded` was gated on `srReq.payment_status === "processing"`, so a verified payment would have settled in Stripe while the row sat in the new state **forever** — replacing one silent hole with another. Caught by walking the state machine, not by the type checker, because these are string comparisons.

**The audit list for `specialist_revenue_requests.payment_status`:** `router/webhooks.ts` (the `succeeded` and `processing` guards), `actions/specialist-revenue/checkout.ts` (re-pay block), `pay-load.ts` (`alreadyPaid`), `payout-sweep.ts` (payout gate `=== 'received'`, reminder ladders `=== 'requested'`), and on the frontend `specialistRevenueShared.jsx` (`REQ_STATUS` map) + `SpecialistRevenueAutomationPanel.jsx` (the `awaiting` bucket) + `SpecialistRevenuePanel.jsx` + `SpecialistReconciliationPanel.jsx`. **Money-critical consumers that key off `=== 'received'` are safe by construction** — a new status is excluded from payouts and revenue totals automatically, which is the right default. **`REQ_STATUS` has a graceful fallback** (unknown status renders its raw name in grey), so a missed frontend entry degrades to ugly rather than broken — do not mistake that for "the frontend doesn't need updating". `see #371`.

---

**372. `notifyByRule` has NO `actionRequired` argument — `dismissible` IS the action-required control, and the rule row cannot override it (2026-08-11). (Notifications.)** Passing `actionRequired: true` is a **`deno check` TS2353 failure**, which is how this was caught. `NotifyByRuleArgs.dismissible` carries an explicit comment: the rule does NOT override it, because action-required rows are cleared by a completing handler and flipping one to FYI would break that contract. So `dismissible:false` = action-required (needs a clear site), `dismissible:true` = FYI with a Done button. This is the code-side counterpart of the already-documented fact that **`notification_rules.action_required` is DISPLAY-ONLY** (#365/#366).

**The judgement call that follows:** only make a bell action-required if something actually clears it. `SPECREV_payment_failed_bell` was first seeded `action_required=true` while the code passed `dismissible:true` — self-contradictory, and nothing auto-clears a dead payment. Both sides were corrected to dismissible/false, matching `SPECREV_recurring_payment_failed_bell`. `SPECREV_transfer_failed` is action-required precisely because it auto-clears on a successful retry. `see #372`.

---

**373. `email_templates` has NO `updated_at` column, and `apply_migration` can silently no-op an INSERT — verify the ROW, never the success return (2026-08-11). (DB / tooling.)** Two separate traps hit in one session.
