First, `update email_templates set body = …, updated_at = now()` fails with **42703 column does not exist** — the table has only `id, pipeline, template_name, subject, body, active, created_at, cc_list, bcc_list, to_list, send_mode`. Its unique key is **`(pipeline, template_name)`**, which is the correct `on conflict` target.

Second and worse: an `insert … on conflict do nothing` into `email_templates` issued through MCP **`apply_migration` returned `{"success": true}` and inserted nothing**, while two `notification_rules` inserts in the SAME migration applied normally. The identical statement then succeeded through `execute_sql` (id 218). Root cause never established — the sequence was healthy (217, next 218), no trigger, no conflicting row, and the non-ASCII-mangling theory was **disproved** (the earlier four-template migration's em/en dashes all survived intact). **Practical rule: after any `apply_migration` that seeds rows, SELECT them back.** A success return is not evidence the DML applied; the migration ledger will record the file as applied either way, so a replay on a fresh DB and the live DB can silently diverge. `see #373`.

---

**374. The generated ROI deck is 27 OR 29 SLIDES and `assess_form.year2` IS THE ONLY THING THAT DECIDES — removing a slide means four coordinated edits, not one, and the single-year path ALSO rewrites slide 24's geometry (2026-08-12, v729/v3 — **AMENDED IN PLACE 2026-08-17 when the deck went unconditionally single-year at a fixed 27 slides, AGAIN 2026-08-18/v759 when TWO-YEAR MODE RETURNED, and AGAIN 2026-08-27/v795 when template v6 added a SECOND, INDEPENDENT mode — the FEE mode — that changes this one's shape count; NOT renumbered**). ALWAYS-APPLIES (tax track). Extends #336/#342.**

**THE CONDITIONAL DROP IS BACK, BUT THE CONDITION IS COMPLETELY DIFFERENT FROM THE ORIGINAL ONE.** As of 2026-08-18 the branch is `const twoYear = !!form.year2 && typeof form.year2 === "object" && !Array.isArray(form.year2)` — **the mere PRESENCE of the nested `year2` group** (#306). Nothing else: no flag, no column, no per-strategy arithmetic. `year2` absent → **single-year, 27 slides**; present → **two-year, 29 slides, nothing dropped**. The 2026-08-17 instruction *"do not reintroduce a conditional drop"* is **HISTORY and no longer applies** — but read the next paragraph before you touch it, because the shape of the condition is exactly what went wrong the first time.

**THE ORIGINAL CONDITION, AND WHY ITS SHAPE MATTERED.** The v3-era test was `singleYear = form.strategies.every(s => num(gross_y2) === 0 && num(invest_y2) === 0)` — **PER-STRATEGY and deliberately NOT on the sums**, because offsetting `+5000` / `−5000` year-2 entries sum to zero while still describing a genuine two-year plan. It died with the strategy list. It is recorded here so a reader who finds `singleYear` in an old bundle, an old deck or an old doc knows what it meant — **and as the standing warning that a "is this two-year?" test computed from AMOUNTS can be fooled, which is precisely why the current test asks about the PRESENCE OF A GROUP instead.** Do not "improve" it into a sum, a total, or a truthiness check on the amounts.

**THE WHOLE DROP BLOCK IS GUARDED BY ONE `if (!twoYear)`, AND IT COVERS TWO SEPARATE SURGERIES.** In single-year mode the handler both (a) removes `slide28.xml` and `slide29.xml` from the package and (b) runs `dropInvestYearsHeader()` on `slide24.xml` — deleting the `{{INVEST_YEARS}}` header shape and shifting the left-column shapes below it up 539650 EMU (#336) — **12 of them on a 2-payment deck, 14 on a 3-payment one, because template v6's third fee row sits in that region and is only present in 3-payment mode. THIS SURGERY IS NO LONGER THE ONLY ONE ON SLIDE 24, AND IT MUST RUN SECOND** (see #453): the fee-row surgery goes first and the expected shape count is passed in from the fee mode. **Year mode and fee mode are INDEPENDENT — all four combinations are legal.** **Both go together**: template v4 carries the header, so a deck that dropped the appendix slides without the slide-24 surgery would render a year line on a plan with no per-year breakdown, and one that did the surgery without the drops would ship 29 slides with a hole. In two-year mode **neither** runs. `SLIDE_PARTS` still lists 28/29 in BOTH modes so their tokens substitute either way, keeping the leftover-token guard uniform — do not make that list conditional. **As of template v5 / v761 the loop and the guard actually run over `TOKEN_PARTS` (= `SLIDE_PARTS` + `ppt/notesSlides/notesSlide23.xml`), which is NINE parts; the notes part is dropped in neither mode**, because what single-year removes are the two appendix SLIDES and those never had a notes part (#336).

**IN SINGLE-YEAR MODE, BOTH `ppt/slides/slide28.xml` (year 1, display position 25) and `ppt/slides/slide29.xml` (year 2, display position 26) are DROPPED from the output zip** (in two-year mode neither is — the block is skipped entirely). A .pptx keeps a slide's identity in four places and PowerPoint calls the file corrupt if any one of them disagrees, so all four go together **per slide**: the `<p:sldId>` in `ppt/presentation.xml`, the `<Relationship>` in `ppt/_rels/presentation.xml.rels`, the `<Override>` in `[Content_Types].xml`, and the part itself plus its `slideN.xml.rels` in the zip. **The rId is resolved dynamically** — find the relationship whose `Target="slides/slideN.xml"`, read its `Id`, then find the `<p:sldId>` carrying that `r:id`. It is never hardcoded, because rIds are template-assigned and a rebuild can renumber them. **Each of the three XML patches asserts EXACTLY ONE match and returns a loud 500 naming the part, the slide and the count found** — the alternative is a silently corrupt deck that only fails when a human opens it.

**THE TRAP IN DROPPING TWO SLIDES INSTEAD OF ONE (2026-08-17).** The two removals edit the **same three shared parts**. Each shared part must therefore be read out of the zip **exactly once**, with the progressively-patched string threaded through both iterations of the loop; reading `entries[PRES]` afresh for the second slide silently discards the first slide's edit and ships a deck whose `presentation.xml` still points at a part that is no longer in the package — a corrupt file that passes every assertion on the way out. Only the final strings are written into `patched`.

**The consequences for anyone downstream.** (a) **A generated deck's slide count is NOT fixed — it is 27 or 29** (the template in the bucket always has all 29; only the single-year output is trimmed). **Read `assess_form.year2` on the plan before assuming either shape**, and never assume fixed display positions after display 24. (b) **A SINGLE-YEAR deck is year-free; a TWO-YEAR deck is not.** The 2026-08-17 claim that *no year appears anywhere on a generated deck* is **HISTORY as of 2026-08-18** — it now holds for the single-year branch only. On a two-year deck slide 24's header reads *"Your investment: 2026 + 2027"* and both appendix slides carry `YEAR`/`YEAR2`, so **a two-year deck DOES go stale in January**; regenerating it changes the years. The handler has always called `new Date().getFullYear()`, in every mode. (c) The two-mode (28 vs 29 slide) behaviour this gotcha originally documented was generated live and number-verified before its own ship; the always-27 behaviour is a strict simplification of that verified path.

---

**375. Accountants DO have a `revenue_decision` as of 2026-08-12 (v730) — the carve-out that suppressed it in four places is gone, and the docs that explain WHY the accountant webhook has no revshare leg are now wrong about the reason (2026-08-12). ALWAYS-APPLIES (members).** For two and a half months the system encoded "accountants don't have a revenue decision" in four independent places: `add_member_full` forced `null` when `member_category==='accountant'`; `automation_ACCOUNTANT_createmember` wrote nothing; `AccountantsPanel` passed `hiddenFields={['revenue_decision']}` so the profile hid it; and `AddAccountantForm` had no field for it. **All four were removed.** `add_member_full` now defaults `'Revenue Share'` uniformly, the onboarding handler writes `'Money Mapping'` on **both** the `members` insert and the `accountant_onboarding` update (mirroring the advisor pipeline — `accountant_onboarding` already had the column, so no DDL), the profile Details/Edit views are byte-identical to the advisor ones, and the Add form carries the advisor form's required select (*"Please pick a revenue decision."*).

**The behaviour change is smaller than the diff, and knowing why matters.** The revshare engines already coalesce `NULL → 'Revenue Share'`, so every pre-existing accountant was **already being treated as** Revenue Share. A DML backfill (`20260812150000_accountant_revenue_decision_backfill.sql`, applied live) made that explicit — **313 accountants → `'Revenue Share'`, 7 → `'Money Mapping'`** (the implementation types `'Implementation - VFO FT (Direct)'` / `'(Advisor)'`), zero NULL left among the 320. **So the only real behaviour change is those 7 implementation-type accountants flipping to money-mapping.** Note 8 NULL rows survive elsewhere (3 advisor + 5 uncategorised) — **consumers must still coalesce NULL, do not treat the column as NOT NULL.**

**The trap this leaves for the next reader.** Several docs justified "no revshare chain on accountant payment" with *"accountants don't have a `revenue_decision`"*. That premise is now false while the conclusion is still true — **the accountant webhook branch has no revshare leg by design and VFO holds the share internally.** The populated column is not a signal to add one. The reason sentences in `flows/stripe-webhook.md` and `architecture/03-edge-functions.md` were rewritten accordingly. **One asymmetry was left standing on purpose-of-scope:** `MemberPortal.jsx:373` still hides the field from an accountant viewing their OWN portal (`{!isAccountant && …}`) — parity was taken to the admin surfaces only.

---

**376. A session's FINAL docs commit left unmerged is a standing trap on the NEXT session — the wrap-up records deploys in SESSION_REFERENCE, so an unmerged docs commit makes `main` claim "DEPLOYED NOTHING" while the git tags say the opposite. Wrap-up completion means the docs commit MERGED, not merely written (2026-08-12, diagnosing the 2026-08-11 THIRD session). ALWAYS-APPLIES (process).** The 2026-08-11 (THIRD) session did deploy both halves — backend **v728** (tag `backend-good-2026-08-11-v728` on `a277e6e`) and the frontend (tag `live-139-specrev-ach-verification`, CDN-verified). But the audit is written **before** the deploy question is asked, so its first pass said the deploys were owed; the follow-up commit that corrected the LIVE STATE cells was authored and then **never merged**, sitting on `claude/vfo-session-setup-aa062e`. `main` therefore carried a confident, wrong "deployed nothing" line, and the next session **provably mis-derived deploy history from it** before git was consulted.

**The rule that falls out: when the docs and git disagree, git is authoritative.** Derive deploy state from `git tag -l 'live-*' --sort=v:refname` (react repo) + `git tag -l 'backend-good-*' --sort=v:refname` (edge repo) and MCP `list_edge_functions`, **then** read the docs — never the other way round. That ordering is exactly what caught this. The same failure mode has now occurred **three times in different clothes** (see the corrections embedded in the v719 and v727 cell histories), and every instance shares one shape: an audit written before the deploy, and a correction that did not reach `main`.

**The fix applied here, and the one to apply next time.** The four-line correction was squash-merged straight to react `main` as **`ce1c340`** (local `git merge --squash` + push — `gh` is not installed on this machine, see ENVIRONMENT GOTCHAS), and this session's LIVE STATE edits are stacked on that corrected baseline. **Operationally: a session is not finished when the docs commit is pushed — it is finished when the PR is merged.** If a deploy happens after the audit is written (it usually does), the correcting commit is part of the deliverable, not an optional tidy-up.

**AMENDED 2026-08-12 (SECOND session) — the SAME failure, in mirror image, one session later.** That session was handed a factual brief stating that `backend-good-2026-08-12-v730` and `live-140` were still unstamped and the frontend still undeployed. **Git said otherwise:** both tags resolved on `origin` (`dd55fb4` react, `22b9336` edge), and the live bundle `https://vfoportal.com/assets/index-CctgYQZ0.js` contained the accountant revenue-decision string and **zero** *"Year 2+"* labels — the deploy had happened after that session's audit was written, exactly as this gotcha predicts. **So the check runs in BOTH directions:** docs (or a hand-off summary) can under-report a deploy as easily as over-report one, and a *"still owed"* claim deserves the same `git tag -l … --sort=v:refname` + `list_edge_functions` + CDN-probe verification as a *"done"* claim. **A CDN probe is the cheap decider for the frontend half** — `curl` the live `index.html`, read the hashed bundle name, `grep` it for a string unique to the change; a tag proves someone stamped it, the bundle proves it shipped.

---

**377. `*_rev_share = "Pending"` is a POST-TRIGGER value, and the nightly sweep RETRIES it — any writer stamping a leg that has not been triggered yet must write NULL, never "Pending" (2026-08-12, caught between v731 and v732). ALWAYS-APPLIES (money).** `"Pending"` looks like the obvious way to say *"this leg is owed and not settled"*. It is not what it means. The ONLY writer of `retainer_rev_share='Pending'` in the organic flow is `automation_TAX_revshare` itself, at step 2, **after** the client's Tax 4 *Client decision 1* confirm has already invoked it — so the value's real meaning is *"the revshare handler started and did not finish"*. `tax_revshare_sweep` encodes exactly that reading: its `isRetry` predicate treats a `Pending` leg as a **crashed run to re-drive**, and the nightly sweep will pay it.

**How it nearly shipped.** The first build of the payment-continuation "retainer revenue share NOT paid yet" checkbox (deployed as **v731**) wrote `retainer_rev_share='Pending'` + `retainer_rev_paid=NULL` for the opted-in state, reasoning that "Pending" described the intent. On the driving case (Dave Clarenbach, `59473-003`, ~**$7,333** of member share) that row would have been **paid by the sweep that same night, jumping Client decision 1 entirely** — the client would never have been asked, and the money would have moved before the decision that authorises it. Only sandbox previews ever ran against v731 (a preview writes nothing), so no real row was ever stamped.

**The rule, and the shape to check.** Write **NULL** — that is what an organic pre-decision row looks like, and every sweep pass is built to leave it alone: the member pass skips NULL, and the planner-rescue pass requires the MEMBER leg already resolved (`revshare-sweep.ts:266`, the #265 fix) before it will touch a NULL planner leg. Fixed in **v732**. Generalise it: **a status column's vocabulary encodes WHO wrote it and WHEN, not just what it means in English.** Before a migration/backfill writes any status value the organic flow also writes, find the organic writer, find every sweep/retry predicate that reads it, and prove your value lands in the same state the organic flow would be in at that point in its life. Cousin of #277 (pre-settling legs so the sweep cannot pay old-system money) and #265 (the stranded-planner-leg rescue).

---

**378. "Settled on old system" display rules must gate on the LEG STATE, not on `legacy_source` alone — a legacy row can carry LIVE revenue-share legs (2026-08-12, v733). ALWAYS-APPLIES (tax / payments UI).** Two tax surfaces decided that a retainer's revenue-share breakdown was historic purely from `plan.legacy_source` being set: `actions/payments/normalize.ts buildTaxRows` (the payments tab, showing the literal string **"Settled on old system"** and suppressing the member/planner/VFO dollars) and `PricingSplitCard.jsx`'s `retainerIsHistoric` (the Pricing & revenue split card, which also drives the Edit-split basis). That was correct for as long as EVERY migrated retainer was pre-settled. It stopped being correct the moment the payment-continuation checkbox began writing legacy rows whose member and planner legs are **NULL and genuinely pay out later** — those rows displayed a settled-forever story over dollars that were about to move.

**Both now gate on `legacy_source && retainer_rev_paid === 'N/A — No Share Due'`** — the leg's own terminal stamp, which is the only thing that actually distinguishes "the migration settled this" from "the migration left this live". **The MAP 1 card was already written this way** (`Map1PricingSplitCard`'s `isHistoric`), which is why only the tax pair had to move; when a display rule exists on both pipelines, diff them before assuming the one you are reading is the reference implementation.

**The disclosed side effect.** A row whose retainer leg was later flipped to `retainer_rev_paid='Yes'` by hand (the visual-migration rulebook's Recipe 5, e.g. Jeff Titus `59473-002`, plan 105) now renders **"Paid to member" + the full breakdown** where it used to say "Settled on old system". That is factually accurate — the member WAS paid — and the change was made with the user's knowledge, but it is a visible change on pre-existing rows, not only on new ones.

---

**379. The payment-continuation "retainer revenue share NOT paid yet" checkbox: what it writes, what it costs, and how to recognise such a row (2026-08-12, v731→v733). ALWAYS-APPLIES (migration / money).** `migration_backfill_tax` takes an opt-in body flag **`retainer_share_unpaid`** (the admin checkbox on `PaymentContinuationTab`'s tax branch) for the case the tool previously could not express: the retainer WAS collected on the old system, but its revenue share was **never paid anywhere**. Ticked, the backfill leaves `retainer_rev_share` / `retainer_rev_paid` / `retainer_rev_completed_at` **NULL** (#377 — never `"Pending"`) and, **only when a stored planner share `> 0` exists**, `retainer_planner_paid` / `retainer_planner_completed_at` NULL too. The planner condition is not cosmetic: `transferPlannerShare` returns `skipped` **with no DB write** when `tax_planner_share` is null, so a planner leg left NULL without a share would sit NULL forever, un-resolvable by any path. **The strategic leg stays PRE-SETTLED regardless** (#277/#335) — the partner's cut of an old-system retainer is not being re-litigated.

**The consequence is a real transfer out of the VFO platform balance.** The retainer money itself is on the old system; when the client confirms *Client decision 1*, `automation_TAX_revshare` pays both legs for real, so **VFO fronts the cash** — a deliberate, user-approved business decision taken on the understanding that the old platform will not also pay (user-confirmed; note #361 — the old system is still live, so that assurance is a fact about behaviour, not about architecture). Both legs resolve inside a single handler run, seconds apart, and idempotency is by construction (the `isResolved` guard).

**Identifying such a row, and the rulebook's two exceptions.** A checkbox row is `legacy_source='old-system'` **AND `retainer_rev_paid IS NULL`** — that pair is the whole signature, and it is what every display rule must key on (#378). In `visual-migration-rules.md` (bumped **v2.4 → v2.5**): **Recipe 5 is FORBIDDEN** on such a row (hand-flipping the retainer leg to `'Yes'` would silently cancel the payout the client is about to authorise) and **Recipe 8's planner-leg precondition is WAIVED** (a NULL planner leg is the intended state here, not a defect to repair). Driving case: Dave Clarenbach `59473-003` (client 156, plan 106, program 1) — $11,000 retainer collected on the old system, *Client decision 1* never clicked, set up LIVE with the checkbox and verified row-level (all three retainer legs NULL, `tax_planner_id=3`, member + planner shares stored as dollars-of-total).

---

**380. When a guard stops short-circuiting, EVERY sibling leg's terminal check has to be re-audited — `tax/revshare.ts`'s strategic guard was `!== "Yes"`, which is not the same as "not terminal" (2026-08-12, v731). ALWAYS-APPLIES (money).** `automation_TAX_revshare` pays three legs in one run. The planner leg had always tested its status against the full terminal list (`Yes` / `Money Mapping` / `N/A — No Share Due`); the **strategic** leg tested only `plan[stratPaidKey] !== "Yes"`. That was harmless for as long as no row could reach the strategic block with a strategic leg already stamped `"N/A — No Share Due"` — and none could, because the member leg's `isResolved` skip returned first on every migrated row.

**#379 removed that shield.** A checkbox row deliberately leaves the member leg unresolved so the handler runs to completion — at which point a migration-settled strategic leg (`"N/A — No Share Due"`, written by the backfill for exactly the reason in #277) would have failed `!== "Yes"` and **re-fired a transfer to the partner for an old-system retainer**. Fixed to a terminal list before it could reach a real row; no production row was affected.

**The transferable rule:** an early-return guard is load-bearing for everything downstream of it, and its removal is not a local change. When you make a handler run past a guard it used to stop at, enumerate every subsequent status test in that handler and check each against the **complete** terminal vocabulary of its column, not against the single value that happened to be common. String comparisons on status columns are invisible to `deno check`. Cousin of #303 (exhaustive branch chains) and #371 (adding a status value means auditing every guard that enumerates that column).

---

**381. The AI PC Admin stall paper-trail: `*_pf_ack_at` + `automation_stall_ack`'s hardcoded `STALL_COLUMNS` map are the pattern for a manual acknowledgement, and a new stall must extend the map, the migration AND the frontend together (2026-08-12, v734). ALWAYS-APPLIES (tracking surfaces).** Every pipeline's reminder cron already stamped two columns per stalled substep — `<stall>_reminder_sent_at` (the 48h chase to the client/advisor/accountant/specialist) and `<stall>_pf_notified_at` (the 96h escalation to the assigned PF / team member responsible / Tracy) — but nothing recorded that a human then actually chased it. **`automation_stall_ack`** (`actions/automation/stall-ack.ts`, the first file in the new `actions/automation/` directory) is that record: body `{pipeline, id, stall, ack}` → `{success, ack_at}`, writing `now()` or `null` to one **`<stall>_pf_ack_at`** column. 26 ack columns across 7 tables — **25 are literally `<stall>_pf_ack_at`; the Regular-Priorities/MAP 4 one is the odd name `map4_stall_ack_at`** (`client_priority_tracks`), so do not generate the column name from the stall key by string concatenation without checking the map.

**`STALL_COLUMNS` is a hardcoded whitelist and that is the security design, not laziness.** It is the ONLY place a column name is produced — `pipeline` and `stall` from the body are **looked up in it**, never interpolated into SQL/PostgREST, and an unknown key 400s. The handler is in **`ADMIN_ONLY_ACTIONS`** and in **no** `TAB_ACTIONS` and **not** in `TAX_PLANNER_ALLOWED_ACTIONS`, so planners and member/client/specialist portals cannot call it at all (deny-by-omission cuts the right way here — verify with a grep of `role-gates.ts`, per #309).

**Three rows, three preconditions — and rendering is gated on the STAMP, not on the stall being over.** The 48h row renders only when `<stall>_reminder_sent_at` is non-null; the 96h row plus its indented `Reached out?` checkbox only when `<stall>_pf_notified_at` is non-null; the checkbox itself is hidden in `readOnly`/`plannerMode`. **Tax 5's and PFT's 48h/96h rows used to render ALWAYS** and became conditional here, and **PFT additionally dropped its `!decResp`/`!ftResp` guards** so the chase history survives the response instead of vanishing the moment the person replies — a paper trail that disappears on success is not a paper trail. **Adding a stall means four coordinated edits:** the migration column, the `STALL_COLUMNS` entry, the cron that stamps `_reminder_sent_at`/`_pf_notified_at`, and the surface's `StallRows`. Miss the map entry and the checkbox 400s; miss the migration and the handler 500s.

**The label `Reached out?` is verbatim across all seven surfaces** (MAP 1, Tax, Advisor, Accountant, Specialist, PFT, Regular) and the recipient noun in the 96h row deliberately is NOT — it reads *"assigned PF"* on MAP 1/Tax/PFT/Regular, *"team member responsible"* on Advisor/Accountant, and hardcoded *"Tracy"* on Specialist, because that is who each cron actually notifies. **`ClientTrackViewV2`'s `pcAdminStepCounts()` header pill deliberately does NOT count these rows** — they are informational history, not gated work — so its "KEEP IN SYNC" comment now covers three optional rows it does not model; do not "fix" that by adding them to the count.

---

**382. AI PC Admin substep labels are an API-adjacent surface: MAP 1 keys its email chips BY LABEL STRING, so renaming a label without moving the key silently detaches the chip (2026-08-12). ALWAYS-APPLIES (tracking surfaces). Extends #289 — read that first for the canonical label set and the surface list (now SEVEN, amended there).** `ClientTrackViewV2.jsx`'s `STEP_EMAIL_TPLS` is an object literal keyed on the **display label** and looked up as `STEP_EMAIL_TPLS[label]`. A rename that misses the key produces **no error, no warning and no chip** — the row just quietly loses its email previews. The same shape exists elsewhere by title: `StepEmailsChip` takes `title={label}` on several surfaces, and the tax-track bell-clearing prefixes are titles too (#366) — **a user-visible string can be a lookup key in this codebase, so grep for the old string before changing it, and change the key in the same edit.**

**The canonical six now mirror across EVERY surface**, admin and member portal alike: *"Engagement agreement created and sent for signing"*, *"Engagement agreement signed"*, *"Engagement agreement signed by CEO"*, *"Payment link sent (ACH or Card choice)"*, *"Payment collected"*, *"Invoice and receipt created and emailed to client"*. `SpecialistOnboarding`'s Stage 4 (*"Agreement sent"* / *"Agreement signed by specialist"* / *"Agreement signed by CEO"* / *"Payment link sent"*) and `MemberMSMTracking`'s member-portal cascade (*"Agreement sent to client"* / *"Client signed"* / *"CEO signed"* / *"Payment received"* / *"Invoice/receipt sent"*) were the two stragglers. **Verified safe before renaming, not after:** MAP 1's `STEP_EMAIL_TPLS` was untouched (it already used the canonical strings), the specialist chips are keyed on their OWN separate titles (*"Onboarding agreement"*, *"Licensing fee"*) not the row labels, and the member portal has no chips at all. **`"(ACH or Card choice)"` was checked for truthfulness** on the specialist licensing row — `license-checkout.ts` really does branch `card` / `us_bank_account` — because a unified label that lies about one pipeline is worse than an inconsistent one.

**Two done-states left deliberately wrong, so nobody "rediscovers" them as new bugs.** `MemberMSMTracking`'s *"Payment link sent (ACH or Card choice)"* is hardcoded `false` and can therefore **never** green in the member portal (pre-existing, untouched). And MAP 1 + Tax's *"Engagement agreement signed"* / *"signed by CEO"* rows are **permanently dateless** — see #384.

---

**383. A bare `YYYY-MM-DD` column must be STRING-SPLIT for display — `new Date('YYYY-MM-DD')` parses as UTC midnight and renders the PREVIOUS day everywhere west of Greenwich (2026-08-12). ALWAYS-APPLIES (frontend dates). The mirror image of #225 (also amended there) — the two together are the complete rule.** `new Date('2026-08-12')` is UTC midnight, which is `2026-08-11 20:00` in ET — so a plain Postgres `date` column formatted through `Date` shows the day before for every US user, every time. A `timestamptz` is the opposite case: it carries a zone, so it **must** go through `Date` to land on the day the viewer actually saw. **Both kinds sit side by side in these tables** (`pay1_date`, `retainer_date`, `c17_followup_sent_date` are bare DATEs; every `*_email_sent_at` / `*_at` is timestamptz), so every date formatter added this session branches on the shape: `/^\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}$/` → split the string, else `new Date()` + `getMonth()`/`getDate()`.

**It was found live, in shipped code.** `AdvisorOnboarding`/`AccountantOnboarding`'s `formatDate` did `d.includes('T') ? d.split('T')[0] : d` then split — i.e. it **sliced the timestamptz down to its UTC date part** before splitting, which is the same off-by-one in the other direction (a 7pm-ET stamp displayed as the next day). Both were corrected to the branching form, and the shared `dateTextStyle` went **11px → 12px, width 32 → 38px** to match the 12px row labels now that MM/DD sits next to every substep. **The displayed dates are non-editable everywhere** — cousin of #369, where the tax track's dates were made display-only by shadowing the shared editable control.

---

**384. MAP 1 and Tax never record WHEN an agreement was signed — the BoldSign webhook flips Yes-flags for those two pipelines and stamps `*_at` only for advisor/accountant, so dating those rows needs the protected `boldsign-webhook` (2026-08-12; USER PARKED IT). ALWAYS-APPLIES (tracking surfaces / BoldSign).** The *"Engagement agreement signed"* and *"Engagement agreement signed by CEO"* rows are dateless on MAP 1 and Tax and **cannot** be dated from existing data. `boldsign-webhook` writes `pipeline_map1.c17_client_signed`/`c18_ceo_signed` and `client_tax_plans.client_signed`/`ceo_signed` as bare `'Yes'` strings with **no companion timestamp**, while the advisor and accountant branches of the same webhook do stamp `agreement_signed_by_{advisor,accountant}_at` / `agreement_signed_by_ceo_at`. **Tax has dead `client_signed_at` / `ceo_signed_at` columns — 0 rows populated, no writer anywhere** — so their presence is a trap, not a data source; `pipeline_map1` has no such columns at all.

**Fixing it requires editing `boldsign-webhook`, which is an explicit-approval file** (deploy with `--no-verify-jwt` or BoldSign gets 401, #180) — so it was raised and **the user deliberately parked it**. Two things follow: do not silently "improve" one of these rows by inventing a date from `updated_at` or from the next step's stamp, and do not read the empty tax columns as "the data exists but the UI is missing". Related: `overview-map1.ts` / `overview-tax.ts` still have Client Overview `at:` gaps — **`c15_final_decision_at` became stampable this session** (`pcadmin-final-decision.ts`, blank on historic rows) **but the overview was not wired to it**, and `implementation_receipt_email_sent_at` is likewise new (`implementation-receipt.ts`); both are pre-existing gaps left untouched.

---

**390. The tax track's prerequisite gating ("natural barriers") is a FRONTEND-ONLY guardrail — `stepGate` decides what is reachable, `isTaskStatused` decides what is done, and `alreadyDone` is what stops it eating history (2026-08-13, react-only). ALWAYS-APPLIES (tracking surfaces). Same class as #353 — a UI guardrail the backend does not enforce.** `TaxPrioritiesTab.jsx` gained a `stepGate(task, phase)` returning `{ locked, hint }` (or `null` for unrestricted), consumed at the top of `renderTask` before `renderTaskInner` and before the plannerMode wrapper. A locked step renders an inert row — full brightness, hollow dot, task name in normal ink, a red circle-slash `LockedIcon` where the control would sit, and a gray 11px hint naming the DIRECT prerequisite. **Nothing server-side refuses these writes**; `tax_save_task` still accepts any status any caller sends. This is process choreography for admins, not authorization, and must never be cited as a security control.

**Three rules make it safe to add gates without breaking live plans.** (1) **`isTaskStatused` stays the single owner of every done-signal** — including the sentinels whose truth lives on `client_tax_plans` rather than in `client_tax_progress` (`tax_hlm_confirm`→`tax4_meeting_date`, `tax_returns_request`→`tax_returns_received_at`, `tax_planner_select`→`tax_planner_id`, `tax_generate_presentation`→`generated_presentation_at`, `tax_presentation_link`→`presentation_send_date`, `tax_refund`→Proceed-or-refunded, and *"Additional information required"*'s status-plus-received-stamp rule). Every predicate reads through it, so a gate can never disagree with the green dot beside it. (2) **`alreadyDone` short-circuits the gate**, so a step that has ever been answered always renders its normal editable control — history stays visible and correctable even when a prerequisite is later un-set. Per-specialist steps key on the composite progress key and `isTaskStatused` does **not** handle those, so that branch is explicit; `tax_implement_decision` additionally accepts the plan column, because legacy plans carry `implementation_decision` with no progress row and would otherwise render locked. (3) **An absent task is vacuously satisfied** — `prereqDone` returns true when the lookup misses, because Holistic (program 1) genuinely lacks the deposit and Green/Red Light steps and gating on a task that does not exist would lock everything downstream of it forever.

**`readOnly` (the member portal) is exempt entirely; the planner portal is NOT.** Members see zero lock rows; planners see the same locks admins do, on top of the existing whitelist inert-wrapper. **One member-visible change was made deliberately:** Tax 5 *Post Allocation* used to be a full block-out (opacity/pointer-events wrapper, rows not rendered at all) and is now ordinary locked rows with the `PhasePill` always present — so members now see those rows where they previously saw an empty section. That is consistent with every other phase, all of which already render their rows to members regardless of state, but it IS a client-facing diff and was approved as such.

**A pre-existing mismatch this exposed, left as-is because it fails open.** `isTaskStatused` keys the generate-deck step on `generated_presentation_at` while that step's own renderer keys on `generated_presentation_url`. A plan with the stamp and no URL therefore skips the lock and shows a live *Generate presentation* button rather than Download/Regenerate. Harmless — but **22 plans were mass-stamped with `generated_presentation_at` this session** (every plan whose *ROI Presentation* step was already answered), so the shape is now far more common than it was.

---

**391. A DIRECTIONAL verdict step is never satisfied by "answered" — "Tax planner review complete" = Stop must OPEN the stop paths and RE-LOCK the proceed paths, and reading it as a boolean shipped a live bug (2026-08-13, caught in user testing). ALWAYS-APPLIES (tracking surfaces). Corollary of #365/#367 — a bell that instructs must leave its instruction performable.** The first build computed `reviewDone` as *any* answer and fed it into `diagnosticChain`. Selecting **Stop tax planning** therefore UNLOCKED the whole forward half of the track — *Assess*, *ROI Meeting booked*, the deck, and a **Proceed** button on the Green/Red Light step, which is exactly the decision the Stop verdict had just refused. The user found it within minutes of the step going live. The fix is `reviewProceed` (status **exactly** `'Proceed with tax planning'`); `reviewDone` was deleted outright rather than left around to be reused.

**The mirror error is just as easy.** The stop path still has to be walkable, or the review-Stop bell (#367) instructs the admin to do something the UI refuses. So the two verdicts open **different** doors: **Green/Red Light is never row-locked at all** — its *Refund* button appears as soon as a `deposit_payment_intent_id` exists and is the program-4 stop route — while its *Proceed* button renders only when `diagnosticChain` holds. And `reviewStop` unlocks `tax_3_decision` **on Holistic only**, because program 1 has no Green/Red step and its Stop bell names that step's decline button as the stop route. **Generalise it: when a status column's values mean opposite directions, enumerate them — never test the column for presence.** Cousin of #371 (adding a status value means auditing every guard that enumerates that column) and #380 (a guard testing one value is not testing "terminal").

---

**392. `TASK_DISPLAY_LABELS` lets a step's DISPLAYED name diverge from its STORED name — and the stored name is a cross-repo lookup key, so every comparison must keep using the raw value (2026-08-13). ALWAYS-APPLIES (frontend labels). Direct extension of #382 — read that first for the label-as-lookup-key mechanism.** Three tax step names were shortened for the UI only: the Green/Red Light step's 103-character name renders as *"Tax Plan Green/Red Light"* with the remainder demoted to a gray sub-line via `TASK_SUB_LABELS`, *"Assess tax planning opportunities (and enter presentation details)"* renders without its parenthetical, and *"Additional information required"* gains a question mark. **`program_client_tasks.name` was not touched and must not be** — it is matched by string in `stepGate`, `isTaskStatused`, the planner whitelist (`PLANNER_EDITABLE_TASK_NAMES`), `renderTaskInner`'s branch chain, and the backend's own `ADDL_INFO_TASK_NAME` in `actions/tax/save-task.ts` — i.e. across both repos.

**The mechanical rule.** `taskLabel(task)` is for rendering; `task.name` is for deciding. In the sweep, 27 render sites moved to `taskLabel(task)` and **25 occurrences deliberately did not** — 9 `title={task.name}` props on `StepEmailsChip` (the bell/template lookup key), 15 branch comparisons, and one row whose visible text was already a hardcoded literal. **The visible consequence to expect:** bells, email chips and notification titles still speak the LONG stored names, so an admin reading a bell sees different words than the row it points at. That is the correct trade — the alternative is renaming a key that four call sites and another repo depend on.

---

**393. Two independent traps in the planner vault-drop bell: it fires at MINT time (the only backend touchpoint), and `resolveCallerPlanner`'s `allowed_admins` precedence is what makes admin uploads silent (2026-08-13, v738). ALWAYS-APPLIES (notifications / vault).** `VAULT_planner_document_added` (FYI, Tracy, pipeline `VAULT`) fires from the new `utils/vault-planner-notify.ts`, called by both planner-reachable signed-upload-url minters — `actions/vault/tax-admin-upload-url.ts` (Sensitive) and `actions/vault/gen-upload-url.ts` (General). **The browser PUTs the file straight to storage afterwards, so the backend never learns whether the upload succeeded**: the bell is emitted when the URL is minted. A PUT that then fails leaves one slightly early bell. That is a deliberate accepted cost — the alternative is no signal at all — and it is commented at both the helper and the migration.

**The admin-silence is not a role check, it is a precedence check.** `resolveCallerPlanner` walks `body.token` → `admin_sessions.email` → **`allowed_admins` (returns null if found)** → `tax_planner_logins` → `tax_planners`, mirroring `resolvePlannerGroupIds` exactly, so an admin who *also* holds a planner login resolves to `null` and fires nothing — matching `middleware/auth.ts`'s own role precedence. It resolves from the token rather than `auth.callerRole` because `vaultGenUploadUrl` is not handed an `AuthContext`, and threading one in would mean opening `router/dispatch.ts` for a zero-action change. **Live-tested three ways on v738: Team Member → General fires, Tax Planner → Sensitive fires, admin → silent.**

**The frontend half has the opposite trap: it must read FRESH.** `ClientVaultTab.jsx` replaces the Sensitive section's *Request documentation* button with a locked hint whenever any live plan lacks `tax_returns_received_at` (the tracked `/tax-upload` request belongs to the Tax 1 step, not to the generic vault requester). It calls `callApi('tax_load_plans')` and **deliberately NOT `loadCachedAction`** — that helper memoizes per-payload for the whole app session, so the warning would persist after the returns actually landed. It fails soft to no-gate, so a client with no tax plan is never blocked. Note the button is rendered **directly in `ClientVaultTab`**, not through `VaultSections`, which is why the member / specialist / planner-panel vaults needed no change at all.

**394. Revenue-share legs are NOT prorated the same way, and a display surface that invents one proration for all of them is wrong exactly where it matters — the MEMBER leg pays IN FULL, STRATEGIC is gross-prorated, and VFOS is a RESIDUAL of the NET installment (2026-08-14, v740). ALWAYS-APPLIES (money). Direct extension of #304 and #252 — read both first.** `pipeline_map1` shares are dollars of the **gross** engagement while the installments the client actually pays are **net** dollars, and the gap between them is `member_contribution` — a discount the member funds (#304). Two display surfaces gross-prorated shares against net installments and were therefore correct on every ordinary row and wrong on precisely the rows where a member had contributed: `Map1PricingSplitCard.jsx` and the MAP 1 block of `actions/payments/normalize.ts`. Found on **Sreenivasulu Vengala** (`pipeline_map1` id **110**: net 2700, gross 5400, `member_contribution` 2700, `member_share` NULL, `vfos_share` 2700), whose card advertised **$337.50** per installment to VFO where the engine keeps **$675**.

**The three legs, and why each is shaped the way it is.** **MEMBER** pays its entered share in full, split across installments by cumulative difference (`cum(i) = round2(raw × i / n)`, take the difference) so the parts sum to the entered amount to the cent — it is **never scaled by net/gross**, because the entered share is *already* net of the contribution and the engine's own comment says a contribution row "must not shrink a second time". **STRATEGIC** genuinely is `share / gross_fee × installment`. **VFOS is not a proration at all** — nothing is ever transferred for it, so it is simply whatever the net installment still holds once the legs that DO pay out are taken off: `base − member(i) − stratPortion`, floored at 0. That is why a member contribution leaves **more** with VFO, not less, and it is the single fact both broken surfaces had backwards.

**The generalisable rule: a share-display surface must COPY `contract-revshare.ts`, never re-derive a proration from the column names.** The columns are all called `*_share` and all stored as dollars-of-gross, which makes "divide by gross, multiply by the payment" look like the obviously uniform answer; the engine does not do that and never did. **The engine and the DB were always correct — this was display-only**, so no money moved and no payout changed. Blast radius was verified against all 30 production `pipeline_map1` rows before the deploy: only ids **110** and **120** (the only two rows with a member contribution, i.e. the only two where net < gross) render differently. `normalize.ts` also had to start selecting `strategic_partner_share` to compute the residual — its callers use `select("*")`, so no loader change was needed, and null-vs-0 semantics were preserved (a leg is null only when NO split was entered on ANY leg; an entered split makes the VFO residual meaningful even where `vfos_share` itself is blank).

**395. Accounting share columns are PROJECTIONS, not payouts — the per-leg payout column is the only thing that knows whether the money moved, and its vocabulary differs per leg (2026-08-14). ALWAYS-APPLIES (money / accounting).** Every Accounting revenue + reconciliation panel prints each slice of a split as a plain dollar figure, which reads as money that has already changed hands. For most of these rows it has not: the slice is arithmetic on the payment amount, computed at render, and the leg that would actually transfer it may be unallocated, held, failed, or simply not yet triggered. **`src/components/admin/shareLegState.jsx` is now the single frontend source of truth** for turning a leg's payout column into a `{note, tone}` — `paid` / `money mapping` / `no share due` / `awaiting payout setup` / `awaiting planner` / `failed — retrying` / `in progress` / `payment clearing` / `awaiting client decision` / `not yet paid`. Pending amounts dim to 0.55 opacity with an amber note; totals and reconciliation aggregates carry a `$X pending` sub-note. **Annotation only — no amount, filter or total is derived from any of it.**

**EXTENDED 2026-08-24 (v778) — two new notes, and one rule that IS derived from the note.** The vocabulary gained **`Held - Suspended`** and **`Held - Paused`** (exported as `HELD_SUSPENDED_NOTE` / `HELD_PAUSED_NOTE`; deliberately Title Case where every other note is lowercase, so the one reading an operator must act on catches the eye), plus `heldReason(state)`. Aggregate cells now carry **TWO** sub-notes — `PendingNote` for ordinarily-owed money and `HeldNote` for money parked behind a suspended/paused member — and they **PARTITION** the outstanding amount: a held dollar is never counted under both. **The bigger change is `isMoneyMappingLeg(state, decision)`, which decides which of the two member columns — Member Revenue Share or Member Money Mapping — a slice lands in, and it is the one place the note IS load-bearing rather than decorative.** A **SETTLED** leg is bucketed by **what actually happened** (its own note: `paid` → Revenue Share, `money mapping` → Money Mapping); only an **UNSETTLED** leg (blank / in progress / held / awaiting / failed) falls back to the member's **current** `members.revenue_decision`. **Reading the current decision on a settled leg would silently restate a past payout as something it never was** — cash that really moved to Flood and Cito stays under Revenue Share even though Cito's record says Money Mapping today. It reads the NOTE, not the raw column, on purpose: MAP 1 / Tax (`legState`) and PIP (`pipShared`'s `pipMemberState`) start from different vocabularies (`'Yes'` vs `'Completed - Revenue Share'`, `'Money Mapping'` vs `'Completed - Money Mapping'`) and both normalise into the same two notes, so one rule covers every track with no per-track variant. `revenue_decision` NULL still means Revenue Share (#375), which is what makes the `(decision || '') === 'Money Mapping'` fallback correct. Wired into all six Holistic / Tax / PIP revenue + reconciliation panels. **The rule: if you add a status that settles money, give it the right note — the note is now what files the dollars.** Cross-ref **#433**, **#240**, **#375**.

**The vocabularies are inconsistent and that inconsistency is load-bearing, not cosmetic.** A strategic leg carries **BOTH** `'N/A'` (what the engine writes) **and** `'N/A — No Share Due'` (what the migration backfill writes, #362/#277), so a terminal check that knows only one of them reports a settled leg as pending forever. `client_priority_tracks.pip_rev_share_status` is worse: **`'Pending'` ambiguously means both in-flight AND failed**, and the richer `revPaidValue` the handler computes is never persisted, so PIP can annotate only to that resolution — it is a schema gap, flagged and not fixed. A **blank** leg is not one state either: on the tax **retainer** it means the revenue share has not fired yet because it fires on the client's decision after the review rather than on the charge (hence the `tax_retainer` context and its `awaiting client decision` reading), while elsewhere a blank simply means not yet paid. **Two consequences for anyone extending this.** First, `SpecialistReconciliationPanel` was already *loading* `payout_status` per line and discarding it — a payout column arriving in a payload is not the same as a surface reading it, so grep the panel, not just the loader. Second, **`SpecialistRecurringPanel` cannot be annotated at all**: `specialist_revenue_recurring_lines` has **no `payout_status` column**, so that panel is deliberately untouched and giving it a note means schema work first. Cross-ref #303 (`Awaiting Connect Setup` is non-terminal), #284 (`Awaiting Planner Allocation`), #377 (`Pending` is a post-trigger value the sweep retries).

**396. Reminder ladders count BUSINESS days now — `delayCutoffIso` is the calendar helper and has ZERO callers, so any new sweep tier must choose its unit deliberately (2026-08-14). ALWAYS-APPLIES (Backend data patterns / notifications).** Every stall and reminder tier in every sweep was converted to `utils/notify.ts` `businessDelayCutoffIso(days)` (Mon–Fri, UTC, no holiday calendar): `tax/revshare-sweep` (Tax 3, Tax 4, Tax 5), `pipeline/contract-revshare-sweep` (MAP 1), `advisor/sweep`, `accountant/sweep`, `onboarding/sweep` (14 tiers through one wrapper), `pft/sweep`, `specialist-revenue/payout-sweep`, `pipeline/contract-check-reminder-sweep`, `regular/map4-followup-sweep`. The two COUNTDOWN horizons — the Tax 2 assess-form reminder and the MAP 1 check-payment lookahead — use the forward walker `businessDayHorizonDateOnly(fromDateOnly, days)` instead. `growth/overdue-sweep` has no delay offset at all, so it early-returns on Saturday and Sunday UTC ticks. **`notification_rules.delay_days` was NOT renumbered — the stored 2s and 4s are unchanged and only the unit moved**, which means a rule row read in isolation tells you nothing about which calendar it is counted against; the caller does. **The calendar survivors are deliberate owner decisions and must not be "finished off" by a later session:** the advisor + accountant **14-day auto-decline**, the **Tax 4 meeting-passed nudge**, the **membership 30-day renewal notice** (and the membership sweep generally, untouched), the **chargescheduled sweep** (installments fall due on real dates, weekends included), the **notifications purge**, **personal reminders**, and **every token / session expiry window**. `delayCutoffIso` was kept precisely so opting back into calendar semantics stays a one-word, visible choice rather than a rewrite — a tier that wants calendar should call it and say why.

**397. `businessDelayCutoffIso` walks whole weekdays FIRST and subtracts the fraction second — reversing that order makes fractional delays non-monotonic across a weekend (2026-08-14). ALWAYS-APPLIES (Backend data patterns).** The Notification Editor exposes `delay_days` with `step=0.5`, so fractional delays are reachable from the UI, not just theoretical. The helper therefore does `Math.floor(days)` weekday hops backwards from now, and only then subtracts `frac * 24h` as plain hours. Doing it the other way round — subtract the hours, then walk — can push the walk's starting day into Saturday or Sunday, where the walk buys back a whole extra weekday, and the result is that a LARGER `days` yields a LATER cutoff than a smaller one. A ladder built on a non-monotonic cutoff fires its "4 day" PF bell before its "2 day" reminder. The invariant to preserve on any edit is simply: **a bigger `days` must always produce an earlier-or-equal cutoff.** The order is documented in a comment above the function; keep the comment with the code if the function moves.

**398. The Tax 5 implementation fee fires from exactly ONE call site — the client's own click — and nothing may ever chain `automation_TAX_charge_implementation` from a sweep again (2026-08-14). ALWAYS-APPLIES (money).** The nightly `tax/revshare-sweep` used to carry a tier that, 24 hours after a Proceed pick with no client response, wrote `implementation_final_decision='Auto-Locked'` and fired the real off-session charge. It is deleted. The only runtime caller of `automation_TAX_charge_implementation` is now `actions/tax/implement-final-decision.ts`, reached when the client clicks a button on `/tax-implement-decide` — grep-verified, and worth re-verifying with the same grep before adding any Tax 5 automation. In its place a Proceed-branch reminder ladder runs on the same shape as every other stall: `TAX_impl_proceed_reminder_email` (default 2 business days) and `TAX_impl_proceed_stalled` (default 4 business days, PF bell), **sharing the `implementation_reminder_sent_at` / `implementation_pf_notified_at` guard columns with the Undecided ladder** — safe only because `implementation_decision` is single-valued, so exactly one ladder can ever match a row; add a third branch value and that sharing breaks silently. A Proceed plan whose client stays quiet now waits forever, which is the intended behaviour. **`'Auto-Locked'` is a HISTORICAL-ONLY value with zero writers** in either the Tax 4 or Tax 5 column; `actions/clients/overview-tax.ts` still reads it and `TaxPrioritiesTab` still renders it as "Auto-locked (legacy 24h flow)", so do not delete the reader while old rows exist. The dead `Auto-Locked && Decline` rejection came out of `implement-final-decision.ts` with it; the surviving rejection is "charge already succeeded → Decline refused" and its JSON flag was renamed `window_expired` → `already_charged` (nothing read the old name). Note the asymmetry: the Tax 4 sibling in `postreview-client-decision.ts` **keeps** its `window_expired` flag because `TaxPostReviewDecidePage` reads it — same rewording, different flag, on purpose.

**399. When a flow doc and a code comment disagree about sweep behaviour, the code comment has been right every time — fix the doc (2026-08-14). ALWAYS-APPLIES (docs).** The Stripe-chain paragraph in `SESSION_REFERENCE.md`, the cron paragraphs in `architecture/01-system-map.md` and several `flows/` pages all still asserted that Tax 4 "Client decision 1" Continue auto-locks the retainer revenue share after 24 hours. That stopped being true long enough ago to have its own registry entry (**#264**), and the code comment beside the sweep tier said so plainly the whole time. The docs outlived the behaviour because a removal edits one file and leaves every description of the removed thing standing. **Two habits follow.** When you delete a timed automation, grep the docs tree for its interval AND its vocabulary (`24h`, `auto-lock`, `auto-charge`, `window expired`) in the same commit, not later. And when auditing, trust the handler and its comment over any prose — a stale doc describing a charge that no longer happens is more dangerous than no doc, because it is the thing an operator quotes to a client.

**400. A tax-planning GROUP can hand its own Tax Assessment PDF over through the client's vault — but the hand-off changes only WHO is being chased; the assess FORM still completes the step, for that group exactly as for every other (2026-08-14, v743/v744). ALWAYS-APPLIES (tax track).** Innovation Consulting Group produces a finished assessment document, so re-keying its numbers was duplicate work for the planner. The carve-out lets the planner deliver the PDF instead: `constants/vault-assess-groups.ts` holds `VAULT_ASSESS_GROUPS = ["Innovation Consulting Group", "Test Group"]` and `isVaultAssessGroup()`, matched **case-exact on `tax_planners.member_type`, which stores the group NAME** — so renaming the group in the Tax Planning Groups UI is the retirement switch, and a renamed group silently falls back to the ordinary form flow. **`"Test Group"` is a PERMANENT fixture, not a leftover**: it is what keeps the whole path exercisable with the test planner login. There is deliberately **no frontend mirror of this list** — an earlier build had one and it was removed; the FE now keys off the plan stamps alone, which is why a group rename needs no deploy on the react side.

**The semantics are the entire gotcha, and they changed once mid-session after live testing — do not re-derive them from the column name.** `assess_vault_uploaded_at` sounds like a completion stamp and is not one. It does **not** mark the step done anywhere: not in `isTaskStatused` (which reads `assess_form_submitted_at` only), not in `actions/clients/overview-tax.ts`'s column-proven `assess_form` branch, not in any gate. What the drop does is (a) stop the chase on the planner — it clears their action-required `Enter the tax planning opportunities for%` bell and the nightly sweep's assess-reminder query excludes stamped rows — and (b) raise an action-required ask on the assigned PF to key the document's numbers into the form. Submitting that form is the only completion path there has ever been. The FE's only concession is a muted sub-note on the not-yet-submitted assess row (*"Tax Assessment PDF in vault — uploaded by X on <date>. Enter its information here."*), not rendered for members, changing no done-state.

**TWO bell titles are load-bearing clear keys and both are matched by prefix.** `save-assess-form.ts` now clears **both** `Enter the tax planning opportunities for%` (the planner's, pre-existing) and `Tax Assessment PDF added for%` (the PF's, new — rule `TAX_assess_vault_uploaded_pf`). Reword either title and the bell becomes unretirable, the #365 failure mode. The assess bell keeps ONE title across both wordings on purpose: `allocate-planner.ts` and `save-task.ts` branch only the **message** (vault groups are told to upload into Sensitive Documents), never the title, so the single clear site still finds it. Both fire guards were widened to require `assess_vault_uploaded_at` null as well as `assess_form_submitted_at` null — a delivered document means stop asking.

**Mint-time, once per plan, any section, any file.** `utils/assess-vault-stamp.ts` is called from BOTH planner-reachable minters (`vault_tax_admin_upload_url` and `vault_gen_upload_url`) immediately after `notifyPlannerVaultDrop`, so it fires when the signed URL is minted — **before the browser's PUT lands** — the same accepted trade-off as Tracy's per-file bell. Admins no-op (`resolveCallerPlanner` returns null for an `allowed_admins` email, which `resolveCallerPlanner` now also returns `member_type` from). The latch is the row match itself: it takes the newest plan for that client allocated to the caller's group with **both** stamps null, so the second and later files are silent. **General section and file type are accepted deliberately** — no name or MIME sniffing — so a wrong-section drop still stamps and an admin unwinds it, which was judged better than a planner's correct document going unnoticed.

**The `|vault` template name must be passed to BOTH the lookup and the draft call.** The sweep's assess reminder selects `TAX_tax3_assess_reminder|vault` for these groups and `TAX_tax3_assess_reminder` for everyone else, then hands **that same chosen name** to `gmailDraftFetch`. The per-template Draft/Send toggle keys off the name given to the draft call, so passing the base name while having loaded the variant is a silent outage, not an error — #356 exactly. Cross-ref **#365** (title-prefix clear contract), **#356** (template name pairing), **#359** (this reminder counts DOWN to the meeting), **#90** (the `program=` its bell link needs), **#385/#386** (Tracy's planner-drop bell, which fires alongside and is unaffected). (2026-08-20: the `|vault` fork now spans FOUR template rows — the 2-day pair `TAX_tax3_assess_reminder` + `|vault` and the early pair `TAX_tax3_assess_reminder_early` + `|vault` — and the two-place-name rule applies independently within each pair.)

**401. The always-loaded bootstrap doc needs a HARD line budget, and anything a command can answer must not be written in it at all (2026-08-14). ALWAYS-APPLIES (docs).** `SESSION_REFERENCE.md` reached 756 lines / 324 KB while its own header still instructed every session to read it in full — which no session could, so every session skimmed instead, and the skimming is what let false claims survive audit after audit (a Tax 4 auto-lock removed weeks earlier, an OWED deploy list already discharged in full, a helper edge function deleted from Supabase with no doc trace). It was rebuilt to a **hard 250-line cap** with a `DERIVE AT SESSION START` block: versions come from MCP `list_edge_functions`, tags from `git tag --sort=v:refname`, the action count from the anchored greps, crons from `cron.job`, sandbox posture from `pipeline_sandbox_config`. **The rule that keeps it small is the write discipline, not the rewrite** — every hub edit is declared ADD / UPDATE / DELETE / NOOP, a superseded value moves to `CHANGELOG.md` in the same pass rather than lingering as a `Prior:` tail, narrative goes straight to CHANGELOG and never lands in the hub at all, and a count over 250 blocks the commit. Nothing was deleted in the diet: displaced prose went to CHANGELOG verbatim, the chain inventory became `architecture/07-server-chains.md`, and the curated gotcha list went 188 → 36 with all 188 still complete here. **The corollary for anyone tempted to "just add one line":** the hub is loaded into every session's context, so a line there costs more than a line anywhere else in the repo, and the test for keeping it is whether removing it would cause a mistake — not whether it is true.

**402. A COUNT written into prose is a promise to update it that nobody keeps — and a file can contradict itself in three places without anyone noticing (2026-08-14). ALWAYS-APPLIES (docs).** The 2026-08-14 verification sweep found, in docs that had passed a staleness audit that same morning: `NOTIFICATION_AUDIT.md` headlining **128 rules in 11 areas** against a live **183 in 16** (five whole areas, including Tax Planners' 14 rules, had no section at all); `README.md` claiming **52 public tables** against **87**, `#1–#395` against #400, `~206 handler files` against ~470; `tables/tax.md` claiming **85 columns** against **160**; `tables/pipeline.md` **~80** against **143**; and `integrations/supabase.md` stating **two** buckets on line 3, **seven** on line 88 and listing **nine** in its table, against a real **20**. Every one of these is cheap to derive (`select count(*) from notification_rules`, `information_schema.columns`, `storage.buckets`) and impossible to keep true by hand. **Write the query, not the number** — and when a count genuinely helps a reader, stamp it `(v: date)` so its age is visible. The self-contradiction is the tell worth remembering: when one file states the same quantity three different ways, it is not one stale line, it is a doc nobody has read end to end in a long time — treat that as a signal to verify the whole file rather than patch the line you happened to notice.

**403. A TERMINAL outcome can satisfy a "cascade complete" predicate, so `locked: !cascadeDone` unlocks exactly the records that are closed — test the negative state FIRST (2026-08-16, v747). ALWAYS-APPLIES (frontend gating / cross-repo).** The Tax 4 *"Detailed tax plan meeting confirmation email"* step (`tax_hlm_confirm`) was gated on `tax3AipcDone`, the predicate for "the Tax 3 AI PC Admin cascade has finished". That predicate is deliberately true when `tax_decision === 'No'` or `tax_final_decision === 'No'`, because **a decline IS the cascade's terminal state** — there is nothing further to do. The gate then read `locked: !tax3AipcDone`, so the one population that must never reach the step — declined plans — was the population it unlocked, while live plans mid-cascade stayed correctly locked. The bug is invisible in testing unless you deliberately walk a decline: on every happy path the gate behaves.

**The shape to recognise:** any predicate meaning "this cascade is settled" is usually a union of *completed* and *abandoned*, and a lock derived from it inherits that union silently. The fix is ordering, not arithmetic — the decline test comes first and returns its own hint (`'Tax planning was declined'`), and only then does the completion test run. **A UI lock is choreography, never a boundary (#353/#390)**, so the same session also added the real refusal in `actions/tax/highlevel-meeting-confirm.ts`: a 400 `"Tax planning was declined for this plan"` read off the same two columns. Grep the bell strands before assuming a decline-lock is safe to add — here `TAX_planner_hlm_ready` fires only on the paid Yes track, so no action-required bell was made unperformable by locking the step (#365).

**404. A RESEND of a confirmation email re-arms every reminder ladder the original send stamped — a reschedule must pass an explicit `reschedule` flag and every ladder-arming side effect must sit behind `!reschedule` (2026-08-16, v746/v747). ALWAYS-APPLIES (notifications / backend data patterns).** Nine confirmation-email steps across five pipelines gained a **Reschedule** affordance that reopens the same date/time/timezone form pre-filled and re-sends the SAME template. The naive implementation — call the send handler again — is wrong everywhere the handler does more than draft mail, because these handlers are also where reminder ladders are armed: `pft/meeting-email.ts` stamps `discovery_email_sent_at` and nulls `discovery_pf_notified_at`; `onboarding/prelim-email.ts` stamps `sif_email_sent_at`; `onboarding/stage2-email.ts` raises Tracy's revenue-share ask. Re-invoking them restarts a chase for a form the recipient has had all along, or re-rings a bell whose work is already done, and **nothing errors** — the only symptom is a nag arriving weeks late to the wrong person.

**The convention that shipped:** the frontend sends `reschedule: true` in the body, the handler destructures it, and each ladder write becomes conditional (`if (decision !== "stop" && !reschedule)`, `const ladder = reschedule ? {} : {...}`, `if (allDone && !reschedule)`). **The inverse case exists too and must be decided per site, not by rule:** on the Tax 2 booking step (`ready-for-tax3.ts`) a changed date SHOULD re-arm — the planner's before-meeting assess reminder is a countdown to the meeting, so the handler nulls `tax3_assess_reminder_sent_at` when the date actually moves and a reminder had already been sent. MAP 4 (`map4-set-meeting-date.ts`) does the same on a genuine date change, clearing `map4_followup_sent_at` / `map4_reminder_sent_at` / `map4_stall_notified_at` (a user-approved decision to re-draft the follow-up ladder). **Two more traps in the same family.** A resend that LOGS its meeting must edit the row in place, not file a second one — `save_onboarding_meeting` gained an optional `meeting_id` UPDATE mode for exactly this, and the Stage 2 affordance is offered only on the newest logged meeting. And rescheduling a send that was originally made as "date not arranged" **upgrades that record to confirmed**; that was reviewed and accepted rather than guarded. (2026-08-20: the reschedule now nulls BOTH assess-reminder stamps — `tax3_assess_reminder_sent_at` and `tax3_assess_reminder_early_sent_at`.)

**405. `roi_meeting_skipped_at` is one-way, and the five steps it removes do NOT share one done-rule — the booking step reads DONE, the other four are EXCLUDED from the counts (2026-08-16, v746). Tax track / done-math.** Skipping the ROI meeting takes out the four Tax 2 - Deeper Dive steps plus Tax 3's `ROI Presentation`. The obvious implementation — drop all five — leaves `Tax 2` rendering as an empty phase with no green, which reads as work outstanding. The shipped split is deliberate: **`tax_3_decision` (the booking step) counts as DONE** because "there will be no meeting" is a real answer to it, which is what makes **Tax 2 read a green Done 1/1** on the phase card and in the hero; the other four drop out of the counts entirely and render as grey `Skipped` rows. The booking row itself renders a green dot with an `ROI meeting skipped` chip and the stamp date in the date column.

**Two refinements that are easy to get wrong on a re-read.** Only the **unanswered** steps drop out — a step actually actioned before the skip keeps its normal row and stays in the counts, because that work did happen (`isSkippedAway` tests `!isTaskStatused(t)`). And the skip row renders on **every** surface, member and planner included, so the history reads identically to everyone. **Both repos must use the same split (#339):** `actions/clients/overview-tax.ts` sets `applicable: reach && !roiSkipped` on the same five — which required giving `ROI Presentation` its own named branch, since it had been falling through to the generic `else`. **The one-way-ness is load-bearing:** `ready-for-tax3.ts` 400s BOTH decisions on a skipped plan, so there is no un-skip and no later booking, which is what lets `save-task.ts` treat `roiSettled` (booked **or** skipped) as satisfying the booking half of the review-Proceed bell. Skipping also mints the PF's action-required `Complete the Client tax planning decision for%` bell (rule `TAX_roi_skipped_decision_needed`), cleared **only** by `actions/tax/decision.ts` on any decision, with a satisfied-on-fire guard so a plan that already carries a decision never gets one (#365). Skip-aware email copy is a separate axis — see the `|skipped` template grid in `utils/tax-decision-template.ts` and #207. **Superseded in part 2026-08-17:** there are now TWO skip routes and this gotcha describes the done-math both share — the ordering differences are **#407**.

**406. A "completed on" column will happily display a FUTURE date, and nobody reads it as a bug — they read it as a fact (2026-08-17). ALWAYS-APPLIES (frontend display / cross-repo).** Three steps on the tax track filled their right-hand completion-date cell from a field that is not a completion date: `tax_hlm_confirm` showed `tax4_meeting_date` — **the meeting being booked, which is by definition in the future** — so Dave Clarenbach's *"Detailed tax plan meeting confirmation email"*, sent 08/14, displayed **08/28**; `tax_presentation_link` showed `presentation_send_date`, the scheduled send day, **even before the step was done**; and `tax_returns_request` preferred `requested_at` over `received_at`, so a completed step showed when it was asked for. Every ordinary step reads `client_tax_progress.completed_date` and was always right — **the bug lives exclusively in the bespoke renderers**, which have no progress row and must therefore name their stamp by hand.

**Why it survived so long:** a wrong date in a narrow muted 55px column is never *obviously* wrong. There is no rendering error, no console warning, and for the large class of plans where the two dates coincide — scheduled on the day it was scheduled for — the display is indistinguishable from correct. It is only visible on a row where the two genuinely differ, and only to someone who knows which one they are looking at. **The general rule: any column headed by a concept ("completed", "sent", "paid") must be fed by a stamp of THAT event, and a field whose name describes a plan (`_send_date`, `_meeting_date`, `_due_date`) is a scheduling field, not an event stamp** — the giveaway is that it can hold a future value at all. When auditing, sort by the column and look for anything ahead of today.

**Two design points settled here, both worth reusing.** (1) A step whose human work is *scheduling* something dates from **when it was scheduled**, and that date must **not move** when the scheduled thing later happens — `tax_presentation_link` pins to `presentation_scheduled_at` and ignores `presentation_email_sent_at` thereafter, because the sweep's send is the system's work, not a person's. (2) Where the stamp is missing on historic rows, **blank beats a wrong date**: 13 plans dated through the retired `automation_TAX_save_meeting_date` (dispatched and gated, **zero frontend callers** — it is why they exist) carry a meeting date and no confirmation stamp, and now render an empty cell with the green dot and the chip still showing the meeting. **The mirror is part of the fix, not a follow-up (#339):** `actions/clients/overview-tax.ts` was flipped in the same change so Client Overview and the tax tab cannot disagree — and note it disagreed *silently*, because both read plausible dates from the same row.

**407. Two orderings through one one-way flag: add a DISCRIMINATOR beside the flag, and never let two asks separated in TIME share one bell title (2026-08-17).** The tax track gained a second *"Skip ROI meeting"* route — `meeting_first` (detailed meeting → confirm → decision → sign → pay) alongside the original `retainer_first`. **The shape that made it cheap:** `roi_skip_mode text` sits *beside* the existing one-way `roi_meeting_skipped_at` rather than replacing or paralleling it, so both buttons still stamp the original column and **every mechanism keyed on "was it skipped?" is route-blind and was not touched** — the skipped-away rows, Tax 2's green Done 1/1, the `ready-for-tax3.ts` refusal, the `|skipped` email axis, the `overview-tax.ts` mirror. Only four FE gates and the bell sequence read the mode. **NULL reads as the original route everywhere**, so pre-column rows keep the behaviour they were created under and no backfill is needed. A parallel timestamp would have forced every one of those call sites to learn about both.

**The bell trap, which is the expensive half.** A title prefix is the WHOLE clear contract (#365/#179), so two asks that must be answered at DIFFERENT TIMES cannot share one. On `meeting_first` the planner is asked for the presentation right after the meeting, then for Client decision 1 only once the retainer is paid — and `Complete the tax plan review steps for%` is retired by a predicate that, on this route, **the presentation alone satisfies**. Raising the second ask under that title would have cleared it on the very next clear-site run: **the bell would retire itself before anyone read it.** So decision 1 got its own prefix, `Complete Client decision 1 for` (the `1` also keeps it from colliding with `Complete Client decision 2 for` under `ILIKE`). **The inverse is equally deliberate and equally safe:** `TAX_roi_skipped_hlm_ready` *shares* its title with `TAX_planner_hlm_ready` on purpose, because the two ask for the SAME thing from the same person at different points on different routes — so the existing clear site retires either with no change. **Two keys, one title = routing differs, instruction is identical. Two titles = the instructions are answered at different moments.** Get that backwards in either direction and the bell is unretirable or self-clearing.

**Three more route-aware details that are invisible until the wrong one ships.** A *dismissible FYI's message* can be as route-wrong as an action-required title is contract-critical — `TAX_retainer_paid_schedule_meeting` told Tracy the planner had been asked to schedule a meeting that had **already happened**, so auditing only `dismissible:false` bells misses it. The sweep's message must name **only** what its clear predicate accepts: naming Client decision 1 on a route where it is still locked makes the bell unperformable, while waiting on it makes the bell unretirable. And `computeTrack` is **strictly positional**, so a route that genuinely reorders steps must reorder the array in `overview-tax.ts` too — widening `applicable` only makes the steps visible, and leaving template order intact reports the wrong `next_action` for the whole pre-payment window and then raises a false skipped-step warning against the very steps that came first.

**408. The hub's VERSION and TAG stamps are written before the version and tag exist, so a correct wrap-up still leaves them stale (2026-08-17). Docs / wrap-up ordering.** `SESSION_WRAPUP.md` orders the save-point tag LAST, in Part 4D, deliberately — "so it can't be stale". But the hub's DERIVE expectations are written in **Part 1A**, three parts earlier, when the deploy has not happened and the tag does not exist. A session that follows the prompt exactly therefore stamps `(v: <today>)` against the version and tag it had *on arrival*, and ships. The 2026-08-17 ROI-skip branch did precisely this: it wrote `v749 / live-149 / backend-good-v748`, then deployed **v750** and tagged **live-150** / **backend-good-v750** — leaving three lines dated *today* carrying *yesterday's* values.

**That is worse than an old date, and the hub says why.** Its own preamble calls a stamp "last confirmed on that date… a staleness hint, not authority", and rule 3 says only stamp what you actually checked. A stamp bearing today's date asserts it WAS checked today — so the one signal a reader has for spotting drift is exactly the signal that gets falsified. The three lines are also the ones a new session reads FIRST, before running anything.

**The fix is ordering, not content:** those three lines (live function versions, `live-*` tag, `backend-good-*` tag) must be re-stamped **after** Part 4D, in the same pass that creates the tag — the DERIVE block now carries that instruction inline. Every other DERIVE expectation (action count, route pages, cron count, advisor baseline) is knowable in Part 1 and stays there. **The general shape: a document that records the result of a step must be written after that step, and a checklist that puts them in the other order will be followed correctly and still produce a wrong document.**

**409. Replacing an object in a Storage bucket is NOT a `supabase storage cp` — the CLI has no overwrite, and `--workdir` silently moves the LOCAL path root too (2026-08-17). Ops / deploy.** Three separate traps sit between "I have the new file" and "the bucket holds the new file", and the 2026-08-17 speaker-notes hot-swap (#336) hit all three in order. **(1) `cp` refuses to overwrite.** `supabase storage cp <local> ss:///bucket/<name>` onto an existing key returns `Error status 400: {"statusCode":"409","error":"Duplicate","code":"KeyAlreadyExists"}`, and `cp --help` (v2.78.1) shows **no `--overwrite` / `--upsert` / `--force` flag** — only `--cache-control`, `--content-type`, `-j`, `-r`. Uploading under a NEW name works fine, which is why the versioned-object path in #336 never meets this. **(2) The obvious workaround opens a live hole.** `storage rm` then `cp` leaves a window — as long as the upload takes, ~27 MB here — in which `tax_generate_presentation` returns its clean-but-alarming 500 *"Presentation template missing"* to whoever clicks Generate. Never do this to an object a live handler reads on demand. **The correct replace is the Storage REST update with upsert:** `PUT https://<ref>.supabase.co/storage/v1/object/<bucket>/<path>` with `Authorization: Bearer <service-role>`, `x-upsert: true` and the real content type — atomic, no window. The human path (Supabase Dashboard upload, replacing in place) is equally atomic and is what #336 already assumes; **on this machine the scripted service-role variant is blocked by the permission classifier**, so the dashboard is the practical route and the agent should hand the file over rather than fight it. **(3) `--workdir` redirects LOCAL paths as well as project discovery.** `supabase storage cp ./file.pptx ss:///bucket/x --workdir C:\vfo-edge-functions` resolves `./file.pptx` against **`C:\vfo-edge-functions`**, not your shell's cwd, and fails with `failed to open file: open ./file.pptx: The system cannot find the file specified` while looking nowhere near where you are. Either `cd` into the linked project and pass a path relative to it (`../Users/.../scratchpad/file.pptx` works and needs no staging), or stage the file inside the project — but note that `C:\vfo-edge-functions` is a protected path here, so the tidy-up `Remove-Item` is refused and staging leaves litter. Cross-ref **#336** (the template this exists to serve), **#342** (assets read BY NAME are coupled to the deploy).

**410. A generated artifact sitting in Drive is NOT evidence of what the code produces — the decks are shared writable BY DESIGN, so any of them may have been edited by a human since (2026-08-17).
