Cross-ref **#298** (the unpin and why it is never re-pinned), **#370** (the same "completed ≠ paid" read on one-time SpecRev), **#371** (why the status column was left alone), **#448** (the select-list contract that makes the template swap work).

---

**476. EVERY FILE IN BOTH REPOS IS CRLF, SO A `\n`-ANCHORED MULTI-LINE `perl`/`sed` REWRITE MATCHES NOTHING AND STILL EXITS 0 (2026-09-08, docs + JSX). ALWAYS-APPLIES (tooling).**

Both working trees are CRLF — `docs/*.md`, `*.jsx` and `*.ts` alike, which is why `git diff` keeps announcing *"LF will be replaced by CRLF the next time Git touches it"*. No editor shows you those bytes. A multi-line pattern written as `foo\n\s*bar` therefore cannot match, because the file actually holds `foo\r\n  bar`.

**The failure is silent and wears the costume of success.** `perl -0pi -e 's/…/…/'` exits **0** having changed nothing; `sed -i` says nothing; the only evidence is an empty `git diff`, which reads exactly like *"the edit was already applied"* — so the natural next move is to carry on, and the change is simply missing from the commit. Single-line substitutions are unaffected, so the trap bites hardest on the largest, least-reviewable edits.

**The rule: anchor multi-line patterns with `\r?\n`, never a bare `\n`, and never accept a rewrite's exit code as proof — confirm with `git diff --stat` on the file you meant to change, every time.** For prose files prefer the `Edit` tool, which matches literal text and fails **loudly** when the match is absent or not unique. Related encoding trap on the same files: a PowerShell redirect can prepend a **BOM**, which breaks anchored greps a second, equally invisible way — write docs with the `Edit` tool or explicit UTF-8-no-BOM .NET IO.

**477. A FLOOR WRITTEN AS "AMOUNT MUST BE > 0" SILENTLY ENCODES "A PAYMENT MUST EXIST" — WHEN "NOTHING FURTHER DUE" BECOMES LEGITIMATE, RE-WALK EVERY CONSUMER THAT READS THE AMOUNT AS PROOF OF A PAYMENT (2026-09-09, tax fee amendment).**

Relaxing `amend-fee.ts`'s implementation-fee floor from `<= 0` to `< 0` looks like a one-character validation change, but the old floor was load-bearing far outside the handler: three downstream sites had been written on the guarantee that a non-declined plan past Client decision 2 *has* an implementation payment, and none of them said so. **The charge chain** (`charge-implementation.ts`) refuses `implAmount <= 0` with a 400, and `implement-final-decision.ts` calls it straight off the client's green click — so a legitimate `$0.00` would have surfaced to the admin as a *FAILED CHARGE* on a plan where nothing was owed, the #468 shape of reporting intent instead of outcome. **The acknowledgement email** would have quoted *"$0.00"* to the client as the payment now being processed. **The step machine** (`utils/tax-plan-steps.ts`) kept *Implementation charged* / *Implementation receipt* / *Implementation revenue share* `applicable`, so the Implementation phase would have read incomplete forever, waiting on a receipt that cannot exist. All three were found by walking the amount's readers, not by any gate. The consumers that were already correct were the ones that happened to gate on `> 0` themselves (`normalize.ts`, `cancel-remaining.ts`, `card-update-shared.ts`) — which is the tell: **grep the column, then classify every reader as "gated" or "assumes a payment", and fix the second list before shipping the floor.**

**The frontend carried a FOURTH consumer that no cross-repo rule points at, and it was the one that blocked the pipeline.** `TaxPrioritiesTab.jsx`'s charge cascade is the visible twin of the step machine and was fixed alongside it (three rows → one *"No implementation fee due"* row) — that much #339 predicts. But the tab's `tax5bAipcDone` predicate completed the AI PC Admin cascade on *"the implementation revenue-share email was sent"*, and at `$0` **that email never exists**, so the bullet stayed hollow and **Tax 6 never unlocked** — a phase gate silently keyed on the existence of a payment, two screens away from the amount and named after nothing to do with it. It needed its own arm (`!(implementation_amount > 0) && implFinal is Proceed/Confirmed`), found by clicking the fixture through to the end rather than by grepping the column. **When you relax an amount floor, walk the flow to its LAST gate as well as grepping the column's readers: a completion predicate keyed on a side effect of the payment (an email sent, a receipt numbered, a leg swept) reads as workflow logic, not as money logic, and will not show up in the grep.**

**478. A FIELD THAT STORES A CHARGE DATE MUST NOT BE EDITED AS ONE — THE MEMBER HAS ALREADY PAID FOR THE PERIOD THEY ARE IN, SO THE SAFE EDIT SHAPE IS "WHEN DOES IT START" AND THE RENEWAL IS DERIVED (2026-09-09, recurring Growth Credit services).**

`gc_subscriptions.next_charge_date` is the nightly sweep's trigger: on that date the recurring sweep deducts the service's credits again and pushes the date one `billing_interval` forward. Giving an admin a field labelled *"next charge date"* therefore hands them a control whose units are not the ones they are thinking in. **`gc_redeem` deducts the credits SYNCHRONOUSLY and sets `next_charge_date` one interval out, so a subscriber is ALREADY PAID for the period they are sitting in** — the date on the row is not "when does this start", it is "when does the member pay for the NEXT one".

The near-miss is the whole gotcha. An earlier draft of `gc_update_subscription_date` stored the typed value straight into `next_charge_date`. Jake, rescheduling a **yearly** service redeemed on **2026-09-09** so that it would start on **2026-09-17**, typed `09/17/2026` — which would have charged **another 10 credits eight days later** and then annually from the wrong anchor. He caught it before clicking Save; nothing in the UI, the handler or the column name would have caught it afterwards, because a second deduction on a subscription is indistinguishable from a legitimate renewal.

**The shipped shape (user decision, "Option B"):** the body carries `start_date` = **day 1 of the current period**, and the handler stores `next_charge_date = addInterval(start_date, billing_interval)` using **the same `utils/gc-recurring.ts addInterval` the redeem path and the sweep use** — one implementation, so a month-end clamp can never differ between the writer and the reader. The dialog says *"Set the date `<service>` starts. It currently starts `<renewal minus one interval>`"*, previews *"Renews `<start + interval>`, then every month|year. No additional credits are taken."* live as the admin types, and refuses a start whose computed renewal is **already in the past** (it would charge on the very next sweep). The refusals are the other half: a non-real date, a cancelled subscription, a non-recurring service, and an optimistic `.eq("next_charge_date", <the value just read>)` that 409s if the sweep moved the row underneath the edit.

**The rule: when an admin edits a scheduling column, ask what the HUMAN is deciding, not what the column stores. If the stored value is a consequence (a charge date, a next-run date, an expiry), take the cause as input, derive the consequence with the same helper the automation uses, PREVIEW the derived value on screen, and refuse the inputs whose derived value is already spent.** A field that reads back what the automation will do is a debugging view, not an editing surface.

**479. AN ACCOUNT ID IS NOT A PAYABLE ACCOUNT: PROBE `capabilities.transfers`, PARK NON-TERMINALLY, AND STOP BELLING ONCE THE LINK HAS BEEN SENT (2026-09-09, Tax + MAP 1 revenue share — the #159 closure).**

`actions/tax/revshare.ts` and `actions/pipeline/contract-revshare.ts` both branched the member leg on a bare `!member.stripe_account_id`. That test has been wrong since Express accounts entered the system: **the admin's "Set Up Payment Details" button creates the account the instant it is clicked**, so `stripe_account_id` is populated the moment somebody ASKS the member to onboard — long before the member can receive anything. The transfer then goes out to a half-built account and Stripe refuses it with *"your destination account needs … capabilities enabled: transfers"*, which the engines booked as a generic transfer **FAILURE** — a terminal-looking status and an action-required bell, re-raised every night, describing a state no admin could fix. It fired for real on 2026-09-03 (Lisa Sheeran, `acct_1UBEcMRyIBJ7Ds0T`, plan 91's `$2,443.75` retainer leg) and sat there for six days.

**Three rules came out of it, and only the first is the obvious one.**

**(1) Probe the capability, once, from ONE helper.** `utils/connect-payout-readiness.ts connectTransfersActive(acctId, key)` GETs `/v1/accounts/{id}` and returns true **only** when `capabilities.transfers === 'active'`; no id, no key, a network error, a restricted account — all false, and it never throws, because a probe failure must not take down a payout run. `utils/specialist-revenue-payout.ts` had carried the identical probe inline since 2026-08-24; it now imports the shared one (#453 — the second copy is the one that goes stale).

**(2) "Not payable yet" is the SAME state as "no account", and both are NON-TERMINAL.** The leg parks at `AWAITING_CONNECT` (*"Awaiting Connect Setup"*), which both sweeps already retry — Tax's `isRetry` lists it, MAP 1's `resolved` test is a positive allowlist that excludes it — so the money pays itself on the first tick after the member finishes onboarding, with no human involved. A half-built account routed to `Failed` instead would have needed a hand rescue (#303: never initialise a payout leg to a terminal value when the transfer is behind a condition).

**(3) A bell's job is the ASK, so once the ask is out the bell must go quiet — and retire itself.** The held bell says *"click Set Up Payment Details"*. `members.connect_setup_email_sent_at` is stamped when that email goes and it carries the DURABLE `/payout-setup` link (#268), so a stamp means *the ask is out and still works* and there is nothing left for an admin to do but wait on the member. The branch therefore raises the bell **only when no link has been sent**, and when one has it `clearJakeFailure()`s **both** the held title and the older `Revenue share transfer FAILED — <client>` title, retiring the nightly noise the old code had been minting. The bell's MESSAGE branches (*"has not finished onboarding"* vs *"no Stripe payout account"*); its TITLE does not, because the title is the clear contract (#365/#411).

**PIP was deliberately left out, and the reason is a property of ITS sweep, not of the probe.** `actions/msm/pip-revshare.ts` writes `pip_rev_share_status='Pending'` on failure, and the 02:00 sweep's PIP pass releases **HELD** statuses only — `Pending` is excluded by design. Parking a PIP leg non-terminally would therefore STRAND it, and its failure bell is the only thing chasing it. **Before copying a "park it, the sweep will retry" pattern into a neighbouring engine, read that engine's sweep and name the status it actually picks up** — the pattern is only safe where the retry exists.

**480. A WORDING CHANGE ON A FIXED-WIDTH TEXT BOX IS A GEOMETRY CHANGE — AND DELETING SHAPES RE-DATES EVERY PER-PART SHAPE COUNT THE HANDLER ASSERTS (2026-09-09, ROI deck templates v7 then v8).**

v7 did one thing: *"Your investment in VFO Specialists:"* → *"Your investment in VFO Tax Specialists:"* on slides 24/28/29, one `<a:t>` run each (the sibling *"Your investment in VFO Services:"* was deliberately left alone, asserted three times). No token moved, no shape moved, no geometry constant changed, and it passed every assertion the handler makes. **It also shipped broken.** Shape 1528's box was fixed-width with `wrap="square"`, the seven extra characters wrapped onto a second line, and that second line overlapped the *"Plan Investment: ${{CASH_OUTLAY}}"* row beneath it — on all three slides, in the first real deck Jake generated. **Nothing in the pipeline can see this:** the token guard passes, the shape counts pass, the sum identities pass, and the renderer that decides where the text breaks is PowerPoint's, not ours.

**v8's fix is two-part on purpose.** `cx 4363200 → 5155530 EMU` is a MEASURED bound, not a rendered one (Inter is not installed on the build machine), so the width alone is an estimate; `bodyPr wrap="square" → wrap="none"` is the belt that makes wrapping impossible whatever the real metrics are. **And when you set `wrap="none"`, replace `<a:spAutoFit/>` with `<a:noAutofit/>`** — autofit lets an editor opening the deck re-fit the box's `cy`, and `cy` is exactly what the handler's Y-threshold constants (`FEE_BELOW_MIN_Y`, `SHIFT_MIN_Y`) are measured against. The sibling shape 1517 already carried `noAutofit`, which is the tell that this is the house pattern.

**The second half is the one that would have 500'd every deck.** v8 also merged each fee row's VALUE shape into its LABEL shape and deleted it — 1527 into 1526, 1531 into 1530, 1533 into 1532, 1536 into 1535 (slide 29 has only the first pair) — so a row is one text box reading *"Retainer: $15,000"*, and slide 29's merged line is still the LITERAL *"Total Fee: $0"*. Four of those deletions were in slide 24's left column, which is precisely the set `dropInvestYearsHeader()` counts: **`SHIFT_EXPECTED_2PAY` 12 → 9 and `SHIFT_EXPECTED_3PAY` 14 → 10**, and `dropFeeRow3()` now expects **ONE** shape carrying both `{{FEE_ROW3_*}}` tokens where it used to expect two carrying one each. Miss either and every single-year deck is a loud 500 in production. `FEE_ROW_SHIFT_EMU` / `FEE_BELOW_MIN_Y` / `FEE_BELOW_EXPECTED` were re-measured off the v8 bytes and are unchanged — because v8 moved no `y` and no `cy` — but they were **re-measured**, not assumed.

**The rules.** (i) **Any text change on a fixed-width box is a layout change until proven otherwise** — widen by measurement AND forbid wrapping, and look at a rendered deck, because no assertion in the package renders anything. (ii) **When a part's shape SET changes, re-derive every per-part count the handler asserts**, and have the build script print the surviving shape ids so the constants have a provenance (`make_v8.py` prints `1514,1515,1517,1523,1526,1528,1529,1530,1532,1535`). (iii) **Keep a NEGATIVE CONTROL**: the simulation runs the v8 constants against the **v7** master and must FAIL loudly in all three surgery modes. A constants change that passes against both masters is not a constants change, it is a broken assertion. (iv) Both versions took the **versioned** path (#342): these three slides carry tokens, so the hot-swap rule does not reach them — upload first, deploy second, and the prior version stays in the bucket as the rollback.

**481. TWO BELLS FOR ONE PERSON ABOUT ONE FILE — WHEN AN ACTION-REQUIRED BELL IS RAISED FOR THE SAME RECIPIENT ABOUT THE SAME EVENT, MAKE THE RAISING FUNCTION RETURN WHETHER IT FIRED, ORDER IT FIRST, AND DROP THAT RECIPIENT FROM THE FYI (2026-09-09, vault drops).**

A planner in a vault-assess group dropping a PDF into a client's vault while the assess form is still outstanding triggered **two** bells to the same assigned PF about the same file: `utils/assess-vault-stamp.ts`'s action-required *"Tax Assessment PDF added for `<client>`"* (the one that means DO SOMETHING) and `utils/vault-planner-notify.ts`'s dismissible *"`<planner>` dropped a document in `<client>`'s vault"* FYI. Both are correct in isolation; side by side the FYI is noise, and noise beside an action-required bell is how action-required bells stop being read.

**The fix is structural, not a filter.** `maybeStampAssessVaultUpload` now returns `Promise<boolean>` — **true iff it actually raised the bell**, false on every early return and on a throw — and its two callers (`actions/vault/gen-upload-url.ts`, `actions/vault/tax-admin-upload-url.ts`) pass that answer to `notifyPlannerVaultDrop` as a new sixth parameter `suppressPf` (defaulting **false**, so every other drop is byte-identical). That required **reordering the two minters**: the stamp now runs BEFORE the notify, where it used to run after. They are independent (one reads and writes tax-plan rows, the other only notifies) and both are wrapped in their own try/catch, so the swap changes nothing else — but the ordering is now load-bearing and is commented as such at both call sites.

**Suppress the RECIPIENT, not the bell.** Tracy receives the FYI and does NOT receive the action-required one, so dropping the whole FYI would leave the person who owns the vault with no signal that a file landed. `suppressPf` therefore nulls exactly one entry in the recipient list, and every non-ICG drop, every later drop and every drop once the assess form is in behaves exactly as before.

**The general rule: "did I already tell this person?" is a question only the earlier notifier can answer, so make it ANSWER — return the fact, do not re-derive it.** A second consumer re-computing the first one's conditions (is this an ICG group? is the assess form still open? is there an open plan for this group?) is a copy that will disagree the first time either side is edited. And whenever you make one notifier's output an input to another, say out loud which runs first and why it is safe to swap them.

**482. A CANCELED PaymentIntent IS NOT A FAILED PAYMENT UNLESS THE ROW WAS BOOKED AGAINST IT — Stripe creates the PI when the Checkout page OPENS and cancels it (`automatic`) when the page expires unpaid 24h later (2026-09-09, `vfo-admin-api` v822 hotfix). ALWAYS-APPLIES (Stripe / money-truth).**

**The mechanism nobody costed.** A Stripe Checkout Session in `payment` mode mints its PaymentIntent when the **page is opened**, not when the payer submits — so a client who clicks a durable link, picks *"US bank account"*, looks at the form and closes the tab has left a live PI behind with our full `metadata` on it. That session expires **24 hours** later, unpaid, and Stripe emits **`checkout.session.expired`** followed by **`payment_intent.canceled` with `cancellation_reason: 'automatic'`**. That is byte-for-byte the same event shape as the ~10-business-day micro-deposit expiry #475 widened this block to catch: same type, same reason string, same metadata. **`cancellation_reason` cannot tell an abandoned page from an expired verification** — both are `automatic` — so nothing *in the event* distinguishes "the client submitted money and it died" from "the client never touched the form".

**The incident.** Pat Hurst (client 123, `client_tax_plans` 87, retainer **$6,750**, `fee_process_version = '2026-08-25'`) opened his durable `/tax-pay` link and chose ACH **twice** on 2026-09-08 — 19:08Z and 21:09Z — without ever submitting. The expiries landed on 2026-09-09 at **13:58Z (×2, redelivered)** and **15:59Z**, each pair being `checkout.session.expired` + `payment_intent.canceled (automatic)`. The widened block resolved him through `resolveStripeFirstPaymentFailure`, found `retainer_status` **NULL**, and — because the generic first-payment branch guarded only on `target.currentStatus !== "succeeded"` — wrote **`'failed'`** and belled `FAILURE_first_payment_declined` (**notification 1898**, *"canceled (automatic)"*). `actions/tax/stripe-checkout.ts` (~line 62) treats **any truthy `retainer_status` outside the recovery states** as *"Payment already completed"*, so **his link went dead** — the one surface he had to pay through, closed by his own act of looking at it. Census across the estate: **exactly one** `retainer_status='failed'` row and **zero** `pay1_status='failed'` rows, so Pat was the only client hit. Remediated by SQL at ~18:30Z 2026-09-09 — `retainer_status` back to NULL, guarded on `retainer_payment_intent_id IS NULL` so it could not clobber a real booking — link live again, nothing re-sent. **Before #475 this event was ignored for TAX / MAP 1 entirely, so the exposure is a regression exactly one day wide.**

**Why `!== 'succeeded'` was the wrong guard and `=== 'processing'` is the right one.** `!== 'succeeded'` asks *"is this row unpaid?"* — which is true of every row that has never been touched, so it treats **absence of a payment as evidence of a failed one**. The question the branch actually needs to answer is *"was money submitted against THIS PaymentIntent?"*, and the only thing that knows is **the row's own booking**: `'processing'` is written by `checkout.session.completed` at submit time and means the debit is in flight — the micro-deposit case this widening exists for. So the fix is `cancelWithoutBooking = event.type === "payment_intent.canceled" && target?.currentStatus !== "processing"` → logged and **ignored**. `payment_failed` behaviour is untouched (that event only exists once a charge was attempted). The three late-ACH arms — MAP 1 P2-4, Tax implementation, Tax final retainer — already required `=== "processing"` and were never exposed; the generic first-payment branch was the only one that had no booking test, because until #475 it had only ever seen `payment_failed`.

**The durable-link semantics were never broken — a stray terminal status closed them.** `/tax-pay` and `/pay` are deliberately re-openable: the page decides what is payable by reading the status column, and it reads a non-recovery value as *"already done"*. That is correct, and it is exactly why **any write of a terminal status is a write to the client's access**, not just to a report. A status column that gates a client-facing link has no "harmless" wrong value.

**The rule: before treating ANY Stripe terminal event as a failure, name the column that proves the row was booked against that PaymentIntent and gate on it — the row's own booking is the evidence a payment was submitted, and the event is not.** Corollaries: (i) an event's arrival proves a Stripe object reached a state, never that *our* row participated in it; (ii) `cancellation_reason` is a label, not a discriminant — do not build a branch on `automatic` vs anything else; (iii) this is **#431/#444**'s positive-allowlist rule pointed at the write side — gate on the one state you mean (`=== 'processing'`), never on "not the one state I know is safe"; (iv) Stripe **redelivers** (**#327**, no event-id dedupe here), so a wrong write lands three times before anyone looks. Cross-ref **#475** — the widening this narrows.

**483. html2pdf.app REFUSES CONCURRENT RENDERS WITH A 403, AND A PDF RENDER THAT DIES BETWEEN THE NUMBER RESERVATION AND THE EMAIL LEAVES A PAID ROW WITH ITS ACCOUNTING NUMBERS RESERVED BUT NOT STAMPED (2026-09-10, production). ALWAYS-APPLIES (money / documents).**

**The mechanism is a burst, not a bug in any one handler.** `membership-sweep-daily` (jobid 16, 12:00 UTC) charges every due plan in one pass, and each successful charge chains `automation_MEMBERSHIP_invoicereceipt`, which renders **two** PDFs through `https://api.html2pdf.app/v1/generate`. Four members charged inside **one second** on 2026-09-10 means **eight renders in about four seconds**, and html2pdf.app answered one of them with **HTTP 403** — `membership invoice/receipt: receipt PDF generation FAILED — status 403`, **12:00:08.787Z**. **The key was fine.** The other three members' invoice *and* receipt renders succeeded seconds either side of it on that same key, and a sweep of 24h of logs found **exactly one** PDF 403. A 403 whose neighbours on the same credential succeed is a **rate/concurrency refusal wearing an auth status code** — do not read it as a bad key.

**Why one refusal is expensive.** `actions/membership/invoice-receipt.ts` allocates its numbers **first** (`allocateDocNumber`, and since #282 it verifies the reservation actually landed), renders **second**, drafts **third**, files the vault PDFs **fourth**, and stamps `invoice_number` / `receipt_number` / `docs_emailed_at` on the ledger row **last**. `renderPdf` had **no retry**, so the 403 returned a 500 from the render step — after the reservation, before everything else. **Ryan Yeaton (member 59334, plan 61, `member_payment_schedule` row 381, $792 card)** was therefore left with: the money taken, `document_numbers` **496 / 497** holding `INV-59334-0001` / `REC-59334-0001` against his plan, and the ledger row carrying **no numbers, no `docs_emailed_at`, and no vault documents**. **Nothing was going to retry it** — Stripe already had its 200 (the failure was two chain hops downstream of the webhook, and #327's redelivery only fires when *we* time out, never on a prompt 500), and the sweep re-drafts nothing. **#282's guard cannot see this**, either: it fires when the *reservation* fails, and here the reservation succeeded — it was the render after it that died. **A reservation is a SPEND**: the number is consumed whether or not a document ever exists.

**The repair recipe — two steps, and the order is the whole trick.** (1) **Stamp the already-reserved numbers onto the ledger row FIRST.** The handler reads `primary.invoice_number || null` and `primary.receipt_number || null` before it allocates, so a row carrying the numbers makes a re-run **reuse** them; a blank row makes it mint `-0002` and orphan `-0001` forever. Leave `docs_emailed_at` **NULL** — it is the handler's own "already sent" early-return (line ~100), so it must stay unset for the re-run to do any work. (2) **Re-run the action through the cron job's OWN stored command**: a `DO $$` block that reads `command` from `cron.job` for **jobid 16**, `replace()`s the JSON body with this row's arguments, and `EXECUTE`s the resulting `net.http_post` — **the service-role key never leaves the database** and never passes through a transcript. This is the same manoeuvre recorded in the **2026-09-02** CHANGELOG entry for re-drafting deleted tax-revshare sweep emails (there: NULL the once-only stamp, then execute jobid 5's stored command). Ryan's re-run at **14:40:53Z** produced the draft and both vault PDFs and kept the `-0001` numbers.

**The fix is one helper, not seventeen retries.** `utils/html2pdf.ts` exports **`renderHtmlToPdf(html, label, extra?): Promise<ArrayBuffer | null>`** and is now the **only** place in `vfo-admin-api` that names the endpoint. **3 attempts**, waiting **1500 ms then 3000 ms with ±20% jitter** (the jitter matters precisely because the failure mode is a simultaneous burst — un-jittered retries re-collide), retrying **403 / 429 / 5xx** and thrown fetch errors and logging **every** failed attempt as `html2pdf <label>: attempt N/3 FAILED — status S`. **Other 4xx (400 bad HTML, 401 bad key, 404, 413 too large) do not retry** — they answer identically every time, so a retry only delays the caller's error. The `ArrayBuffer | null` contract is deliberate: all twenty call sites kept their existing null/throw handling, their own log lines and their own `return json(..., 500)`.

**The rule: any third-party render or upload sitting BETWEEN a number reservation and the send needs a bounded retry — and any repair of one must REUSE the reservation, never mint a fresh one.** Corollaries: (i) if a step cannot be retried, do not reserve before it; if it must be reserved first, the repair path is part of the design, not an afterthought; (ii) treat a 403 from a metered third party as a concurrency signal whenever the same credential is succeeding around it; (iii) a handler that is chained by a webhook has **no** automatic second chance — the 200 is already gone (**#327**); (iv) the burst is created by the sweep, so widening a sweep's per-tick batch widens this exposure. Cross-ref **#282** (`allocateDocNumber` returns its candidate even when the INSERT fails), **#280** / **#281** (the membership document engine and its card-fee rule), **#327** (redelivery, and why a fast 500 gets none). `see #483`.
